# Development Plan (FC_NEXT_STEPS.md)

Living plan for the Fin project — open Change Requests, known issues, ongoing improvement themes, and a chronological history. Companion to [FC_PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md](project-description.md), which describes the *current* state of the project.

**Single-source rule (2026-06-12):** each CR's full spec, as-built detail, and decision log live **only in its CR file** under [CRs/](../cr/). This document carries status + sequencing + open items (a few lines per CR); [../cr/README.md](../cr/README.md) carries the one-line roll-up. Don't restate CR detail here. (The pre-restructure full text is archived at [Archive/FC_NEXT_STEPS_FULL_2026-06-12.md](../archive/FC_NEXT_STEPS_FULL_2026-06-12.md).)

---

## 1. Change Requests

### 1.1 Open / In-Progress

<a id="cr083"></a>
- **CR083 — Budget: the Latest Estimate (LE). 🔨 IN-PROGRESS — P0a + P0b LIVE (v3.31.0), migration 072.**
  `/budget-le` — create an LE, read it in **Chart-of-Accounts order** with the categories rolled up
  (117 rows), open any category's **month-by-month worksheet** and type the estimate months. Plus the
  **FY-landing strip** on `/budget-vs-actual` and a **deterministic deviations section**. Nine
  endpoints under `/budget/le`. Full record in [CR083 §17](../cr/cr-083-budget-latest-estimate.md).
  ⚠️ **Finalise and recut are NOT built, and before finalise ships the full-year budget must be
  snapshotted onto the LE** — `BUDGET FY` and the variance are read **live** from `budget_entries`
  today, which is right for a draft and wrong for a frozen artefact, since the owner edits the budget
  in-year (30 rows since April, 22 backdated). That needs a column, so a migration. Warnings
  L1/L4/L6, P1's accept path and drift **L2**, and §11.4's seed-next-year all remain.
  **Design history:**
  Both review passes returned *revise* and the technical pass **falsified three of the CR's own
  headline figures** (recorded in its §16, not quietly patched); **all ten owner decisions then
  resolved in one `/question` pass** (§15). **Runs in parallel with the CR082 TY2025 remainder, in a
  git worktree** — Known Issue #23 is live and the only contended files are `config/routes.jsx` and
  `utils/excelExporter.js`. **P0a + P0b committed together.**
  A full-calendar-year LE — actuals to a cut plus estimate months seeded from the budget — in **its own
  two tables**, because **eleven functions plus a view** read `budget_entries` ignoring `version_id` and a second
  budget *version* would double the forecast base year and every budget report.
  **Board order after round-2 sign-off:** tag prod → **CR082 P0a** (the `coa-traits` leak — security,
  goes first regardless) → **CR083 P0a** in the worktree, merged as its own shippable unit →
  **CR082 P2** freeze-on-file + TY2024-as-filed (the deadline items) → **CR083 P0b**, targeting the
  **September close** → CR083 P1 → P2 only once the CR §11.4 gaps are closed. CR066 P0, CR064 P2 and
  CR060 stay behind all of it.
  **P0a is separable and worth doing on its own:** the FY-landing figure is arithmetically
  `budget_FY + (actual_YTD − budget_YTD)` = `−137,555 + 34,556 = −102,999`, both terms already on
  `/budget-vs-actual` — so a KPI there plus the scope exclusion delivers the headline in about half a
  day with **no schema at all** (it reuses `accounts.is_transfer`). ⚠️ **It is deliberately additive
  and does NOT re-base the existing page** — `getCashFlow`'s transfer exclusion is a *name-string*
  match that misses `Return of Capital` and `Valuation - Historical` and never excludes
  `Unrealized G/L`, so re-basing would move numbers the owner reads weekly (CR083 §11.2).
  Two findings the CR turned up that stand **whether or not it is ever built** — see §2.1/§7 of the CR:
  **(i)** `POST /budget/versions/:id/copy` accepts an **arbitrary `budget_year`, including the same
  one, with no guard** (`server/src/v2/routes/budget.js:57-69`) while eleven readers ignore `version_id`
  — one mis-click silently doubles the forecast base year; **(ii)** the 2026 budget's dimensions are
  badly incomplete — **72 rows / −86,796 have no category** and **32 rows / −99,191 have no account**
  (only −38,364 has both), which already distorts `/budget-vs-actual` variance today and made the
  owner's own allowance question unanswerable by account.
  **The owner's answers cut about a third of the build.** The LE is **primarily a landing number**,
  secondarily an input to next year's budget, and **explicitly not a frozen series** — so the Compare
  tab, the Versions tab, the LE-to-LE walk, the trend chart, `PY_SEASON`, `L11` and the two
  `proposed_*` sources are all out. The grid narrowed to **one YTD ACTUAL column + Aug–Dec editable
  (ten columns, no modes, no sticky column, no horizontal scroll)**, which removes the CR082
  print-clipping hazard at its root rather than mitigating it; proposals ship as an **inline advisory
  with no accept button** (CR081 measured the accept flow at 0/15, twice). **P2 gains what was never
  in the CR: seed next year's budget from an LE.** The last open question closed on evidence — all 72
  uncategorised allowance rows are from the 2026-01-29 import and **none** added since, while the
  owner has added **131 itemised categorised rows on top of them**: legacy, not incremental.

<a id="cr082"></a>
- **CR082 — A Taxes section, first form: FinCEN Form 114 (FBAR). ✅ COMPLETE 2026-08-16 — every phase built and every open item closed.** P0b+P1+P2+P3 LIVE ON PROD (migration 070 dev + prod 2026-08-15, code deployed 08-16); **TY2025 complete in the app: 16 lines, every one carrying a figure** (13 computed, 3 typed), aggregate $2,627,821, threshold exceeded. **The seven remaining items closed and deployed 2026-08-16** ([§11c](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md#11c-the-remaining-items-closed-2026-08-16)): **P0a** the `coa-traits` account-number leak · **year-scoped `review_state`** (migration **071**) · the **§12b.14 carry-in guard** · **freeze-on-file wired to a button, plus amendments** · **TY2024 seeded as filed** (31 lines, $1,462,652, matching the client copy) · **XLSX** · **Part I filer block** (TIN/DOB, Postgres only). **Four defects surfaced while closing them, none visible to the suite:** P0a's frontend half would have wiped real account numbers on any unrelated rename (and every selected account at once in a multi-edit) had it followed this roadmap's own one-line prescription; **item 15a blocked its own filing** (an unknown maximum sat in `needs_attention`, which `freezeYear` refuses, so the only way to file one was to override the guard protecting every other line); the filed-vs-recomputed diff **said "nothing has moved" when nothing had been checked** — found within a minute of seeding TY2024, which is exactly the verification §9 said that seed would buy; and the typed-figure dialog erased `manual_reason` on a re-edit, which is why the three TY2025 typed lines carry no source. **Deployed to prod 2026-08-16** — migration **071** applied first (deploy Step 2b), TY2024 seeded on prod, and the running API re-checked: no full account number for any of 230 accounts. ⚠️ **Nothing has been rendered in a browser** — no renderer on this host, and §11b's eight owner-found defects are the standing evidence that green gates and a correct page are different claims.
  ⏱ TARGET TY2025, DUE 2026-10-15 (61 days).** Owner is on the automatic extension (confirmed
  2026-08-15), so this is date-bound: **P1+P2 is the shippable unit** and the **freeze ships with
  P2**, not after it — you can file by transcribing from the browser, so the export is the
  convenience while the freeze is the only thing that makes filed figures recoverable. **Both
  review passes returned *revise*; the technical pass falsified four of the CR's own load-bearing
  numbers, all recorded in its §12** rather than quietly patched — the worst being the CR
  converting PLN at a stale ≈0.2344 in §5 while §4 argued that a plausible rate asserted as an
  authoritative one is this project's most-repeated failure. **P0 is security, not features:**
  `GET /api/v2/util/coa-traits` (`routes/util/coa.js:24-31`) already returns `AccountNumber` for
  every active account to any unauthenticated caller and `COATreeRow.jsx:78` renders it, so
  "masked in the UI" was false as drafted — that endpoint and nginx basic-auth land **before any
  number is entered**. ~~Also seeds **TY2024 as `filed`**~~ — **this never happened.** `tax_fbar_filings` holds one row,
  `(2025, 0, draft)`. §9 called it "the cheapest correctness gate available" and it was silently
  skipped; the TY2024 figures live only in gitignored `Samples/Tax/` worksheets. Still worth doing:
  it turns a future run into a verification against a known-good output.
  Full detail: [CR082](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md). A new `Taxes` nav category whose
  first page produces the FBAR worksheet: which accounts are foreign (an explicit per-account
  designation, **not** a currency filter — `Wise - USD` is foreign, a EUR property is not an
  account), their account numbers and institution blocks entered **once and remembered**, and the
  maximum value each reached during the calendar year. **Fintable carries no account numbers** —
  verified against `bank-feed/contracts/v1/README.md` and both converters; the feed's only
  institution datum is `institution_name` per *connection*. Three findings shape it. **(1) The
  maximum must be taken over end-of-day balances, never a row-ordered running sum:** verified on
  prod, `PKO`'s 2025 max is **631,678.72 PLN ($175,842)** end-of-day against **793,548** under
  `ORDER BY (date, id)` — what `findLedgerWithRunningBalance` actually does — and **4,393,629**
  under `(date, amount DESC)`. 2025-06-23 saw 4,294,000 PLN in and out, net 0.00; transactions
  carry a date and no timestamp, so the naive answer is not one wrong number but a **range chosen
  by an arbitrary tie-break**, +$45,060 to +$1,047,226 of maximum that never existed. **(2) It must
  include the January-1 carry-in** — 12 accounts carry in above their in-year maximum, including
  `Wise - USD` (1,718.27 vs 1,552.20, the CR's own flagship reportable example) and
  `United Beverages` (30,425,000 vs 27,642,000 PLN), and eight with no in-year activity return NULL,
  which must never render as 0. **(3) History is not stable** —
  `calibrate()` rewrites `opening_balance`, one constant across **every** historical date
  (migration [069](../cr/cr-080-feed-accrual-reconcile-mode.md) is the case study), and `mtm`/`accrue`
  insert back-dated rows; so a filed year is **frozen into a snapshot**, not recomputed, and
  reopening shows filed-vs-recomputed rather than overwriting. Conversion uses the **Treasury
  Dec-31 rate**, prefilled from our frankfurter series and visibly marked as a prefill until the
  owner pastes the real one — **and the direction is a trap: Treasury quotes foreign-per-USD where
  `exchange_rates` stores USD-per-foreign**, so EUR and GBP are plausible in either direction and
  pasting the wrong one moves the maximum ~38% toward under-reporting; the field states the
  direction and a plausibility gate refuses a rate far from the prefill. Exports are **XLSX +
  print-PDF worksheets** (owner decision, 2026-08-15) — reusing `excelExporter.js` and
  `xlsx@0.18.5`, no new library; BSA batch XML and filling FinCEN's Adobe-only XFA 114 are both out
  of scope, with reasons. Six owner (not design) calls in §8 — the Polish operating companies, the
  CVC funds / `PKO TFI`, the PLN credit cards, joint accounts, signature authority over accounts
  not in fin at all, and Form 114a — **none of which blocks the build**, since report-only lines
  and the typed-override path hold every shape they can take.

<a id="cr080"></a>
- **CR080 — The `accrue` reconcile mode. ✅ COMPLETED — v3.28.0 (2026-08-11), migrations 065–067.**
  Full detail: [CR080](../cr/cr-080-feed-accrual-reconcile-mode.md). `Wise - USD` (8) and
  `WISE - EUR` (13) are **Wise Assets** balances — a money-market fund, not cash. The feed
  delivers the monthly `ACCRUAL_CHECKOUT … Assets service fee` but **never the yield those fees
  are charged against**, so fin drifts below the feed a little every day with no transaction
  behind it. Neither existing mode fits: `calibrate` folds a *recurring* flow into a constant at
  opening (migration 046's documented failure), and `mtm` books the right shape to
  `Unrealized G/L` — an **expense** category, so yield never reaches income or anything
  tax-facing. **Part A shipped** (migrations **065** + **066**, prod 2026-08-11). **Part B built**
  — third mode, per-mapping category with no default, and a guard on **implied annualised yield**
  rather than mtm's %-of-balance test, which a missed $500 transfer (12%) would have walked
  straight through. 14 tests; the two carrying the safety argument are **falsified against the
  unfixed code**.
  - **Building it corrected the design twice, both ways that would have shipped a lie.** The draft
    seeded the accounts to `accrue` in the schema migration as "inert" — it was not: any
    unrecognised mode fell through to `calibrate`, and migrations land *before* code, so a
    Reconcile click would have destroyed Part A. Split into **067** (column, pre-deploy) and the mode
    flip moved out of migrations entirely — it is configuration; an unknown mode now throws. And the draft's booking rule selected
    **zero** observations — this feed labels rows at or *ahead* of their sync, so "synced after the
    day ended" never happens; now `LEAST(balance_date, synced_on − 1)`.
  - **Remaining:** the first real run happens at **month-end** — today both accounts refuse, correctly. Dev's ledger lacks 065–067 (applied prod-first); `sync-db-prod-to-dev.sh` resolves it. Against prod today both
    accounts correctly **refuse**, one per guard, and the feed's day-jitter (±1.5) exceeding one
    day of accrual (0.40) is why this belongs on the monthly reconcile, not the weekly loop.
  - **Not reviewed** — neither cr-technical-reviewer nor cr-signoff-pm has seen it.

<a id="cr079"></a>
- **CR079 — The plan in today's money. IN-PROGRESS: deflator core built, wiring not started.**
  Full detail: [CR079](../cr/cr-079-real-terms-view.md). Every forecast figure is **nominal** and
  nothing says so — Base's 4,674,650 at 2062 is about **1,921,719** in 2026 money (a 2.43×
  factor). Increment 1 is a pure, 16-tested deflator that reads the same step function the engine
  does, inflates pre-base years so the 2025 actual is comparable, and returns **null** when a
  scenario has no inflation so the view disables rather than claiming nominal = real.
  **Increment 2 is deliberately not half-done:** money reaches the Review by four independent
  paths and a partially deflated page is worse than a nominal one, because both look like money.

<a id="cr078"></a>
- **CR078 — Selling costs on a disposal. PROPOSED, needs §6 decisions first.** Full detail:
  [CR078](../cr/cr-078-disposal-selling-costs.md). Every disposal books **gross** proceeds: no
  agent fee, transfer tax, legal or broker cost anywhere in the model. Property sales total
  **5,566,755** on Base (3–6% = 167K–334K) and business sales **5,445,368** (1–3% = 54K–163K).
  **The base year matters most** — its 1,239,753 folds into the sweep's opening cash, which is
  pinned every year, so the error rides the whole horizon (CR049 §1). **Per-ROW**, because
  `CVC`'s 2,033,048 of capital returns must stay at zero while a property sale carries 3–6%.
  The cost comes off cash **and** off the gain — netting only the first would tax money never
  received. **§6 has four questions to settle before code**, chiefly netted-silently vs shown.

<a id="cr077"></a>
- **CR077 — Cash Health panel: integrity vs. assumption advice. PROPOSED, not started.** Full
  detail: [CR077](../cr/cr-077-assumption-advisor-tab.md). Owner-requested 2026-08-09 and
  **deliberately sequenced after CR076 §8 step 6** — three of D2–D6 change what the numbers ARE,
  and advice built over figures under repair would teach the owner to trust a value about to move.
  Two tabs: **(a) Integrity** (the model is not doing what your data says) and **(b) Assumptions
  to consider** (the engine is faithfully applying an input worth a second look). The new tab is
  the missing home for [CR076 §11](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md) — `Social Security` at
  0.25 × inflation against a statutory full-CPI COLA, `Purchases` at 0.5, `OCME` at −20. Reuses
  CR074's dismissal machinery wholesale, including **expiry when the figures change**. Six
  deterministic advisories specified for day one. The local LLM is a *second* stage under one
  constraint: **nothing generated may state a number or assert what the engine does.**

<a id="cr076"></a>
- **🔴 CR076 — Forecast model review (2026-08-09). §8 COMPLETE and fully deployed** — steps 1–3 in
  v3.20.0, steps 4–5 in v3.21.0, step 6 (D2–D6) in v3.22.0 (§14–§18). **§7 and §11 hold open owner
  decisions**, and [CR077](../cr/cr-077-assumption-advisor-tab.md) is now unblocked. Full detail:
  [CR076](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md). Five parallel reviewers over the engine, the
  warning rules and a prod copy, plus perturbation scenarios.
  - **§2 is a P0 documentation correction and is APPLIED.** The published net-worth figures came
    from a SQL roll-up that read `Bank Accounts` — a per-module **annual cash movement** — as a
    balance. **The app was never wrong; the roll-up was.** Correct at 2062: Base **4,398,898** ·
    Buy Business **9,474,620** · Downside **1,881,988** · Upside **7,733,471** · SRQ **−829,508**.
    Error up to 894K and **not constant**, so comparisons were contaminated too. CR075 §6/§10 and
    `status.md` annotated. *Rule earned: a forecast figure in any document comes from the app's own
    exported functions or the engine, never a SQL re-derivation written for the occasion.*
  - **§3 — 3 more warning rules wrong** (R7 false on all 20 rows it fires on; W2's headline
    double-counts a cumulative quantity; R5's loss branch claims an offset the engine does not do),
    making **5 of 8**. Plus: the cash-sweep audit CSV is written **before** the sweep is re-run to
    convergence, so it describes a forecast that was discarded.
  - **§4 — 8 money-moving defects**, largest first: D1 the convergence loop's stale copy of the
    growth formula (−39,715, one line); D2 the sweep's opening cash uses `closing_balance` (11,277);
    D3 foreign cost basis re-translated yearly (−51,659); D4 base-year tax still reads the typed
    amount; D5 a stream's final year halved twice; D6 same-year gains and losses never net;
    D7 an empty inflation list silently yields 0% for 36 years; D8 a PeriodStart−1 assumption is discarded.
  - **§5 — two labels teach the wrong model:** the growth hint's clauses are swapped (70 of 110
    streams carry a multiplier), and a yield stream has no rate field at all.
  - **§7 — owner decisions:** price the cash, selling costs on disposals, a nominal-vs-real view,
    `OCME` at −20, and the 12-of-15 base-year-vs-first-forecast-year gap.
  - **§8 is the fix order.** §9 lists what was not independently re-verified (D3/D4/D5).

<a id="cr070"></a>
<a id="cr071"></a>
- **CR070 + CR071 — ✅ SHIPPED in v3.15.0 (2026-08-05).** Full detail in
  [CR070](../cr/cr-070-module-inputs-by-type.md) / [CR071](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md)
  and the §1.2 release entry. **Three things stay open and are not carried by either CR file's status:**
  - ✅ **APPLIED TO PROD 2026-08-06** — Base's 2062 net worth −31.3%, CVC's NAV and 2033 exit
    unmoved, 2027 tax −34,800. Full result: [CR071 §7](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md#7-the-4-data-edits--applied-to-prod-2026-08-06).
    *The record of the decision, as it stood:* **Dry run DONE 2026-08-05; the mechanics work and
    the owner's re-confirmation is what is missing ([CR071 §6](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md)).**
    Measured on a prod copy: **§4's direct predictions hold exactly** — 2027 tax +25,800.00 in all five
    scenarios *to the cent*, CVC income −27,898 tapering to −5,232 across 2027–2033, and CVC's NAV,
    disposals and 2033 exit **0.00 every year**. **But the cascade is ~20× what §4 quoted**: less income
    means less cash swept, compounding for thirty years — **P&L −2,866,707 and net worth at 2062
    −1,992,856 across the five scenarios** (≈ −400K to −640K each). Probably correct rather than a defect
    (the plan was compounding money that does not exist) but the owner decided on ≈158K + ≈33.5K, two
    orders of magnitude smaller. **Also learned: the UI cannot make the Fidelity half of this edit at
    all** — the card hides Amount in yield mode and the payload sends the hidden value straight back — so
    it must be scripted through `PUT /modules/:id`. Only three BASE modules need editing (80/93/94); no
    variant overrides them, so the sync propagates.
  - **The original wording, retained because it is still the rule:** these edits MOVE NUMBERS. Remove CVC Fund VIII's
    `yield` stream (a PE fund's realizations are its `Dispose` rows; the yield was a second, phantom
    distribution — ≈22,650/yr double-counted 2027–2033), and clear the vestigial typed amounts on
    `Fidelity Fixed Income` (46,000) and `Fidelity Stocks` (40,000), which their own card hides in
    yield mode while the base-year deferred-tax block still reads them (≈25,800 of Period-1 tax per
    scenario). Both owner-answered 2026-08-05. **Apply to a throwaway prod copy first with a
    line-by-line delta report, then prod, then regenerate all five scenarios — for these two the
    sums gate INVERTS: an unchanged number is the bug.**
  - **P6 (the original cut) stays cut:** a **selling cost on disposals** (no input on any of prod's
    21; US ≈6%, Spain ≈10–13%) and a **PE total commitment** (so `unfunded = commitment − Σ Invest`
    can be warned against sweep capacity). Two fields and a migration; revisit once CR071's warnings
    have shown whether they matter. *(The "P6" in CR070's as-built table is a different, QA-driven
    fix — the flow-module `Account` — not this.)*
  - **Two projections kept by hand have now drifted the same way twice in two days** — `HasValuation`
    (v3.14.2), then `CashSweepPriority`/`CashSweepTarget` (v3.15.0): present in the module LIST
    query, absent from the DETAIL the editor loads from, so the form was guessing at state it should
    have been told. Deriving both from one source is the obvious follow-up and is **not** done.

<a id="cr069"></a>
- **CR069 — [Forecast streams: one Modules section](../cr/cr-069-forecast-streams.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE · v3 · P0 v3.13.1 · P1+P2 v3.14.0 · P3 v3.14.1; migrations 057–060, all applied dev + prod.*
  - **P3 as built (migration 060; `e65fb5c`) — the contract step, live in v3.14.1.** Stream cards in the Modules editor, ~2,770 lines of `FCExp*` deleted, four tables + eleven columns + `expense_pct` + `has_valuation`'s DEFAULT dropped, the legacy write fields and the read-side projection retired. **Net −3,875 lines.** Gate: sums identical to the cent on a prod copy **with the tables dropped** (4,030 / 0). The one thing P3 got wrong — deleting `FCExpConfirmDeleteModal`, which three other pages use — was caught by the **production build alone**: lint does not resolve imports and those pages have no unit test. Also fixed the e2e suite's root cause (subshell pid; see the closed Known Issue).
  - **P2 as built (migrations 058/059; `d5d8eb7`, `5a91e9d`, `1b1d3b8`+): LIVE ON PROD (v3.14.0).** One stream evaluator replaces three; `fcbuilder-incexp.js` deleted; 60 Expenditure items are now modules with `has_valuation = FALSE` and one stream; `/incomeexpense` answers 410 and the Expenditures step left the nav (6 → 5) because the engine stopped reading that table in the same deploy. **Gate: per-(scenario, account, year) `forecast_entries` sums identical TO THE CENT — 4,030 rows / 0 differing on a prod copy, 3,798 / 0 on dev.** 803 backend · 396 frontend · 7 e2e · six ratchets · 59-file chain from empty. **Eleven defects caught before prod, by four different mechanisms.** *The gate:* a recursion seeded at index 0 while a valuation module's axis starts at its `base_date` — `Property Costs`, `Barkeria Income` and `UB Income` vanished entirely; three Downside overrides re-pointed but not translated; one Upside override naming `income_amount`, a column the engine stops reading, which would have applied cleanly to nothing for 36 years; `getBaseYearValues` still reading the retired table. *Dev, where the prod copy was clean:* an `income_pct` override moved 504 rows, and behind it `syncEntity` **silently dropping** unknown patch keys and `scheduleEqualsBase` ignoring the nested rows of the two-level schedule (it destroyed the same override twice) — both now fail loud, migration 059 translates the rest. *Security review:* `replaceModuleStreams` had no CR050 variant interception (a module edited in a variant vanished on the next sync); `HasValuation` accepted and applied nowhere, so **no API path could create a flow module**; retired columns still written, re-breaking 058's own post-condition on the first variant save; AI Review's apply path writing a dead column and a dead table while reporting success. *Code review:* two that reproduce today — auto-adjust's row-constructor `IN` meant `('a', NULL) IN (('a', NULL))` is NULL, so a line-less expense (`Sarasota House`) could never be cut, and **`FCReview` called the now-410 `/incomeexpense` from the live bundle** with the error swallowed, silently losing graph point-adjust — plus the convergence loop reading module columns the write path had stopped maintaining, stream USD conversion moved after the `fxrates[0]` override, and the CR041 gate losing `pct_of_value`. **Two of my own comments were false and are corrected in place:** the float-ordering rationale (IEEE-754 addition is *commutative*) and the `-0` one. **Also found: the e2e suite had been testing a server orphaned on :3998 since July 14** — three weeks against stale code (Known Issue below). **Prod conditions:** `migrate.js --dry-run` must report APPLY 057/058/059, not BASELINE (prod's ledger lacks 057 — applied by hand with `psql -f`); and the Step 2b→3 window runs the old engine on backfilled data, so deploy idle then force-sync the variants, regenerate all five, and re-run the gate.
  - **P1 as built (migration 057, applied dev + prod 2026-08-04):** `forecast_streams` + `forecast_stream_changes` + `forecast_modules.has_valuation`, all **inert** — zero readers, zero writers, and inertness *proved* rather than argued: a prod copy regenerated before and after the migration gives **4,030 sum rows and 158 labels, byte-identical**. Migration review caught four things before it touched a database, three of them silent. **The CR's own `UNIQUE (module_id, direction, fc_line_id)` constrains nothing when `fc_line_id` is NULL** — the identical trap P0 had just paid for on `forecast_entries`, written into the fix for it — now two partial indexes, plus a third taking Decision 3's one-yield-per-module rule off the route (which `copyScenario`/`syncVariant`/auto-adjust bypass) and into the database. The magnitude CHECK now covers `amount_usd` too, because the two source populations use **opposite sign conventions** (inc/exp stores an expense negative on 33 of 48 rows; modules store it positive on 30 of 30). The `pg_constraint` post-condition is schema-qualified — unqualified it reads 4 and aborts on the second schema, and CR027 is schema-per-tenant on `main`. **The best catch is a comment:** the backfill instruction frozen into this append-only file said "negate expense-direction rows", which is right for the money flags and **wrong for `Percent %`** — a *rate*, whose sign is a direction of change, not of money. `Children` carries **−100% in 2032** (the kids leave, the expense goes to zero); negated it doubles. **58 of 113 rows**, and neither §6.4's assertion nor the sum gate would have caught it. Verified: 57-file chain on an empty Postgres, idempotent, fast default (no rewrite), every constraint probed with live inserts.
  - **P0 as built:** inc/exp entries and their audit trail are keyed on the **item's name**, not the FC line it posts to — `Retirement Home`, `Car Purchase Chris` and `Social Security` stop filing under `Living Expenses` / `One-Off Items` / `Total Salary`. **Gate on a real prod copy** (pg_dump → scratch DB on the dev Postgres, all five scenarios regenerated before and after): **4,030 (scenario, account, year) sum rows, zero differing**; entry counts identical; module labels 143 → 158, exactly the 15 expected and 0 lost. 791 backend tests (788 → 791), no frontend change. The old "last-write-wins ON CONFLICT" comment was false in *both* halves — the clause has never fired (`entry_type` is never written and NULLs are distinct in a Postgres unique index), so rows were always additive and only attribution was lost — and now says so. Checked rather than assumed: the `Tax` item emits no entries in *either* run because it carries a **−100% Percent change in 2027** that zeroes its series permanently; pre-existing and deliberate. **Prod's stored entries keep the old labels until the five scenarios are regenerated** — sums identical either way, so the regenerate is an owner call at release, not a deploy side effect. Owner-directed: collapse the Expenditures/Modules split so everything is managed through Modules. A module becomes *identity + optional valuation + N first-class **streams*** (direction · FC line · mode `amount|yield|pct_of_value|derived` · signed change schedule `Percent %/Fixed $/One-Off $/Spread %` · CR046 window · CR047 tax on income streams); every `forecast_income_expense` item is then a module with `has_valuation = FALSE` and one stream, and the engine keeps **one** stream evaluator instead of three divergent ones — CR064 P6's steps table is a re-implementation of the Fixed $ change migration 001 already had (math verified identical). Streams-as-rows answers CR064 §5 structurally (nothing hidden to go stale); `has_valuation` retires the CR041 "MV 0 = never owned" trap that forced the split. Retires step 4 (6 → 5), ~2,770 lines of `FCExp*`, four tables, the `incexp` variant entity type. **P0 ships first and alone:** inc/exp entries label by *account*, so three prod items (`Retirement Home`, `Car Purchase Chris`, `Social Security`) are invisible under their own names — and the "last-write-wins ON CONFLICT" comment is false (`entry_type` NULL, NULLs never conflict; rows are additive). **Gate everywhere: per-(account, year) `forecast_entries` sums identical to the cent, all five scenarios, prod copy** (exact SQL in CR §9.1). Key review catches now baked in: change amounts stay **signed** (20 prod Fixed $ rows are deliberate expense *reductions* — `abs()` would invert them); the backfill rides P2's deploy (nothing dual-writes, so an early backfill goes stale on the first edit); at P2 the old `/incomeexpense` write routes **410** and the step leaves the nav (no silent writes into the retired model between P2 and P3); the base-year deferred income tax (fcbuilder-module.js:670-689) is the one live builder divergence and is keyed on `has_valuation`; `copyScenario` and the six hand-kept column/schedule lists are in scope. **Sequencing (PM sign-off): CR064 P2/P4/P5/P10 build behind CR069 P2** — `copyScenario` is in this CR's scope, and the annual close is not needed before the 2026→2027 boundary; CR064 owner-decision items may proceed. **Two owner sign-offs at kickoff:** Decision 9's variant-inheritance coarsening (affects the 3 live incexp overrides), and whether the three yield-mode modules' dead typed amounts + their Period-1 tax survive long-term (CR §6.1 preserves them; deferred decision).

<a id="cr068"></a>
- **CR068 — [Mobile Actuals Search](../cr/cr-068-mobile-actuals-search.md)** — *✅ COMPLETED · v3 · **P1 + P2 shipped in v3.13.0 (2026-08-04)**; P3 (row actions) deferred by decision. No migration.* Owner-requested: *a proper actuals search on mobile with filters like the desktop — account, category, period — easy to use on a phone.* There was **no mobile Actuals page at all**: `/trans-actual` had no entry in `DESKTOP_TO_MOBILE`, so the redirect fell through to `?? "/m"` and a phone opening it was silently dropped on the home screen; `/m/ledger` is a single-account ledger with no period, category or text filter, and its running balance is only meaningful *unfiltered*. `/m/transactions` is a search box over three chips (period · accounts · categories), each opening a full-screen sheet. **No backend work was needed for P1** — the endpoint already took `fromDate`/`toDate`, repeated `account=`/`category=`, `description` and amount bounds — and the page holds an `ACTUAL_CONFIG`-shaped filter object so `useTransactions` is reused verbatim and the two Actuals pages cannot drift on what a period means. The search box hits the **server**, debounced, rather than filtering the loaded page the way the desktop box does: at a 100-row page size that searches a hundredth of the data and reports "no results" for rows that exist. **P2 fixed a shipped desktop defect the mobile tiles could not copy** — see §1.2.

<a id="cr067"></a>
- **CR067 — [Forecast Multi-Compare](../cr/cr-067-forecast-multi-compare.md)** — *🟡 IN-PROGRESS · v3 · **P1 + P2 built and verified on dev 2026-08-03** (`bf07214`, `1f6d79a`); P3 deferred. Not released, not deployed. No migration.* One base scenario and any of its variants overlaid on a single trajectory chart — base bold, each variant its own hue, the same five metrics Compare already toggles (Net Assets · Total Assets · Net Cash Flow · Income · Expenses). Owner-requested. [CR040](../cr/cr-040-forecast-scenario-compare.md)'s Compare page is two-scenario **by construction** (its own non-goal #1), so "how do all my variants sit against the base?" today means opening it four times. Cheap because `buildScenarioMatrix` is already **pure and single-scenario** and returns all five metrics; `compareMatrices` is the pairwise layer on top, and this page simply does not call it. No tables, KPI cards, commentary or AI panel — the chart alone.
  - **The one real trap:** the union-of-years alignment lives in `compareMatrices`, and each matrix trims to `>= its own PeriodStart`, so plotting the arrays **positionally** would silently shift two scenarios with different `PeriodStart` against each other — no error, a wrong chart that looks right. Series key by **year**, not index. Not hypothetical: [CR064 P2](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) (the annual close, decided 2026-08-02, not built) is precisely about `PeriodStart` moving per scenario, so a positional build passes every test written today and breaks the first time that ships.
  - **P1 touches CR040's shipped page**, deliberately: the trajectory chart is extracted into a shared `FCTrajectoryChart` rendered by both, rather than hand-keeping a second copy of the metric list, palette and tooltip config (the drift `FCStepNav` was rewritten to end). It ships **first and alone**, with its own tag, so a Compare regression reverts without taking the feature with it. The gate needs writing: `FCCompareCharts.jsx` and `FCCompare.jsx` have **no component tests at all**, so "existing tests pass" would assert nothing about the component being moved — a render test comes first, against the current component, and must pass unchanged after.
  - **P3 — the last-generated badge — is deferred and may be dropped.** The drift that motivated it is a *dev* artifact: on prod all five scenarios were built within two hours of each other, so the badge would render five near-identical dates, and it measures build *age* rather than staleness (a CR050 variant sync rewrites modules without touching entries). The one case that must not stay silent — a selected scenario with **no entries**, which would otherwise be an absent line — is handled in P2 and needs no endpoint.
  - **The cross-base non-goal has a shelf life of about a year — but not for the reason first recorded.** *(Corrected 2026-08-03 after reading the code; the original claim was wrong and its "fix" would have broken the annual close.)* `copyScenario` creates a **root**, and that is **correct**: a new year's base is a base, not a variant of last year's, and `trg_fc_reject_nested_variant` refuses both a variant-of-a-variant and turning a scenario that already has variants into one — so "carry `parent_scenario_id` on the copy" would either be rejected or permanently bar `2027 Base` from having variants of its own. Two creation paths exist and both behave correctly: `copyScenario` (root) and `createVariant` (`forecastVariants.js:961`, sets the parent). Dev's `Base_Buy Business` is a root because it was made by **copy**, not by the variant path — not a defect. **The real gap is the one [CR064 P2](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) item 4 already names:** the *variants* do not follow the yearly copy, so `2027 Base` starts with zero variants and this page draws a single line until they are re-created against it.
  - **As built (dev):** `/forecast-multi-compare`, step-less in Forecasting like Equity. `FCTrajectoryChart` shared with Compare; `useScenarioSeries` loads N scenarios through `useQueries` (per-scenario cache and errors; the balance report deduped by `PeriodStart − 2`, one request for all five, cache-shared with `useReports`); palette validated with the dataviz six-checks against **Fin's own** surfaces (light worst-adjacent CVD ΔE 9.1 / normal-vision 19.6; dark 8.4 / 19.3), its light-mode contrast WARN discharged by listing every variant with its swatch **and** its name. A variant with no generated forecast is named rather than silently absent. **Verified on dev against real data:** with only `2026 Base` plotted, both pages produce identical y-domains on all five metrics. 364 frontend tests (+31), lint 0 errors, six ratchets, clean build.
  - *Not in scope: cross-base overlay, any change to `/forecast-compare`'s behaviour, a generate action on the page, or any engine/schema change.*

<a id="cr066"></a>
- **CR066 — [Every category with activity reaches an FC line](../cr/cr-066-fc-line-mapping-completeness.md)** — *🔴 OPEN · v3 · P0 is an owner decision per row (no code); P1/P2 scoped, not built. **Next up**, at the owner's request (2026-08-03).* Twelve COA categories carrying **−78,689 of 2025 expense and +31,474 of income** map to no FC line, so they sit outside the forecast entirely — base-year P&L, every FC-line row on the Review page, and the modelled years — with no screen reporting it. Surfaced by v3.11.14: putting the actuals column onto the stacked breakdown made the 2025 stack (487,897) disagree with the `Expense` header above it (566,586). **Neither figure is wrong** — the header reads the ledger's COA level-1 total, every child row is an FC line resolved through `categoryToLineMap`, and a leaf with no line is counted by the first and neither the second nor the chart. They had always disagreed in that column; nothing had ever drawn them side by side.
  - **P0 — decide each row.** `Property One-Off` (−47,187) is the largest and reads as a plain oversight, though "one-off" may argue for deliberate exclusion from a run-rate forecast. The five-row `Patrick - *` cluster (−36,500, plus `Travel - Patrick` −2,502) looks **deliberately** held apart from `Living Expenses`/`Children` — if that spend continues the forecast understates expense by ~39K/yr; if it is ending, exclusion is right and should be *recorded* as a decision. `Tax Adjustment` is **+8,078** — a credit inside Expense, to be understood before it is mapped onto a modelled cost line. `Healthcare - Other` (−303) and `Utilities - Garden` (−276) are almost certainly oversights.
  - **P0 (income) — probably a non-issue, and that is the interesting part.** `Rental - Spain` (+31,306) is likely **already in the forecast** via the SP property modules' `bs_module_income` path (the `CR046 Rent Line` carries no categories at all), so mapping the category too would **double-count** it. Verify against a generated scenario's `SP - *` income before touching it.
  - **P1 — the app should say so.** "Unmapped" currently means two indistinguishable things — *modelled elsewhere, correctly* and *not modelled at all*. A coverage line on `/forecast-mapping` (with a way to mark a row deliberately excluded, so the list can reach zero) plus a `Scripts/` ratchet. A new COA category is unmapped by default, so this recurs by construction — these twelve did not arrive at once.
  - **P2 — the Review header and its children** reconcile once P0 says whether a permanent gap exists. Do not build it first.
  - *Identical on dev and prod, so it is the mapping and not a stale snapshot. Not in scope: changing how `getCellValue` resolves the header row (the COA total is the truthful ledger figure), or re-opening v3.11.14 (a breakdown totals its children, which is now what it does).*

<a id="cr064"></a>
- **CR064 — [Forecast: the annual close, the assumptions key, and the module form](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md)** — *🟡 IN-PROGRESS · v3 · P0/P1/P3 built and gate-verified (731 backend / 298 frontend, six ratchets, lint 0 errors); **nothing deployed**; migration **052** pending on dev and prod.* Started as one question — *we customised the module form for Loan, should the other types get the same treatment?* The answer is **no**, and the reasoning is the CR's §5: `module_type` is a free-text list the owner edits (prod carries both `Asset` and `asset`), and `fcModulePayload` sends every field on every save — so a type-gated form **hides** a live value rather than clearing it, which is why CR062's Loan carve-out needed a preview endpoint and a confirmed delete *per type*. Looking for the answer turned up three defects instead.
  - **P0 (built) — the editor computed foreign-currency USD at FX = 1.** `resolveFxRate` read `Rates.USDPLN`/`Rates.USDEUR`; the live document spells them `{PLN, EUR}` (the engine and FCExpSetup both read `PLN ?? USDPLN` — this was the one reader that did not), so it found nothing, returned **1**, and then **multiplied** by it where the engine **divides**. An unmatched €390,000 property posts **$390,000** instead of ~$453,000, and since `fcbuilder-module.js` seeds the USD series from the stored column and `index.js:833` derives the module's FX from the ratio, it also reports an implied rate of **1.0** into the FX-effect attribution — and the sync effect **overwrites** a correct hand-typed figure with it. Latent only because all ten live non-USD modules are matched (that branch uses the ledger's own ratio); the module the owner had open when this started was an unmatched **EUR** one. Extracted to `utils/fcModuleFx.js` with 15 tests, including one that asserts the *other two* readers still accept both spellings so the gap cannot reopen from the other side.
  - **P1 (built, migration 052) — a rename strands a scenario's assumptions.** Period, inflation, FX and tax live in `forecast_assumptions` keyed by scenario **NAME**; `PUT /scenarios/:id` renamed the row and nothing else. The failure is not the loud one it looks like: generate throws once, the owner saves Forecast Settings to clear it, that write refreshes only `scenarios` from the DB names, and generate then **succeeds** with an empty inflation list — `buildRates` seeds `entries[0]?.Rate ?? 0`, so **0% inflation for 36 years, silently** (the CR045 §1 / CR048 class). Prod carries **five** dead names from renames that already happened. `renameScenario` now does both in one transaction and `updateScenario` **throws** on a name so no caller can bypass it; 052 prunes the orphans, **refusing** when they sit alongside a live scenario with no inflation entry of its own — the shape of a rename, where pruning would destroy live assumptions (falsified on a prod copy). *The id-stamping design was drafted and rejected*: `syncAssumptions`'s `rekey` would have carried the **base's** id onto every variant row (reintroducing the same 0%-inflation bug), and the override differ strips `{Scenario, id}` but not `ScenarioId`, so every variant would report a phantom `inflation`/`FX` override — the CR050 v3.0.110–112 class.
  - **P3 (built) — the form, without touching type.** A section collapses to a `+ Add …` line when every field in it is blank or zero, and opens the moment anything is set: it **cannot hide a live value by construction**, so it needs no confirm, no preview and no delete path, and it works for all nine types plus the tenth the owner invents next. Tax is **0-for-103 modules**; Real Estate never uses Income (0 of 40), Business never Expenses (0 of 18), Liability and Asset use neither. Per-type **labels** only (Private Equity → *Capital Call* / *Distribution*, Fixed Income → *Coupon Spread*), where a lookup miss costs a noun rather than a value. **And the blank-row question is answered by disproving its premise:** the two nameless prod modules are not Cancel's doing — CR042 made New Module a client-side draft in `11fc3b5` on 2026-07-13 and both rows were written on 2026-07-14 — they are **Generate**'s, which saves the draft before it builds. `assertModuleBody` now refuses a module with neither an account nor a name (one guard, every write path), so **Cancel needs no change at all**. §4.4 was withdrawn on inspection: setup status is already a column, an inline `select` and a filter.
  - **P2 (designed, needs an owner decision, NOT built) — the annual close.** Every module in all five scenarios is anchored at **2025-12-31** while every scenario runs `PeriodStart = 2027`, so each carries **two base years at once** and the module anchor is **a year behind the scenario's own**. `crud.refreshModulesFromActuals` already re-bases a whole scenario from the ledger and is wired to the copy-scenario modal — what it does not do is move `PeriodStart` with the anchor, offer market value for non-loans (correct as a default, wrong as the only option: for the four Fidelity accounts the ledger balance *is* market value), preview anything, or run in place. The open question is whether the year rolls **in place** on one long-lived scenario or keeps **minting a copy** — a question about how the owner works, not one the code can settle.
  - **P4 / P5 (designed, not scheduled):** plan-vs-actual for the live year; sensitivity runs on CR053's existing scratch harness.
  - **P6 (built, migration 055) — income a business can express.** Owner: *"there is no way to change how this grows relative to inflation — only the yield adjustment, which is not relevant to a business."* True, and the cause was worse: **the typed amount was not being used at all.** Income has two mutually exclusive modes and the yield wins on a **single** row, discarding `income_amount` — and **all six income-bearing modules in prod are in yield mode**, so all six had a dead Income Amount. United Beverages: **192,266 PLN typed** while the engine books **77,163 USD** for 2027 = `avg(3,846,154 / 3,870,192) × (2.5% − 0.5%)`, to the dollar. CR003 built `IncomePct` as a *deposit interest rate* — right for Fidelity Fixed Income, wrong for a company. P6 adds `income_growth_rate` (a multiplier of inflation, read like the existing `Growth`; NULL = 1 = unchanged) and `forecast_module_income_steps` (permanent level changes; owner chose *typed in the year it happens, keeps its real value after*, applied in **full** in its year — deliberately not the July-1 half-year convention), **states the live mode in the form** so a dead amount is visible, and hides Yield Spread on a business **by data rather than by type** — §4.1's collapse rule extended to schedules, so it disappears when there are no rows and returns when there are, which a type gate could not guarantee. **Dormant, proved on real data:** prod copy regenerated with the old engine and the new one — **7,916 entries across all five scenarios byte-identical**; then Barkeria moved to amount mode at 0.5× inflation with a 2029 +10,000 step regenerates to **55,687.52 → 56,383.59 → 67,088.39 → 67,927.00 PLN**, matching by hand to the cent. *Left undone on purpose:* moving the six live modules off yield mode changes their numbers (a per-business owner decision), and the base-year income tax still reads the ignored amount — UB's 2027 tax is 30% × 192,266 PLN while every later year taxes the yield, so the first year is under-taxed by ~8,400 USD. Fixing that changes existing numbers and would destroy this phase's byte-identical proof, so it gets its own phase.
  - **Fell out of it: CI has been red on `main` since `2d49ff3`.** Migration **050**'s `found <> 1` guard aborts on a data-free database, and CI applies the whole chain to an empty Postgres before seeding — the **third** instance of this class after 046, and again nothing announced it ([Known Issue #12](#3-known-issues)). Amended under the `.claude/rules/migrations.md` "unavoidable" clause (an aborting migration cannot be repaired by a later one, which is the same reason 046 was amended); the chain now builds all 52 files on an empty database. **Leaves ledger checksum drift on dev and prod to be accepted deliberately.**

<a id="cr063"></a>
- **CR063 — [Chart of Accounts ordering (and the dropdowns that read it)](../cr/cr-063-coa-ordering.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE · v3 · **shipped as v3.10.0 (2026-07-31)**, migration **049** on dev and prod.* Four owner requests on one page, and the third turned out to expose a structural gap. **The order of the Chart of Accounts is nobody's decision.** `accounts.display_order` has been in the schema since the seed and `findAll`/`getChildren`/`getBalances` all order by it — but `getTree()`, the one path every tree, report and dropdown actually goes through, **selects it and then sorts `ORDER BY path`, where `path` is `ARRAY[id]`**. So the COA page, Balance Sheet, Cash Flow, the budget worksheet and the forecast account list all render in **insertion order**. It looks deliberate only because the original seed inserted rows in a sensible order. It also cannot simply be switched on: the column is a **global** 0–207 sequence, not a rank within the parent, and `create()` hard-codes **0**, so 22 accounts share that value and — measured on dev — **68 of 229 rows would move** the moment the sort started honouring it, in three reports and not just on the COA page.
  - **P0 (built, verified in a browser):** *Analyze PS Data* removed — PS is no longer a data source — and with it the 115-line handler, the results banner, the **quick-add missing accounts/categories** path that consumed its output (including the add-modal branch that fired `sync-to-transactions` and re-ran the analysis) and the three now-dead `quickadd` modes in `COAEditModal`. **The backend `/ingest-ps/analyze-ps` endpoint stays** — `UploadPS.jsx` calls it too, and "Upload PS: keep" is a standing CR042 decision. The tree gained the reports' four expand/collapse controls (all + one-layer, both directions), on the shared button primitive rather than a page-local class — the button-CSS guard rejected the bespoke one. Proven by clicking, since neither half is visible to a unit test: 231 rows → collapse all → **2** → +1 level → **8** → **34** → −1 level → **8** → expand all → **231**, each button disabling at its own extreme, no console errors.
  - **P1:** migration **049** rewrites `display_order` as a per-parent rank, ranked by **today's visible order (`id`)** rather than by the existing value — ranking by the existing value would bake the 68-row reshuffle in instead of preventing it. The success criterion is that **nothing moves**: the migration and the `getTree` flip (`ARRAY[display_order, id]`, the form already used in `ingestPs.js`) ship together, verified by diffing Balance Sheet / Cash Flow / budget worksheet before and after. `create()` then **appends** (`MAX(sibling)+1`) instead of prepending — which is the real fix for the complaint that opened this, a new account landing somewhere arbitrary. Plus `POST /coa/reorder` taking the **whole sibling list**, not a direction: idempotent, single transaction, and able to reject a stale client whose id set no longer matches.
  - **P2:** ↑/↓ buttons in the existing row-action cluster (owner's choice over drag-and-drop — no dependency is installed, and it keeps keyboard access), disabled at the first/last sibling and **disabled entirely while a search or filter is active**, because in a filtered view the row above is not the sibling it would swap with.
  - **Built, and one sequencing error worth keeping:** half of P3 belonged in P1. `findAll` and `getBalances` were already sorting `ORDER BY a.display_order, a.name` — on the **global** sequence — so making the column a per-parent rank does not leave them unchanged, it **breaks** them (every parent's rank-1 child first, then every rank-2). The parity run caught it in seconds (`/accounts?leafOnly=true` came back scrambled while the trees were fine), and a shared `SORT_PATH_CTE` now serves all four flat-list readers. *A rank scoped to a parent silently breaks every consumer that treated the column as global* — found by diffing responses, not by reading. **Parity holds:** `reports/balance`, `reports/cash-flow`, `budget/cash-flow` and `budget/category-groups` byte-identical across the migration; both COA trees identical in name sequence. 10 new backend tests (704 total) and 9 new frontend, with the ordering claims **falsified first** — reverting the sort flip and the append default turns 3 red, reverting `findPLeaves` a 4th, and restoring `useCoa`'s sorts turns the frontend assertion red (its fixture had to be changed to make that possible: the old tree was alphabetical, so it could not tell COA order from a `.sort()`). Clicked end to end: an arrow moves a category, ends disable, a search disables every arrow, and reordering on the COA page changes the **forecast New Module → Account** dropdown with no reload.
  - **P3:** the three alphabetical `.sort()`s in `useCoa` come out — including `bsLevel2Options`, added **2026-07-31** precisely because tree order buried a new category, and explicitly released by the owner now that the order is theirs to set — and the two flat list endpoints (`findPLeaves`, `GET /accounts/categories`, both `ORDER BY a.name`) learn the tree rank via the same recursive CTE.

<a id="cr062"></a>
- **CR062 — [Forecast Loan Module, and the Equity report it makes possible](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE · v3 · **all three phases live on prod (2026-07-31)** — P0 + P1 as **v3.8.0** (migration 047), P2 as **v3.9.0** (migration 048) · migrations 047 (P1) / 048 (P2) · pass 1 `revise` (6 blocking) → pass 2 `revise` (3 must-resolve, approved on scope/track/priority).* **The forecast cannot model a loan at all**, and the owner is actively modelling a Sarasota house purchase — `2026 SRQ House Purchase` already carries a `Sarasota House` module at MV 0 with 45,000 of running costs, in a plan that cannot express how the house is paid for. Worse than absent: **liability interest is sign-inverted** (`isLiability ? val : -val` on a negative balance), so the first mortgage entered with a rate would have **credited** the bank line ~25,000 every year, in the model the purchase decision rests on. Dormant today only because all 15 modules on a liability account carry `expense_amount = 0.00`. Two more verified defects behind it: the G6 "liability interest" tests drive `ExpensePct`, whose column was **dropped in migration 008** and is hard-coded to 0 by the loader (unreachable from real data, so there is no working way to charge interest on a liability); and `Dispose` is **silently zeroed** on a negative balance, leaving `Invest` as the only working principal primitive. Design: store the five assumptions and **derive** the schedule every generate (never materialize rows that rot), key the engine on `loan_interest_rate` rather than the user-editable `module_type`, and let interest on the *average* outstanding supply the July-1 half-year convention for free. **P0 = the sign fix alone** (provably dormant, one commit, ships first). P2 secures a loan against **any** asset module and adds an Equity report — asset value gross and net of debt, plus asset income less debt service.
  - *The review passes moved it more than they polished it:* base-year interest was missing from `getBaseYearValues` and therefore from the **cash sweep's opening cash** — the exact CR049 §1 class, in the function CR049 created to end it; a draw dated in the base year **vanishes on write** (`writeValuesToCategoryRow`'s `valueOffset` discards it) leaving a liability with no cash, and *which* year that is differs per module because `base_date` is not uniform; a blank Interest Line **drains cash into no P&L row at all**; and the driving scenario turned out to be a **CR050 variant** of `2026 Base`, so the loan runs through force-sync on every build — the uncovered case being an inherited loan whose schedule fails to re-materialize, which reads as a flat balance rather than an error.
  - *Built (this bullet records the dev build; all of it is now on prod — see the phase notes below):* **P0** `7965b89` — the sign fix alone, provably dormant (every scenario has 3 modules on a liability account and **zero** with `expense_amount != 0`; `2026 Base` 1,599 entries and `2026 Downside` 1,575 regenerate byte-for-byte). **P1** — migration 047, a pure `fcbuilder-loan.js` derivation, the interest branch, the `getBaseYearValues` third UNION, eight write paths, and the Loan form. **684 backend / 230 frontend tests, six guards green.** Real proof on dev: an 800,000 mortgage at 6% drawn 2027 and ending 2047 gives **+776,000 of cash in the draw year** (800,000 less a half-year's 24,000 of interest), 46,800 of interest in 2028 on the 780,000 average balance, and closes at **exactly zero in 2047** with 1,200 of final-year interest. The form was opened in a browser on the real module — Loan section, straight-line fill and the amortization editor all render, Valuation/Expenses/Income/Invest/Dispose correctly gone, zero console errors.
  - *Two guards were falsified against unguarded code before being kept:* a stale CR046 expense window turns the interest series into `0 0 0 13,797.66 …`, and a leftover `expense_amount` makes CR041's ownership gate halve the draw year a **second** time — `−5,000` where `−10,000` is right, i.e. 25% of a year's interest.
  - *Found in passing, pre-existing — **fixed in v3.9.1**, and it was worse than first reported:* **`POST /modules` silently dropped nine columns**, not four. `repo.createModule` carried its **own** hand-written INSERT list, separate from `updateModule`'s allow-list, and the two had drifted; **five** of the nine were fields the route had already mapped into `moduleData` and believed it was saving (CR046's four window dates + CR047's `income_tax_rate_override`), the other four simply unreachable (`tax_rate_override`, `setup_status`, both `cash_sweep_*`). POST returned **201** and the values were gone. Same class as the bug CR047 §5 fixed one layer up, one layer down — which is exactly why the *pattern* is the defect and not the omissions: both paths now read one exported `MODULE_COLUMN_DEFAULTS` map (which carries the defaults too, because *"what column"* and *"what does absent mean"* are one question — `??` where NULL is load-bearing, `||` where 0 and NULL agree). Falsified first (the window date came back `null`); the second test is the durable one — it reads `information_schema` and asserts every writable column is reachable, so **the next migration that adds one trips a test** instead of a scenario quietly computing something else.
  - *P1 completed:* the retype-to-Loan **confirm** is wired — it names what will go ("1 Dispose · 1 Yield Spread · its income/expense window"), and pressing Cancel was verified in a browser to leave the module Real Estate with its window and both schedules untouched. Shipped as **v3.8.0**; migration 047 applied by deploy Step 2b and recorded, prod re-verified dormant (15 liability modules, **0** able to reach the fixed branch).
  - *P2 (shipped as **v3.9.0**, migration 048 applied to prod):* `secured_asset_module_id` + **`/forecast-equity`** — asset value gross, less the debt secured on it, equals equity; then asset income less asset expense less loan interest = **net income**, and net income less principal = **net cash after debt service**. Two bottom lines on purpose: principal is a transfer, so folding it into the P&L would misstate one and dropping it would misstate the other. **No engine work** — `forecast_entries` already carries `(year, account, module)`.
  - *The trap P2 exists to close:* `copyScenario` inserts modules one at a time, so a naive copy carries the **source** scenario's module id — the copy's report would read the **source scenario's house**, with both figures real. Fixed with an `oldId → newId` second pass, mirrored in the variant sync via `origin_base_id`, and asserted by migration 048's `DO` block so a regression fails at deploy rather than in a report. The test asserts the link points at a module **in the copy**, not merely that it is non-null.
  - *Two of my own defects, caught by this CR's own tests and guards:* the module PUT **validated after writing**, so a rejected secured-asset link (or an interest line removed from a loan) was persisted and *then* 400'd — worse than no guard; now checked against the state the write would produce, before it happens. And the Equity page referenced `var(--text)`, which **is not a token** — it silently ignored the theme until `check-dead-tokens` failed the build.

<a id="promote-cutoff-ui"></a>
- **Make the bank-feed promote cutoff an explicit choice at mapping time** — *🔧 small, unblocked, real risk reduction.* `account_source_mappings.promote_from_date` is **read-only everywhere in the frontend** (help text + the diagnostic page; no API write path), so today mapping an account means "promote its entire staged history" and there is no way to say otherwise. That is exactly what turned the 2026-07-14 Black Card mapping into **31 duplicate transactions** — $8.4K gross but net only +$267, so a balance check could not see it. Migration 043 (2026-07-30) pinned the two live NULL cutoffs and `setBankFeedMapping` now pins one whenever an account is mapped, which blocks a *later*-arriving older row — **but it does not close this class**, because the Black Card duplicates were already staged at the moment of mapping. Closing it needs a date field on the mapping UI plus a write path, so the owner states the cutoff deliberately when adopting a feed account.

<a id="cr059-sheet-retirement"></a>
- **Retire the Sheet path — but its three uses have three different lifetimes** — *⏳ date-triggered, CR059 P4 tail.* Since the 2026-08-10 cutover **nothing in the live ingest path reads the Sheet**; fin's data is entirely API-sourced. What still depends on it:
  1. **The rollback.** `FINTABLE_SOURCE=sheets` + a container recreate reverts ingestion. This is why the adapter, converter, Google service account, mounted keyfile and `FINTABLE_SHEETS_ID` are all still present. **Retire first** — target ~2026-08-24, then do the P4 tail in [§8](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md): remove the service account + keyfile + `googleapis` dep, revoke the Sheet share, update [`secrets-inventory.md`](secrets-inventory.md).
  2. **The hourly equivalence watchdog** (`bank-feed scripts/p2-shadow-run.sh`, `17 * * * *`, into a throwaway shadow DB that touches neither the live feed nor fin). It reads **both** upstreams and compares. **Keep this longer than the rollback:** it is the only thing continuously proving the API is not silently dropping rows the Sheet still carries — the one cutover failure mode no other check covers — and it costs one container plus an hourly read. Still green as of 2026-08-12 (`matched=2860`, 3 accepted exceptions).
  3. **Incidental:** `scripts/crosswalk-plan.js` and the Sheet converter unit fixtures. Only matter if the crosswalk is ever regenerated.
  **Do not delete the Google credentials while (2) is still wanted** — the gate needs them to read the Sheet side.

<a id="cr059-exception-expiry"></a>
- **⚠️ 2026-08-31 — five equivalence-gate exceptions expire, and the gate will start FAILING** — *⏳ date-triggered, deliberate.* `bank-feed scripts/compare-exceptions.json` holds **2 account + 3 transaction** entries, all Revolut, all `intermittent: true` with `expires: 2026-08-31`. Past that date the gate fails until a human re-reads them. **That is the designed prompt, not a bug** — `intermittent` exempts an entry from the stale check, which is a deliberate weakening, so the expiry is what stops "temporarily accepted" becoming "permanently invisible". **Recorded here because the date lived only in the JSON and in nobody's calendar**, and a red hourly gate whose cause is undocumented is exactly how a gate becomes something people learn to ignore. On the day: re-read each entry against reality, then either re-earn it with a new expiry or delete it. Context in [CR059 §16/§18](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md).

<a id="promote-currency-guard"></a>
- **Refuse to promote a staged row whose currency disagrees with the target account** — *🔧 small, unblocked, general form of a fix we just made specifically.* Nothing in the promote path checks that a `bankfeed_staging` row's currency matches `accounts.currency`, so a mis-attributed row books a foreign amount into an account as if it were native — and **no balance check can see it**, which is the same invisibility that let the Black Card incident survive review. This is not hypothetical: fintable serves four Revolut **EUR** transactions under a wallet it labels **"(USD)"** ([CR059 §18](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)), and on the Sheet path two of them were promoted onto Revolut-USD before bank-feed `6d108ec` fixed the converter. Migration **050** closed that one path by making the mapping ignore-only, but the class is open for every other account: any upstream re-attribution, re-consent or shared-name collision can reproduce it. A guard costs one comparison at promote time; the design question is whether a mismatch should **block** the row (safe, but a legitimately multi-currency feed account stops promoting) or **flag** it for review on the reconcile page. Related: [the promote-cutoff write path](#promote-cutoff-ui) — both are "the mapping should state its assumptions rather than infer them".

<a id="cr060"></a>
- **CR060 — [Feed connection health (read-only)](../cr/cr-060-feed-connection-health.md)** — *🔄 IN-PROGRESS · v3 · bank-feed side built 2026-07-29 (`cc6a9bb`), **not deployed**; fin recon page to do.* Fintable reports `healthy` / `needs_reconnect` / `status_text` per bank connection and the Sheet path cannot express any of it. **Bank Pekao has been unhealthy since 2026-07-24** with nothing in the stack able to report it, and a 2026-06-06 GoCardless re-consent silently cut Revolut from three wallets to one — **unnoticed for seven weeks**. PSD2 consents expire ~90 days, so this recurs by design. Read-only and independent of the cutover: it uses the read-scope token CR059 already has and works while `FINTABLE_SOURCE=sheets`. The write half (minting reconnect links) is deliberately excluded — it needs a write-capable credential in an unattended service, which CR059 §10 argues against.
  - *Its first live run found a third problem in ten minutes:* **Capital One is stale past the 26h threshold** and nobody knew. Revolut and Pekao both report `needs_reconnect` — so Revolut needs re-linking *again*, the 2026-07-28 attempt did not take. Two Wise connections report a provider outage while `healthy: true`, which is why `provider_notices` is reported separately from `needs_attention`: folding non-actionable chatter into the alarm is how an alarm gets ignored.

<a id="cr061"></a>
- **CR061 — [Investment holdings and market prices](../cr/cr-061-holdings-and-prices.md)** — *📋 PLANNED · v3 · split out of CR059 §15B+C.* `securities` and `quicken_price_staging` are **0 rows** — the absence that made [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) derive returns from ledger postings, [CR058](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md) rebuild brokerage history from Quicken, and [CR020](../cr/cr-020-stock-investment-module.md) sit as a skeleton. Fintable serves daily holdings snapshots (quantity, price, market value, cost basis) for the six Fidelity accounts, plus public split-adjusted equity prices where fin has **no** price source at all. Constraints to design around: `cost_basis` is the position total not per share, holdings have **no history pagination** (one call per day per account), and the default IEX feed is a single exchange cacheable up to an hour.

<a id="cr059"></a>
- **CR059 — [Fintable API ingestion](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)** — *✅ **DONE — cut over 2026-08-10, ingest is the API exclusively** (`FINTABLE_SOURCE=api`; no sync has used the Sheet since). What remains is dated, not built: [retire the Sheet rollback](#cr059-sheet-retirement) ~2026-08-24 and the [2026-08-31 gate-exception expiry](#cr059-exception-expiry) · v3 · pass 1 `revise` (5 blocking, all restated) → pass 2 `revise` (5 must-resolve).* Fintable now serves its own **REST API (V2)**, so bank-feed no longer has to scrape the Google Sheet Fintable exports into. The Sheet costs us a **display-name join** for tx→account (the 2026-07-14 Black Card incident: 31 duplicates, $8.4K gross, **net +$267** so no balance check could see it), Excel-serial dates, floats, a currency that has to be dug out of a JSON string with a different path per upstream, institution identity **guessed** from raw shapes (17 `bank_connections` rows for ~11 real institutions), a full two-tab re-read every hour, and a Google service account + keyfile in the path. The API gives `account_id` on every transaction (the name join stops existing), exact decimal amounts, `YYYY-MM-DD` dates, first-class `connection`/`provider`/`institution_name`, `updated_since` incremental sync, the true `last_successful_update`, and **holdings** — the securities tables [CR019](../cr/cr-019-quicken-import.md)/[CR022](../cr/cr-022-bank-feed-parallel-import.md) left at 0 rows (deferred to its own CR).
  - *The whole plan hung on one measurement (Phase 0, now run):* **do the API's ids equal the ids we already store?** Ours are the Sheet's account UUIDs and composite tx hashes, and they are load-bearing into fin — 31 `account_source_mappings`, 2,080 `bankfeed_staging` rows, **1,362 of them already promoted to the ledger**. If the ids differ and we just swap adapters, every row re-stages as new and re-promotes: the Black Card shape at ~1,000× scale, again net-of-payments invisible. Same-ids ⇒ drop-in swap; different ⇒ a two-database crosswalk (fin migration **043**).
  - *Second, independent version of that risk:* the API can serve **more history than the Sheet tab held** (our earliest feed row is 2026-04-30) and **5 of 31 mappings still have `promote_from_date = NULL`** — "promote everything". Mitigated by a date floor, an insert-count guard that aborts and rolls back, and filling those 5 cutoffs before cutover.
  - *MCP connector (owner's question):* worth adding for ops/triage, **not** for ingestion — it is OAuth+PKCE with 1-hour tokens, requires the write-capable `mcp:use` scope, and shares the same rate buckets. PAT for the service, MCP for the humans.
  - ***P0 ran 2026-07-28 and answered all five questions.*** Ids **differ** (0/31 accounts, 0/1,387 sampled transactions overlap) — but the account crosswalk turns out to be **exact, not heuristic**: our Sheet transaction ids are `{api_account_id}--{hash}`, so our own data already carries the API's account ids (verified 15/15 where the id is composite; the other 14 resolve on name+currency with balances agreeing). Transactions need content matching — **1,324/1,387 (95.5%)** on `(account, date, amount)`, and **all 63 misses are the four Chase/Akoya accounts**, where the API's `date` is the *posted* date and its `auth_date` is exactly what we store. `?include=raw` carries every provider field the health-check SQL depends on. Tier is **office** (`can_sync`, 13/50 connections). Back-fill risk: 3 of 29 accounts hold rows 1–2 days older than ours.
  - *Three things P0 found that no amount of reading the docs would have:* the documented `pending=true|false` filter **422s** (only `0|1` works — the first probe run returned zero rows for all 29 accounts); the `type` field is **not** the documented taxonomy but the raw bank product name (`Karta kredytowa PKO VISA Infinit`), so the keyword inference stays; and **two mapped Revolut wallets (8325 PLN/EUR) no longer exist upstream** — `GET /accounts` includes disabled accounts, so Fintable has deleted them, while fin still maps them to Revolut-PLN #214 and Revolut-EUR #16. Also visible for the first time: the **Bank Pekao connection is unhealthy** since 2026-07-24.
  - *Next:* two owner decisions (§12 — the Chase date basis, and what to do with the dead Revolut wallets) → pass 1 (`cr-technical-reviewer`) → P1.

<a id="cr058"></a>
- **CR058 — [Quicken-era valuation anchors](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md)** — *✅ DONE (rev 5 built) · v3 · shipped to dev 2026-07-28, **prod 2026-07-29**.* [CR019](../cr/cr-019-quicken-import.md) §22's value-only promote tracks **cash but not holdings**, so a brokerage account's reconstructed history drifts wherever the cash/securities split moved: on dev the imported Fidelity Stocks curve read **negative in every pre-2020 year**, worse than the flat plug it replaced. Anchors each year-end to **Quicken's own Net Worth Report** — trustworthy because an independent share-and-price walk over the QIF's own 384,172 price blocks reproduced it **to the cent in 22 of 24 years** — with one reversal at the 2023-01-01 handoff so the PocketSmith era and today's balance are untouched. Anchors post to a **new non-P&L leaf**, deliberately *not* `Unrealized G/L`: each one mixes real market movement with liquidation timing, ~535K of money-market sweep churn and a gap in Quicken's own share history, so feeding [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) would reintroduce exactly the confident-but-wrong return series its two review passes removed. Also adds a **`preserve-today` calibration mode** — PS-anchoring drags the feed-owned Fidelity accounts back to a stale May-2026 PocketSmith balance (−42,552.71 on Fidelity Stocks). Migration **042**. Scope is **Fidelity Stocks only**; IRA and Cash Mgt need their own QIF exports and report columns.
  - *Owner decisions:* range through **2022-12-28** · **annual** granularity (parameterised — a monthly re-run is a different CSV, not a code change) · sentinel **root-fixed + guarded** · targets in a **pinned CSV** · the 2020 anchor **corrected at source**, not absorbed.
  - *Three defects found by the dev rehearsal, all fixed and shipped ahead of the CR:* the investment-QIF parser **discarded each cash row's real `L` category** (306 of 328 pre-cutoff rows) and **booked `XOut` backwards** (50 rows, +642,391.62 on the wrong side, ~$1.28M of overstatement) — `d4bf7da`, live in prod; and PocketSmith had booked **two Fidelity Stocks transfers as credits** — `f99a8c6`, applied to dev + prod. That last one is the interesting number: correcting it collapsed the 2020 anchor from **−1,445,246.13 → −9,331.73**, i.e. Quicken and PocketSmith now agree on 2020 to within 2.4%. Two independently built reconstructions converging is better evidence than the anchors could ever assert about themselves.
  - *Reviews:* pass 1 `revise` (9 blocking) → rev 2 → re-check (2 arithmetic defects rev 2 shipped) → rev 3/4 → re-check (§3.1 reproduces byte-for-byte; six sections stale) → **rev 5**. **Pass 2 (cr-signoff-pm): GO**, positioned **first** among the four IN-PROGRESS CRs — CR023's remaining 13-account tail should be sequenced *after* it so the reconcile services have one owner at a time.
  - *Built and rolled out:* `114f690` (migration 042) · `3fd09bc`+`a1c280e` (preserve-today) · `52445f8` (anchor writer + pinned CSV) · `247b307` (quicken-verify). Prod reproduced dev **to the cent** — same `opening_balance` (874,489.75), same 27 anchor rows, today unchanged at 1,157,037.74, `quicken-verify` 7 pass / 2 expected warnings / 0 failures. Loaded via `copy-quicken-to-prod.js` rather than a re-upload (CR019 §23 G2), so the 152 name mappings were not re-done by hand. Backup `Backups/fin_backup_pre_cr058_20260729_011206.dump`. Owner confirmed the Balance Trends curve on dev before prod.
  - *§9 step 5 — `/investment-returns` capture: **DONE 2026-07-30**, and the headline is the row that did **not** change.* CR058 replaced a **flat 1,201,328 plug line running 1998–2019** with the real market-value curve (29,436 → 642,514) and ~440K of real dividends/interest. But `priceReturn` stays **0** and the return column stays **`—`** for every year through 2024, before *and* after: anchors are `is_transfer` rows, CR056 buckets them as **`flow`**, and a flow moves value without generating return. **That is §3.3 working as designed** — routing anchors to `Unrealized G/L` would have manufactured a confident 25-year performance series out of liquidation timing and sweep churn; the report abstaining is the honest answer. *Owner-facing point: the new history is a **value** series, not a **performance** one.* `fxEffect = 0` and `unattributed = 0` in every period, so the all-USD invariant now holds end to end. (The rolled-back "before" also showed inflated income; that state is **not** a faithful pre-CR058 reconstruction — a batch rollback leaves the nine PocketSmith sign fixes in place — so it is deliberately not reported as a delta.)
  - *Three build-time findings the two review passes missed:* `--check` **cleared the anchors before reading them**, reporting drift across all 26 dates of a healthy series (write paths must clear for idempotency; the read path must not) · an **unquoted thousands separator** in the target CSV would have parsed as `1`, a silently wrong money value no downstream check could catch (now a hard error on cell count) · the first preserve-today commit landed on a **misread of a single-suite result while the combined run was red**, root-caused to the test harness leaking a sentinel row (`a1c280e` now deletes sentinel transactions before sentinel accounts).
  - *Re-anchored 2026-07-29* after five further sign defects were fixed at source ([§9.2](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md#92-second-sign-fix-wave--2026-07-29)). Only **three** anchors moved — 1998-03-20 → −931,889.73, 2021 → +33,069.42, 2022-12-28 → +378,131.96. **Σ, the reversal and every interior anchor including 2020's −9,331.73 are unchanged**, because a uniform lift of the pre-2021 ledger cancels between `ledger(D)` and `Σ prior` in the sequential formula. `--check` caught the drift unprompted — 25 of 26 dates — within a day of shipping the feature that guards exactly this.

<a id="ps-transfer-sign-defects"></a>
- **PocketSmith transfer sign defects — Fidelity accounts CLEARED, non-Fidelity untriaged** — *🔧 PARTIAL · found 2026-07-28 during CR058, second wave 2026-07-29.* A transfer whose two legs carry the **same sign** is money appearing or vanishing with no counterparty. **Nine defects confirmed and fixed** across Fidelity Stocks and Fidelity Cash Mgt (`f99a8c6`, `4238e33`, plus the 2026-07-29 wave), on dev and prod, with account 27 re-anchored after.
  - *The second wave came from a much better method, and it is the reusable part.* The Fidelity QIF's 2020–2022 rows sit in `quicken_staging` **cutoff-dropped and unpromoted** — never entered the ledger, so an *independent* record of exactly the era PocketSmith owns, immune to circularity.
