Comparing `sum(amount)` per `(date, magnitude)` bucket between the two systems isolates every disagreement in one query, and the ACH **PPD trace numbers** then prove two rows describe one event. **Fidelity Stocks 2020–2022 now has exactly one unresolved disagreement left** (below). By contrast the same-signed-cluster heuristic returned 42 candidates, most of them artifacts of its own ±3-day chaining — an unrelated transfer of equal magnitude landing nearby, or a legitimate one-legged transfer to an account with no Quicken export (Fidelity IRA).
  - **Fixed 2026-07-29:** Fidelity Stocks 2021-04-09 **+25,000 → −25,000** (PPD 1035141375; Quicken `XOut → Chase`, and Chase's ledger shows it arriving) · Cash Mgt 2021-07-19 **+20,000 → −20,000** and 2021-11-05 **+15,000 → −15,000** (Chase Quicken rows name `[Fidelity Cash Mgt]` as counterparty) · Stocks 2022-06-29 **+3,699.99 → −3,699.99** (Quicken `XOut → Fidelity EUR`) · Stocks 2022-06-14/15 **±800,000 both backwards**, nets to zero either way so no balance moved, corrected because each row contradicted its own description and a monthly anchor re-run would expose the swing.
  - **Left alone, needs the Fidelity statement:** Stocks **2022-11-02, ~187,689** — Quicken has +300,000 in from Cash Mgt and **two** −112,310.56 legs out to Fidelity EUR; PocketSmith has +300,000, a −300,000 "YOU EXCHANGED", and **one** −112,310.56. A modelling difference over a USD→EUR conversion, not a sign error. Flipping a sign here would move real money to satisfy a heuristic.
  - **Still untriaged (non-Fidelity):** PKO - Deposits 2023-01-10 **+50,000** against a PKO **+50,000** *"WYGAŚNIĘCIE LOKATY"* — a maturing deposit should *reduce* the deposits account, so the modelling of that account needs owner input before any row is touched · PKO EUR / SP - Panorama Mar 4 (2022-06-01) and PKO / SP - Sea Senses (2022-06-10), both legs positive on what read as property purchases. *Probably legitimate:* the Wise clusters (an inbound conversion leg plus a send correctly shows two credits) and the 2017/2018 PKO+Santander pairs.
  - **The fix itself shipped half-applied, and that is the more instructive defect.** `fix-ps-transfer-signs.js` ran `UPDATE transactions SET amount = …` and **never touched `base_amount`**, so all nine rows carried the **old sign in `base_amount`** on dev and prod — 1,504,114.38 of divergence on Fidelity Stocks, 70,000.00 on Cash Mgt. It hid for the same structural reason the original defects did: **the checks that exist read the column that was right.** Balance Sheet and Balance Trends read `amount`; `base_amount` is the USD read path — [`refreshFromActuals`](../../server/src/services/forecast/crud.js#L54) seeding forecast `base_value_usd` (Fidelity Stocks maps to 4 modules and is a cash-sweep source, so a scenario copy would seed ~1.5M rich — the CR045 §1 / CR049 shape), Cash Flow USD mode, budget summaries, and [category totals](../../server/src/v2/repositories/accounts.js#L190). Found by `/investment-returns`: on an **all-USD** selection its FX plug must be **exactly zero by construction**, and it was reporting −1,435,914 for 2020. *Fixed 2026-07-29* — the UPDATE now **negates** `base_amount` (negating preserves the row's FX rate; copying `amount` would silently rewrite it to 1.0), a repair pass heals rows the old version half-corrected, and `currency='USD' ⇒ base_amount = amount` is now asserted **inside the write transaction** and by [`usdBaseAmountInvariant.test.js`](../../server/src/v2/scripts/__tests__/usdBaseAmountInvariant.test.js). Zero violations on both stacks; 527 backend tests green; anchors unaffected.
  - *Why this matters beyond the rows:* `opening_balance` is a plug, so today's balance stays right while every date **before** the bad row is wrong — **no balance check can see it**, which is how nine of these survived six years. **The generalisation is worth more than the rows:** a defect survives exactly as long as every check reads the column it did not corrupt. The tool is [`fix-ps-transfer-signs.js`](../../server/src/v2/scripts/fix-ps-transfer-signs.js) (idempotent, dry-run default, aborts if today moves, asserts the USD invariant); the good sweep is the Quicken-vs-PocketSmith bucket comparison in [CR058 §9.2](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md#92-second-sign-fix-wave--2026-07-29), the weak one is the cluster query in §1.3.
  - *Also checked, benign:* 54 non-USD rows where `base_amount = amount` — **49 are zero-amount** (0 = 0 holds trivially) and the other 5 are WISE - EUR rows totalling **€0.40**, sub-cent conversions that round to the same figure.
  - **A third sign defect, this one in the importer: `Math.abs()` turned every Quicken reversal into a second credit.** Found by pulling on `quicken-verify`'s own *"within-import-dupes: 7 groups, 8 extra rows — verify these are genuine"* warning, where **two were not duplicates**. `insertInvestmentCashRows` ran `Math.abs(gross_amount)` under a comment asserting income is positive "regardless of how the QIF signed" it — true of 6,003 rows, false of the 3 negatives, two of which are income (a `Div` reversal, and a `-125.69 IntInc` memo'd **"CORP INT ADJUSTMENT"**). Income overstated **330.84**. **The anchors hid it**: they tie each year-end to Quicken's report, so an intra-year overstatement is absorbed and every year-end still tied to the cent — only `/investment-returns` was wrong. Fixed by preserving the sign, with a regression test asserting the **pair nets to zero** and **falsified against the old code first** (`Expected: -39.73, Received: 39.73`). Repaired by **rollback → re-promote → re-anchor**, not by patching rows — the three +39.73 ledger rows are indistinguishable without tracing memos back to staging, and re-deriving from staging is the operation CR019 already provides. Dev + prod; dupes warning fell **7/8 → 6/6**; 528 tests green. *A warning that says "verify these are genuine" is an instruction, not a disposition.* See [CR058 §9.2.2](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md#922-mathabs-turned-every-reversal-into-a-second-credit).

<a id="fid-stocks-2022-ledger"></a>
- **~~Fidelity Stocks' 2022 ledger is ~467K adrift~~ — RESOLVED the same day, and the resolution is the instructive part** — *✅ CLOSED 2026-07-30.* Quarterly custodian anchors produced modest values for 2016–2021 (mostly under 30K) and three large ones for 2022 (**+187,681 / −467,227 / +514,765**). I logged that as a data defect and held 2022 back to a single annual anchor, reasoning that pinning each quarter would let the anchors *absorb* a defect — §1.3's lesson. **That was wrong**, and the arithmetic says so exactly: fin's Jan–Sep 2022 flows net **+559,234.08** against the custodian's **+258,046.88**, a difference of **301,187.20 — the 2022-09-30 gap to the cent.** It reconciles completely.
  - *The cause is structural, not corrupt data.* The custodian's **−1,166,021.87** "change in investment value" includes, per its own footnote, *journaled securities* as well as price movement. **A cash-flow ledger has no market component between anchors at all** — so on an account taking **1.46M of additions while losing 1.17M of value** in one year, mid-year points are *expected* to be far off. Large 2022 anchors are the anchors doing their job.
  - *Resolution:* quarterly anchors now run **2016-03-31 → 2022-12-31** (28 points), applied to dev and prod. All 47 targets tie to the cent.
  - *The mistake is worth remembering:* after a week of finding genuine sign defects, a large number looked like another one. The discriminator was arithmetic — reconciling both sides' flow totals — not judgement. **"This anchor is suspiciously large" is a hypothesis, not a finding.**
  - ~~*Still open from this thread:* the journaled-securities movements themselves.~~ **CLOSED 2026-07-31 — the data does not support the concern.** Every journaled row in fin is an internally-paired `JNL VS A/C TYPES (Margin)/(Cash)` reclassification, netting to zero within its own account: IRA 6 rows / 0.00, Stocks 44 / 0.00, Options 142 / 0.00, Bond 29 / +66.25. Nothing leaves untracked, and the anchors pin all four to the custodian at 47/41/22/8 dates and would have absorbed it regardless. See [CR058 §12.7](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md).

<a id="feed-month-end-mtm"></a>
- **Month-end MTM marked against a STALE feed balance — guard SHIPPED, two of three defects closed** — *🔧 PARTIAL · found 2026-07-30 by cross-checking custodian statements, which is the only thing that could see it.* fin's only valuation source is the feed, so fin marking itself against the feed is a tautology; a custodian statement is the first genuinely independent record these accounts have ever had.
  - **(1) Stale-feed marks — GUARDED.** The monthly MTM books against the `bankfeed_balances` row *labelled* month-end, which is only as good as the feed being current that day. Twice it was not. **Bond 2026-05-31**: the 05-31 row was not fetched until **06-04**, so the mark fell back to an earlier date and left the ledger a **constant 9,815.27** below the feed, which June's mark then inherited as its base. **Stocks + IRA 2026-06-30**: all four Fidelity feeds went flat **06-28 → 06-30** (one connection stalling — [CR060](../cr/cr-060-feed-connection-health.md)'s exact class) and the 06-30 row was not fetched until 07-03; the true closes arrived on the row dated **07-02**, matching the statements for Bond, Cash Mgt and IRA **exactly** and Stocks within a cent. `mtm()` now refuses to mark when **(a)** no feed row is dated month-end, or **(b)** the balance is identical across three consecutive observations — a security-holding account does not sit still for three days. Reported either way as `stale_feed`/`stale_reason`; `force` overrides deliberately. *Both tests reproduce the real incidents and were **falsified** against the unguarded code — an earlier draft used a 41% mark and passed with the guard disabled, because the pre-existing implausibility check was doing the blocking. It was testing the wrong guard.*
  - **(2) Bond's MTM "not pinning" — FIXED 2026-07-30 by re-marking.** The constant 9,815.27 was the stale May mark. With the 05-31 feed row now settled, a re-run gives **+2,445.41** where the original booked **−7,369.86** — a difference of exactly 9,815.27. Re-marked on prod; Bond now equals the feed at both 05-31 and 06-30 and its anchors still tie. *Stocks and IRA needed no May re-mark — their marks reproduce unchanged, so only Bond's May was stale.*
  - **(2b) The JUNE marks — RESTATED 2026-07-31 (prod).** `restate-mtm.js` rewrites a mark from the custodian's own statement, because a stale mark *cannot* be re-marked: the feed row for that date IS the stale value. Applied: Stocks −41,219.86 → **−12,802.54**, IRA −4,145.11 → **−901.46**, Bond **+1,859.57** at March and +328.69 → **−4,612.38** at June, Cash Mgt **+4,863.51** at March and **−5,161.51** at June. All land on the custodian to the cent; all four anchor sets re-`--check` clean. Checking **March first** is what split the accounts — Stocks and IRA tie there to under a dollar (June isolated), while Bond and Cash Mgt were already adrift as the two with no Quicken history, so each needed **both** quarter-ends or Q1's drift would have landed in June's return. Two approaches were rejected: an anchor + next-day reversal (leaves today untouched but leaves June's *and* July's unrealized wrong, since the next mark still absorbs the error) and pushing the gap to a past period (shifts every later balance uniformly, so the delta — and the return — is unchanged). Detail in [CR058 §12](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md). *Superseded description follows for context:*
  - ~~The guard cannot fix them retrospectively.~~ It correctly refuses to re-mark 2026-06-30 for all three (feed flat 06-28→06-30), but the marks already in the ledger were written from that same stale row. Against the custodian at 2026-06-30: **Stocks −28,417.32, IRA −3,243.65, Bond +3,081.50**. The settled 30-June closes exist in the feed on the row dated **07-02**, so correcting them means either marking a date against a later-dated row (which the engine deliberately does not do) or extending each account's anchor set through 2026-06-30 — a design decision about whether 2026 belongs to anchors or to marks. *Today self-corrects at the next month-end mark; it is the June historical point that stays wrong.*
  - *Dev is NOT a faithful copy for this work:* dev and prod diverged on Fidelity Bond by 9,815.27 across two rows with different descriptions and amounts, so dev did not reproduce the stale-mark condition at all. The first dry-run was run there and showed nothing wrong. **Rehearse feed/MTM behaviour against prod data, not dev.**
  - **(3) Cash Mgt never marked at all — CLOSED.** `reconcile_mode = 'calibrate'`, zero MTM rows ever: correct while it held cash, wrong once it held CDs (86% "Other" today). Now anchored to 22 custodian quarterly points (`9028694`), which books the missing market movement as dated rows. Its `opening_balance` plug of −14,436.32 is now redundant and can go to zero **as a consequence** — deliberately not done first, because it would have moved a today-balance that is correct.
  - **Bond anchored 2026-07-30** — 8 targets (opening + 7 quarterly 2024-06 → 2025-12), Σ 2,437.70, all tie on dev and prod. Its pre-2024 history lives in account 27 (the splice), so that is the whole addressable range, and **Bond has no independent witness**: Quicken ends 2022-11 against a fin era starting 2024-06, zero overlap. The custodian is the only source and the account matches **by construction** — stated rather than left for a reader to assume it carries the IRA's evidential weight.
  - **Cash Mgt now MARKS TO MARKET — migration 046, dev + prod 2026-07-31.** This delivers the "real end-state" the note below anticipated. Its feed mapping was the only Fidelity one still on `calibrate`, and it had **zero mtm rows ever written**. `mtm` is right on evidence, not analogy: the account holds **brokered CDs** whose market value genuinely moves, its cash flows match the custodian exactly, and its gap **flips sign** between quarters (+4,863.51 at March, −5,161.51 over Q2) — an accumulating bookkeeping error does not change direction. Order was the fix: restate the quarter-ends from the statements **first**, so the movement lives in dated rows, *then* switch the mode. **The plug is still deliberately not reset** — see below, which remains correct.
  - **The Cash Mgt plug reset is CANCELLED, correcting an earlier plan here.** A plug is a single constant, so it is right at exactly one point in time: wrong for 2020 (the anchors fix that) and **right for 2026** — fin sits 298 from the custodian at 2026-06-30 *with* it. The handoff reversal preserves today by design, so the plug stays load-bearing post-handoff; zeroing it moves today by +14,436.32 and breaks the one date it gets right. The real end-state is **continuous month-end marking**, now trustworthy given the guard above — mark 2026 monthly and the plug can go to zero without moving anything.
  - **STILL OPEN — the +2 lag may be SYSTEMATIC, not a stale-connection artifact.** The settled month-end close arrives on the feed row dated **month-end + 2**, matching the custodian's statement on all four accounts (three exact, one a penny). **Fidelity Options exposes the wider problem:** its 06-30 row was *not* flat, so the stale-feed guard would never have flagged it — yet its June mark is still ~**679.51** overstated against the 07-02 row. If the row dated D always carries the D−2 close, then **every** mark is slightly wrong and the flat run was merely the visible extreme, which would mean the guard should **prefer the settled row** rather than only refuse a stale one. **July's mark is the test** (2026-07-31 is a Friday, so calendar+2 = Sunday 08-02 and business+2 = Tuesday 08-04 — they disagree, which is what makes it decisive). Feed history only reaches 2026-05-31, so no earlier month-end can be used as a second observation.
  - **STILL OPEN — Fidelity Options (28) has NO custodian coverage.** The statements cover four accounts (`194-901660`, `X27-230910`, `X94-929946`, `Z31-443539`); Options has none, so it can be marked but never anchored. It sits **+9,672.51** above its feed today (unmarked July movement, which the July mark clears) on top of the ~679.51 June error. Needs a statement from the owner before its June mark is restated — restating on the +2 inference alone would bake in an unverified figure.

<a id="stocks-handoff-plug"></a>
- **Fidelity Stocks read up to 258K high across 2023 — FIXED; and the sweep that followed came back clean** — *✅ CLOSED 2026-07-31 (dev + prod).* fin is exact at 2022-12-31 and jumps **+321,173.66** the next day: §3's **handoff reversal**, which undoes the anchors to preserve today and thereby assumes the post-handoff ledger is right. For Stocks the raw PocketSmith-era ledger sat 321,174 above the custodian on day one.
  - *The owner corrected the diagnosis, and it changed the fix.* The gap does not erode — the custodian **rises to meet fin** through real gains fin never books (+330,764 across 2023–24 against a 321,174 reversal, which is why it lands near zero by 2025). That killed a plan drafted an hour earlier: booking the unrealized series would have made 2023 **worse by 63K**, because booking gains *adds* value and fin was already too high. **The level correction (−257,911) and the unrealized figure (+62,801) are unrelated in magnitude and in sign** — two jobs, not one.
  - *Fix:* eight quarter-ends 2023-03-31 → 2024-12-31 anchored to the statements (`fid-stocks-2023-2024`), reversal at **2025-01-01** rather than at the end, because from 2024-12-31 the ledger is already right. Σ **−776.00**, **today unchanged at 1,185,455.06**, so §12's 2026 marks and the feed-owned balance are both preserved. All 8 tie to the cent; the original 47-target set still `--check`s clean.
  - **The sweep found no second instance.** All four accounts against all **113** statement dates: IRA (41), Cash Mgt (23) and Bond (8) **tie everywhere**; Stocks ties at 38 of 41, the exceptions being three 2025 quarter-ends off by −5.78 / +119.43 / **+551.92**. *Still open, low priority.*
  - **The generalisable lesson:** a handoff reversal is a **single constant**, right at exactly one date and wrong everywhere else — Cash Mgt's 14,436.32 plug one level up. Worse, here it **cancelled** by 2025 against two years of unbooked gains, so it never failed loudly. Any anchor set whose handoff lands on a ledger not independently verified *at that date* carries the same trap.

<a id="returns-attribution"></a>
- **The anchors were reported as capital contributions — attribution SHIPPED** — *✅ CLOSED 2026-07-31 (dev + prod).* Investment Returns showed **Unrealized G/L = 0 for Fidelity Stocks 2020–2024**. Two causes: no `Unrealized G/L` postings exist before **2025-01-31** (so the row was honestly zero), and — the serious one — `Valuation - Historical` carries `is_transfer = TRUE`, which CR056's `bucketOf` sends straight to **`flow`**. The adjustments holding the historical balance were therefore reported as **capital paid in or taken out**: +163,865.07 inside 2023's net external flows, +156,532.59 inside 2024's, corrupting `avgCapital` and every percentage derived from it.
  - *Fix:* [`attribute-unrealized.js`](../../server/src/v2/scripts/attribute-unrealized.js) writes a **balance-neutral pair** per period — `+U` → `Unrealized G/L` (88, → `price`), `−U` → `Valuation - Historical` (229, → `flow`) — so `U` moves between buckets and **no balance changes by a cent**, the invariant it refuses to commit without. **Stocks 291,910.35 / IRA 119,034.85** across 35 paired periods each, 2016-06-30 → 2024-12-31.
  - *`U` is a **difference of levels**, never a level.* The first CSV row is a baseline that writes nothing — Stocks' 2016-03-31 level is **−34,952.66**, and booking a level as a period's return would invent an embedded loss that never happened that quarter.
  - *The guard that matters:* it refuses any date on or after an account's first existing `Unrealized G/L` posting (2025-01-31 — PocketSmith's own entries). Double-counting there would leave balances **correct**, so nothing downstream would notice. **Falsified** by extending the CSV past that date.
  - *Result:* Stocks 2023 reads flows **−53,132** · unrealized **180,283** · total return **218,312**, against +127,150 · 0 · 38,029 before; ending market values byte-identical. Return percentages exist for 2020–2024 for the first time (2022 **−18.95%**, 2023 **+20.22%**, 2024 **+18.03%**).
  - **Not claimed:** unrealized only — realized gains stay in `incomeTotal` — and **1998–2015 has no statement coverage**, so those anchors remain flow-classified. `Valuation - Historical` still legitimately carries the unexplained remainder.

<a id="statement-decomposition"></a>
- **Fidelity statement decomposition — extracted; one route dead, one viable** — *🔎 INVESTIGATED 2026-07-31, nothing booked.* The statements carry Fidelity's own period decomposition (`Beginning + Additions + Subtractions + Transfers Between Fidelity Accounts + Change in Investment Value = Ending`), now parsed for every account with the reconciliation asserted per block: **51 statements, 0 failures**. Three layout traps, each caught by the invariant rather than by reading — `Transaction Costs` is a **sub-line** of Subtractions (adding it double-counted), `Transfers Between Fidelity Accounts` **is** a top-level addend, and 2016–2020 say "Beginning **Net** Account Value" (without which every pre-2021 account silently found no block).
  - **`Change in Investment Value` is NOT a return, and cannot be made into one.** Its own footnote includes *"transactions from Other Activity In or Out"*: in 2023 `X27-230910` reports **+2,680,973.57** YTD on an account that moved **+107K**, offsetting a −2.5M `Exchanges Out`. Portfolio level is no better. A test named *"CHANGE IN INVESTMENT VALUE IS NOT A RETURN"* now fails first if anyone tries. This also resolves a label error: CR058 §9's cited **−1,166,021.87** is reproduced exactly — as the **YTD to September**, not the annual (−1,594,887.43).
  - **What works: market value minus cost basis**, from the `Total Holdings` line — immune to the contamination, because a transfer moves both together. Extracted for all **117 account-statements**. Cross-validated: the two methods agree to single digits on the low-transfer IRA (2021 **exactly**, 2022 within 10) and diverge up to **31×** on transfer-heavy `X27-230910`. The resulting portfolio series is economically coherent — down 2018 (−45,852) and 2022 (−202,913), up 2019/2021/2023 — where the other gave −1,609,830 and +2,772,524.
  - *The trap worth remembering:* the column set **varies with what the account holds**, and the second figure means different things in each shape. A cash-only account reads `Total Holdings $1,278,965.19 $0.00` — market value and EAI. Reading that `$0.00` positionally as a cost basis invents a **1.28M unrealized gain**. Falsified: fabricating it fails two tests.
  - **DECISION PENDING — whether to book any of this.** Nothing is written to the ledger. Δunrealized is the *unrealized component only*; total return also needs realized gains and income, both separately disclosed. And **account-level** series still carry a transfer artifact at the 2024 split (~196K of embedded gain moved from `X27-230910` to `Z31-443539`) — only the portfolio-level series is clean. Detail: [CR058 §12.8–12.9](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md).

<a id="sentinel-root-fix"></a>
- **`opening_balance_date` sentinel — root fix DONE** — *✅ CLOSED 2026-07-30 (migration **045**).* Every balance reads `opening_balance + SUM(amount) WHERE transaction_date >= opening_balance_date`, so that date is a **floor** and rows below it are invisible to every report. The remaining half of this item is now closed on both sides.
  - **Code:** the unbounded sums are gone. There were **five**, not the three previously logged — `reconcileToFeed.calibrate` and `.mtm`, `reconcileManual.calibrate`, `.mtm` and `resetOpening` — and the two `mtm` ones had a month-end ceiling but no floor, which is why they were missed. All five now go through one place, [`openingBalanceWindow.js`](../../server/src/v2/services/openingBalanceWindow.js), which sums over exactly the window the balance is later read through, floor **and** ceiling. *Three copies is why the bug survived `f14a37f` — that commit fixed these same paths **moving** the sentinel and left the sums behind. One function means the next fix cannot miss four of them.*
  - **The source of the sentinel:** `accounts.create()` hard-coded `'2000-01-01'`, so every account created since migration 022 **re-introduced the sentinel 022 existed to remove** — the column DEFAULT has been `1990-01-01` since 022, and the create path was overriding it. Now `1990-01-01`.
  - **Data:** migration **045** retired the sentinel on all **11** affected accounts, reducing `opening_balance` by exactly what the lower floor makes visible so **every balance is unchanged**; it snapshots each balance beforehand and raises if any moves by more than a cent. Only **Chase Checking** actually hid anything — a 1999-12-31 row worth **1,950.61**, invisible to every read — but the other ten were latent traps for any pre-2000 row added later. Applied dev + prod: 0 accounts left on the sentinel, **0 hidden rows anywhere**, and both anchored Fidelity accounts still tie.
  - *Unblocks:* **Fidelity Cash Mgt** for CR058 anchoring — CR058 §4.2's guard aborted for it precisely because of this sentinel.

<a id="fidelity-owner-actions"></a>
- **Owner actions — Fidelity IRA + Cash Mgt backfill** — *⏳ PARTLY UNBLOCKED 2026-07-30.* **The Quicken Net Worth Report regeneration is no longer needed.** Fidelity's own combined statement (`FA_*.pdf`) carries a per-account beginning and ending value for Stocks, Bond, Cash Mgt **and the IRA**, and the IRA line matched fin **to the cent** at 2026-05-31 (292,025.45) — so valuation targets come from the custodian instead, which is a better source than a report we would have to ask Quicken to rebuild. Parser: [`parse-fidelity-statement.js`](../../server/src/v2/scripts/parse-fidelity-statement.js) (`d2482fb`). Still needed from the owner: **QIF exports** for Fidelity IRA and Fidelity Cash Mgt (transactions only), plus quarter-end statements for the backfill range. Account 30 still carries the `2000-01-01` sentinel, so CR058 §4.2's guard will abort for it until the sentinel work above lands — correctly, as a signal rather than a defect.
  - **Verified Fidelity account-number map** — each confirmed by an exact balance match against fin at a statement boundary, *not* by reading description text: `Z31-443539` → **Stocks (27)** · `X27-230910` → **Bond (31)** · `X94-929946` → **Cash Mgt (30)** · `194-901660` → **IRA (26)**. Options (28) is not covered by either statement; `Z53-443686` is the likely candidate. *An account number inside a transfer description is the **counterparty's** text, not an identifier of the row's own account* — CR058 §1.3's prose read it the other way and is corrected in place; the sign fixes are unaffected because each rested on Quicken's own `XOut` plus the counterparty ledger, never on the numbers.
  - **fin disagrees with the custodian at almost every month boundary** — 16 comparisons over 4 accounts and 4 dates: Stocks `+137.97 / −0.53 / 0.00 / +28,417.32`, IRA `+1.25 / +0.44 / 0.00 / +3,243.65`, Bond `+14,163.00 / +1,859.57 / +9,815.27 / +6,733.77`, Cash Mgt `+5,459.34 / +4,863.51 / +2,068.00 / −298.00` (2026-02-28, 03-31, 05-31, 06-30). **Same disease CR058 cured for 1998–2019, untreated for the feed era:** today is pinned by the feed and correct, but every past point is only as good as whenever an MTM row happened to land. Stocks and IRA are near-exact except on a **shared** date (06-30), which points at the reconcile rather than the accounts; Bond and Cash Mgt are wrong at every boundary by varying amounts, so not one missing holding nor a fixed accrued-interest offset. Belongs to [CR061](../cr/cr-061-holdings-and-prices.md) — fintable supplies holdings *going forward*, statements supply the *backfill*, and the two check each other.

<a id="cr057"></a>
- **CR057 — [Book Income at Source](../cr/cr-057-book-income-at-source.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE — shipped v3.6.0 (2026-07-28), migration 041 · v3 · (pass 1 `revise` → rev 2 → pass 2 **GO**). Owner walked it on dev and booked 2 of the 7 rows.* Investment income is recorded where the **cash landed**, not on the holding that earned it, so [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md)'s report gives United Beverages a **confident 0.00%** against ~25M PLN of average capital. A per-row **Book at source** action in the Ledger writes a **three-leg booking** — income at the holding, transfer out of the holding, and the original row re-categorized as the transfer in — leaving the holding's book value, PKO's balance, net worth and every P&L category total unchanged while moving the *attribution*.
  - *It overturns CR056's own P2 design.* CR056 rejected moving the income to the holding as identity-breaking; that objection only ever covered a **one-legged** change. With the transfer leg, `Δ totalReturn = Δ(EMV−BMV) − Δ netFlows = 0 − (−X) = +X = Δ income`, and `bucketOf` has no fall-through, so both legs land in the intended bucket by construction. Restating also corrects the Budget worksheet and P&L drill-downs, which a report-side mapping never would. The "Distributions received elsewhere" **mechanism** is therefore deleted from CR056 P2; the CVC / `Other Investments` **data gap** stays there as a reduced bullet.
  - *Pass 1's headline finding, worth remembering beyond this CR:* the draft's central test — "the reconciliation identity must stay 0.0000" — **could not fail**. `fxEffect` is computed as a plug (`totalReturn − (income + price + unattributed)`), and the code comment says so outright, so the identity closes for any input, correct or corrupt. Replaced with falsifiable before/after assertions, the load-bearing one being **`FX effect` unchanged to the cent** — the single number that moves if a `base_amount` copy is off. The other four blocking findings were the same shape: the category guard, the provenance store, the undo guard and the scope were each *asserted* rather than constructed.
  - *Scope:* the 7 UB + Barkeria rows (**5,319,292.39 PLN / 1,380,393.02 USD**). Three of them are loan-related rather than dividends (`LOAN AGREEMENT ON JULY 17, 2023`, a VAT refund, loan interest); **the owner reviewed and kept all seven**, with `Return of Capital` (217) documented as the per-row alternative — cheap to switch to, because undo is guarded.
  - *Deferred, with reasons:* **CVC** needs its own design — the 6 rows are **USD** on Fidelity Bond while CVC Fund VIII/IX are **EUR**, so a cross-currency leg needs a rate policy this CR declines to take · `Other Investments` (6 rows) are all already on holdings and 2 are negative, i.e. **mis-signed, not mis-placed** · **`Transfer - Historical`** (2,741 rows) is a *different shape entirely* — adding the missing counter-leg is **not** balance-neutral, and because every unrecorded distribution was silently absorbed by the following mark (`mark = target − book`), each added `−X` leg must be paired with a `+X` mark adjustment. Own CR.
  - *Build:* **migration 041** — `income_restatements` (with a `leg_snapshot` so undo *refuses* on divergence rather than silently moving book value) **plus** the `Transfer - Distributions` COA row created inside the migration. Reusing `transfer_match_groups` was rejected on inspection: its user-facing **Unlink** button would delete the audit trail, its `create()` cannot join an outer transaction, and group membership *excludes* rows from auto-matching — it would have **prevented** the match it was meant to record. Two endpoints (`book-at-source` + `/undo`, `dryRun` shared with the write path), one new `BookAtSourceModal` under `features/`. Ships **minor v3.6.0**.
  - *Expect one visible regression in exchange:* on Cash Flow By Account, PKO's `Financial Income - UB Dividend` becomes `Transfer - Distributions`. For a *cash*-flow report that is arguably less informative — the cash really did land in PKO — so eyes-on on dev before the prod write.
  - *Follow-up that keeps this from decaying:* a [CR038](../cr/cr-038-home-dashboard-attention.md) attention-strip rule — *"income in a holding-attributed category posted to a non-holding account"*. Recurrence is only ~2 rows/year, which is exactly why nothing will remind the owner otherwise.

<a id="cr056"></a>
- **CR056 — [Investment Returns report](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE — **P1 shipped v3.5.0** (2026-07-27). Rev 2 after a pass-1 technical review (verdict *revise*, 6 blocking) and a pass-2 PM sign-off (verdict *revise on scope*, P1 GO); four further rules revised after the owner clicked the built page (two-column layout, average-capital denominator + three return rows, the coverage rule, and the `marks` interval).* `/investment-returns`: account (parent rolls up) + period + interval → realized income and price return per interval, absolute (USD ⇄ LC) and as **realized / unrealized / total %** on average capital `(open+close)/2`. Ledger-derived — the CR019/CR022 securities tables are **0 rows**, so the numerator is the existing `Unrealized G/L` postings plus auto-derived income categories, with MV on the Balance Sheet's additive basis. **Shipped in P1:** table + two chart panels + the four exhaustive buckets (incl. a visible `Unattributed` row) + coverage/cadence/chain banners + the USD⇄LC toggle + supported-span auto-clip + the **`marks`** interval. **470 backend / 195 frontend / build ✓ / five guards ✓.** **P2 (below).** No migration; additive endpoint only.
  - *Why it needed two revisions:* rev 1 asserted the reconciliation identity instead of constructing it and was off **$8,781.87** on Fidelity IRA 2025 (category 206 and NULL-category rows sat in no bucket); `Transfer - Securities Trades` turned out to carry **real deposits** (Fidelity Stocks 2020 **+$200,460.86** — two `Fid Bkg Svc` ACH transfers), so it is now a flow, not an internal offset; a whole-period Dietz fallback produced a **+2,064%** headline on United Beverages; and the "has it ever been marked" rule showed a never-revalued **€422K** property at a confident **0.00%**.
  - *Day-one reality, recorded so it isn't discovered by clicking:* ~22 computable data points. Fidelity is marked monthly only from **2025-01**, so **no Fidelity calendar-year return exists**; United Beverages ($20.7M, the largest single asset) anchors its marks on **31 March** and has **no** computable calendar-period return; ~$32M of assets have never been revalued at all.
  - *Second payoff:* it is a data-quality instrument. The `Unattributed` row surfaces genuinely wrong ledger rows — the $200,460.86 of ACH deposits filed as securities trades, and `Car Purchase/Sale −$29,954` sitting on a brokerage account in 2022. **Read-only**: repairs happen in the transaction editor, never as correcting entries.
  - *P2 / follow-ups (deferred, not dropped):* ~~**"Distributions received elsewhere"**~~ — **mechanism superseded by [CR057](../cr/cr-057-book-income-at-source.md)**, which fixes this in the **ledger** instead of the report: the "cannot join the identity without breaking it" reasoning held only for a *one-legged* change, and the three-leg booking closes the identity by construction. What remains here is the **residual data gap** CR057 defers — the 6 `Financial Income - CVC` rows (**cross-currency**: USD on Fidelity Bond, EUR funds) and the 6 `Financial Income - Other Investments` rows (already on holdings, 2 negative ⇒ mis-signed, not mis-placed) · `Unattributed` **drill-down + deep-link** to the transaction editor · `returnPctExFx` · split `FX effect & rate drift` into revaluation vs `base_amount` booking drift · Sunday-boundary FX reconciliation test · growth-of-$1 panel (must chain Dietz returns, not track the balance) · multi-select accounts (`HierarchyFilter` chips + combined Total) · **`accounts.marks_to_market` flag** (the exact answer to the coverage rule, if the never-marked holdings are to be revalued on a schedule — then the flag is the worklist, not overhead) · per-security attribution and a true realized-vs-unrealized split, which need CR019's investment-side promote plus a price source.

<a id="cr052"></a>
- **CR052 — [Partial FX Exposure on an Expense Line](../cr/cr-052-forecast-expense-fx-exposure.md)** — *📋 PLANNED — **deferred pending CR-051 real-world validation** (2026-07-15).* Spec revised through an engineering review and a PM sign-off gate; **not cleared to build**. First use CR-051 on the real budget (~a week) to answer the open premise — how many categories are actually part-foreign, and is split-into-lines genuinely too clunky — then settle the two forks (§4 organic/USD-adjust split, §3 coexist vs unify) and re-request sign-off. May be shelved for split-into-lines if only a few categories are foreign.* CR051's intended completion for **matched/auto-loaded aggregates** (e.g. Living Expenses, base value pulled from the budget and read-only). CR051 only lets you set a currency on *unmatched* lines and only whole-line; the owner's real case is "20% of this line is PLN." Tag **"X% is `<currency>`"** (matched or not) without touching the amount or unmatching; the engine carves the slice, fixes a native amount at base year and floats it with FX (owner-chose the floats-with-FX model over hold-at-20%). Clean engine change: multiply the line's normal USD trajectory by `(1−Σpct)+Σ pct·FX₀/FXᵢ` — **byte-identical when no exposure is set or FX is flat**. New child table `forecast_incexp_fx_exposure` + **migration 040**; multi-currency mix (Σpct ≤ 100); **coexists** with CR051's whole-line currency (denomination vs partial exposure — orthogonal, exposures only offered when currency = USD); rides CR050's schedule rails for variant sync/override. Caveat (documented): the tagged % is a base-year *starting* share and drifts as FX moves — the point of modelling FX, not a bug.
<a id="cr051"></a>
- **CR051 — [Foreign-Currency Expense Lines](../cr/cr-051-forecast-expense-currency.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE — **shipped v3.2.0, live in prod** (2026-07-15).* Currency selector (USD/EUR/PLN, expense-only, disabled while Matched) on the Expenditures modal; native-amount entry with `base_value_usd` **derived server-side** at the base-year FX (client value ignored, so it can't rot; 400 if the scenario has no FX rate); the table shows the native amount under the USD. Plus the **F1 engine guard** — the incexp builder now fails loud on a missing/zero FX rate for a currency in use (it would have divided by 1.0 → silent ~4× overstatement, or by 0 → Infinity; latent until a non-USD line existed). **407 backend** (13 new), **195 frontend**, **7/7 Playwright** incl. a new CR051 browser round-trip; lint 0 errors. No migration, no engine behavior change for USD scenarios. Original scope/decisions below.* Some real spending is in **PLN/EUR**, not USD, so its USD cost floats with the złoty — invisible when modelled as a flat USD number. The surprise: the rails already exist — `forecast_income_expense.currency` (migration 001) is on every line, the engine already inflates in native currency then converts per-year via the `FX - PLN`/`FX - EUR` assumptions ([`fcbuilder-incexp.js`](../../server/src/services/forecast/fcbuilder-incexp.js), loaded at [`index.js:188`](../../server/src/services/forecast/index.js#L188)), and **Modules already ship a currency picker**; the **Expenses modal has none**. So this is a UI-exposure job — **no migration, no engine change**. Owner-ratified design: a mixed-currency line is **split into separate lines** (one currency each; per-line mix and a scenario exposure knob both rejected); foreign lines inflate at the **single scenario rate** then convert at FX (per-currency inflation deferred); the user enters the **native** amount (`base_value` = source-of-truth, `base_value_usd` derived at base-year FX — the engine ignores `base_value_usd` for incexp, so its rot is cosmetic, not a wrong number); currencies offered are **USD/EUR/PLN** only. Work: currency selector + native-amount entry in `FCExpModal`, confirm `currency` is in the write whitelist (CR043 N10), native+USD display, a confirming engine test, and a `fcFieldLabels` entry so a CR050 variant can override currency legibly. **Open:** matched (USD-anchored) lines — likely allow currency ≠ USD only on unmatched lines.
- **CR050 — [Forecast Scenario Variants (inherit-unless-overridden)](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md)** — *🟡 IN-PROGRESS — **live in prod through the v3.1.0 minor** (v3.0.108→v3.1.0, 2026-07-14), migration 039; awaiting owner acceptance + the adopt decision.* A variant inherits every item from its base unless overridden; overrides are captured at write time across modules, income/expense, schedules **and** assumptions (FX/inflation/tax/sweep — via `reconcileAssumptionOverrides` on `PUT /assumptions` and the sweep-band `PUT /scenarios/:id`), materialized into real rows so the engine/Review/Compare are unchanged. The Scenarios-page panel *is* the variant's definition, revertible per field. **Verified in prod:** a zero-override variant builds **byte-identical** to its base; an override pins its field while the base's *other* changes still flow through. **20 DB-backed variant tests, 394/394 backend, 195/195 frontend, all CI guards green.** The five post-ship fixes (v3.0.110–112) were all in how edits are *captured and reported* as overrides — DATE-as-instant, float noise, missing schedules, and the assumption-editing UI bypassing the override system (a **silent-data-loss** bug: a variant's FX edit was invisible AND erased by the next sync) — never in the inheritance machinery. v3.1.0 also shipped **Compare "Generate both"** (rebuild A and B so the diff isn't against a stale scenario) and the **Expenses draft-add modal** (matching Modules; no more blank "All" rows). **Open (owner):** the `adopt-variant` dry-run for "2026 Downside" (22 differences — **8 modules differ only in `base_value_usd`**, the copy having rotted, not a downside assumption) and "2026 Upside". *Original scope below.* A scenario copy is a severed island: you duplicate 30 entities to change one field, nothing records *which* field made it a downside, and the copies then rot (fix a cost basis in Base and "2026 Upside" keeps the wrong one). The hand-enumerated copy has also silently dropped columns **twice** — CR045 §1 (`cash_sweep_priority` ⇒ −$3.35M unfunded) and CR048 (the whole assumptions slice ⇒ 0% inflation). CR050 makes a variant **inherit every item from its base unless overridden**: `parent_scenario_id` + a `forecast_scenario_overrides` **JSONB patch keyed to the base row's id** (field-level — `NULL` is already load-bearing in these columns, so "NULL = inherit" is unavailable), and a **sync step that materializes base ⊕ overrides into real rows**, so the engine, Review, Compare, AI review and the audit CSVs keep reading an ordinary scenario and **do not change** (a read-time overlay was rejected: reads are not funneled — engine loaders, repo finders and `crud.getBaseYearValues` are three separate paths, which is exactly the CR049 drift). Sync derives its column list from `information_schema` — the direct fix for the drop-a-column bug class. Also lands the forecast API's first real scenario-create route (creation is currently a side-effect of `PUT /assumptions`; "Make Default" is `localStorage` only). Migration 039, all-nullable ⇒ parentless scenarios behave exactly as today; "2026 Upside" converts in place via `adopt-variant`.

<a id="cr042"></a>
- **CR048 — [Forecast Model Review: Engine Fixes + Ratified Assumptions](../cr/cr-048-model-review-fixes.md)** — *🟢 OPEN — shipped v3.0.90 (2026-07-12), no migration.* Outcome of a full conceptual review of the forecast model. **Fixed three ways the model contradicted itself:** (A1) the income↔sweep convergence loop solved the income-depends-on-MV / MV-depends-on-sweep fixed point for the **primary module only**, so a *backup* the sweep drained kept paying dividends on its full pre-sweep balance — Fidelity Stocks, fully liquidated by 2060 in "House Purchase", kept yielding ~$25K/yr on money that was gone, in exactly the years the plan is short (2062 dividend $24,182 → **$488**); also closed a USD-vs-LC units mix in that loop and its disregard of the CR046 window. (A2) **cost basis was spent twice** — the sweep realized gains against basis the builder's *scheduled* disposals also assumed, understating tax; the sweep now claims only basis surviving every future year (P2c's backward-pass pattern). (A3) the loop re-taxed income at the **gains** chain, silently overriding CR047 on every rebuild. Verified on a restored prod copy: Base + Downside **byte-identical** (the parity cases), House Purchase 3 yrs/−$732K → **3 yrs/−$1.05M** (larger and honest). **Ratified by owner:** 0% cash (deliberate), flat-tax/brokerage world (2062 net assets = a *living*, pre-estate figure), losses stay non-deductible, final-year tax bunching. **False alarm withdrawn:** the budget Tax item carries a −100% change at 2027, so it is zero from 2027 and never double-counts the engine's tax. **Open:** equity-growth experiment ("2026 Base - Market Returns" scenario generated — stocks 2.0× vs 1.0× inflation, $5.05M vs $1.20M in 2062 — owner to read on `/forecast-compare`); FX stress into Downside (owner magnitudes). **Known issue:** the API scenario-copy endpoint does not copy per-scenario assumptions (inflation/FX/tax) — the UI does that half client-side; should move into `copyScenario`.
- **CR047 — [Income-Only Tax Rate Override](../cr/cr-047-module-income-tax-override.md)** — *🟢 OPEN — shipped v3.0.84 (2026-07-12), **migration 038**.* `tax_rate_override` (migration 010) overrides a module's tax rate for **both** realized capital gains and income — two different taxes. United Beverages' dividend is received **net of Polish tax**, so the only incremental US tax on the income is ~3%, while a future **sale** of the business is still an ordinary capital gain at the scenario rate; setting `tax_rate_override = 3` would have silently under-taxed the disposal for the whole horizon. New `income_tax_rate_override` splits them: income = income override ?? module override ?? scenario; gains = module override ?? scenario (the CR045 sweep-liquidation tax is a *gain*, so it is unaffected). NULL falls back ⇒ every existing module byte-identical (X4); **0 is a real rate** (income taxed at nothing), not "unset". Verified on dev against the real UB module: tax −$30,962 → −$3,715 in 2028, exactly 3/25 of the 25% scenario charge, **with the disposal-year gains tax untouched** (X1). Opt-in only.
- **CR046 — [Module Income/Expense Window + Hierarchy Breakdown Graph](../cr/cr-046-module-income-window-and-hierarchy-graph.md)** — *🟢 OPEN — shipped v3.0.81 (2026-07-12), **migration 037**.* Two owner tweaks. **(1) Future-start income/expense:** `income_amount` was a base-year figure compounded at inflation from the base year, and the only thing that could delay it was CR041's ownership gate — which fires only for an asset **acquired** mid-plan. So "I own this flat today and start renting it in 2030" could not be expressed. Four nullable DATE columns (`income_start_date`/`income_end_date`/`expense_start_date`/`expense_end_date`) bound **when** each stream runs, never how much — the amount stays a base-year figure compounded at inflation. NULL = unbounded ⇒ existing scenarios byte-identical (test W5); ownership still wins (W7); rent that hasn't started isn't taxed in the base year (W6); the **copy path carries the columns** (the CR045 §1 bug class). **(2) Hierarchy breakdown graph:** double-clicking any `/forecast-review` row now expands it into the accounts beneath it as a stacked bar (level 1 → its level-2 accounts; level 2 → its leaves; nothing beneath ⇒ the old single line), on both the balance sheet and the P&L — previously only "Net Assets" did this. New pure `fcBreakdown.js` util; the page now aggregates raw entries by the account they were actually written against (`entryMaps` is rolled up to level 2, which is what made it useless here). Fixed in passing: `graphSeries` overwrote each series' color with the 6-entry line palette, so the Net Assets stack never used `BAR_CHART_COLORS`. **Open:** the window is hard on/off — a *stepping* amount would need a changes-schedule mirroring `forecast_incexp_changes`.
- **CR045 — [Forecast Cash Warnings & Forced Liquidation](../cr/cr-045-forecast-cash-warnings-liquidation.md)** — *🟢 OPEN — opened 2026-07-12; **Phases 1, 1b, 2 shipped (v3.0.75–80), prod regenerated.*** Started from the owner seeing the bank line dive to −$3.7M on `/forecast-review`; ended up finding **four silent-wrong-number engine bugs**. (1) `copyScenario` carried the legacy `cash_sweep_target` but never `cash_sweep_priority` (migration 031), so every **copied** scenario ran with no sweep module — excess cash still capped at the high band (so it looked plausible) while shortfalls went unfunded; prod's "2026 with House Purchase" carried 14 shortfall years / −$20.1M. (2) **P1b:** the sweep iterates `PeriodStart…PeriodEnd`, so the BaseYear was never visited and its corrected cash delta — computed and logged under a comment promising it "matches what the Review displays" — was never read; the sweep opened on the stale ledger balance and held the band a full BaseYear NCF *above* the displayed bank line. (3) **P2a/P2b:** a forced sweep liquidation was a **tax-free** bare transfer while a scheduled disposal of the same shares was taxed, and withdrawn funds went on compounding inside the module forever. (4) **P2c:** a module that is both a sweep source *and* has scheduled disposals was **sold twice** — the builder caps disposals against the *pre-sweep* balance (Fidelity Stocks: ~$950K over-committed). The sweep may now never drive a module below zero in any future year. **Phase 1** ships the cash-health warnings pane (W1–W6) — the engine had written 14 shortfall entries no screen ever showed — and it found bug (2) on its first healthy-scenario run. **Byte-parity deliberately broken**; prod's shortfalls are now materially larger and correct (House Purchase 3 yrs/−$2.67M → 9 yrs/−$8.14M). Whole-asset liquidation mode **dropped** by owner decision: only a *tagged* (= ranked) asset is ever auto-liquidated; everything else is flagged and the owner decides. Open: §6 Q2 (compare-page warnings), Q4 ("schedule a disposal" affordance).
- **CR042 — [UI Look & Feel Modernization](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md)** — *🟡 OPEN — **U1–U5 ALL SHIPPED** (v3.0.69 → v3.0.97) and **owner-accepted** in the v3.0.96/97 acceptance pass. Remaining is **owner-input IA only**: the calibration→Settings move and collapsing the six Forecast steps in the sidebar. ("Upload PS": **settled 2026-07-13 — KEEP, no change**; it already sits under Data Sources and stays as a PocketSmith-CSV escape hatch — do not delete it or `/api/v2/ingest-ps`.) The four adoption guards now ratchet **down**: buttons 123→122, naked hexes 170→167.* Verdict: polished shell, ~4 generations of pages underneath. Themes: token gaps, IA consolidation, Home net-worth hero, `<DataTable>`/`<Modal>` primitives **with CI adoption guardrails**, chart-theme convergence, Forecast inline-style migration. **Decisions:** refine Mindful Minimalist (no rebrand); **report consolidation approved** (Balances 4→1, Cash Flow 2→1, Budget 3→1, balances-first with checkpoint — closes the §2 owner-decision item); sage stays brand / money-positive moves to distinct emerald; selective Radix (Dialog/Menu/Select) for the Modal, DataTable hand-rolled.

- **CR043 — [Code Structure & Architecture Improvement Program](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md)** — *✅ COMPLETE (Phases 0–3; only long-horizon Phase 4 remains) — shipped v3.0.63 → v3.0.104, incl. **N10** (forecast writes reject unknown fields instead of silently dropping them — how CR046/CR047 shipped broken) and the **first restore drill** (v3.0.96 — it passed). **Remaining:** the lint gate is still advisory (**64 errors** — `no-unused-vars` and `no-undef` are at zero; the rest are 36 `set-state-in-effect` + 21 `react-refresh` + 7 hooks-rules, each needing a browser rather than a batch edit); N8's `{data,meta}` envelope; the `util.js` hygiene split; Phase 4 (TypeScript, Playwright). Original 2026-07-11 detail follows.* *Phase 1.3 (N2) + Phase 0 both DONE 2026-07-11.* 1.3: `generateForecast` transactional + advisory-locked (parity held, +3 tests, ~4.5× faster). Phase 0 (Opus): vitest now in CI (blocking), dead deps dropped (tensorflow/arquero/root-axios; nodemon→dev), docker.sock + mongo_backups mounts removed (prod+dev), dead files/debris deleted (Balance/BudgetInput pages, useAPI/useModal/useFormState, old/psAPI/mongo, ASSUMP_FILE) — two review items reclassified (ForecastContext deferred: provider still mounted; DataTable.css kept: live global stylesheet). 262 backend / 121 frontend / build green. **Phases 1.1 + 1.2 DONE 2026-07-11** (Opus): 1.1 migration runner (`server/db/migrate.js` + `npm run migrate`, `schema_migrations` ledger with auto-baseline, deploy Step 2b, dev adopted/36 baselined); 1.2 route-contract tests for forecast+budget (shared `_httpApp` harness, 25 DB-backed characterization tests pinning envelopes/validation/CRUD before the 2.1 split; pinned N8's bare-array `GET /modules`). 298 backend green. **Phase 1.4 DONE 2026-07-11** (Opus, scoped): `AppError` class + `validate.js` throws it (behavior-identical); `app.js` error middleware now logs unexpected 5xx server-side (shape unchanged); strict field-whitelist/type validation on `PUT /scenarios/:id` (N10 down-payment); 301 backend green. Deferred: `{data,meta}` success-envelope unification (N8) → Phase 3.3; module/incexp write validation → Phase 2.1. **Phase 1 complete. Phase 2.3 (Fable) DONE 2026-07-11:** engine restructured load → compute (pure builders) → persist; **danfojs + entire TensorFlow chain removed** (`frame.js` LabelFrame; node_modules → 61MB); N9 minimum (sweep fails loud on missing `Bank Accounts` anchor; the item-on-Bank-Accounts crash fixed); byte-identical md5 parity incl. clean `npm ci`; 301 backend green. **All remaining items are Opus-safe** (2.1/2.2/2.4, Phase 3, CR042 UI) — per-item guidance in the CR §Model guidance.* 14 new findings beyond the documented debt — highest severity: **frontend Vitest suite never runs in CI** (N1, ~5-line fix), **`generateForecast` non-transactional + no concurrency lock** (N2), docker.sock mounted into fin-server (N4), dead `@tensorflow/tfjs-node`/`arquero` deps (N3), no migration ledger (N11). Corrects stale §5.1 entries. Sequenced Phases 0–4: safety nets → backend guardrails → extraction → frontend consolidation. **Decisions:** migration runner pulled forward from CR027A into v3 (approved); TanStack Query approved (useCoa first, then shared report hooks for mobile dedup); Radix reconciled as selective-only.

### 1.2 Completed (chronological, latest first)

- **v3.31.0** (2026-08-18) — **minor: [CR083](../cr/cr-083-budget-latest-estimate.md) P0a + P0b — the budget Latest Estimate.** **Migration 072** (dev 08-17, prod with this release): `budget_le` + `budget_le_lines`, additive, seeding nothing and adding **no column to `accounts`**. **NO forecast numbers move** — nothing here touches the engine, and §8.1's refusal to let the LE feed the CR075 base year is the reason. **P0a** puts the FY landing on `/budget-vs-actual`: cut 2026-07-31 (7 months), landing **−102,998.92** against a budget of **−137,554.99**, variance **+34,556.07**. It is deliberately **additive** and does not re-base that page. **P0b** is `/budget-le` itself — create an LE, read it in **COA order** with parents rolled up (117 rows against 93 leaves), open a category's **month-by-month worksheet** and type the estimate months; a new LE **carries the prior one forward**, falling back to budget only where it has none, and **no new `source` value was needed because the provenance travels with the number**. The summary is **seven columns** and measured at 1440 `scrollWidth == innerWidth`, which is what keeps the print block simple instead of re-solving CR082's clipping. **The deviations section was asked for as an LLM feature and ships as arithmetic** — CR081 measured AI-proposed edits at **0/15, twice**, `status.md` names the next build as the consequence preview *with no LLM*, and CR077's rule is that a model runs *over* the deterministic rules; measured, six categories deviate by >$2,000 YTD and every figure is a division. Its trigger is **not** "actual differs from budget": a back-loaded budget can be far off YTD and still land where it says, so materiality is measured on **the effect on the remaining months**. Seven flags, ranked, totalling **+13,952.49**; `Option Trade` is **refused and told why** (churn 29.0×) rather than silently dropped, and a figure the owner typed is tagged **"you typed this"**. **Two defects the owner found that no gate could:** a category that only spends *after* the cut was invisible **and unestimable** — no row to click, which is exactly what a genuinely new expense looks like (`Purchases - IT Costs`, no budget line); and post-cut spend flagged on every category lit **39 of 118 rows**, narrowed to the two cases that mean something it is **4**, including `FL - Car Costs` at 903 already spent against an 800 estimate for the rest of the year. **Migration review changed four things while the file was still uncommitted:** uniqueness had been keyed on **`name`** — which §6 says never to do, and review demonstrated the hole with `'le-08-26'`, a trailing space and a name whose cut disagreed, all accepted, leaving **two live LEs on one cut**; `superseded_by` gained `ON DELETE SET NULL` (deleting the newest of a recut chain raised a raw FK the picker would have surfaced as a 500); a CHECK now stops `status` and `superseded_by` contradicting; and `budget_le_month_end_chk` was binding the **timestamptz** overload of `date_trunc`. **Two review rounds falsified seven of the CR's own figures** (three in round 1, four more in round 2 — three of those inside paragraphs round 1 had just corrected), all recorded in its §16 rather than patched away. ⚠️ **Finalise/recut are NOT built, and `BUDGET FY` is read live**, which is right for a draft and wrong for a frozen artefact — snapshotting it needs a migration before finalise. 1,003 backend / 517 frontend / five CI guards / lint at baseline; verified in both themes in a browser.
- **v3.30.1** (2026-08-16) — **patch: [CR084](../cr/cr-084-save-time-consequence-preview.md)'s wait now looks like a wait.** No migration, no engine change, **no forecast numbers move**. Owner-reported, and a real gap rather than polish: the preview modal opened on the RESPONSE, so for the second or more it takes to run **two real engine builds** the screen did nothing at all — indistinguishable from a dead button, on the one control whose entire purpose is to say *"wait, look at this first"*. The modal's own loading branch existed and was effectively **unreachable**. It now opens on the **click**, with a spinner and *"running the forecast twice…"*; the module name is passed separately so the title is right before the data lands; a preview FAILURE closes the dialog rather than leaving a permanent spinner, and returns the error to the editor where the unsaved change still is. `prefers-reduced-motion` keeps the affordance and drops the animation. Measured in a browser: **spinner at 140 ms, results at ~1.0 s.** **A second defect the same check found:** the confirm button read **"Saving…"** while the preview was still building — contradicting the note directly below it, which says nothing has been saved yet, on the one screen whose whole purpose is that distinction. Three states, not two: *"Working it out…"* → *"Saving…"* → *"Save this change"*, disabled throughout, verified by sampling every 120 ms rather than once (a single sample had reported it enabled, which was the measurement being wrong rather than the button). 517 frontend / lint 0 errors / six ratchets.
- **v3.30.0** (2026-08-16) — **minor: [CR082](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md) is COMPLETE — every open item closed, including the account-number leak.** **Migration 071** (dev + prod). **NO forecast numbers move**; nothing here touches the engine. **Seven items** ([§11c](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md#11c-the-remaining-items-closed-2026-08-16)): **P0a** — `GET /util/coa-traits` served a full `AccountNumber` for **all 230 accounts to any caller** and now serves a mask plus a boolean, with the full value from `GET /util/coa/:id/account-number` one account at a time · **year-scoped `review_state`** (071) so excluding a 2026-opened account no longer hides it from TY2026 · the **§12b.14 carry-in guard** · **freeze-on-file wired to a button, plus amendments** · **TY2024 seeded as filed** (31 lines, **$1,462,652**, matching the client copy) · **XLSX** · **Part I filer block** (TIN/DOB, Postgres only, masked, excluded from `/util/appdata` and refused by its POST). **Four defects surfaced while closing them, none of which any gate could see.** P0a's frontend half would have written an empty string over real account numbers on any unrelated rename — and over every selected account at once in a multi-edit — had it followed [this roadmap's own one-line prescription](#2-open-backlog-non-cr-items); **item 15a blocked its own filing** (an unknown maximum sat in `needs_attention`, which `freezeYear` refuses, so the only way to file a legitimately unknown figure was to override the guard protecting every other line); the filed-vs-recomputed diff **reported "nothing has moved" when nothing had been *checked*** — found within a minute of seeding TY2024, which is exactly the verification §9 said that seed would buy; and the typed-figure dialog **erased `manual_reason` on a re-edit**, the cause [§11b](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md#11b-shipped-to-prod-2026-08-16) noted without finding. **Also fixes the CI v3.29.0 left red** — [CR084](../cr/cr-084-save-time-consequence-preview.md)'s `forecastPreview.test.js` borrowed three rows a from-scratch database does not have (sixth instance of [#21](#3-known-issues)'s class, four days after the audit that found none), and two e2e specs predated CR084 putting a preview dialog in front of every module save. ⚠️ **Verified against the running prod API, not the deploy log:** no full account number for any of 230 accounts, TY2024 `filed` with 31 lines, TY2025 unchanged at 16 lines / **$2,627,821**. ⚠️ **The Part I filer block is EMPTY** — the owner still has to enter name/TIN/DOB/address once. ⚠️ **Nothing rendered in a browser** (no renderer on this host); §11b's eight owner-found defects are the standing evidence that green gates and a correct page are different claims.

- **v3.29.0** (2026-08-16) — **minor: [CR084](../cr/cr-084-save-time-consequence-preview.md) — what an edit does, before you save it.** No migration. **NO forecast numbers move** — the preview writes only to a throwaway copy, and no engine code changed. From [CR081 §13](../cr/cr-081-ai-line-assistant.md), which both its reviewers preferred to the AI assistant it was carved out of: a consequence preview serves EVERY edit rather than only AI-proposed ones, and needs no token contract, no action schema and no benchmark table. **The case for it, measured this session:** `Sarasota House`'s growth was an unset field rather than a belief, and 0 → 1.0 moved SRQ by **916,000** — nothing at the point of saving suggested that field carried nearly a million dollars. **What it does:** on Save, net assets before → after in nominal **and** today's money, plus which scenarios move and which do **not**. `Fidelity Stocks` at 1× → 2× inflation reads **4,031,239 → 10,063,962** (+6,032,723; +2,480,015 in 2026 dollars). **⚠️ BOTH sides are built**, from one copy: `forecast_entries` is the result of the last build, and *"an engine change moves nothing until the scenarios are REGENERATED"* is in the infrastructure guide precisely because stale entries beside fresh inputs is this system's NORMAL state — reading stored entries as "before" would blame the one edit being previewed for every un-regenerated change since. A test poisons the stored entries and asserts they never reach the result, verified by making it fail. **The blast radius is COMPUTED, never asserted:** CR081 §5 claimed "a base edit propagates to four variants" and live data contradicted it — a variant with its own override for that module keeps its value — so both `moves` and `doesNotMove` are returned, the second being the half a fixed number hides. **`withScratchScenario`** is CR053's throwaway lifecycle **extracted** and shared, carrying its three load-bearing rules verbatim (standalone-not-variant, prune the assumptions DOCUMENT, cleanup in a `finally`). **`services/moduleWrite`** is the PUT route's 24-branch body→columns mapping extracted, so the preview and the save cannot apply different transformations; the preview then runs `replaceModuleSchedules` (guarded exactly as the route guards it — it DELETEs before it reinserts) and `replaceModuleStreams`. The test that proves the extraction earned itself previews a **stream** edit: a stream amount does not live on the module row at all, so a column-only preview would have shown "no change" to an owner who had just edited the amount. **⚠️ Three defects only a BROWSER found** — the wire-payload/column mismatch (400 on the first click), a preview failure that **saved anyway** (a silent write reported as nothing; it now blocks), and a modal that never opened because the shared `Modal` primitive takes `open` and the component passed `isOpen` — HTTP 200, no console error, every other gate green. **One hazard fixed rather than inherited:** the scratch teardown's prune was a read-modify-write over four `forecast_assumptions` rows every scenario shares, so it could overwrite an owner's concurrent inflation save — the quiet CR064 failure that builds at 0% for the whole horizon. CR053 made it rare; a preview per save makes it routine, so the filter now runs INSIDE the UPDATE. `copyScenario`'s half is untouched and recorded as residual, because it is shared with every other caller. **Also in this release:** the AI Review prompt now states that `growth_rate` is a **multiplier of inflation**, after prod showed the model proposing `growth_rate → 4` for `Fidelity Stocks` reasoning "a more realistic nominal growth rate for US equities" — it read 4 as 4% where 4 means ~10%/yr; the context line printed `1% (×inflation)`, a multiplier wearing a percent sign, and now reads `1× inflation = 2.50%/yr`. 981 backend (+7) / 517 frontend / lint 0 errors / six ratchets.
- **v3.28.3** (2026-08-14) — **patch: the AI apply path could write to a scenario the review was not about.** No migration. **NO forecast numbers move.** Closes roadmap Known Issue #22 and is [CR081](../cr/cr-081-ai-line-assistant.md) **P0a**, carved out of a CR that was itself **DEFERRED** the same day on a two-pass review (pass 1 *revise*, pass 2 *defer*). **Four defects on a SHIPPED path, two of them found only by the review.** (1) `POST /ai-review/apply` took `{ action }` with **no `reviewId`**, so the server never knew which scenario the conversation was about; `applyAction` checked the module *existed* but never that it **belonged to the reviewed scenario** — a review of `2026 Downside` could write a module in `2026 Base`, whose edits then fan out to four variants, and the confirm dialog never named a scenario so nothing surfaced it. (2) **`update_scenario` bypassed the CR050 reconcile**, which lives in the scenarios ROUTE and not the repository: a sweep-band change applied to a variant reported success and was then erased by the next sync rewriting the band from base ⊕ overrides — accepted, stored, silently reverted, the shape the route's own comment calls *"the one failure mode this feature must not have"*. (`updateModule` DOES intercept, which is why the module path was safe.) (3) **`current_value` was rendered from the MODEL** — `applyAction` never read it server-side — so a hallucinated "from" value meant the owner approved a change against a state that was never true: [CR077 §4](../cr/cr-077-assumption-advisor-tab.md)'s own rule (*nothing generated may state a number*) broken on the one surface where a number is ACTED on rather than read. (4) The dialog was a **confirmation, not a preview** — it showed the input and never the consequence; that half is deferred to the successor. **Shipped:** `reviewId` required; a shared **`resolveAction`** validator behind both the dialog and the write, so the two cannot drift (the front end had already drifted, still advertising an `incexp_id` action the server refuses outright); `current_value` read from the row and the model **no longer asked for one**; the variant reconcile called; **one `audit_log` row per apply** — that table has existed since migration 001 and **nothing had ever written to it**; and the dialog now names the **scenario** and marks a variant. **10 tests, the first this path has ever had** — four are characterization of guards that already existed, written so the refactor could not silently drop them, and both new guards were verified by disabling them and watching the right test fail. **⚠️ The verification itself cost something:** the live check ran after a `docker compose restart` of `server-dev`, which runs a **built image with no source mount** — so it re-ran the OLD code, exercised the unfixed path, and *accepted* the write, setting `New Business`'s growth to 2.0 on dev. Recovered from prod (byte-identical) and the entries fingerprint never moved (`New Business` is `exclude`); the rule is now in [guides/infrastructure.md](../guides/infrastructure.md) — *verifying a fix against a stale binary can exercise the very bug you removed.* **Also this release:** [CR079 §7](../cr/cr-079-real-terms-view.md) shipped Compare in today's money at v3.26.0, and [CR076 §13](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md) + [CR077 §7](../cr/cr-077-assumption-advisor-tab.md) record the first full advisory walkthrough — **all 15 walked, every one already deliberate, NO model change** — plus two rules guarded that were firing on streams **not in the plan** (17 → 15). 918 backend (+10) / 507 frontend / lint 0 errors / six ratchets.
- **v3.27.0** (2026-08-11) — **minor: the currency defect closed at its source, all three shapes (migration 064).** [Known Issue #19](#3-known-issues) started as one account in [CR064 P13](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md)'s close-out and was **understated in our own record** — it is **nine** parent rollup accounts labelled `USD` over non-USD children, and `forecast_modules.currency` is seeded from the linked account. **(1) The labels — migration 064.** Eight relabelled, not seven: 056 fixed the five MODULES on account 65 and left the ACCOUNT saying USD, so the source of that 18,250 phantom liability was still in place and the next module linked to it would have inherited the same label again. `Tax Liabilities` is deliberately untouched — a USD and a PLN reserve under one parent has no single correct label, and its module is correctly USD. The predicate is **derived** rather than a hardcoded list, which is safe here and was not in 056: there the two numeric columns' ratio merely *looked* like a PLN rate, here the children's currency is a stored fact. **Zero balances move** — all nine hold no transactions of their own. **(2) Shape 3, the unmappable currency (engine).** `fxColumn` maps only PLN and EUR and the valuation branch was an `else if` with **no else**, so anything else silently kept rate 1. Falsified before fixing: with the guard off a **£10,000 module posts $10,000** instead of $12,700 — a 21% understatement with nothing to say so, and reachable, since `WISE - GBP` is a live account. Now throws. **Scoped to the unmappable case only**: a *mappable* currency whose assumption column is missing keeps its rate-of-1 behaviour, because tightening that WOULD move numbers and this change is held to "no number moves". **(3) Shape 2, the self-consistent lie (`fcWarnings` R11).** Values that AGREE because both were typed in local currency under a USD label are invisible to **both** engine guards — the numbers are consistent and simply wrong. The linked account is the only independent witness, so the module payload now carries `AccountCurrency`. **A warning, not a throw**, because `Tax Liabilities` is a legitimate disagreement and refusing it in the engine would block a generate over a judgement call; two id prefixes so CR077's classifier splits them — USD-over-local is **integrity**, one non-USD over another is **advisory**. **The audit gated the migration rather than following it:** every reader of `accounts.currency` was checked first (`reports.js` and `getOpeningBankCash` convert each account's own zero balance; `refreshModulesFromActuals` keys on `t.account_id`; `manualReconciliation.js` excludes accounts with children by design; `restate-mtm.js` is per-account and never a parent), and all nine `account_source_mappings` rows proved to be retired `pocketsmith` name-keys that do not read currency. **Also fixes a red `main` that nothing had announced** — see [Known Issue #20](#3-known-issues): `crud.openingBankCash.test.js` threw when the `Bank Accounts` root was absent, which `ci-seed.sql` never creates, so **five consecutive CI runs had failed** while every local run passed. The suite now seeds that root and removes only what it created; dev's real root verified to survive. **886 backend across 65 suites / 507 frontend**, lint 0 errors, six ratchets at baseline, and the full chain + `ci-seed.sql` green on an empty Postgres. **Numbered 064, not 063** — another thread committed a `063` mid-build.
- **v3.26.1** (2026-08-10) — **patch: a scenario COPY dropped the selling cost, and `Sarasota House`'s growth field was never set.** No migration. **Moves ONE scenario's numbers**, deliberately and measured. Two findings from one question — *is SRQ's −1,392,889 viable?* **(1) The data.** `Sarasota House` carried `growth_rate = 0.0000`, i.e. **flat nominal for the 20 years it is held**, while `US - Casarina` and `US - Nokomis` both run at **1.0 (full CPI)** — it was the only US property not at full CPI. `growthPctForYear` returns `growthPct × inflation[i]`, so 0.0 is not "no assumption", it is the assumption that a Florida house never appreciates. Owner confirmed (2026-08-10) the field was **unset, not a belief**. Set to 1.0 **on the base module and synced** — a direct write to a variant row is reverted by `syncVariant` — then all five regenerated: the four holding the module on `exclude` came back **byte-identical (same entry count AND same sum to the cent)**, so only SRQ moved, **−1,392,889 → −476,930 (+915,959)**. It is still negative, and the remaining deficit is structural: the house is bought **entirely for cash** (`House Morgage` is `exclude` everywhere), so the sweep liquidates ~1.5M of portfolio in 2028 and pays CR078 costs to do it, holds an asset with **no rental income** and 45,000/yr of cost, and sells at a 7% cost in 2048. **Financing is the untested lever.** **(2) The bug that found itself.** The first measurement was taken on a scratch **copy** and said **+1.8M**, landing SRQ positive. It was wrong: `copyScenario`'s disposal INSERT enumerated six columns against a seven-column table, so the copy lost `disposal_cost_pct` and sold the house keeping **100%** of the proceeds. **The same column [v3.25.2](../cr/cr-078-disposal-selling-costs.md) had fixed the day before in the variant-sync path** — two hand-maintained lists, one column added, one list updated. It presents as *a copy reading better than its original*, which is the worst way for it to show up, and it was caught only because the inflated number was being used to measure something else; trusted, it would have argued for a house purchase on a cost that had gone missing. Fixed, and guarded by `copyScenario.columns.test.js`, which **does not enumerate columns** — it reads each child table's real columns from `information_schema` and asserts a copy round-trips every one, across disposals, investments and amortization, with distinctive non-null fixtures because a null fixture passes whether or not the column is copied (failure-patterns §5). Falsified against the unfixed code. **[failure-patterns.md](failure-patterns.md) §4 extended:** a hand-maintained column list is the same shape as two copies of one formula, and where a list must be enumerated, the test must derive its expectation from the schema rather than enumerate it a second time. 883 backend across 65 suites.
- **v3.26.0** (2026-08-10) — **minor: [CR079](../cr/cr-079-real-terms-view.md) increment 3 — Compare in today's money; and the advisory set walked with the owner for the first time.** No migration. **NO forecast numbers move**, and this release is unusual in that the *intended* number change was withdrawn once measured. **Compare (CR079 §7):** v3.25.0 deferred this on the reasoning that the Review carried all four money paths and was the harder proof. That held — Compare needed **one** conversion. `deflateMatrix` is applied to matA and matB **before** `compareMatrices`, so the table, charts, commentary and all four KPI cards read one already-converted set; deflation is linear, so `deflate(B) − deflate(A)` equals `deflate(B − A)` and converting the INPUTS removes any path to a partially converted page. Each scenario deflates by **its own** inflation — the main reason this belongs on Compare at all, since a scenario assuming higher inflation carries a larger nominal 2062 figure for reasons unrelated to the plan — but the **anchor is shared** (the earlier of the two base years), because anchoring each on its own would make every delta the difference of two different currencies. **Both or neither:** if only one side can build a deflator, none is applied. `FCCompareAIPanel` takes only scenario NAMES and fetches server-side, so the LLM keeps seeing nominal. **⚠️ The hand-listed field set is guarded this time:** `deflateMatrix` works off named fields, the same construction that dropped `disposal_cost_pct` from the variant sync one release earlier, so `fcCompareRealTerms.test.js` asserts the lists against the keys a REAL matrix carries — verified by adding a probe field and watching it fail *by name*, then removing it. It also pins that `deflateMatrix` does not mutate its input, since FCCompare derives the deflated view from the nominal memo every render and writing through would deflate twice, producing a plausible smaller number rather than an error. **The browser check earned its keep:** `compare-real-terms.spec.js` failed first run because Compare's DEFAULT baseline is `Base Case`, a scenario present in `forecast_scenarios` but carrying no `PeriodStart` in the assumptions doc — no base year, no honest deflator, toggle correctly **disabled**. The spec now asserts both states rather than stepping around the default.
