Known limit stated rather than papered over: the seed has one *generated* scenario, so the browser runs with A ≡ B and the DELTA path is covered by unit test. **The advisories (CR076 §13, CR077 §7):** 17 rows across five scenarios, collapsing to four questions, walked one at a time. **Outcome: no model change — every judgement was already deliberate.** The five `basis = market` assets ($3.75M) are a deliberate 2025-12-31 revaluation; CVC's 3.75% NAV growth plus a ~4.2% yield is ~8%/yr total and Fund IX returns **2.4× gross** on $408,779 of calls, which are ordinary PE outcomes rather than double-counted ones; the four spending multipliers sit around a real external benchmark (~1%/yr real decline through retirement) with three MORE conservative than it. **⚠️ Two of the 17 fired on streams that are not in the plan** — `New Business` (`amount = 0.00`) and `Tax` (a **−100% `Percent %` change at 2027** retires it permanently, superseded by the per-stream `tax_rate_override` that charges Polish income tax where it is earned). Both guarded with tests; live count **17 → 15**. The zero guard is `amount`-mode ONLY, because all 20 `yield` and 10 `derived` streams store 0.00 while paying real money; the retirement guard exempts a stream revived by a later `Fixed $`, which ADDS to the level rather than scaling it. **The `Tax` advisory was investigated on a wrong premise of mine** — sized as "a flat $55,103 for 36 years, a $1.25M understatement", with the owner agreeing to move it to 1.0. Applying it and regenerating produced a **byte-identical fingerprint**, which is what exposed the −100% row; the edit was reverted and dev returned to `105e5ea4d48ddf8db9efa67e30592651`. *The measurement caught it; the reasoning did not* — [failure-patterns](failure-patterns.md) §1 again. **The pattern underneath both guards, recorded as a limit:** these rules read module CONFIGURATION and never the generated RESULT, so a fully-configured stream can contribute exactly nothing and nothing in the warnings layer can tell. 502 frontend (+11) / **10 e2e** (one new) / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / no migration / no regenerate.
- **v3.25.2** (2026-08-09) — **patch: the variant sync silently dropped `disposal_cost_pct`.** No migration. **Moves nothing on its own** (prod carries no rates yet); it is the prerequisite for the rates landing correctly. Found by setting [CR078](../cr/cr-078-disposal-selling-costs.md)'s selling-cost rates on `2026 Base` and regenerating all five: **only Base moved.** The four variants came back byte-identical — and that was the finding, not the result. `syncVariant` reads a MODULE's columns from `information_schema`, which is CR050's deliberate fix for exactly this class after `copyScenario`'s hand-maintained list omitted `has_valuation`. But a child TABLE's columns are still **hand-listed** in `SCHEDULE_TABLES`, and migration 062's new column was never added — so four scenarios silently kept **GROSS proceeds on 5.5M of property sales**, and nothing errored. Fixed in both places the column must be named (the schedule column list and `interceptSchedules`' patch builder) and guarded by **`variantSchedules.test.js`**, which asserts every `SCHEDULE_TABLES` list against the **live schema** and names the offending column when it drifts — DB-backed on purpose, so the next column added to any of those four tables fails there rather than in a scenario nobody re-reads. It fails on the pre-fix code. **This is migration 057's own warning coming true.** That file recorded, in 2026-08, that *the module columns ride along free, a child table does not*. Writing it down was not enough, because nothing enforced it. 880 backend across 64 suites.
- **v3.25.1** (2026-08-09) — **patch: doc sync after a six-release day.** No code, no migration, no numbers. A `/close` on an already-closed tree, which is what turned it into an audit rather than a release: nothing was pending, so the work was checking whether the docs still told the truth. **Three defects found.** (1) `project-description.md` never recorded **`forecast_module_disposals.disposal_cost_pct`** — the schema fact CR078 added a release earlier. (2) **CR076's status header still read "§8 steps 1–3"** when §8 had been complete since v3.22.0, and it omitted that §11 and §21 shipped too. (3) **CR079's header still read "awaiting release"** after it had shipped in v3.25.0. All three are the drift `/close` exists to catch, and none was visible from the code. `status.md` cut **122 → 94**: the *Live infrastructure* block moved to [guides/infrastructure.md](../guides/infrastructure.md) — it changes on a completely different clock from a session snapshot — and the shipped-work paragraph collapsed to one linked sentence per CR. Every cut has come from **moving** something, never from deleting what is true. The new guide also records WHY each infrastructure rule exists, since every line of it is a rule something has already broken. The **Next** list was stale in the other direction: it still called for selling costs as CR-sized work that had already shipped. It now says the thing that is actually true — the capability is **dormant and worth nothing until a rate is typed** — alongside `Retirement Home`'s money basis, which CR079's toggle has just made checkable by eye. 876 backend / 491 frontend / 9 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets.
- **v3.25.0** (2026-08-09) — **minor: [CR079](../cr/cr-079-real-terms-view.md) — the plan in today's money, on the Review.** No migration. **NO forecast numbers move** — a display transform over data already loaded. Every forecast figure was **nominal** and no surface said so: at 2.5%, 2026 → 2062 is a **2.43× factor**, so Base's headline **4,674,650 is about 1,921,719** of today's purchasing power. It is also the question the owner had been answering all week — `Social Security` at full CPI vs 0.25, `Purchases` at 0.5, `Retirement Home`'s 200,000 dated 2052 are all real-terms judgements made against a nominal-only display. **Five design decisions taken one at a time before any wiring** (`/question`): **Review only** (it carries all four money paths, so doing it completely there proves the approach on the hardest surface; Compare reaches its numbers through a separate `buildScenarioMatrix` path and would have doubled the area a partial application could hide in); **banner + labelled KPIs** (a screenshot crops to the figures, not the control — the same reasoning as CR074's *dismissed is never invisible*); **resets to nominal every visit** (nominal is the basis the export, the audit CSVs and every figure in the docs speak, so the page agrees with its surroundings by default and switching is a deliberate act); and **the export stays nominal always**, because a file loses the banner and money with no stated basis is the failure this whole review has been removing. **The deflator core** reads the SAME step function `fcbuilder-setup.buildRates` walks — a deflator built from a different reading of the same rows would silently disagree with the numbers it deflates. Base year exactly 1; years **before** it are INFLATED so the 2025 actual is comparable with everything to its right; a scenario with no inflation returns **null** and disables the control rather than rendering a deflator of 1.0 that would claim nominal and real are the same thing (the display-side twin of CR076 D7); null and blank pass straight through, because a missing cell is not a zero. **A unit test caught a real defect:** `Number(null)` is 0 and passes `Number.isFinite`, so a missing base year anchored the series on **year zero** and produced deflators around 10³⁰⁰. **Wired at the `FCReviewTable` prop boundary**, so the table component is untouched and each deflated view derives from its NOMINAL memo — never chained off another, so nothing is deflated twice. The bank series is the one that had to be right: it **accumulates**, so it is deflated once AFTER accumulation; deflating during would divide each year's addition by a different factor and produce a balance that is nobody's money. **⚠️ A naming trap avoided rather than hit:** `FCReview`'s existing `baseYear` is `sortedYears[0]` = PeriodStart − **2**, the last ACTUAL year, while `baseYears` (plural) holds PeriodStart − 1. Anchoring on the obvious-looking variable would have deflated to 2025 money and labelled it 2026 — R7's shape exactly. **Two things the gates could not see.** `real-terms.spec.js` (new, browser) asserts what jsdom cannot — the cell changes, the banner is visible WITH the numbers, and unchecking restores the original string; its first run **SKIPPED**, because the Review is empty until the engine has run, which is the graceful-degradation path working and would have read as a pass on a less explicit spec. And `FCReview.css` **did not exist** — the page imports `PageLayout.css` only — so the new stylesheet was **orphaned**, never imported and never loaded, while every gate *including the browser check* passed, because they assert behaviour and text rather than appearance. Caught by noticing the file was staged as ADDED rather than modified. *A new stylesheet is not wired by existing.* **Also in this release:** `Fixed $` / `One-Off $` change rows now state **which year's money** they are in ([CR076 §21](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md)) — the engine adds them RAW, so `Social Security`'s 20,000 dated 2035 is 2035 dollars and `Retirement Home`'s 200,000 at 2052 is about **105,000** in 2026 money. Verified against the engine BEFORE the label was written (20,000 at 2035 → 25,602 at 2045 = 20,000 × 1.025¹⁰), which is the discipline CR078 broke earlier the same day. 876 backend / 491 frontend / **9 e2e** / lint 0 errors / six ratchets.
- **v3.24.0** (2026-08-09) — **minor: [CR078](../cr/cr-078-disposal-selling-costs.md) — a selling cost on a disposal.** **Migration 062.** **DORMANT: no forecast number moves** — no disposal carries a cost, so all five scenarios regenerate byte-identical; the numbers move when the owner types a rate, and *that* edit gets its own measurement. Every disposal booked **GROSS** proceeds: no agent commission, transfer tax, legal or broker fee, no Spanish plusvalía. On `2026 Base` that is **5,566,755 of property sales** (3–6% all-in = 167K–334K) and **5,445,368 of business sales** (1–3% = 54K–163K), none of it modelled. **The base year is the sharpest case** — its 1,239,753 folds straight into the sweep's OPENING CASH, which the sweep pins to its band every year, so a 4% error rides all 36 forecast years instead of washing out (CR049 §1 again). **Per ROW, and the live data is the argument:** a property sale carries 3–6%, a business sale 1–3% negotiated, and `CVC Fund VIII`/`IX`'s **2,033,048 of capital returns carry nothing at all** — they are distributions modelled as disposals, and any coarser rate would silently charge a selling fee on them. **The accounting decides the code:** a selling cost reduces the AMOUNT REALIZED, so it comes off the cash **and** off the taxable gain — netting only the cash would have the plan pay tax on money it never received. Verified on prod data to the cent: `SP - Panorama Mar 4` at 5% moved cash **453,488.37 → 430,813.95** (exactly 5%) and 2027 tax **12,670.99 → 5,868.66** (exactly 30% of the cost), then reverted. **Migration 062** adds `disposal_cost_pct numeric(6,4)`, **nullable with NO default**, plus a CHECK in `[0,100)`. **NULL ≠ 0**: NULL is *no cost modelled*, 0 is *considered and free* — a `DEFAULT 0` would read identically to the engine but would assert the owner had considered and chosen zero for 20 existing disposals, and CR077's advisory needs the distinction to know what to ask. Applied to dev **through `migrate.js`**, probed with real writes (−1 rejected by the CHECK, 100 by the numeric precision, 5.7500 lossless), post-conditions **structural only** — no row count, which is the 046/050/052 defect and vacuous on an empty database. **⚠️ The ordering bug is the most instructive part.** The first implementation computed the cost **before** the Full-disposal branch while its comment claimed it ran *after* — so `disposeValues` was not yet final, the cost read zero for a full sale, and **the gain moved while the cash did not**: precisely the half the CR's own §3 warns about. [failure-patterns](failure-patterns.md) §1 again, a comment describing intent rather than behaviour, committed in the same file as the warning against it. Caught by **measuring on real data, not by reading**; a test now pins the Full case. **Write path covers BOTH sites** — `replaceModuleSchedules` and the create route — because a field added to one and not the other is the projection drift [CR073](../cr/cr-073-two-recurrence-guards.md) closed after three occurrences in three days. NULL round-trips in both directions, verified through the live API. `normalizeTransfers` carries `CostPct` **only when set**, so an empty field never becomes 0 and an Invest row is unchanged on the wire. **Still open:** the CR077 advisory for a property disposal with no cost (CR078 §4) — deliberately left until the first rates are typed, so it does not fire on a plan that has simply not been filled in yet. 876 backend / 475 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets.
- **v3.23.0** (2026-08-09) — **minor: [CR077](../cr/cr-077-assumption-advisor-tab.md) increment 1 — Cash Health splits into *Integrity* and *Assumptions to consider*.** No migration. **NO forecast numbers move** — a reading surface over the existing derivation, and the only release in this sequence that needed no regenerate. Owner-requested: the panel mixed two kinds of statement under one heading and one severity scale — *"`Business Loan` is configured but excluded"* (a DEFECT to fix) beside *"`CVC Fund IX` both pays a yield and returns capital"* (a JUDGEMENT only the owner can make). They demand different things, so the owner was re-triaging the same calls on every render, which is exactly what CR074's dismissals exist to relieve. **Measured on prod's 2026 Base: 4 integrity, 12 advisory** — the integrity tab now holds four genuine rows where the single list was heading past twenty. **Classification is CENTRAL, not per-`push`:** an unlisted id defaults to **integrity**, the louder of the two, so a rule that forgets to classify itself lands in the tab that gets READ rather than the one that gets ACCEPTED; and every exported producer returns classified warnings so no consumer can receive one without a `kind`. The test is not severity — it is whether something is WRONG or merely worth DECIDING. **One new rule, `escalation-below-inflation`:** `growth_mult` is a multiplier of inflation, so below 1 shrinks in real terms — and the form's hint said the OPPOSITE until v3.21.0 while **70 of 110** streams carry an explicit multiplier. Direction changes the sentence: an income below inflation is a risk the owner carries, while **an expense below inflation makes the plan LOOK BETTER**, which is the more dangerous of the two because nothing else on the page flags optimism. Blank never fires (blank means 1). **⚠️ A rule was written and then DELETED.** `idle-cash-unpriced` fired on every scenario that has a sweep — which is all of them, always. A rule that cannot NOT fire carries no information: it is documentation wearing a warning's clothes, it is precisely the noise CR074 removes, and it permanently suppressed the all-clear (gated on `warnings.length === 0`). **A unit test caught that, not review.** The underlying point stays a decision in CR076 §7 Q1. *The test for any new advisory: could this be absent on a plausible plan? If not, it is not a finding.* **CR074's three properties are preserved PER TAB**, and the empty states are worded differently on purpose — an empty *integrity* tab is a claim about the PLAN (CR045 §1's all-clear, correctly scoped), an empty *advisory* tab only about the list (*"not a statement that your assumptions are right — only that none tripped a rule"*). Saying "cash stays funded" from the advisory tab would be CR074's property-2 mistake in a new place; the section-level all-clear still requires BOTH tabs empty. **Deliberately NOT in this increment: the LLM stage.** The deterministic rules are the foundation it would prioritise or phrase, and shipping the generator first would put a confident sentence about the engine on the one panel where a wrong one does most damage (CR045 §1: a $20M shortfall sat there unremarked). When it comes, the constraint is fixed — **nothing generated may state a number or assert what the engine does**. **Also applied this day (data, owner decisions):** `Social Security` → **full CPI** — it escalated at 0.25 × inflation = 0.625%/yr against a statutory COLA, so 20,000 in 2035 reached only 23,664 by 2062, losing ~40% of its purchasing power; now 38,956. The owner chose full CPI **with cuts modelled separately**, keeping how the benefit is INDEXED and whether it will be CUT as two visible inputs rather than one multiplier carrying both. Measured on dev, applied to prod, fingerprints byte-identical; Base +168,460, Downside +213,414. Caveat recorded not fixed: the 20,000 is a `Fixed $` row, added raw in the money of its own year, so it is **2035 dollars** (~13,300 today). And `OCME` at −30 (−75%/yr) confirmed a **deliberate write-off** — no code change, R10 goes on flagging it and is dismissed rather than suppressed, which is CR074 behaving as designed. **Net assets at 2062:** Base **4,674,650** · Buy Business **9,750,208** · Downside **2,574,049** · Upside **8,047,180** · SRQ **−596,919**. 870 backend / 475 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets — the dead-token ratchet caught a `--text` that does not exist in this codebase (`--ink` does).
- **v3.22.0** (2026-08-09) — **minor: [CR076](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md) §8 step 6 — D2 through D6, and §8 is COMPLETE.** No migration. **⚠️ MOVES FORECAST NUMBERS — all five scenarios.** Five separate defects, each built, tested and measured ONE AT A TIME on dev against an engine first proven idempotent, so every dollar of the combined move is attributable. **D2 — the sweep's opening cash was the last reader still using `closing_balance`**, the method `reports.js` abandoned as *"prone to stale PS data"*; everything else in Fin is `opening_balance + Σ(amount)`. On prod 12 of 17 bank accounts agreed exactly and **5 did not** — `PKO EUR` stored **−4,848.85 EUR** against a book value of **+151.15** (the sign simply wrong), and `PKO TFI` had **no `closing_balance` row at all** so 6,000 PLN counted as zero. Totals 358,267 vs 369,544, a gap of **11,276.93** — exactly the constant offset the Review's Bank Accounts row carried in every year of every scenario, and the series `fcWarnings` W1/W4 judge "below the low band" against. **The app was never wrong**; after the fix the bank row reads the band itself. Extracted to `crud.getOpeningBankCash` so it could be TESTED against seeded rows (six DB-backed cases, each seeding a wrong stored balance so the old code fails by construction) — and the extraction caught its own regression when `const startingCash` broke CR075's `+= correctedBaseYearDelta`. **D3 — the USD cost basis was re-translated every year** instead of carried, so `United Beverages`' basis fell from 1,113,492 (its stored acquisition rate of 3.5923) to 888,889 once Downside's 4.5 bit: **224,603 USD of basis vanishing with no transaction behind it**, taxed as a gain and never booked as one. **This one needed an owner decision and §9 had flagged it unverified for good reason** — the engine computes the gain AND the tax in local currency, which is *correct* if the gain is taxed where the asset sits, and wrong only for a **USD-functional** taxpayer. Owner decision 2026-08-09: **USD-functional (US person)**, which the engine's own tax comment already assumed. Basis now walks the same recurrence as its LC twin: initial at the acquisition rate, contributions at the rate of the year they are made, releases pro-rata on the same (currency-independent) fraction. Verified on the mechanism: UB's 2037 tax **−399,658.41 → −347,999.69**, a difference of **51,658.72** — the predicted 51,659 to the cent, and 23% of the vanishing basis. **D4 — the base year's income tax still read the TYPED amount** after CR075 made the base year the budget, so income and the tax on it came from different sources. Only two streams reach that block and each owns its FC line outright: `Barkeria` agrees either way (66,799, but only because its budget was derived from the typed figure), while `UB Income` typed 500,000 PLN = 128,205 USD against a **192,266** budget. UB's 2027 tax now reads **−44,221.09** = 192,265.63 × 23% exactly. **The first fix here that REDUCES net worth** — it charges tax that was being missed. **Upside moves only +24.33**, and that is the sharpest verification in the set: its UB stream is typed **750,000** PLN, and 750,000/3.9 = 192,308 already matched the budget, so there was nothing to correct. **D5 — a Full disposal halved the sale year twice**, two independent ways: the CR046 window may already have halved that index (the acquisition gate guards against exactly this and says *"that would leave 25%"*; the disposal path did not), and an MV-driven stream is **already** half because the disposal zeroes `marketValues[dispIdx]` and a yield averages it with the prior year. Both verified at exactly 2×: `CVC Fund VIII`'s 2033 dividend **3,462.52 → 6,925.05**, `Sarasota House`'s 2048 Property Costs **−19,367.68 → −38,735.36**. **SRQ moves opposite to the other four**, because the two halves push opposite ways and `Sarasota House` exists only there — one sign everywhere would have been the suspicious result. **D6 — a capital LOSS now offsets a same-year GAIN.** `SP - Panorama Mar 4` disposed at a gain and was taxed while `SP - Sea Senses` disposed at a LOSS on the same day and the loss was worth nothing. Done as a scenario-level pass (an offset is a fact about two modules at once), additive as a `_gain_netting` credit so no module's arithmetic is rewritten, **bucketed by rate** (netting a 23% gain against a 30% loss would invent a number), capped at the smaller side, with a 0%-rate module neither paying nor absorbing. One credit of **8,679.67** per scenario — larger than the 7,368 first estimated, correctly, because that predated D3. **It reintroduced the exact bug D2 had just closed:** the first cut wrote only the `Taxes` row, so the Review saw the relief and the sweep did not, and the bank line read 208,679.67 against a 200,000 band. A module writes its P&L row AND a matching cash row; the netting now does too. **Checking the bank line against the band is the cheapest test that engine and app still agree**, and it has now caught two separate divergences. **Net assets at 2062:** Base 4,442,681 → **4,571,404** · Buy Business → **9,647,452** · Downside → **2,442,858** · Upside → **7,942,686** · SRQ → **−678,505**. Also in this release: `status.md` cut 135 → 86 by lifting the recurring-failure table into its own [failure-patterns.md](failure-patterns.md), which now holds seven named shapes. 870 backend / 468 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.21.0** (2026-08-09) — **minor: [CR076](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md) §8 steps 4–5.** No migration. **⚠️ MOVES FORECAST NUMBERS — `2026 Downside` only, +112,337.11; the other four scenarios move by exactly 0.00.** **Step 4 — the growth hint said the opposite of the arithmetic.** The stream card read *"Blank = 1 = inflation. 0 = flat in today's money."* The engine is `pct = inflation × mult` compounded on the prior level, so **1** is what holds its value in today's money and **0** freezes the CASH amount and sheds ~2.5%/yr in real terms — both clauses backwards. This is load-bearing: **70 of 110** amount streams carry an explicit multiplier, most below 1, and the new text spells out 0.5 explicitly for that reason. The numbers entered while the label was wrong are now an **owner question** ([CR076 §11](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md)): `Social Security` at **0.25** against a statutory full-CPI COLA is the one to check first; `Purchases` 0.5 sheds ~36% of purchasing power by 2062. `Healthcare` 1.1 and `Tax` 0 look deliberate. **New rule R10** catches the adjacent hazard — `growth_rate` is a MULTIPLIER of inflation and nothing checked its magnitude, so `OCME Sp. z o.o.` at **−20** (read as −50%/yr, decaying 56,500 PLN to 113 by 2032) sat unflagged in all five scenarios. Threshold 5, set well clear of 1.5 — the largest value legitimately in use — so it cannot fire on a real choice; **verified against prod: exactly 5 firings, 105 modules silent.** Flow modules exempt. **Step 5a — D7, a missing inflation rate now FAILS LOUD.** `buildRates` returned `entries[0]?.Rate ?? 0`, so a scenario with no inflation row silently produced **0% for the whole horizon** — and since CR072 §8 growth is `growth_rate × inflation` and a yield is `inflation + spread`, that stops every asset appreciating, every yield paying and every stream escalating, for 36 years, with no error and a page that renders perfectly. The asymmetry was the argument: the FX path already fails loud on exactly this shape while moving far less of the model, and the four assumption documents key scenarios **by name**, so a rename is one edit away from this state (which is what the test seeds, rather than an empty list nobody would ship). A rate of **0 is a REAL rate and is allowed through** — deliberate zero inflation is legitimate to model, and that test doubles as the falsification since it returns the same `[0,0,0,0]` the old code produced for an *absent* rate. Moves nothing: all five scenarios regenerate byte-identical. **Step 5b — D8, the base year now reads the rate declared FOR it (⚠️ moves numbers).** `buildRates` builds from PeriodStart, so a row dated **PeriodStart−1** survived only as the loop's seed and was overwritten the moment a PeriodStart row existed — but the frame's first column **is** PeriodStart−1, so the base year read an assumption the owner never declared for it. `2026 Downside` declares **FX 2026: PLN 3.9** and 2027: 4.5, and the engine struck the **2026** column at 4.5, making the 3.9 inert. `loadScenarioConfig` now resolves `scenario.BaseYearRates` via `rateAtYear` — the same step-function walk `buildRates` uses — and both `idx < 0` sites consume it: the builder's FX loop and growth series **and the convergence loop's mirrors of both**, because that loop overwrites the builder's rows and a difference there would be D1 all over again in the FX column. Inflation gets the same treatment though nothing moves today: `fcbuilder-module.js`'s own comment argues CR072 §8 was right *because* inflation is declared for 2026, and that argument dies the moment a 2027 row is added. **Measured** on dev with the engine proven idempotent: Downside 1,937,949.90 → **2,050,287.01 (+112,337.11)**, Base/Buy Business/Upside/SRQ **0.00**. Every PLN module's 2026 value scales by exactly **4.5/3.9 = 1.153846** (`United Beverages` 3,250,000 → 3,750,000). **The clincher:** all five scenarios now report the SAME 2026 `Barkeria` value (**1,024,967.14**) where Downside alone read 888,304.85 — they declare the same 2026 FX, so their base years must agree, and Downside's stress belongs in 2027 where it is declared, not a year early. 849 backend / 468 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.20.0** (2026-08-09) — **minor: [CR076](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md) §8 steps 1–3 — the model review, and the net-worth figures it corrected.** No migration. **⚠️ MOVES FORECAST NUMBERS** (D1), and **corrects five figures this project had published**. Owner-requested five-reviewer audit of the whole forecast model — warning rules, P&L side, balance-sheet side, cash sweep, and a scenario runner doing perturbation tests. **[CR076 §1](../cr/cr-076-forecast-model-review.md) records what is SOUND and is the larger half:** growth is one inflation-multiplier convention applied identically to income and expense, cost basis never grows while contributions raise it, partial disposals apportion basis pro-rata (the arithmetic closes exactly over a module's life — `CVC Fund VIII` proceeds 785,376.56 − basis 500,000 = 285,376.55 = the sum of its four realized-gain rows), the gain is **proceeds − basis at the DISPOSAL year** (verified numerically, because a warning had asserted the opposite), the half-year convention is real, loans read interest off the realized balance and retire to exactly zero with nothing after, no balance-sheet stock is summed across years, and the sweep's availability cap is forward-looking. Gates green, engine idempotent, dev byte-identical to prod. **⚠️ §2 — the P0, and it was OURS.** Net worth at 2062 was being quoted in CR075, `status.md` and this roadmap from a SQL roll-up that summed `forecast_entries` by account — which reads `Bank Accounts`, a per-module **annual cash movement**, as if it were a balance. At 2062 Base those rows are `Living Expenses −201,945 / Retirement Home −256,017 / Travel −85,903 / …`: one year's spending. The sweep pins cash to a 200k–300k band, so the −682,531 the roll-up used could never be a balance. **The app was never wrong** — `FCReview.jsx` and `fcCompareUtils.js` both carry the bank row as a running balance seeded from the ledger. Corrected via the app's OWN `buildScenarioMatrix` rather than a third re-derivation, and cross-validated against an independent stock-based reconstruction agreeing to within **exactly 11,277.06** (= D2, the separately-identified bank-seed defect), with the reconstructed cash path landing on **exactly 200,000.00** and touching 300,000.00 in sweep-deposit years. The error ran +894K to −84K and was **not constant**, so it contaminated comparisons and not just levels (a measured delta was off ~4.4%). *Rule earned: a forecast figure quoted in any document comes from the app's own exported functions or the engine, never a SQL re-derivation written for the occasion.* **D1 — two copies of the growth formula, drifted.** `index.js`'s convergence loop kept its own mirror of the market-value series and then UPDATEs the rows the builder wrote, so the mirror wins. CR072 §8 added the pre-PeriodStart clamp to `fcbuilder-module.js` and left the mirror on the old formula — so every MV-driven stream was struck on the **previous** year's balance pair: prod's `Fidelity Stocks` 2027 dividend read **27,723.71**, the average of its 2025 and 2026 values, beside a stored 2027 balance of **1,438,381**. One module, one year, two market values, and the whole yield series one compounding year short. Now **one** implementation (`growthPctForYear` in `fcbuilder-common.js`) called from both, with `growth-formula-parity.test.js` failing if either re-derives it (comments stripped first, so the formula may still be described). Measured on dev against an engine proven idempotent, then the extraction verified **byte-identical** to the measured fix: Base 4,398,898 → **4,442,681 (+43,783)**, Buy Business +43,738, Downside +55,962, Upside +43,734, SRQ +46,203. The 2027 dividend now reads 28,416.80 — the builder's own figure. **839 tests passed while this existed**, because each side was internally consistent and nothing compared them. **§3 — three more warning rules wrong, making 5 of 8.** **R7** was false on all 20 rows it fired on: the engine indexes a disposal against the **module's own base year**, not PeriodStart, so a 2026 sale on a 2025-based module executes — prod shows `US - Nokomis` booking 394,875 of proceeds with **no balance row left**, one row below a warning saying the balance stays on the books forever. CR075 §5 had fixed the rule's INPUT and nobody checked its SENTENCE. It becomes `disposal-in-base-year-*` at `info`, saying what actually happens; the original claim survives only for a disposal before the *base* year (0 prod rows). **W2** summed a quantity that is cumulative by construction — `runningCash` is declared once outside the sweep's year loop and the shortfall entry never tops it up — showing **$1.2M** where the true terminal gap was 1,017,119; now the worst year. **R5**'s loss branch promised an offset the engine never applies. **Three tests that pinned the wrong claims were rewritten, not deleted**; W2's now uses prod's real SRQ rows so it fails against the old code with exactly the figure the owner was shown. **The cash-sweep audit CSV** was written from the FIRST sweep pass, before Step 7b deletes the sweep rows, re-runs to convergence and inserts different entries — so `FCCashSweepModal`, the owner's only window into the sweep, described a forecast that had been discarded: on SRQ it claimed every year landed at exactly 200,000.00 with no shortfall while the entries carried `Cash Shortfall −1,017,119.05`, and on Base it claimed `Fidelity Stocks` was liquidated in 2061-62 when the entries never touched it. Now written last, from the committed run, and gained the **`Shortfall` column the header never had** (`sweepLog` always carried the field). Verified on dev: CSV 114,652.46 / 970,915.88 against committed entries −114,652.46 / −970,915.88, and 2062 reports `shortfall` rather than a `sweep_out` that never happened. `rebalanceEntries` now also comes from the committed run. **Still open in CR076 §8:** the swapped growth hint (§5 — *"0 = flat in today's money"* is backwards, and **70 of 110** streams carry an explicit multiplier, most below 1), D7/D8 fail-loud guards, and D2–D6 one at a time behind the gate. **§7 holds seven owner decisions** — price the cash, selling costs on disposals, nominal-vs-real, `OCME` at −20 (which the engine reads as −50%/yr). 839 backend / 466 frontend / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.19.0** (2026-08-08) — **minor: [CR075](../cr/cr-075-base-year-is-the-budget.md) — the base year is the budget.** No migration. **⚠️ MOVES FORECAST NUMBERS, deliberately and with owner sign-off** (see below). Owner-found by reading the Review's 2026 column: *"why are CVC, Dividend and Interest Income all zero?"* All three are **yield-mode** streams, and a yield stream's `amount` is **0 by construction** — the card hides the box because a yield is a rate, not a figure — while `getBaseYearValues` summed each stream's typed amount. So year −1 was never the budget; it was the modules' inputs, diverging two ways: **129,000 of budgeted investment income over 3.3M of market value read as zero**, and even the amount-mode streams disagreed (`UB Income` 128,205 against a budget of 192,266). Income understated by **193,071**, net by **152,802** — and `index.js` does `startingCash += budgetNCF`, so that figure is the **cash sweep's opening cash**, which the sweep pins to its band every year, so the error rode all 36 forecast years instead of washing out. **The third instance of the CR049 §1 failure mode in this one function**, after the ~65K liability-gated expense branch and the ~400K unconverted-FX one — and the function already carried a branch for exactly this shape on LOAN streams (*"the stream amount is 0 on a derived stream by construction"*), never extended to `yield`. **The fix** reads `budget_entries` grouped by FC line via the same recursive CTE `fcLines.getBudgetTotals` uses, verified against the budget **line by line: 15 lines, 0 mismatches, totals to the cent**. It DELETES the machinery both earlier bugs lived in — the CR046 window filter and July-1 half-year (a budget entry is dated, so six monthly rows are six months of money), the CR062 loan-interest derivation, and the CR064 P8 per-currency conversion (`base_amount` is already USD; verified PLN 3.227, EUR 0.860). **Owner decisions 2026-08-07:** budget only with **no derived fallback** — a derived figure beside a budgeted one double-counts the moment a line is partly budgeted and cannot be told apart on screen; and **the budget's own recorded rates** rather than the scenario's FX assumption, so 2025 actual and 2026 budget agree with each other and the FX break sits where the projection begins. Consequence stated rather than hidden: `budget_entries` has no `scenario_id`, so **all five scenarios now share one base year** (−137,555) where before they differed (Upside −226,254, Downside −291,934) — those were never different budgets, only different module inputs. **New R9 warning** pays the price of budget-only by making a gap visible: a module that implies base-year money the budget does not carry is reported rather than reading as a real zero. Keyed to fire on a **yield** stream despite `amount = 0`, since a rule testing the amount alone would miss exactly the case that caused this CR; a stream with no line at all stays R6's finding. Fires **0×** on prod data, and was proven able to fire by parking a budget line on dev. **⚠️ A SECOND BUG fell out of it.** R9's first render said *"Nothing is budgeted … in 2024"*. `computeForecastWarnings` was passed `periodStart: years[0]`, but FCReview unshifts **PeriodStart−1 AND PeriodStart−2** onto `sortedYears` for the actual and budget columns — so `years[0]` is PeriodStart−2. **R7 compares disposal dates against it, and on prod silently missed 20 disposals dated 2026-07-01 across all five scenarios** (`US - Nokomis`, `SP - Sea Senses`, `Tax Liabilities`, `SP - Panorama Mar 4`, four per scenario): **Full** disposals in the budget year that the engine never executes, so the balances they were meant to clear sit on the books for the whole plan with nothing saying so. The real PeriodStart is now passed explicitly and pinned by a test; the panel went 13 → 17 issues. Same class as [CR071 §8](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md#8-r5-was-wrong--owner-found-2026-08-06-fixed-in-v3181) — **2 of the 8 detection rules have now been found wrong this way, and the rest are unaudited.** **Measured** on a dev re-synced from prod, with the engine first proven idempotent (two regenerates byte-identical): net worth at 2062 — Base 2,845,816 → **3,243,520 (+397,705)**, Buy Business +397,132, Downside +513,774, Upside +230,666, SRQ −337; SRQ's shortfall **−3,195,733 → −1,837,896**, starting a year later. The mechanism is coherent: Base retains +391,799 more Fidelity Fixed Income, exactly offsetting −391,799 less swept, earning +341,425 more interest and paying −102,428 more tax. Nothing disappeared in the switch; four lines appeared. **All 14 failing tests were rewritten to the new contract, not deleted** — including one that would have kept PASSING vacuously ("rent starting 2028 is not 2026 income" returns 0 because no budget row exists, not because a window was honoured). 834 backend / 465 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.18.1** (2026-08-07) — **patch: [CR071](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md) §8 — the R5 warning was WRONG, owner-found.** No migration, **no forecast numbers move** — R5 is a reporting rule and the engine was right all along. The owner opened `Barkeria Sp. z o.o.`'s Module Output beside its warning: the panel said *"is sold without realizing any gain — cost basis and market value are both $3.9M, so the sale realizes no gain and pays no tax"*, while the module's own 2040 row read **REALIZEDGAIN 334,294**. R5's comment carried the mistake — *"the gain is dispose × (1 − basis/market), so it is zero exactly while basis equals market"* — which is true, but reads the equality at the **base date** while the engine reads it at the **disposal year**. The basis is flat (only an Invest moves it) and the market value compounds at the module's growth, so by the disposal the two have separated and `fcbuilder-module.js`'s Full branch books `market(disposal) − basis`: **1,339,163 − 1,004,870 = 334,293**, the screenshot to the dollar. **Measured on prod: wrong on 30 of the 35 modules it fired on** — 25 that grow (a gain IS realized) and 5 that SHRINK (a capital LOSS is realized); only the 5 flat ones were ever right. Wrong in the direction that matters, too: it told the owner a taxable sale was tax-free, which is precisely the CR071 failure class — a number that looks authoritative while being wrong — committed by the feature built to catch it. The finding stays (basis identical to market on the base date is usually a placeholder typed from today's value, and then the gain since PURCHASE is understated however the module grows); only the claim changes, branching on growth: flat keeps the original wording, positive says *"is taxed only on growth since the base date"*, negative says *"is sold at a loss against its cost basis"*. Verified in a browser on the real panel — all seven basis-equals-market modules carry the wording matching their growth and **zero rows still claim "realizes no gain and pays no tax" on a module that grows**; falsified by restoring the unconditional claim. **The lesson: a rule that asserts what the engine does must be derived from the engine's own formula, not from a restatement of it.** Eight detection rules, a full suite and a browser QA all passed, because every one checked that the warning FIRED and none checked that what it SAID was true. 835 backend / 460 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.18.0** (2026-08-06) — **minor: [CR074](../cr/cr-074-dismissible-cash-health-warnings.md) — dismissing a cash-health warning.** Migration **061** (`forecast_warning_dismissals`, additive, 0 rows on first apply). **No forecast numbers move** — nothing here touches the engine, the modules or the entries; the warnings are a pure client-side derivation and the only new state is which of them the owner has read. Owner-requested: the panel was showing 21 issues, most of them already judged and accepted (`"Barkeria Sp. z o.o." income is taxed at the full rate` is a design decision, not a defect), and a list that long is one nobody reads. Per-row `×`, **Dismiss all**, **Show N dismissed** / **Restore all**. **The design is defined by what it must not be able to do.** [CR045](../cr/cr-045-forecast-cash-warnings-liquidation.md) §1 built this panel because a **$20M unfunded shortfall sat on this page unremarked** — a quiet page mistaken for a healthy plan — and a dismiss button is precisely a way to make the page quiet. So three properties are load-bearing: (1) **dismissed is never invisible** — the header keeps counting the FULL number and says how many are dismissed, one click shows them struck through; (2) **all-dismissed is not all-clear** — with everything accepted the panel says exactly that in its own state rather than falling through to `Cash stays funded across every forecast year`, which is a claim about the PLAN and would become a lie (the all-clear is now gated on the raw `warnings.length`, before dismissal is applied); (3) **a dismissal EXPIRES when the warning changes.** `sweep-source-exhausted` is the same id whether the drain lands in 2061 or 2041, so a dismissal keyed on the id alone would let one click in a comfortable year permanently silence the rule — including the moment it turns urgent. Dismissals are stored against `warningFingerprint`, an FNV-1a hash of the warning's severity, years, amount and **detail sentence** (where the rules put their figures — *"Cost basis and market value are both $3.9M"*); a dismissal suppresses a warning only while the fingerprint matches, and a returning one is flagged `staleDismissal` and says *"You dismissed this before — it is back because its figures changed"*, because otherwise the owner re-reasons from scratch about something they already judged. Scoped **per scenario**: warning ids embed a module NAME and the same module carries different numbers in each, so accepting a finding on Base must not silence it on Upside. Three routes under `/forecast/warnings/dismissals` (GET map · POST upsert of one-or-many, so **Dismiss all is ONE request** not N racing writes against the unique index · DELETE one or the whole scenario). A dismissal **with no fingerprint is refused 400** — it could never expire. Writes are optimistic and reconciled, and a store that cannot be read shows **everything** rather than nothing. Verified in a browser on the real 25-issue panel: 25→24 on one dismiss with the header reading `25 issues · 1 dismissed`, **surviving a reload** (the reason this is in the DB and not localStorage), 0 visible after Dismiss all with the **all-clear banner absent**, back to 25 on Restore all — and with the stored fingerprints overwritten in the database to simulate the figures moving, **both warnings came back** with the stale notice. 835 backend / 459 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.17.1** (2026-08-06) — **patch: [CR073](../cr/cr-073-two-recurrence-guards.md) — two recurrence guards — plus [CR072](../cr/cr-072-valuation-module-inputs.md) P7, the drill-down modal.** No migration; **no forecast numbers move** (the guard refuses a state no prod row is in, and the projection refactor was proven key-for-key identical against the old code still running on prod). **CR073 (1) — Known Issue #2's missing guard**, open since 2026-08-04: `Sarasota House` carried an expense of 45,000 with no FC line, the engine's stream loop skips the P&L row (`if (!line) continue`) while the CASH path below takes every stream, so **−1,203,432 across 21 years** left Bank Accounts and appeared on nothing — Net Cash Flow and Expenses disagreeing by exactly that with no screen able to say why. CR062 closed this shape for loans (`assertLoanHasInterestLine`) and **nothing guarded an asset**. `assertStreamsHaveLines` now runs on POST and PUT, keyed on the AMOUNT being non-zero because that is what makes a stream produce a flow at all — a 0-amount stream posts nothing in any mode, and **15 such rows exist on prod** which stay legal. **0 prod rows are in the refused state.** On PUT it checks only what the body sends, so an edit that never mentions streams is not refused on the strength of a stored row. The editor checks the same condition first and names the card, so the reachable path is a sentence rather than a raw 400. **CR073 (2) — the LIST/DETAIL module projections**, two hand-kept PascalCase lists of ~35 keys that drifted **three times in three days**, every time DETAIL missing what LIST had, every time surfacing as the editor guessing at state it should have been told (`HasValuation` v3.14.2 · the sweep fields v3.15.0 · `fc_line_name` v3.16.0). Now one `moduleCommonFields` both endpoints spread; `loadModuleStreams` joins `fc_lines` (its bare `SELECT *` was the v3.16.0 half); and `forecast.projection-parity.test.js` asserts on the RESPONSES — a test of the helper alone would pass while one endpoint quietly stopped spreading it. Where the two genuinely differ (`Type` capitalised for display vs raw for the editor's select; disposal SCALARS vs ROWS) it is pinned, so a later "fix" cannot flatten a real difference. Verified against prod's old code: **key sets identical in both directions, zero type changes**; the 31 value diffs are dev-vs-prod data, provable because each appears in BOTH its snake_case and PascalCase form. **CR072 P7** ships in the same release: the reference drill-down is a real modal reusing the Actuals page's own machinery (`useTransactions`, `ACTUAL_CONFIG`/`BUDGET_CONFIG`, `useTransactionSelection`, `TransactionTable`) with the shared `HierarchyFilter` for categories — type-to-narrow, right-click solo, All pill — opening on the line's own accounts. Its first attempt failed because `TransactionTable` takes `{entry, rowId, isSelected}` wrappers, not transactions: every cell read `entry[column.key]` off `undefined` and the throw unmounted BOTH dialogs, while the isolated test passed throughout **because its mock returned zero rows**. Three further defects caught before shipping — `monthEnabled:false` fell through to the CURRENT month (a full-year figure would have opened one month), the budget row would have queried actual transactions, and the per-currency totals silently capped at 500 loaded rows. The two `[→ Market Value]` / `[→ Cost Basis]` buttons moved onto the Reference block, beside the figure they copy, having sat in the footer next to Save. Owner closed CR072's three open readings: the point-4 parenthetical is explanatory not a filter, USD stays read-only derived, and cost basis is an input on the form since no cost-basis data exists anywhere. Also found and recorded as **Known Issue #18**: a fresh DB enforces `fc_lines.line_type`'s CHECK while dev and prod have no such constraint (migration 007 was auto-baselined on both), so new fixtures passed on dev and failed only in the e2e run. 824 backend / 447 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.17.0** (2026-08-06) — **minor: [CR072](../cr/cr-072-valuation-module-inputs.md) — the balance-sheet module form, and the budget year the engine skipped.** No migration. **⚠️ This release MOVES FORECAST NUMBERS, deliberately and with owner sign-off** (see P5). **P2/P3/P4:** the owner's structure — everything but Type and Currency moves under Valuation, which splits into **Reference value** (the base date, where the observed balance now sits beside it), **Assigned value** (the four figures), and **Forecast assumptions** (growth + gains rate). The standalone Tax section is gone. An **unmatched** module COLLAPSES the two value blocks — measured: all 20 unmatched prod modules are zero on both and carry a base date that has not closed, while all 90 matched ones have both values and a valid date, **110 of 110**. **It is collapse and not hide, and pass 1 of the CR review is why:** `is_matched` is not an engine branch (nothing in `fcbuilder-*` reads it; `market_value` is booked off `has_valuation` alone), so hiding would have had the residue panel assert "the forecast ignores them" — false — and offer a **Clear button that zeroes a live figure**. Two further silent-money bugs were caught before shipping: `is_matched` already switches which FX rate the USD conversion uses **and that effect writes back on open with no user action**, so hiding those fields would have persisted a different number through an invisible control (the derivation is now suspended while collapsed); and `BaseDate` was blanket-exempt from the residue detector on a justification that only held for FLOW modules. Base dates are capped at the last **closed** year-end, with an out-of-range stored year still offered and marked — a select with no matching option renders blank, and a blank control over a real date is how an axis gets rewritten by accident. **P5 — the budget year grows** (`fcbuilder-module.js`): growth was gated on `idx = year − PeriodStart >= 0`, so the gap between a module's base date and the forecast got **zero** growth — `Barkeria` sat at 3,918,992 in both 2025 and 2026 and 2027 came out at exactly ×1.02, the asset growing **once across a two-year gap**. The clincher that this was an off-by-one and not a policy: **inflation is already declared for `Year: 2026` at 2.5%** while the engine first applied it in 2027. Measured old-code vs new-code on a fresh prod copy: the series steps forward exactly one year (Barkeria 2026 1,004,870 → 1,024,967), **+1,284,241 net worth at 2062 and +1,469,849 P&L across five scenarios**. **811 tests passed while this bug existed** — nothing covered pre-PeriodStart growth; three now do. **P0/P1** (shipped as regressions of v3.16.0): the start-year label read *"from 2023"* on flow modules and *"from 2025"* on valuation ones, neither being a year that produces anything — both now name real PeriodStart, which **resolves to null rather than guessing the current year** on a scenario-name miss. The stream reference re-anchors on PeriodStart−2/−1 instead of the module's base year. **P6 + QA:** the Module Output table's two pre-horizon rows show actual and budget in italic with a legend instead of "—", converted at that row's own FX; the stream-card reference — **which had been DEAD since v3.16.0**, because both totals routes return `{data, year}` and `unwrap` only strips a lone-`data` envelope, so every loop threw *"is not iterable"* into a silent catch — now works, via a `Rest.rows` helper that returns a list or nothing; and both actual rows **and** the budget row drill into the accounts behind them, grouped by (account, currency) with local beside USD, because `Property Costs` draws on three currencies and one account carries four. New `GET /fc-lines/actual-breakdown` and `/budget-breakdown` reuse their totals' own recursive CTE and simply stop grouping, so the parts **provably** sum to the whole (verified to the cent, 35 and 32 accounts). 814 backend / 440 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build.
- **v3.16.0** (2026-08-05) — **minor: a line's real history beside the number that plans it, and the Actual field that never worked.** **(1) The Actual comparison was broken on every module that had a line.** It read `fc_line_name` off the stream — a label the **LIST** query's join supplies and the **DETAIL** query the editor loads does not — so it resolved `undefined` and reported *"Actual (no line set)"* while `Children` plainly carried `fc_line_id` 13. **The third time these two projections have drifted in three days** (`HasValuation` v3.14.2, the sweep fields v3.15.0), and [CR070](../cr/cr-070-module-inputs-by-type.md)'s own as-built had already called deriving them from one source "the obvious follow-up… not done here". The fix keys on **`fc_line_id`** — the column both projections carry and the one the engine branches on — and resolves the label from the already-loaded `fcLines` for display only; the row lookup matches on the id too, since matching display strings is how a renamed line silently becomes "no transactions". **(2) Three years of history on each stream card**, owner-requested: actual (base−1, the last complete year) · budget (base) · actual (base, YTD). Both endpoints already existed; nothing on the form had asked the budget one for a line. Placed on the **stream card** rather than the header because the FC line is a property of the *stream* — the header field is removed as duplicate, and a module with two expense streams on different lines made one header figure ambiguous anyway. **The caveat is the feature:** the totals belong to the LINE, so every line shared by more than one module says so (`Property Costs` — 76,656 across six modules — against a card's 34,717 is not a comparison, which is exactly the mistake the old `Account` field made). A zero budget is omitted rather than shown, since 2025 keeps none and a rendered `0` reads as a deliberate plan of zero. **(3) The start-year default was NOT changed to the period start, against the request** — measured refusal: a flow module already anchors at `PeriodStart` ([fcbuilder-module.js:131](../../server/src/services/forecast/fcbuilder-module.js#L131)), so blank already means 2027 there; and a start date is stored July 1, so `applyStreamWindow` **halves its first year** — defaulting the box to 2027 would have turned a 34,717 expense into 17,358, silently, on every new stream. The label was the real defect and now names the module's own first year and states the half-year rule. **The same TDZ trap as CR070's type vocabulary appeared twice more and was caught before commit both times** (`fcLines`, then a duplicate `baseYear`). 811 backend / 428 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build. No migration.
- **v3.15.1** (2026-08-05) — **patch: the forecast module type filter groups by what the module IS, and counts each option.** Nine types in one alphabetical list put `Expense` between `Business` and `Fixed Income`, burying the only distinction that matters. Now three `<optgroup>`s — **Assets / Debt / Flows** — which is the split `has_valuation` already makes, the same flag that decides which fields [CR070](../cr/cr-070-module-inputs-by-type.md)'s form shows. Checked rather than assumed: **on all 170 prod rows the type string and the flag agree**, so the grouping is backed by data and not only by the words. Each option carries the count it would yield, taken with every *other* filter applied and the type filter left out — counting the raw list would overstate the moment an account or status filter is on, and a number that is only right when nothing else is set is worse than no number. **Cosmetic only, like `TYPE_LABELS`, and for the same reason:** types are free text derived from the scenario's own modules, so the map *will* miss one day — a miss costs a heading, never an option, because anything unrecognised lands in a trailing **Other** group and stays selectable. **A filter that silently drops an option hides modules**, so that invariant is asserted directly rather than implied by an example. Biggest first inside a group; case and stray whitespace still find their group. 811 backend / 428 frontend (+7) / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build. No migration.
- **v3.15.0** (2026-08-05) — **minor: forms that fit the module, and warnings where the numbers disagree with intent.** **[CR070](../cr/cr-070-module-inputs-by-type.md) + [CR071](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md), no migration.** Answers the owner's *"a fully customized input for each type of module"* — but as **three rules, not nine forms**, because `PUT /modules/:id` is `!== undefined`-gated (a form that stops sending a field PRESERVES it) while `POST` uses `?? 0` (it zeroes it), so naive per-type hiding is strictly *worse* than the collapse-when-empty it replaces. Capabilities gate on data the engine already branches on (`has_valuation`, `market_value` sign, `loan_interest_rate` ∪ type); type may only ADD (templates, labels, warnings); and a **residue detector** reports any field the form does not render that still holds a value — which is precisely what [CR064 §5](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) demanded before it would allow hiding at all. An Expense module — 50 of prod's 170 — stops presenting Cost Basis, Market Value, Growth, a Gains Tax Override, Invest, Dispose and a Sweep Priority, none of which the engine reads on it. Double-click now opens the editor and the stale read-only drawer is **deleted** (174 lines): six of its rows could not render a value after CR069 turned flows into stream rows. **CR071 ships 8 detection rules** in `fcWarnings.js` — 13 warnings across 34 real modules, and **the four orphaned-line warnings match CR069 §13's four exactly**, which is the calibration signal worth having. **Gate: per-(scenario, account, year) `forecast_entries` sums identical to the cent on a prod copy, 4,030 rows** — nothing in either CR moves a number. **QA found six more things, five of them dead or lying CR069 leftovers:** `Yield Spread` + `Yield Spread Entries` were still rendered, accepted input, and were **not in `MODULE_WRITE_FIELDS`** — typed, saved, silently dropped since v3.14.1; a flow module's `Account` named one of the four accounts its line covers (`Car Expenses` → `Car - Insurance`), and the `PY Actual` beside it was looking the module up in the **balance-sheet** report, so on a `profit_loss` account it was permanently blank rather than merely wrong — replaced by a new **`GET /fc-lines/actual-totals`**, the exact sibling of the existing budget query. The schedule sections then adopted the stream-card idiom, including the coloured left edge, which is read off the engine (`fcbuilder-module.js:534`, `fcbuilder-loan.js:18`) and keyed on the FIELD so a per-type relabel cannot flip it. **A page-crashing regression that only e2e caught:** the derived type vocabulary read `modules` fifteen lines before `useModules` declared it, so `/forecast-modules` threw on load while **the unit suite was fully green**. 811 backend / 421 frontend / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build. **The two CR071 §4 data edits did NOT ship** — they move numbers and need their own change event (see §1.1).
- **v3.14.2** (2026-08-05) — **patch: a credential stopped being served, and two CR069 P3 regressions repaired.** **(1) SECURITY.** `GET /api/v2/util/appdata` returned the whole appdata document to any caller — v3 has no auth — including a live `anthropic_api_key` (108 chars, non-empty, reachable over the Tailscale origin) that **nothing in `server/` or `frontend/src` read**: AI Review goes through the ocr-llm gateway. A dead credential leaking on every page load that touched appdata. The stored value is deleted from **both** homes (the appdata JSON file and the `app_data` table, each backed up to the gitignored `Backups/` first) and the key rotated by the owner. The endpoint now filters on the key **NAME** rather than a fixed list — the failure mode is a key nobody thought to add to a list — and **omits** the field rather than masking it, since a mask still leaks the length. **Checked rather than assumed: it was never in git history** — the historically-tracked `appdata.json` blobs carry no value, and the older string matches are code references in `.env.example` and `aiReview.js`. **(2) D1 — "Create module from unmatched item" had been dead since v3.14.1.** It posted `IncomePct`, which CR069 P3 removed from `MODULE_WRITE_FIELDS`, so the route 400'd — and the catch logged to `console.error`, so the button did nothing and *said* nothing. The bad field is gone, but **the fix that matters is that a failed write now surfaces** through the modal's existing error prop: a 400 that looks like a no-op is how this survived a release. **(3) D2 — no UI path could create a flow module.** `HasValuation` had been accepted by both routes since CR069 P2, but `buildModulePayload` never sent it, so every module made from the form became a balance-sheet module whatever its type said. **Absent must mean TRUE, and that direction is the whole risk:** `editForm.HasValuation` is `undefined` on the create path, so a plain `Boolean()` would have flipped every new Real Estate, Business and Stocks module to a flow module — the engine zeroes its base value, market value and growth, CR041's gate zeroes its streams, and a new property books nothing forever with no error. Both directions are pinned by tests. `GET /modules/:id` now projects the flag too: the **list** endpoint did and the **detail** one did not, which is how the payload builder came to be guessing. 804 backend (+2) / 391 frontend (+2) / 8 e2e / lint 0 errors / six ratchets / clean build. No migration.
- **Unreleased (2026-08-05)** — three tail items from the v3.14.1 close-out, none user-facing, so no version bump.
  **(1) [Known Issue #15](#3-known-issues) fixed** (`11e9e59`): the deploy now refuses a migration absent from **both** ledgers — see that entry for which fix candidate was taken and why the behavioural ones could not hold.
  **(2) `cr051-currency.spec.js` un-skipped** (`29b3a75`): the e2e seed grows `E2E PLN Wallet`, zero-balance and transaction-free so it cannot move `money-paths`' literal 108,500 net worth. **8 e2e, 0 skipped.** The spec now also asserts the USD twin the server derived from what the *picker* sent (400 PLN ÷ 4 = 100), which its own name always claimed and it never checked — falsified by flipping it to 400 and watching the run fail.
  **(3) dev's database refreshed from prod** — dev had drifted (a P3 test edit left an empty stream card that zeroed module 80's income). Dev now regenerates **4,030 sum rows identical to prod's, to the cent**. Two things worth keeping from it: **the deploy's own backup predates the migration that same deploy applies** (Step 1 backs up, Step 2b migrates), so restoring from one lands you one migration short — the runner then applies it, which is the intended path but only if you look; and dev's seven scenario overrides were captured to `Backups/dev_overrides_before_refresh_*.json` before the restore. Prod's set turned out to be the richer one (9 overrides, and `Buy Business` a proper variant where dev's was a parked non-variant); the single genuine loss is dev's yield-mode `Spread %` Upside override — **the exact shape that caught `scheduleEqualsBase`'s nested-row bug during P2** — recoverable from that JSON or from `Backups/dev_before_refresh_*.dump` (both **local to this host only** — `Backups/` is gitignored).








- **v3.14.1** (2026-08-04) — **patch: [CR069](../cr/cr-069-forecast-streams.md) P3 — stream cards, and the retired model is gone (migration 060).** The contract step of expand → migrate → contract, and the phase the owner actually sees. **Net −3,875 lines.** A module's flows are **cards** now (`FCModulesStreams`), one per stream, each carrying its own line, mode, amount, growth, window, tax and change schedule. The difference is structural rather than cosmetic: the Expenses and Income *sections* rendered COLUMNS that existed on every module whether or not it had that flow, and `fcModulePayload` sent all of them on every save — so hiding a section never cleared its value, which is exactly why [CR064 §5](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) refused to gate the form on module type and why CR062's Loan carve-out needed a preview endpoint and a confirmed delete. **A card is a ROW:** a module with no expense has no card because it has no stream, and removing the card removes the row — so the thing §5 could not safely do per-type is now free. The card also refuses to offer a control its mode does not read (no amount on a loan's `derived` interest, `Spread %` only in yield mode), and a mode change drops the change rows the new mode cannot read, which is CR062's retype discipline in miniature. **Migration 060** drops `forecast_income_expense`, `forecast_incexp_changes`, `forecast_module_income_pct` and `forecast_module_income_steps`, the eleven `income_*`/`expense_*` columns, the long-dead `expense_pct`, and `has_valuation`'s DEFAULT — migration 057's own obligation (b), deferred in P2 with its reasoning written down and discharged here. **Its pre-condition is the file's real content:** it REFUSES to drop anything if items exist without flow modules, and its post-condition asserts the stream tables survive, because the two outcomes a contract step must never produce are "destroyed the source of data that has no replacement" and "left no model at all". Also retired: the legacy per-direction write fields, the read-side projection that translated streams back into columns, the incexp repository section, and ~2,770 lines of `FCExp*`. **Gate: per-(scenario, account, year) `forecast_entries` sums identical TO THE CENT on a prod copy WITH the four tables dropped — 4,030 rows, 0 differing.** 802 backend / 389 frontend / 7 e2e (1 skipped), lint 0 errors, six ratchets at baseline, clean build, 60-file chain from empty. **What P3 got wrong, and what caught it:** `FCExpConfirmDeleteModal` was never Expenditures-specific — `COAManagement`, `FCScenarios` and `FCModuleManage` all use it — so deleting it by name would have taken three pages down. Lint passed (ESLint does not resolve imports) and the unit suite passed (those pages have no unit test); **only the production build caught it**, which is the argument for the build being a gate rather than a formality. Renamed `FCConfirmDeleteModal`. **And the e2e suite's root cause is fixed, not just its symptom** — see the Known Issue below, now closed. *Left open at release, CLOSED 2026-08-05 (`29b3a75`) — see below:* `cr051-currency.spec.js` was re-pointed at the Modules page but still skipped, because the form's Currency is a select derived from account traits and the e2e seed's only forecast account is USD; the server half is fully covered by the route test and the survives-a-reopen property by `write-paths` against a stream card.
- **v3.14.0** (2026-08-04) — **minor: [CR069](../cr/cr-069-forecast-streams.md) P2 — Forecast Expenditures and Modules become ONE thing (migrations 057/058/059).** A module is now *identity + optional valuation + N first-class **streams***; `fcbuilder-incexp.js` is **deleted**, and the three divergent implementations of "a money stream that grows from a base-year amount" collapse into one evaluator proved equivalent to all three by tests written against the old formulas. 60 Expenditure items became modules with `has_valuation = FALSE` and one stream; `/incomeexpense` answers **410** and the Expenditures step left the nav (6 steps → 5) in this same deploy, because the engine stopped reading that table in it — leaving the page reachable would have meant saves that succeed and are ignored. **The gate, on every run: per-(scenario, account, forecast_year) summed `forecast_entries`, all five scenarios, regenerated before and after — identical TO THE CENT.** 4,030 rows / 0 differing on a prod copy; 3,798 / 0 on dev. **Eleven defects were caught before production by four different mechanisms, and that is the story of this release.** *The gate itself:* the recursion seeded at index 0 while a valuation module's axis starts at its `base_date` — two years earlier — so it compounded from a zero the projection had just written and `Property Costs`, `Barkeria Income` and `UB Income` **vanished entirely**; three Downside overrides re-pointed but not translated (`base_value` means nothing on a module whose money is on a stream); one Upside override naming `income_amount`, a column the engine stops reading, which would have applied cleanly to nothing for 36 years; and `getBaseYearValues` still reading the retired table that `syncVariant` had just re-materialised from base *without* those overrides. *Dev, where the prod copy was clean:* an `income_pct` override moved **504 rows**, and behind it `syncEntity` **silently dropping** any patch key that was neither a schedule key nor a column, and `scheduleEqualsBase` comparing only the parent columns of the new two-level schedule — so an override differing *only* in its change rows read as equal to base and was pruned. It destroyed the same override twice before the cause was found; both now fail loud, and migration **059** translates the keys 058 missed. *Security review:* `replaceModuleStreams` had **no CR050 variant interception**, so a module edited inside a variant read back correctly and vanished on the next sync; `HasValuation` was accepted and applied nowhere, so **no API path could create a flow module**; the retired columns were still written, letting the first variant save re-break 058's own post-condition; and AI Review's apply path wrote a dead column and a dead table while reporting success — on the one path an LLM recommends and the owner applies with a click. *Code review:* two that **reproduce on today's data** — auto-adjust resolved a line with a row-constructor `IN`, and `('a', NULL) IN (('a', NULL))` is NULL, so a line-less expense (`Sarasota House`) could never be cut; and **`FCReview` called the now-410 `/incomeexpense` from the bundle shipping in this same deploy**, with the error swallowed by a `.catch`, silently losing the graph point-adjust affordance from every FC-line series — plus the convergence loop reading module columns the write path had stopped maintaining, stream USD conversion moved after the `fxrates[0]` override, and the CR041 gate losing `pct_of_value`. *Migration review:* proved the sign guards bite by running three deliberately-wrong mutations of the backfill (each aborts), and found that **prod's `schema_migrations` ledger stopped at 056** because 057 had been applied with `psql -f` — harmless here (`--dry-run` reports APPLY, and 057 is idempotent) but the other branch would have **baselined 058/059 as applied without running them**. **Two of the author's own comments were false and are corrected in place, not deleted:** the float-ordering rationale (IEEE-754 addition is *commutative*; only associativity fails, and every module has one stream) and the `-0` one (`JSON.stringify(-0)` is `"0"`). **Also found, unrelated but consequential:** the e2e suite had been testing a server **orphaned on :3998 since July 14** — three weeks against stale code — now [Known Issue](#3-known-issues). 803 backend (+12) / 396 frontend / 7 e2e, six ratchets, clean build, 59-file chain from empty. The legacy per-direction fields are still accepted and translated (expand → migrate → contract); P3 replaces the form with stream cards and drops four tables.
- **v3.13.1** (2026-08-04) — **patch: [CR069](../cr/cr-069-forecast-streams.md) P0 — an income/expense item's entries carry ITS name, not the FC line it posts to.** Three of twelve items on prod's `2026 Base` emitted **no entry under their own name**: `Retirement Home` filed under `Living Expenses`, `Car Purchase Chris` under `One-Off Items`, `Social Security` under `Total Salary` — so Retirement Home's **−200,000 in 2052** was visible only as Living Expenses carrying two rows that year, and every breakdown, per-module query and audit click-through was blind to all three. The balance-sheet builder has always labelled by module name; `fcbuilder-incexp` alone labelled by *account*. **The comment claiming this was safe was false in both halves.** It said two items on one account "overwrite each other via ON CONFLICT … last-write-wins": the conflict target ends in `entry_type`, **no writer in the engine sets it**, and NULLs are distinct in a Postgres unique index — so the `DO UPDATE` branch has **never once been taken** and the rows were always inserted side by side. That is precisely why the *totals* were always right and only the *attribution* was lost, and why nothing ever looked wrong. The clause is kept (it is correct for a future writer that does set `entry_type`); the comment no longer credits it with work it never did. The audit CSV moves to the same key, so the Review breakdown's click-through still resolves — and two items on one line now get a trail each instead of one silently overwriting the other's file. **Gated on a real prod copy, not a fixture:** `pg_dump` restored into a scratch database on the dev Postgres (dev's own database untouched), all five scenarios regenerated before *and* after — **4,030 (scenario, account, year) sum rows, zero differing**, entry counts identical per scenario (1647/1637/1649/1731/1721), module labels **143 → 158: exactly the 15 expected** (3 items × 5 scenarios) and **0 lost**. The other nine items already had a name equal to their line, which is why only three were ever hidden. **One item was checked rather than assumed:** `Tax` emits no entries in *either* run — it carries a **−100% `Percent %` change in 2027**, so its base compounds from zero and `buildFcEntriesPayload` skips zero cells. Deliberate, pre-existing, identical on both sides of the gate. **791 backend tests** (788 → 791, three new attribution tests); no frontend change, so the six ratchets are untouched. Prod's stored entries kept the old labels until the five scenarios were regenerated after the deploy — sum-neutral by the gate, so that rebuild was a deliberate step, not a deploy side effect. *Also in this commit range:* the [CR069](../cr/cr-069-forecast-streams.md) design itself — one module section, streams as first-class rows — drafted and revised through a two-pass review (technical → four blockers, all folded in; PM → conditional go, both conditions applied).
- **v3.13.0** (2026-08-04) — **minor: [CR068](../cr/cr-068-mobile-actuals-search.md) P1 + P2 — the mobile Actuals search, and the totals tile that was adding currencies together.** **P2 is the part worth reading:** the owner's screenshot showed `PLN TOTAL (453.64)`, `EUR TOTAL (116.23)` and `EXPENSES (BASE) (569.87)` — and 453.64 + 116.23 = 569.87. The "base" tile was the per-currency figures **added together**, which is not a quantity of anything; the Base Amt column on the same screen summed to 254.27. `getTotalsAmount` reads the *local* `Amount`, correctly, for the per-currency tiles — and the income/expense split then summed those same figures across currencies. `BaseAmount` was in the response all along and simply never read. **Same class as [CR064 P8](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md), and invisible in a single-currency period, which is why it survived.** On real dev data the scale is not four rows: July 2026 read **(68,064.39)** against a true base of **(46,321.61)** — a **21,742.78** overstatement in one month. The fix is shared (`summarizeActualTotals`) so the new mobile page cannot compute "base" a second way, and its test uses the screenshot's four rows as the fixture with an explicit assertion that the old number can never return. **Two further defects found in `getActualEntries` while fixing it:** (1) it was **sent** `description`, `valueFrom`, `valueTo` and `currency` by the Actuals page and destructured **none** of them, so typing a search term narrowed the table and left the money above it unchanged — now filtered with the same predicates the transactions repository uses; (2) the `LIMIT` truncated **silently**, and callers sum the rows they get back, so a wide period reported a total that was simply short — now fetches `limit + 1` and returns `truncated`, and both pages label the figure instead of printing a floor as if it were complete. (3) A third, incidental: `Description1` read `row.description` and `transactions` has **no such column** (it is `description1`/`description2`), so every entry came back with an undefined description and the Budget-vs-Actual popup rendered an em-dash for **every** row; `Description2` was never mapped at all. **The modal ratchet did real work:** two new sheets would have taken the bespoke-dialog baseline 14 → 15, so `MobileSheet` was extracted as the shell's *one* dialog and both render through it — the baseline is a **rename**, 14 before and after. `react-refresh/only-export-components` likewise caught a helper exported from a component file (21 → 22), which moved to `treeSections.js`. `set-state-in-effect` **fell 34 → 33** and is re-baselined. **Three layout defects only a real browser showed** — the month inputs overflowing (flex `min-width: auto` against a native control's intrinsic width), `type="search"` drawing WebKit's clear button beside ours, and the unfiltered chip labels pushing the third chip off a 390 px viewport. 396 frontend (+32) / 788 backend (+9) tests, lint 0 errors, six ratchets clean, production build OK, dark mode verified.
- **v3.13.0** (2026-08-04, also in this release) — **the ops defects v3.12.0's close-out named, plus one claim it got wrong.** (1) **`deploy-to-production.sh` now refuses a dirty build tree** — see [Known Issue #17](#3-known-issues) for the scope and why it is scoped that way. (2) **both images now carry the commit they were built from** (`org.opencontainers.image.revision` + `com.fin.build.dirty`), read back out of the running containers at the end of the deploy — so "what is prod running?" is answerable from prod. The `git archive HEAD` build that #17 originally proposed was **rejected on inspection**: prod bind-mounts five host paths that an exported tree does not have. (3) **`bump-version.sh` printed `✓ Updated <pkg>` for work it had not done.** The package.json mirror matched on the *old version literal* (`s/"version": "$CURRENT_VERSION"/…/`), which only fires when a package.json already agrees with the `VERSION` file — neither ever did (`frontend` sat at Vite's default **0.0.0**, `server` at **1.0.0**), so the sed no-opped on every release for the life of the script while the ✓ printed unconditionally. Harmless in itself, since nothing reads those versions, but *a green tick for work that did not happen is what made the drift invisible* — the same shape as the "harm was nil because someone checked" pattern in #17. Now replaces the **first** `"version"` key (dependency entries are `"pkg": "^1.2.3"`, never `"version":`) and reports by comparing the file before and after: `✓ Updated` when it changed, `· already at X` when it did not, `· no "version" key — skipped` when there is nothing to do. Both package.json files are now at **3.12.0** and track releases from here.
