Verified: first run updates and says so, second run reports "already at" and touches nothing; only the top-level version line changes in each file. **(4) A correction, not a fix:** the close-out claimed `copyScenario` wrongly drops `parent_scenario_id` and that [CR064 P2](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) should carry it onto the yearly copy. **That was wrong and the "fix" would have broken the annual close** — a new year's base is a base, and `trg_fc_reject_nested_variant` refuses both a variant-of-a-variant and turning a scenario that already has variants into one. `copyScenario` creating a root is correct; the real gap is that the *variants* do not follow the copy, which CR064 P2 item 4 already names. Corrected in status.md, CR067 §6 and CR064 §3.2.
- **v3.12.0** (2026-08-03) — **minor: [CR067](../cr/cr-067-forecast-multi-compare.md) — Forecast Multi-Compare, and the release that makes prod reproducible again.** Owner-requested from a screenshot of the Compare trajectory chart: *the same graph, base scenario in a bolder line, one or more variants by checkbox, other tables not needed.* `/forecast-multi-compare` is exactly that — a base and up to six of its variants as trajectory lines (base `strokeWidth 3`, variants 1.75), the same five metric toggles Compare has, and nothing else. [CR040](../cr/cr-040-forecast-scenario-compare.md)'s page is two-scenario **by construction** (its own non-goal #1), so the fan question meant opening it once per variant. Cheap because `buildScenarioMatrix` was already **pure and single-scenario** and yields all five metrics; `compareMatrices` is the pairwise layer on top and simply is not called. **Two reviews before any code** (technical -> *revise*, PM -> *go*), and both earned their keep. The technical pass killed the claim that the chart was reusable unchanged — it is hard-wired to the pairwise shape — which forced **P1**: extract a shared `FCTrajectoryChart` rendered by *both* pages. **Its gate had to be built first**, because `FCCompareCharts.jsx` and `FCCompare.jsx` had **no component tests at all**, so "existing tests still pass" would have asserted nothing about the component being moved; the parity test was written against the *shipped* component, passed 6/6 there, and passes unchanged after. It also caught that a matrix carries **`netCashFlow` twice** with different null semantics — Compare plots `cash.get("Net Cash Flow")`, and reading the top-level array draws **0 where Compare draws a gap**, which would have passed on today's data. **The one real trap is the alignment:** the union-of-years logic lives in `compareMatrices`, and each matrix trims to `>= its own PeriodStart`, so plotting positionally silently shifts scenarios against each other — no error, a chart that looks right. Keyed by **year**, tested explicitly with 2027-2029 against 2028-2030; every scenario shares a `PeriodStart` today, so a positional build would have passed the browser check, the parity check and the eye, and broken the first time [CR064 P2](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) ships. The PM pass demoted the staleness badge to a deferred **P3** on evidence: the drift motivating it is a *dev* artifact — on prod all five scenarios build within two hours of each other — and it measures build *age*, not staleness, since a CR050 variant sync rewrites modules without touching entries. Its one load-bearing case (a variant with **no** generated forecast, otherwise just an absent line) shipped inside P2 with no endpoint. Loading is `useQueries`, not a hand-rolled effect: per-scenario cache and error isolation, so ticking a sixth checkbox fetches the sixth scenario instead of refetching all six; the balance report is keyed on `PeriodStart - 2`, which is both the dedupe (one request for all five) and a cache share with `useReports`. Palette **validated, not chosen** — `validate_palette.js` against Fin's *own* surfaces (light worst-adjacent CVD dE 9.1 / normal-vision 19.6; dark 8.4 / 19.3); the light-mode contrast WARN is discharged by listing every variant with its swatch **and** its name, and a variant's hue follows its position in the base's own list so clearing one checkbox does not repaint the rest. Route is deliberately step-less, like Equity, so the stepper and sidebar still agree on 1-6. **Also recorded — and the reason this is a release rather than a deploy:** P1 and P2 had *already reached prod* at 02:11 inside another thread's v3.11.16 deploy, because `deploy-to-production.sh` builds the frontend from the shared working tree and P2 was still uncommitted. Harm nil and **checked rather than assumed** — the live page renders all four prod variants (five lines, base bold, correct hues, zero page errors), which incidentally cleared the four-variant gate the CR had deferred to post-deploy — but the `v3.11.16` tag contained only P1, so prod matched no tag. **Fourth instance of that class, now [Known Issue #17](#3-known-issues)** with two fix candidates (build from `git archive HEAD`; or refuse a dirty-tree deploy). 364 frontend tests (+31), lint 0 errors, six ratchets at baseline, no migration.
- **v3.11.16** (2026-08-03) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P13 — a forecast module labelled USD over PLN values (migration 056).** Found while answering a *modelling* question, not a bug report: should the credit-card modules be forecast as a **ratio of expenses**, stable now and reduced later in life? **No** — measured on prod, **4,772 card transactions since Jan 2025, two of them interest charges**. The cards are paid in full, so the balance is **float** (the statement lag), not borrowing, and float cannot change net worth under any treatment: if it grows by 2,000 you kept 2,000 of cash longer, and paying it down is cash out against liability down. A ratio model buys a more accurate *gross* balance sheet and nothing else. Two mechanics settled it without new code: **`growth_rate` on a liability is a trap** — growth feeds `unrealizedGainValues`, which reach `marketValues` but **not** `cashChange`, so `growth_rate = 1` walks the balance to −126,092 by 2062 while cash never moves, **inventing 74,255 of wealth destruction**; and the **correct lever already exists**, since `transferValues = -dispose - invest` does reach cash (negative `Invest` = more float and cash in, positive = paydown and cash out, `Periodic` spreads it). Measuring the float is what exposed the defect: five `PLN Credit Cards` modules carried **`currency='USD'` over PLN values** (`market_value −24,542.66` vs `market_value_usd −6,832.01`, implied 3.5923), inherited from account 65 — a **parent rollup holding no transactions of its own**, mislabelled `USD` in `accounts`. Proved rather than inferred: that account's own ledger stood at **−24,129.55 PLN on 2025-12-31**, the modules' `base_date`, and `exchange_rates` gives **3.5922** that day. `fcbuilder-module.js` reads the FX assumptions only when `Currency !== 'USD'`, so the branch never ran, `fxrates` kept its `fill(1)`, and the PLN amount posted onto a USD balance sheet; the `MarketValueUSD` override repairs **index 0 only** — the `base_date` year, not even an output column at `PeriodStart 2027` — so the one correct year was invisible and all 36 wrong ones were not. **`2026 Base` and `2026 Downside` each carried 18,250 USD of liability that does not exist**, verified in `forecast_entries` (2026…2030 all −24,542.66). **A wrong non-USD label announces itself; a wrong USD label is silent forever** — so the engine now **throws** on the contradiction rather than healing it (3.5923 is a plausible PLN rate, and auto-inference would work here then invent a currency next time). Exactly the five bad rows trip the guard; **none of the other 110 modules does**. The test reproduces the *defect*: with the guard disabled the fixture posts `[[2026,-24542.66],…]`, byte-identical to the real rows. Applied dev + prod, both balances refreshed to their `base_date` ledger, `USD Credit Cards` enabled, the four variants synced **from base** (a direct write to a variant row is reverted by `syncVariant`), all five scenarios regenerated — every scenario now carries an identical, correct card position (PLN −6,187, USD −27,187). Also **corrects v3.11.15's "~52K of missing debt"**, which was computed on the mislabelled number; the true position was **28.6K**. 779 backend / 333 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline. *Not done:* the parent rollup account is still labelled `USD`, so the next module linked to it inherits the same defect — the guard now catches it at generate time instead of letting it through. **This sentence understated the scope and is corrected by [Known Issue #19](#3-known-issues): it is NINE rollup accounts, not one, and the guard covers only one of the three shapes the defect takes.** Tier 3 (a deliberate paydown) needs a start year and pace from the owner; `PLN Credit Cards` already models a crude version via a `Full` disposal at 2038-07-01, which verifies correctly on both sides.
- **v3.11.15** (2026-08-03) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P12 — Net Assets added the debt instead of subtracting it.** Owner, on Compare: *"Assets are 599K higher and Liabilities 500K higher, which makes sense — but Net Assets is 1,099K higher, which makes no sense; it should only be 99K."* Right on both counts. A liability's balance is stored **negative** (`US - Loans` = −500,000), and two places already read it that way and say so — `useOverview.js` (*"assets + liabilities; // liabilities stored negative"*, the Home net-worth hero) and CR062's `equity.js` (*"the debt subtracts itself"*). Forecast **Review** and **Compare** both computed `Assets − Liabilities`, which **adds** the debt; the error is always `2 × |liabilities|`. Not just the comparison — `2026 Buy Business`'s own Review reported net assets 1,000,000 too high. **Why it stayed hidden:** every liability module in every scenario sits at `setup_status = 'new'` (or `exclude`), which the engine skips, so forecast liabilities were always exactly **0** — and at zero `A − L` ≡ `A + L`. The `Business Loan` in `2026 Buy Business` is `complete` and is the **first liability ever to reach a forecast**; CR062 made loans expressible and this is the first scenario to use one. **A third site would have broken quietly:** `FCReviewTable`'s Change-in-Net-Assets bridge re-signed liabilities with its own `-1`, which against an already-negative balance made *taking on debt read as a gain* — and it reconciled only because `netAssetsByYear` made the identical mistake one level up; fixing the two totals alone would have left the bridge disagreeing with its headline while the `Other` residual absorbed twice the loan. **The guard is the fixture, not the formula:** `fcCompareUtils.test.js` pinned the defect with a **+200** mortgage, so `910 − 200 = 710` passed while every real scenario with debt was overstated; the fixture now stores it negative and expects the same 710, plus an explicit test that more debt *lowers* net assets — both falsified against the unfixed code. No migration, no data change, no regenerate. *Owner's call, unchanged:* `PLN Credit Cards` / `USD Credit Cards` (−24,543 / −27,295) are `new` in all five scenarios, so ~52K of real debt is absent from every forecast. 774 backend / 333 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline.
- **v3.11.14** (2026-08-03) — **patch: the Forecast Review graphs start at the actuals year, not at PeriodStart.** Owner-found, from a screenshot: *"is there any way to include the values from the actual year and budget year on these graphs rather than just starting from forecast?"* The table has carried both pre-forecast columns since CR049 — 2025 from the ledger, 2026 from `budget_entries` — but **two different code paths fed the graph modal and only one knew about them.** The checkbox → line chart passed `rowValues`, resolved through `FCReviewTable`'s own `resolveCashValue`, and was always right; the double-click → stacked breakdown passed `getCellValue`, which returns `null` for cash rows in both years *by design* (its comment says "handled by FCReviewTable via baseYearBudget"), so the bars were 0 and `buildBreakdownSeries` then dropped any child that was all-zero. Fixed by lifting that overlay out of the component into `utils/fcCashValue.js`, so the table and the breakdown builder resolve a cell the same way — no new data, no endpoint, no migration. Verified end-to-end in a browser against dev: `2026 Base`'s Expense breakdown now draws **9 bars in 2025 · Actual** and **8 in 2026 · Budget**, and both columns tie to the table for 2026 (502,392) and every forecast year. **The balance-sheet half is guarded, and the guard is the point.** The ledger holds *every* leaf while the engine writes many balance rows at level 2 (`Fidelity Stock`, `Bank Accounts`) and never at their leaves — so overlaying first would have flipped rows that sensibly fall back to a line chart into a stacked bar populated in 2025 and **empty for every forecast year after it** (Bank Accounts: a 24-segment stack with one filled column). The overlay therefore runs *after* the all-zero filter, reaching only leaves the engine already models — 3 rows gain a real 2025 column (`CVC Investments`, `PL Investments`, `PL - Properties`), the rest are untouched. A test earned its keep here: the first guard tested `value !== 0`, which cannot tell *the engine wrote zero* from *the engine wrote nothing*, and would have pasted a ledger balance over an asset sold that year; it now keys off entry presence. The two pre-forecast columns are also **named and drawn differently** — axis reads `2025 (58) · Actual` / `2026 (59) · Budget`, bars at 0.5 opacity against 0.85, and a note above the chart — because three bases in one chart otherwise read as one trend. 332 frontend tests (319 + 13 new), lint 0 errors, six ratchets at baseline. *Reconciling the result found [CR066](../cr/cr-066-fc-line-mapping-completeness.md) — see [Known Issues #16](#3-known-issues).*

- **v3.11.13** (2026-08-03) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P11 — reset an overridden module in a variant back to its base.** The Modules page has shown `INHERITED` / `OVERRIDDEN n` / `LOCAL` since CR050, but could not **undo** an override: the only revert in the UI was the per-field control on the Scenarios page, so a module pinned by accident here had to be un-pinned somewhere else, if you knew that panel existed. The endpoint was never missing — `DELETE /scenarios/:id/overrides/module/:baseEntityId` has shipped since CR050 and, with no `?field`, clears every field and **re-syncs the variant in one transaction**, so the row returns holding exactly what the base holds. Two things were: the **id** (a revert is addressed by the *base* row, and `rowInheritance` returned only `{status, fields}` — it now carries `baseId`), and a **way to press it** (a `Reset to Base` action that appears only when the selection is a variant row actually carrying an override; on a base scenario, an inherited row or a variant-local row there is nothing to revert to, so it is not rendered rather than rendered disabled). Confirms first — the variant's own values are discarded — and **names the fields that will go**, from the same payload the badge renders, reusing CR062's retype-confirm component rather than growing a fifth bespoke dialog; it also says a regenerate is needed. Modules only: `incexp` overrides would work identically through the same endpoint, but an untested second call site is not worth shipping blind. 774 backend / 319 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline.
- **v3.11.12** (2026-08-03) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P9 — the amount's anchor year was labelled from the wrong place.** P8 (v3.11.10) replaced "(Base Yr)" with the real year and added a derived first-forecast-year hint; both read the module's own `BaseDate`. **The engine does not.** It grows the amount with `periodNum = year − periodStart + 1`, so the figure is anchored to `PeriodStart − 1` and `base_date` never enters the income calculation. With 18 of 21 prod modules at `base_date = 2025-12-31` against `PeriodStart = 2027`, the label was wrong for all but three: **CVC Fund VIII** read `Income Amount (2025)` for a figure the engine treats as **2026**, and hinted `→ 2026: 26,445 EUR` for income its own module output books in **2027**. Both now read from `PeriodStart`, so the form and the module output agree. **The owner's underlying question** — *"what is this box supposed to represent, when we have actual 2025 and budget 2026?"* — has an uncomfortable answer that this fix only half addresses: a module carries **two anchors at once**. Its value series starts at `base_date` and takes no growth until `PeriodStart` (which is why CVC's 2025 and 2026 rows are identical), while its income/expense amounts anchor to `PeriodStart − 1`; and the amount is absent from the module's own output for the base year while `getBaseYearValues` puts it into Review's base-year column. **A correction to P8 §8.5 is recorded with it:** the argument against moving the anchor rested partly on the per-module deferred base-year tax, and **0 of 110 modules carry a tax override of either kind**, so that half of the argument is theoretical. The owner's original instinct — the budget is the budget, this box is next year — is logged as **P10** (designed, not built): base-year P&L from `budget_entries`, module amount = first forecast year, sweep opening cash from the same source. Costs a migration and a regenerate. 770 backend / 319 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline.
- **v3.11.11** (2026-08-02) — **patch: the forecast rebuilt on [CR064 P8](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md)'s corrected opening cash.** P8 (v3.11.10) fixed a base year that summed module amounts in **mixed currencies** — `2026 Base` went **+144,395 → −254,728** — and because `index.js` folds that base-year NCF into the **cash sweep's opening cash**, the stored `forecast_entries` were still built on the inflated figure: the base-year column and the projected years disagreed on prod. The regenerate was held until the owner confirmed United Beverages' `income_amount = 500,000` is **PLN** (2026-08-02) — which is exactly how it was stored, so **no data was corrected**. All five scenarios regenerated on prod. *Residue left to the owner, not changed for them:* the 500,000 was meant for **2027** while the field is the **2026** anchor, so it projects **512,500 PLN** next year; **487,805** would give exactly 500,000. The form now shows that derived figure beside the input, which is precisely what §8.5 added it for. No code change beyond the version.
- **v3.11.10** (2026-08-02) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P8 — the base year was summed in mixed currencies, and it seeds the cash sweep.** Owner: *"I don't know where 500,000 USD of UB Income is coming from."* Two answers: the 500,000 was their own edit moving United Beverages to amount mode — and **it is not USD, it is 500,000 PLN printed in a USD column**. `getBaseYearValues` summed each module's `income_amount`/`expense_amount` **in that module's own currency** into a figure every consumer reads as USD; and the column labelled `(BUDGET)` is fed by that function, never by `budget_entries` at all, so the header was wrong independently of the currency. `2026 Base`: UB Income shown **500,000** against a real **128,205**, Barkeria **55,000** against **14,103**, the four properties' costs added as `20,000 PLN + 10,000 EUR = 30,000` of nothing. The proof was one column to the right — 2027 showed **131,410** for the same stream (`500,000 × 1.025 ÷ 3.9`), the same line 3.9× apart in adjacent columns. **Never only cosmetic:** `index.js` folds this base-year NCF into the **cash sweep's opening cash**, so the sweep opened on an FX-inflated number for the whole horizon — the CR049 §1 failure mode in the very function CR049 created to give the base year one source, and the old code's own comment called the currency handling *"pre-existing and out of scope"*. **Measured on a prod copy, base-year NCF: `2026 Base` +144,395 → −254,728** (Buy Business the same; Downside −140,117 → −317,768; Upside −163,339 → −333,634). Regenerated both ways: **no new shortfalls**, but the sweep sells **+1.26M more** across the horizon (transfers 14.24M → 15.50M) — the cost of opening on the real number. Fix: group by `m.currency` and convert through CR051's `baseYearFxRate`, the same rate the engine divides by, so the base-year column and Period 1 now agree; a currency with no rate **throws** rather than falling back to unconverted. Also: the inc/exp branch reads `base_value_usd` not `base_value`; the amount field is labelled with its **year** (`Income Amount (2026)`) and shows the derived first forecast year beneath it (`→ 2027: 512,500 PLN · 131,410 USD`, suppressed in yield mode and for pct-of-value expenses); and the Review column is relabelled **`(Base Yr)`** in the table and the print/export path. The anchor was deliberately **not** moved to Period 1 — the sweep's opening cash and the per-module deferred base-year tax both need a base-year figure, and `budget_entries` are per category. *Still open: the data* — whether UB's 500,000 meant PLN or USD is unanswered, so nothing in the data was touched. 770 backend / 319 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline. **Prod's base-year column is corrected on read (verified live: `2026 Base` NCF −254,728), but the stored forecast entries still carry the OLD opening cash — a regenerate is deliberately deferred until UB's 500,000 is settled, so the plan is rebuilt once on a number the owner has confirmed rather than twice.**
- **v3.11.9** (2026-08-02) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P7 — the FC-line budget hint added up three currencies.** Owner, looking at United Beverages: *"192,266 showing to be allocated is USD, but when I enter the same amount it is entered as PLN."* Correct. `fcBudgetTotals` is `SUM(budget_entries.base_amount)` — **always USD** — while `income_amount`/`expense_amount` are in the **module's** currency, so on a PLN module the hint compared a USD budget with a PLN input and reconciled to zero when the two matched **as digits**; `otherModulesAmount` was worse, summing raw amounts across currencies (the four properties on one expense line add `20,000 PLN + 2,500 + 5,000 + 2,500 EUR` to "30,000" of nothing). **It had already cost a number:** UB's dividend budget is **690,000 PLN = 192,266 USD** and the module holds **192,266** — the USD figure in a PLN field, with the hint reporting **"Remaining: −0"**. Inert only because UB is in yield mode, which discards the amount — and **v3.11.8 made amount mode usable**, so switching UB across would have booked **~53,600 USD instead of ~192,266**, a quarter of the plan's largest income line, arrived at by trusting the form. `allocateBudget` now sums in **USD** and presents in the **module's** currency with the rate shown (`Budget: 749,837 PLN (192,266 USD @ 3.9)`), converting at the **scenario's** FX rather than the ledger's historical one — deliberately, because the engine will convert the amount at the scenario rate, so "Remaining: 0" now means *this module books exactly the budgeted USD*. A row whose currency has no rate is **excluded and counted**, never treated as USD. USD modules are unaffected (every rate is 1). 8 new tests, including that 192,266 into a PLN module against a 192,266 USD budget **no longer reconciles**. *Not fixed here — the data:* UB's stored amount is still the USD figure (inert, and correcting it is a number-changing owner decision), and Barkeria plus the four properties are **ambiguous** rather than provably wrong. 761 backend / 311 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline.
- **v3.11.8** (2026-08-02) — **patch: [CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P6 — income a business can express** (migration **055**). Owner: *"there is no way to change how this grows relative to inflation — only the yield adjustment, which is not relevant to a business."* True, and the cause was worse: **the typed amount was not being used at all.** Recurring income has two mutually exclusive modes and the yield wins on a **single** `IncomePct` row, discarding `income_amount` — and **all six income-bearing modules in prod are in yield mode**, so all six carried a dead Income Amount. United Beverages: **192,266 PLN typed** while the engine books **77,163 USD** for 2027 = `avg(3,846,154 / 3,870,192) × (2.5% − 0.5%)`, verified to the dollar against the generated entries. CR003 built `IncomePct` as a *deposit interest rate* — right for Fidelity Fixed Income, wrong for a company, whose profit is not a percentage of its own valuation, and nothing documented that it silently overrides the amount. Adds `income_growth_rate` (a multiplier of inflation, read like the existing `Growth`; NULL = 1 = unchanged) and `forecast_module_income_steps` (permanent level changes — owner chose *typed in the year it happens, keeps its real value after*, applied in **full** in its year, deliberately **not** the July-1 half-year convention). **States the live mode in the form**, which is what makes a dead amount visible, and hides Yield Spread on a business **by data rather than by type** — §4.1's collapse rule extended to schedules, so it disappears with no rows and returns the moment there are, which a type gate could not guarantee. **Dormant and proved so on real data:** a prod copy regenerated with the pre-change engine and the new one gives **7,916 entries across all five scenarios byte-identical**; then Barkeria moved to amount mode at 0.5× inflation with a 2029 +10,000 step regenerates to **55,687.52 → 56,383.59 → 67,088.39 → 67,927.00 PLN**, matching the hand-computed example to the cent. Every hand-maintained list that has dropped a column before was updated with it (`copyScenario`'s INSERT, `MODULE_COLUMN_DEFAULTS`, the CR050 variant `SCHEDULE_TABLES`, `replaceModuleSchedules`, the route contract, `previewLoanRetype`). *Two things left undone on purpose:* moving the six live modules off yield mode changes their numbers (a per-business owner decision), and the base-year income tax still reads the ignored amount — UB's 2027 tax is 30% × 192,266 PLN while every later year taxes the yield, under-taxing the first projected year by **~8,400 USD**; fixing it changes existing numbers and would destroy this release's byte-identical proof, so it gets its own phase. **Also recorded:** migration 055 and the P6 engine code reached prod as **work-in-progress** at 20:21 via another thread's deploy — `deploy-to-production.sh` builds from the shared working tree and applies every pending migration file, the same hazard migration 051 records for 044. Harm nil and checked rather than assumed (column NULL everywhere, table empty, no P6 UI in the served bundle). 761 backend / 303 frontend, lint 0, six ratchets at baseline.
Release-level history; detail in the linked CR file or [§7 Migration History](#7-migration-history).

- **v3.11.3** (2026-08-02) — **patch: three silent-wrong-number defects, all owner-visible in the forecast.** Migrations **051** (applied prod ahead of the release) and **052**.
  - **[CR062 §11.2](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md) — a secured-asset link set inside a *variant* was erased by the save that set it.** Owner-found: "2026 Buy Business" read *"No asset carries debt"* while both of its loans were secured. **Two id spaces reach variant sync's cross-row resolution and nothing in the value tells them apart** — an *inherited* link comes off the base row (a **base** module id), an *overridden* one comes out of the patch, where the picker offered the variant's own modules (a **variant** id). Step 5 of `syncEntity` mapped base→variant unconditionally, so a variant id missed the map and `|| null` unsecured the loan — inside `interceptWrite`'s own transaction, so **the PUT that saved the link returned 200 with the link already gone**. Now resolved by asking which scenario the target actually sits in (ours ⇒ keep, a base row ⇒ translate, otherwise NULL), which also makes a **variant-local** asset able to secure a loan — something CR062 §8.2 had ruled out as unrepresentable. *§8.2 named this exact trap and got its premise backwards* ("the override patch stores a base-scenario module id"), and its verification query — *no link crosses a scenario boundary* — **is satisfied by NULL**, so it could only ever have caught the wrong-scenario case, never the erasure. Step 5 had shipped with **no test at all**; the four new ones cover both id spaces, the variant-local target and the tombstoned target, and all four were **shown failing** against the old resolution first. No data was lost — the override patches are the record — and prod's two links were restored by running the fixed sync against them.
  - **[CR064](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) P0/P1/P3 — one question, three defects** (migration **052**). *Should the other module types get a custom form like Loan's?* **No** (§5). But looking turned up: the module editor read FX under `Rates.USDPLN`/`USDEUR`, which do not exist in the live document, so it fell back to **1** and then **multiplied** where the engine divides — an unmatched €390K property posts **$390K** (**P0**); `forecast_assumptions` is keyed by scenario **NAME** and `PUT /scenarios/:id` renamed the row and nothing else, which two saves later reads as **0% inflation for 36 years, silently** (**P1** — `renameScenario` is now one transaction, `updateScenario` **throws** on a name, and 052 prunes the five dead names while **refusing** when the orphans look like a rename); and the two blank prod modules are **Generate**'s doing, not Cancel's (**P3**). **P2 — the annual close — decided 2026-08-02: keep minting a copy each year**, designed but not built.
  - **[CR059](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md) — migration 044 reached prod 72 seconds before it was committed, and was incomplete.** An untracked file in `server/db/migrations/` is not inert: another thread's deploy scans the directory. It rewrote two of fin's **three** feed-id columns and missed `bankfeed_balances`, so all 27 live mappings read "no feed" on the reconcile page. **The feed stalled rather than corrupting** — 255 staged rows unmapped, **0 promoted, 0 ledger rows** across the whole ~14-hour window — and **051** reversed it from the same generated pairs (applied prod 2026-08-02, ahead of this release).
  - **CI is green again.** Migration **050**'s `found <> 1` guard was unconditional and aborted the chain on a data-free database — **the third instance of that class** (046 was the first, and its own fix note says exactly this), so `main` had been red since `2d49ff3`, again unannounced. Amended under the migrations rule's "unavoidable" clause with 046's skip branch; the full 52-file chain now builds on an empty Postgres. **Leaves ledger checksum drift on dev and prod**, to be accepted per file after confirming equivalence. Known Issue #12 — *nothing announces a red `main`* — remains open and has now cost three separate incidents.
  - *Docs:* starter pack synced to **v1.6.4** with Fin's learnings, and five review agents (`.claude/agents/`) rewritten with Fin's facts.
- **v3.11.2** (2026-08-01) — **patch: CI had been red for two days and nothing said so.** No user-facing change; one migration (**050**, applied dev only).
  - **The gate was failing across three releases and a prod deploy.** `main` went red on 2026-07-30 and stayed red for **30 pushes** — through v3.10.0, v3.11.0 and v3.11.1 — because a red run is visible only to whoever opens the Actions tab. **Two independent causes, the newer hiding the older.** (a) Migration **046** asserted a *production data fact* unconditionally ("expected exactly 1 mtm feed mapping for Fidelity Cash Mgt, found 0"); CI builds its database from the migration chain and seeds it *afterwards*, so it has no mapping at all, and the abort took `backend-tests` **and** `e2e` down before either ran a test. (b) Underneath it, four DB-backed suites borrowed rows only the *dev* database holds — the first `asset`/`liability` account, the first `fc_lines` row, the first `balance_sheet` account — and 29 tests died in `beforeAll` with `Cannot read properties of undefined`; `quicken-promote` separately wanted an `Interest Income` leaf the seed never carried, and reported it as an FK violation rather than the engine's own error, because the securities tests clear their staged rows at the *end* of the test body, which a failing test never reaches. *The pointed one:* `usdBaseAmountInvariant`'s own header warns about "the ambient-data trap that turned this repo's CI red for four consecutive runs in July 2026" — one query above the borrow that did it again.
  - **Fixes.** 046 gains a zero-mapping skip branch; a mapping that exists and is not `mtm` still raises, **including the renamed-account case it was written for**, since the row is keyed by `external_name` and a rename leaves it in place reporting `calibrate`. Each suite seeds and cleans up its own fixture — the invariant test inside the transaction it already rolls back, so nothing persists there at all. `Interest Income` joins the other two `INVST_INCOME_LEAF` names in `ci-seed.sql`, and the shared promote cleanup clears securities staging so the next failure in that file reports itself. **Editing an applied migration** is the [migrations.md](migrations.md) 041 exception — a file that aborts the chain cannot be repaired forward, because nothing later runs; verified equivalent (dev and prod each hold exactly 1 mtm / 0 calibrate, so the edited file takes the same branch and prints the same NOTICE), and the ledger checksum re-recorded on both.
  - **Verified against the real recipe, not asserted:** a throwaway `postgres:16-alpine` → the full migration chain → `ci-seed.sql` → **714/714 backend**, and `Scripts/e2e.sh` **8/8**. Also 714/714 against dev.
  - **[CR059](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md) §18 — migration 050, the P4 prerequisite** (applied dev; **prod deferred to the cutover**). fintable's API serves four Revolut EUR transactions **twice**, once under the current EUR wallet and once under a legacy numeric-id wallet it labels "(USD)" — same dates and amounts, different transaction ids, so two genuine upstream records. Nothing is wrong today, but the date floor blocks only the four *known* duplicates: a new row filed there later would book a EUR amount into a USD account, invisible to a balance check for the same reason the Black Card incident was. Mapping 531 becomes ignore-only.
  - **Left open:** nothing notifies on a red `main` — no branch protection, no failure notification, and `deploy-to-production.sh` does not consult CI. See [Known Issue #12](#3-known-issues).
- **v3.11.0** (2026-07-31) — **minor: scenario lineage reaches the pickers, the anchors stop reading as capital contributions, and a reorder that worked says so.** Three strands, no migration.
  - **[CR050 §10](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md) — a variant was indistinguishable from a base in every dropdown in the app.** CR050 §5 specified the marker (`↳ variant of 2026 Base`) and it never shipped, so "2026 Base", "2026 Downside", "2026 SRQ House Purchase" and "2026 Upside" read as four peer scenarios in all seven pickers. **The blocker was the payload, not the markup:** every picker reads `GET /assumptions`, which formatted scenarios without lineage. It now carries `ParentId` (additive) and one shared `scenarioOptions()` helper **regroups** — bases keep alphabetical order, variants move under their own base; an orphaned variant renders top-level rather than being dropped, since an option missing from the list is unselectable; the option value stays the bare name, so nothing that stores or sends a scenario name changed. The lineage panel now also answers *what differs* on the **base** side (a collapsed table: variant · field count · the overridden items in words, lazily fetched, each name selecting that variant) and tints a scenario that sits in a lineage. *The real data cannot falsify the grouping* — "2026 Base" sorts before all three of its variants, so flat and grouped are identical there; the ordering tests therefore use a base that does **not** sort first and were falsified against a flat implementation (4 of 6 red), and the route test asserts the parent **id value**, since `ParentId: null` on every row would pass a presence check and tell the dropdown nothing.
  - **[CR058](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md) — the valuation anchors were being reported as capital contributions.** `Valuation - Historical` carries `is_transfer = TRUE` and CR056's `bucketOf` sends that to `flow`, so the adjustments holding the historical balance appeared as money paid in: **+163,865.07** inside Fidelity Stocks' 2023 net external flows. [`attribute-unrealized.js`](../../server/src/v2/scripts/attribute-unrealized.js) re-buckets it with a **balance-neutral pair** per period — **Stocks 291,910.35 / IRA 119,034.85** across 35 paired periods each — and return percentages exist for 2020–2024 for the first time. Also in the strand: the 2023 handoff reversal was a plug that read Stocks up to **258K high**.
  - **[CR063 §10](../cr/cr-063-coa-ordering.md) — "move up/down does not work".** It did; there was no way to tell. Reorder was the one mutation on the page that did not toast, and the arrows belong to whichever row the pointer is over — so the toast now **names** the account. Behind it, a real ~200 ms stale-click window: the busy guard was released when the POST resolved while `coaRows` refreshes only when the reload lands, so a second click computed its plan from the old rows and posted a **still-valid** sibling set that the server accepted with a 200 and that quietly reverted the first move. The guard now spans the whole cycle. *A test artifact nearly took the blame* — the scroll pane appearing to jump on every reorder was Playwright auto-scrolling before its own click.
- **v3.10.0** (2026-07-31) — **minor: [CR063](../cr/cr-063-coa-ordering.md) — the Chart of Accounts order is the owner's** (migration **049**). Nobody had ever chosen it. `accounts.display_order` has been in the schema since the seed and `findAll`/`getChildren`/`getBalances` all order by it — but **`getTree()`, the one path every tree, report and dropdown funnels through, selected it and then sorted `ORDER BY path`, where `path` is `ARRAY[id]`**. So the COA page, the Balance Sheet, the Cash Flow report, the budget worksheet and the forecast account list have all rendered in **insertion order**; it read as deliberate only because the original seed inserted rows sensibly. **The correct form was already in this repo** — `ingestPs.js` builds the same recursive CTE with `sort_path = ARRAY[display_order, id]` — which is the uncomfortable part: the fix was written down years before the defect was noticed. It could not simply be switched on: the column was a **global** 0–207 sequence rather than a per-parent rank, and `create()` hard-coded **0** (22 accounts tied on dev), so **68 of 229 rows would have moved** — across three reports, not just the page being changed. Migration 049 therefore backfills a per-parent rank ordered by **today's visible order (`id`)** and *deliberately not* by the existing `display_order`, which would have baked the reshuffle in rather than prevented it; its success criterion is that **nothing moves**, and that was checked externally, not asserted: `reports/balance`, `reports/cash-flow`, `budget/cash-flow` and `budget/category-groups` are **byte-identical** across the migration, and both COA trees keep an identical name sequence (differing only by the added `id`/`display_order` fields). `create()` now **appends** at `MAX(sibling)+1`, `POST /util/coa/reorder` takes the **whole** sibling list (idempotent, one transaction, **409** on a stale client — a per-row nudge cannot detect staleness at all), the COA page gains **↑/↓ arrows** disabled at a group's ends and whenever a search or filter is active, and the three alphabetical `.sort()`s in `useCoa` come out along with the two flat `ORDER BY a.name` list endpoints.
  - *The sequencing error, and it is the reusable part:* **half of P3 belonged in P1.** `findAll` and `getBalances` were already sorting `ORDER BY a.display_order, a.name` — on the **global** sequence — so making the column a per-parent rank does not leave them unchanged, it **breaks** them: every parent's rank-1 child sorts first, then every rank-2. The parity run caught it in seconds (`/accounts?leafOnly=true` came back scrambled while the trees were still fine) and a shared `SORT_PATH_CTE` now gives all four flat-list readers the tree's depth-first rank. **A rank scoped to a parent silently breaks every consumer that was treating the column as global** — found by diffing responses, not by reading them.
  - *Falsified, not asserted:* reverting the sort flip and the append default turns **3 of 10** new backend tests red, reverting `findPLeaves` a **4th**, and restoring `useCoa`'s sorts turns the frontend assertion red. That last one required **changing the fixture to make it capable of failing** — its tree was alphabetical, so it could not tell COA order from a `.sort()` at all, which is the same shape as a test that passes against the bug it names.
  - *Also in this release, from the same request:* **Analyze PS Data is gone** — PS is no longer a data source — with its 115-line handler, results banner, quick-add path and three dead modal modes. **The backend endpoint stays**, because Upload PS calls it too. And the tree gained the reports' four **expand/collapse** controls (all + one-layer, both directions), on the shared button primitive after the button-CSS guard rejected a page-local class. *This half was already running on prod ahead of the tag* — it rode the v3.9.2 working-tree build (recorded under that entry); this release is what puts it in a tag.
  - *Left dev's `Assets` group swapped* — one scripted browser run mutated shared state with no undo written alongside it. Caught by re-reading the table rather than trusting the script, and restored through the API. **A UI-driving script that mutates shared state needs its restore written at the same time as its action.**
  - 704 backend / 27 frontend suites / six guards green.

- **v3.9.2** (2026-07-31) — **patch: the Equity report was filed where nobody looked for it (owner-found).** [CR062](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md)'s `/forecast-equity` shipped under **Reports & Graphs**; the owner went looking for it under **Forecast**. CR062 §8.1 had specified the Forecast sidebar in the first place — the implementation moved it on a CR042 invariant (*"the Forecasting group is exactly the six steps FCStepNav numbers, in the same order"*), and **that read the rule too broadly**. The invariant is about the **numbered** list: [`Sidebar.jsx`](../../frontend/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx) prefixes `N. ` only when a route carries `step`, so an unnumbered entry after step 6 leaves the stepper and the sidebar still agreeing on 1–6. **What must stay out is a `step` on that route, not the route itself** — and the comment in `routes.jsx` now says so, since the old comment is what produced the wrong conclusion. No `ForecastProvider` wrapper: the page's `useScenarios` is self-contained. *Verified in a real browser against the built bundle* — a config assertion would only restate the config — reading Forecast as 1–6 plus an unnumbered *Forecast Equity*, and Reports as Balances / Cash Flow / Investment Returns / Category Trend. Frontend only, no migration, no API change.
  - *Shipped in the same prod image, deliberately and at the owner's call:* **CR063 P0** (the *Analyze PS Data* removal, −363/+151 lines across five COA Management files) was **uncommitted in the shared working tree** when this release was cut, and `deploy-to-production.sh` runs `docker compose build --no-cache` over the working tree rather than a git checkout. So the prod bundle contains code that this tag does **not**. Written down because a future bisect against `v3.9.2` will not reproduce what prod actually ran.
- **v3.9.1** (2026-07-31) — **patch: two problems [CR062](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md) found in passing, neither caused by it.** **(1) `POST /forecast/modules` silently dropped nine columns.** `createModule` carried its **own** hand-written INSERT list, separate from `updateModule`'s allow-list, and the two had drifted; **five** of the nine the route had already mapped into `moduleData` and believed it was saving — CR046's four window dates and CR047's income tax override. POST returned **201** and they were gone: no error, nothing to notice. *That is the v3.0.86 defect one layer down* — same CR046/CR047 fields, same shape of whitelist, `fcModulePayload` then and the repository now — which is what makes **one-list-per-path the bug** rather than any individual omission. Both paths now read one `MODULE_COLUMN_DEFAULTS` map, and it carries the defaults too, because *"what column"* and *"what does absent mean"* are the same question. Falsified first (the window date came back `null`); **the second test is the one that outlives the fix** — it reads `information_schema` and asserts every writable column is reachable through the repository, so the next migration that adds one trips a test instead of a scenario quietly computing something else. **(2) Migration 041's checksum drift is resolved, not silenced.** The in-place edit (`10eb270`) was **unavoidable** — 041 aborts the chain on a hard-coded `parent_id = 200` that CI's serial-id database cannot satisfy, so nothing later can repair it — but the runner could only ever *warn*, so every deploy would report it forever, and **a warning nobody can clear is a warning everybody learns to scroll past.** Equivalence was **proved before it was accepted**, rather than trusting the commit message: `Transfer - Distributions` is id 228, parent `Transfers`, `is_transfer` TRUE on **both** databases, and re-running the current file inside a transaction produces **no INSERT, no UPDATE and no error** on either. `migrate.js` gains `--accept-drift=<file>` — per file, never a blanket sweep, and a dry run never accepts. Applied to dev and prod; both ledgers now report **zero drift and zero pending**, and dev's also picked up 046/047/048, which had been applied by hand and never recorded. The rule is now written down in `.claude/rules/migrations.md`, because the next person to hit this otherwise either edits a migration freely or lives with a permanent warning. Verified on prod itself with a throwaway module (id 433): `income_start_date`, `expense_end_date` and `income_tax_rate_override` all persisted, then deleted. 694 backend / 238 frontend tests, six guards green.
- **v3.9.0** (2026-07-31) — **minor: [CR062](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md) P2 — secure a loan against an asset, and report the equity** (migration **048**). `forecast_modules.secured_asset_module_id` (self-FK, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) plus **`/forecast-equity`**: asset value gross, less the debt secured on it, equals **equity**; then asset income less asset expense less loan interest = **net income**, and net income less principal = **net cash after debt service**. **Two bottom lines on purpose** — principal repayment is a *transfer*, so folding it into the P&L misstates one question and dropping it misstates the other. **No engine work**: `forecast_entries` already carries `(year, account, module)`, so the report is a read over what the build already wrote. *The trap it exists to close:* `copyScenario` inserts modules one at a time, so a naive copy carries the **source** scenario's module id and the copy's report would read the **source scenario's house** — with both figures real. Fixed with an `oldId → newId` second pass, mirrored in the CR050 variant sync via `origin_base_id`, and asserted by migration 048's `DO` block so a regression fails at **deploy** rather than in a report; the test asserts the link points at a module **in the copy**, not merely that it is non-null. *Two defects of my own, both caught by this phase's own tests and guards:* the module **PUT validated after writing** — a rejected secured-asset link (or an interest line removed from a loan) was persisted and *then* 400'd, which is worse than no guard, and is now checked against the state the write would produce; and the Equity page referenced `var(--text)`, which **is not a token**, silently ignoring the theme until `check-dead-tokens` failed the build. The chart is deliberately **not** a stacked area: negative equity is a normal state here (the SRQ plan draws its mortgage in 2027 and buys the house in 2028, so 2027 is **−800K**), and stacking made *"value = equity + debt"* silently stop being true the moment it went negative.
- **v3.8.0** (2026-07-31) — **minor: [CR062](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md) P0 + P1 — the forecast can model a loan** (migration **047**). It could not before, **at all**, and the gap was worse than absent: `expenseValues[i] = isLiability ? val : -val` did not correct a sign on a negative balance, it **inverted** one, so the first mortgage entered with a rate would have **credited** the bank line ~25,000 a year — in the model a house-purchase decision rests on. Two more verified defects behind it: the "liability interest" path drives `ExpensePct`, whose column **migration 008 dropped** and which the loader hard-codes to 0 (unreachable from real data since), and `Dispose` is **silently zeroed** on a negative balance, leaving `Invest` as the only working principal primitive. **P0 shipped alone first**, provably dormant — 15 liability modules, **zero** able to reach the branch, and `2026 Base` / `2026 Downside` regenerate byte-for-byte. **P1** stores five assumptions and **derives** the schedule on every generate (never materializes rows that rot — the CR049/CR050 pattern), keys the engine on `loan_interest_rate` and **never** on the user-editable `module_type`, and charges interest on the **average** outstanding, which *is* the July-1 convention — so the draw year carries exactly half a year for free. The end year repays the **remainder**, which is what closes a loan at exactly zero instead of 40 cents short (100/9 at 4dp sums to 99.9999% and would fire the balloon warning on the one-click happy path). Retype-to-Loan **asks before it deletes**, naming what will go. *The two review passes moved it more than they polished it:* base-year interest was missing from `getBaseYearValues` and therefore from the **cash sweep's opening cash** — the exact CR049 §1 class, in the function CR049 created to end it; a draw dated at the base year **vanishes on write**; a blank Interest Line **drains cash into no P&L row at all**; and the driving scenario is a **CR050 variant**, so the loan runs through force-sync on every build. *Two guards were falsified against unguarded code before being kept:* a stale CR046 expense window turns the interest series into `0 0 0 13,797.66 …`, and a leftover `expense_amount` makes CR041's ownership gate halve the draw year a **second** time. Real proof on dev: an 800,000 mortgage at 6% drawn 2027 ending 2047 gives **+776,000** of cash in the draw year (800,000 less a half-year's 24,000 of interest), 46,800 of interest in 2028 on the 780,000 average balance, and closes at **exactly zero** in 2047.
- **v3.7.4** (2026-07-31) — **patch: the bank-feed ingest was taking the first 500 rows and calling it the window.** One `limit: 500` call, the `offset` the client already accepted never used, and silence when a page returned *equal to* the limit — so a truncated import and a complete one were indistinguishable, and the truncated one reported success. **It is why the Revolut duplicate stayed invisible: the row that would have exposed it was never fetched.** Now follows pages to a short one and reports `pages`; past **40** full pages it **throws**, because a service ignoring `offset` returns a full page forever and looks exactly like a genuinely huge window — failing loud beats staging an unknown fraction. Offset paging is sound against *this* endpoint specifically: it orders by `transaction_date DESC, id DESC`, which is total (`id` is unique), and new rows land at the **front**, so a concurrent sync shifts later pages and can cause a **re-read, never a skip** — and a re-read is harmless, staging being idempotent on `(source, external_id)`. **The risk of the fix was never fetching more, it was promoting more, so that is what was verified:** the wider pull surfaced **3 rows fin had never seen** — Black Card manual-statement rows including an **$11,527.84 payment** — every one already in the ledger under its Plaid id, and every one correctly held by that account's `promote_from_date` (the cutoff set after the Black Card incident). **930 more rows fetched, 3 new to staging, zero reached the ledger.** 4 tests, falsified against the single-call version first — 3 of 4 fail, the short-page case being a deliberate control. 623 backend tests green; no migration. **Also in this release — every dialog in the app was painting *under* the navigation that opened it.** The shared [`Modal`](../../frontend/src/components/Modal/Modal.jsx) primitive shipped at z-index **40/41** while `.navbar` is **9999** and `.sidebar` is **50** (sticky page headers are 1000), so the stacking was wrong app-wide from CR042 U4 onward — and unobservable, because a 520–720px centred dialog never reaches the chrome. The `min(95vw, 1400px)` Forecast module-output panel does: in sidebar layout the sidebar covered its title, scenario line and currency toggle. Now **10400 / 10401** — above both nav surfaces, deliberately still below toasts and the PWA update prompt (99999), since a dialog must not hide the notification saying what just happened. Verified in a browser in **both** nav layouts (`localStorage.navLayout`), which is the only way to see it: the defect is invisible in whichever layout you happen to test, and the e2e suite only ever ran one of them.
- **v3.7.3** (2026-07-31) — **patch: Forecast "View Output" opened a panel that took no clicks at all, including its own ✕.** It reads as a frozen page, and nothing about it looks wrong: correct DOM, correct data, correct state, handlers attached, no error anywhere. The module editor is a Radix dialog ([CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U4's shared [`Modal`](../../frontend/src/components/Modal/Modal.jsx)) and **stays open behind** the panel; a modal Radix layer sets `pointer-events: none` on `<body>` and re-enables it **only on the layers it owns**, so [`FCModuleAuditModal`](../../frontend/src/features/Forecast/FCModuleAuditModal.jsx)'s hand-rolled overlay — a sibling `<div>` at `zIndex: 1100` — painted on top and swallowed every event. Radix also `aria-hidden`s everything outside its dialog, so the panel was **absent from the accessibility tree** as well: unusable by mouse *and* invisible to a screen reader, from two independent causes. Fixed by routing it through the shared primitive in `bare` mode — a real nested layer, plus the ESC key and focus trap it never had. **The test is the part worth keeping:** unit tests cannot see this class (jsdom has no hit-testing, and RTL fires events on the node directly), so **a browser refusing to click is the only witness** — [`e2e/nested-modal.spec.js`](../../frontend/e2e/nested-modal.spec.js) makes Playwright's actionability check the assertion, and it was **falsified against the unfixed component first**, failing with `<div class="modal__overlay"> intercepts pointer events`. It also locates by text and CSS rather than by role *on purpose*: role queries fail there for the **other** reason (aria-hidden) and would have masked the one that matters. 8 e2e / 222 frontend tests / six guards green; frontend only, no migration. **The general rule, worth more than the fix: any bespoke overlay opened from inside a `<Modal>` is dead on arrival** — `FCCashSweepModal`, `FCAddFromLinesModal` and `FCAddFromActualsModal` are safe today only because nothing opens them from within an open dialog, which is a property of their callers, not of the components.
- **v3.7.2** (2026-07-31) — **patch: the Revolut misattribution, fixed on both sides — and the point is that neither half was shippable alone.** **(1) bank-feed `6d108ec`:** the tx→account id guard read `raw.accountId` (MX/Chase) and `raw.account_id` (Plaid) but not `raw.ext_nordigen_acc_id` (GoCardless), on a comment asserting GoCardless's id *"does not surface here"* and that its names *"don't collide"*. Both false — it is in every GoCardless raw, and **the names need not collide for a name join to be wrong**: fintable *mislabelled* the `⚡ Account` column on four rows and the join believed it, filing four EUR transactions under a USD wallet that carries no USD row and no balance. Measured over all 1,834 stored rows: 687 carry the id, **all 687 resolve to a known account**, 683 were already filed correctly and **exactly 4 move**; an unrecognised id still falls back to the name join. **(2) fin:** a fintable id is `{provider_account_id}--{event_hash}` — the prefix changes on re-consent *and* on re-attribution, the suffix is the event (one Revolut top-up is on file under **three** prefixes and one suffix). `promote()` did not know that, so shipping (1) alone would have **traded a misattribution bug for a duplication bug**; it now skips a staged row whose event hash already sits on a bank-feed transaction for that account, deliberately ahead of and independent of `BANK_FEED_DEDUP_ENABLED` — turning the fuzzy matcher off means *"do not guess"*, and an exact id match is not a guess. Both fixes falsified against their old code first. **Verified on the real chain, not only in tests:** bank-feed re-filed the 4 rows → prod re-ingested → promote returned **0 inserted**, Revolut-EUR 193/98.13 and Revolut-USD 4/1.46 unchanged, USD mapping live again; the guard itself was proven on dev against the genuine duplicate the API now serves (the 2026-06-08 €25 under two prefixes), which **linked** to the existing row instead of inserting a second. 619 backend / 167 bank-feed tests green; no migration. *Prod data cleaned separately:* 2 duplicates deleted, their staging rows suppressed. *Confirmed live in passing:* bank-feed's `(account_id, external_id)` conflict key **copies rather than moves** a re-attributed transaction — 4 stale rows deleted by hand, exactly as §2's item predicts. The CR059 API converter joins on `tx.account_id` directly and never had defect (1), so the cutover **retires** this class rather than inheriting it.
- **v3.7.1** (2026-07-31) — **patch: two bugs, one shape — a second copy of state that nothing kept in step.** (1) **No bank-feed transaction could be deleted from the UI, ever.** The owner hit *"violates foreign key constraint `bankfeed_staging_promoted_transaction_id_fkey`"*; the constraint is **NO ACTION** and [`repositories/transactions.remove()`](../../server/src/v2/repositories/transactions.js) issued a bare `DELETE`, so the failure was not specific to those two rows — **every** promoted feed row was undeletable since the feed shipped. `remove()` now releases `promoted_transaction_id` **and sets `suppressed = TRUE`** in the same transaction. *Releasing alone would have been worse than the bug:* `promote()` re-promotes any staging row with `promoted_transaction_id IS NULL AND suppressed = FALSE`, so a "fixed" delete would have resurrected the row on the next refresh. The regression test was **falsified against the old code first** and asserts the owner's exact error string. No migration; 616 backend tests green. *The rows that triggered it were themselves a symptom:* fintable serves one Revolut EUR wallet under **three** provider account ids as consents are renewed, and bank-feed's name-join filed the stale-id copies under the **USD** wallet — prod was cleaned and the upstream attribution bug belongs to [CR059](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md) P3a. (2) **A COA category created in the UI was invisible to the rest of the app.** The owner added `US - Investments` and it did not show in the Forecast **New Module** account list. Nothing was wrong with the account — `GET /accounts/tree?section=balance_sheet` served it as a level-2 child of Assets with its `Misc Investments` leaf — but [`COAManagement`](../../frontend/src/pages/COAManagement.jsx) holds its **own** fetch of the COA and after a save refreshed only that, while every other consumer (forecast module account list, category selectors, transaction filters, Cash-Flow-by-account) reads the shared [`useCoa()`](../../frontend/src/hooks/useCoa.js) TanStack query with `staleTime: 5 min` and **no invalidation on any COA mutation**. So adds, renames, moves and deletes were all app-wide invisible until the cache aged out or the page was reloaded — self-healing, which is precisely why it had never been reported as a bug. All four mutation paths now go through one `reloadCoaAfterMutation()` that invalidates `["coa"]` as well as reloading the page's own copy. Also, the module account list now **sorts alphabetically** rather than following COA tree order: tree order put the new category ninth, between `Historical Assets` and `US - Mortgages`, so a list that *did* contain it read as one that did not. *Note the flat A–Z list interleaves asset and liability categories* — acceptable, because it is a flat list with no group headers either way. Frontend only, no API change, no migration; 222 frontend tests green.

- **v3.6.11** (2026-07-30) — **patch: three silent-wrong-number defects, a label that was lying, and a promote-cutoff pin — plus CR059's two review passes and CR060 shipped.** The four fixes share one shape, and it is the reusable lesson: *a defect survives exactly as long as every check reads the column it did not corrupt.* (1) **`Math.abs()` turned every Quicken reversal into a second credit** — `insertInvestmentCashRows` forced `gross_amount` positive under a comment asserting income is an inflow "regardless of how the QIF signed" it, true of 6,003 rows and false of the 3 negatives, two of which are income (a `-39.73` Div reversal and a `-125.69 IntInc` whose memo reads *"CORP INT ADJUSTMENT"*). Income overstated **330.84**; found by pulling on `quicken-verify`'s own *"7 groups, 8 extra rows — verify these are genuine"* warning, where two of the seven were a booking and its own reversal collapsed to the same sign. Repaired by rollback → re-promote → re-anchor, and the regression test asserts **the pair nets to zero** (asserting a row exists, or its magnitude, passes happily against `Math.abs()`) — falsified against the old code first. (2) **The PocketSmith sign fix shipped half-applied:** `fix-ps-transfer-signs.js` updated `amount` and never `base_amount`, so all nine corrected rows carried the old sign in the **USD read path** — 1,504,114.38 of divergence on Fidelity Stocks. Hidden because Balance Sheet, Balance Trends, the anchor tie-out and `quicken-verify` all read `amount`. The UPDATE now **negates** `base_amount` (negating preserves the booked FX rate; copying `amount` would rewrite it to 1.0), with `currency='USD' ⇒ base_amount = amount` asserted inside the write transaction and in a test that injects a broken row and asserts on **that row's id**, so ambient data cannot make it vacuous. (3) **`/investment-returns` stopped calling an all-USD plug "FX effect"** — on a USD row the two amount columns are the same number by definition, so a divergence means one was written without the other; the label now reads *"Unreconciled — USD amounts disagree"* in the flag colour. That **provably-zero quantity is what exposed (2)**, reporting −1,435,914 of "FX" on an account that has never held a foreign currency: a quantity that must be zero by construction is a far better detector than a plausible-looking one. (4) **Migration 043 pins the promote cutoff** on the two live bank-feed mappings that still had NULL — "promote every staged row whatever its date" is the mechanism behind the Black Card incident's 31 duplicates ($8.4K gross, net **+$267**, so no balance check could see it). Each pins to the earliest row already staged, so behavior today is unchanged while a *later*-arriving older row is blocked; `setBankFeedMapping` applies the same pin at mapping time. **Not claimed:** this does not close that class — those duplicates were already staged when the account was mapped; closing it needs a deliberate cutoff choice in the UI, which has no write path (see the item above). Pass-2's suggested `CURRENT_DATE` default was **rejected on inspection** for exactly that reason: an account mapped today would promote nothing and the owner could not correct it. Also in this release: **[CR060](../cr/cr-060-feed-connection-health.md) deployed** (bank-feed side — upstream connection health on `/v1/health/feeds`, which found Capital One stale on its first run), and **[CR059](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md) rev 4** after two review passes that overturned three of its own claims and **deleted its riskiest phase**. 533 backend tests / 41 suites green; 164 in bank-feed.

- **v3.6.10** (2026-07-29) — **feat: the Add-Transaction amount field takes a calculation (owner-requested).** The amount you want to post is often one you have to work out first, and doing that in another window loses the working. `11,000-9,600` now posts **1,400.00**. New `utils/amountFormula.js` — a hand-written **recursive-descent parser**, deliberately **not `eval` / `new Function`**: there is no reason a money field should be able to execute anything, and three tests assert that `alert(1)`, `1;2` and `(()=>1)()` are rejected rather than run. Supports `+ - * /`, parentheses, unary signs, decimals and comma-grouped thousands; a plain number is just the trivial case of the same parse, so **every amount that worked before works unchanged**. **The comma rule is the load-bearing part.** A comma is accepted only in a genuine thousands position (`\d{1,3}(,\d{3})+`) — `1,5` is **rejected**, not read as 15. A European decimal comma would otherwise yield a silently 10×-wrong money value that nothing downstream could catch, and this codebase has already been bitten twice by exactly that shape (CR058's unquoted thousands separator; CR046/CR047's silently dropped fields). Rejecting is recoverable; a wrong number in the ledger is not. `1,2345` gets the *comma* error rather than the generic parse error, because the reason matters more than the refusal. Results round to cents so the preview is exactly what gets stored (`11000/3` → 3,666.67; `0.1+0.2` → 0.30, not 0.30000000000000004), division by zero is an error rather than `Infinity`, and a live **`= 1,400.00 EUR`** preview appears only once the field holds arithmetic — a money value should never be a surprise. **222 frontend tests** (+10) **/ build ✓ / six guards ✓.** Verified end-to-end in a browser: a formula submitted through the real modal stored `1400.00 EUR` with `base_amount 1659.69` USD, and the test row was deleted from dev. Frontend only; no API change, no migration. *Scope note: the Ledger's Add modal only — the single-row edit amount and the Manual Calibration balance field still take plain numbers.*
- **v3.6.9** (2026-07-29) — **patch: the v3.6.8 "Reset opening" button was a layout defect (owner-found).** Two full-size `generate-report-button`s in one action cell wrapped onto separate lines and **overlapped the row below** — a second heavy green button for a rare action, in the slot that belongs to the row's primary one. Replaced with an **`Opening` column** carrying the plug itself (`opening_balance`, right-aligned, between `Type` and `Computed` so it reads *opening → computed*) and a small underlined **Reset** beneath the figure, mirroring the page's existing `.bfd-balance-reset` idiom in the Current-balance cell. This is better than tidier spacing would have been: the column *surfaces the number*, so you can see at a glance which accounts carry a plug instead of inferring it from which rows grew a button. Action cell is back to one Reconcile per row, and row heights are uniform. Tokens only (`--muted`, `--danger-strong`, both with dark overrides) — no new hex, no new button class; button-css / inline-hex / dead-token guards all still green. Verified in a browser in **both themes** via the app's own `data-theme` (the reset resolves to `#828E9B`, the dark `--muted`). Frontend only; no API, no migration.
- **v3.6.8** (2026-07-29) — **patch: "Reset opening" — zero the `opening_balance` plug ([CR033](../cr/cr-033-manual-calibration.md), owner-requested).** Every ledger row reads `opening_balance + Σ tx`, so an account whose real history starts at its first transaction carries a pre-history balance that inflates **every** date. **CVC Fund VIII** held **28,106.29** dated `2000-01-01` — more than two years before the fund's first capital call — so the 2022-04-03 `Capital Call` of 97,342.11 rendered a running balance of **125,448.40**. New `POST /manual-calibration/reset-opening/:accountId` + a per-row **Reset opening** button (shown only where the plug is non-zero), and an **Opening balance** button in the Ledger toolbar (single-account view) that navigates there — the defect is *visible* on the Ledger and fixable only on Manual Calibration, so it links out rather than adding a second write path to the account row. It sets `opening_balance = 0` and **deliberately nothing else**: compensating automatically would mean inventing a transaction and choosing its date and category, whereas the existing Reconcile action already books the gap as a *dated, visible* Unrealized-G/L row. **The two traps were found by building it, not by reading it.** (1) In `calibrate` mode Reconcile re-anchors `opening_balance = entered − Σ tx`, restoring the **exact** plug — CVC Fund VIII is in that mode, so the obvious *Reset → Reconcile* sequence is a **no-op**; the confirm now says so and points at the mtm switch. (2) On Quicken-promoted accounts `opening_balance` is a **computed anchor** (CR019 §22, [CR058](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md)), not a plug, so zeroing it would move every anchored valuation date — blocked behind a force override, though **currently dormant**, since all four accounts carrying `quicken%` rows are fed and never reach this page. The confirm spells the consequence out in figures (*"computed goes 594,365.00 → 566,258.71"*) because the reset moves **today's** balance and net worth, not just the old rows. Proven on a dev copy of prod — first row **125,448.40 → 97,342.11**, closing **594,365.00 → 566,258.71**, dev restored afterwards — and both pages driven in a real browser with zero console errors. **523 backend** (4 new; the Quicken-guard test confirmed able to fail) **/ 212 frontend / build ✓ / six guards ✓.** No migration.
- **v3.6.7** (2026-07-29) — **patch: [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) IRR abstained on closed-out positions (owner-found).** v3.6.5's IRR refused to compute whenever **nothing valued the period**, on the reasoning that an IRR taken off a *cost-basis* ending value reports ~0% as though the asset had never moved. Sound for the case it was built against (`US - Nokomis`), and wrong for a **closed** one: `CVC - MIP` has **no `Unrealized G/L` postings at all** and ends at **0.00** — a fund that took 347K PLN in 2017 and returned ~3.78M through 2024 — so the rule blanked the one number that describes it, for a reason that cannot apply when the ending value is zero. A closed position's ending value is **exact, not a guess**: every dollar in and out is known, and IRR is the *only* correct return measure for a holding that was never marked and no longer exists. The test is now whether the ending value can be trusted — **either** a valuation in the period **or** `|Ending MV| < 1` — with `total.irrBasis` reporting which (`valued`/`closed`) and the page saying *"position closed at zero — no valuation needed"*. Verified read-only against **prod**: CVC - MIP **63.93%** (closed) and United Beverages **74.98%** (valued) now compute, while `Fidelity Stock` and `US - Nokomis` still correctly abstain over a window that neither valued nor closed them. Also corrected: the first fixture test asserted a *guessed* 30–60% range; the real answer is 67% (IRR weights CVC's year-3 distribution heavily — it alone returns 3.1× the money in), so the test now pins the solved rate **and** independently asserts NPV ≈ 0 at it, proving the root rather than my expectation of it. **519 backend / 212 frontend / build ✓ / five guards ✓.** No migration.
- **v3.6.6** (2026-07-28) — **patch: two JSX `ReferenceError` crashes, and the lint hole that let both of them ship.** Owner-reported: the **mobile home page** (`/m`) was an error screen while every other `/m` route worked. `ba4ef7f` ("delete 44 dead variables", 2026-07-13) had rewritten the launcher map as `CARDS.map(({ to, label }) => …)` while the body still rendered `<Icon size={24} />` — `ReferenceError: Icon is not defined`, **two weeks live in prod**. The same commit made the *identical* edit to `MobileTabBar`; that copy survived only because it happened to trip the **args** ignore pattern (which had no capitalized escape hatch yet) and turned the blocking gate red from v3.4.8 to v3.6.0. **MobileHome produced no finding whatsoever** — `npx eslint MobileHome.jsx` returned clean on the broken file, the build passed and all 211 tests passed. **The cause is structural, not a slip:** `<Foo />` parses to a JSXIdentifier and core ESLint builds **no scope reference** from it, so *both directions* of the lookup were blind — an identifier used only in JSX looks **UNUSED**, and one that does not exist at all is not **UNDEFINED**. The project compensated for the first half with capitalized `varsIgnorePattern`/`argsIgnorePattern`, which papered over the symptom and, being an *ignore*, could never buy the second half. Fixed by installing `eslint-plugin-react` for **exactly two rules** — `react/jsx-uses-vars` and `react/jsx-no-undef`, **not** its `recommended` set (mostly prop-types and stylistic; hundreds of findings unrelated to correctness) — and **removing** the escape hatches (`^[A-Z_]` / `^(_|[A-Z])` → plain `^_`), which `jsx-uses-vars` makes safe: an unused component import is a real finding again. **The new rule immediately found a second, worse crash.** `TransActual` renders `<CategorySelector>` in the split-transaction modal but has not imported it since **`5e638aa` (2026-03-28)**, which removed the import and **one** of its two usages and missed the other. Because the throw is inside TransActual's own render, clicking **Split** on the Actuals page did not merely break the modal — it took the **entire page** to the ErrorBoundary, for **four months**. Removing the hatch also surfaced four dead identifiers it had been hiding (unused `Inbox` imports in `BudgetWorksheetV2`/`TransActual`/`TransBudget`, a dead `CURRENT_MONTH` in `TransactionFilterBudget`). **Both fixes verified in a real browser, not just by the rule:** `/m` in an iPhone-13 viewport (8 cards, 8 icons, net worth rendered, zero console errors) and the split modal opened on a real 5,000 PLN transaction (both category selectors populated from the COA tree); deleting either fix reproduces its exact `ReferenceError`. The mobile fix also carries a **render** regression test (`MobileHome.test.jsx`) rather than a data assertion — reverting one token turns it red; the `TransActual` case needs none, because `jsx-no-undef` is now the permanent guard for the whole class. **212 frontend tests / lint 0 errors, 78 warnings (debt count unchanged, `check-lint-debt.sh` still passes) / build ✓ / six guards ✓.** No migration, no API change. *Also riding along, dormant:* four CR058 commits of **CLI-only** Quicken scripts (`quicken-anchor`, `quicken-promote`, `quicken-verify`) — verified unreachable from the running app (nothing outside `scripts/` imports them, nothing runs them on boot), so they change no prod behavior. *Takeaway: a guard that cannot see the language construct it is guarding is not a guard, and the ignore pattern that silenced the symptom is what kept anyone from noticing.*
- **v3.6.5** (2026-07-28) — **patch: [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) refinement — IRR line + value/return split.** Owner-requested after living with the report. A **money-weighted IRR (annualized)** now sits on one line below the table, solved on the **actual dated cash flows** rather than the per-column aggregates: opening market value as the initial investment, every external-flow transaction at its own date on the investor's sign convention, closing market value as proceeds; **bisection** over `[-99.99%, +1000%]` (Newton diverges on the sign patterns real ledgers produce). It complements the average-capital percentages instead of replacing them — those answer *what did the assets do*, IRR answers *what did my money earn*, and they separate when contributions are large or badly timed (Fidelity Stock: 30.28% cumulative vs **19.79%** IRR, the gap being annualization over ~19 months). It **abstains** on no sign change, on spans under 30 days, and — the one that matters — when **nothing valued the period**, since an IRR off a cost-basis ending value would report ~0% as though the asset had genuinely not moved (`US - Nokomis` correctly returns `—`). Layout: `Ending market value` moved up under the components that explain the move from `Beginning market value`, both balance rows bolded, and a spacer separating the value block from the return block. Also folded in: the **known limitation at ship is gone** — United Beverages' 0.00% realized was fixed at the source by [CR057](../cr/cr-057-book-income-at-source.md) booking income at the holding, and this report needed **no change** to pick it up (UB now shows 3.98% / 3.34% realized alongside its price return). **502 backend** (5 new xirr tests) **/ 211 frontend / build ✓ / five guards ✓.** Frontend + one new `total.irr` field; no migration.
- **v3.6.4** (2026-07-28) — **patch: the Book-at-Source conversion label pointed the rate the wrong way.** The cross-currency
  preview read *"at PLN/USD 0.239682"*, which parses naturally as **PLN per USD** — and at that reading the arithmetic is
  nonsense (158,114 × 0.239682 ≈ 37,897, not the 659,682.41 shown). `exchange_rates` stores the inverse: `from_currency=PLN,
  to_currency=USD` is **USD per PLN**, and the conversion divides by it. The label now reads **"at 4.1722 PLN per USD"**, the
  direction a reader would actually multiply, so the shown figure can be checked by eye. **Display only** — the same rate, the
  same conversion, and both legs still cancel exactly in the holding's currency and in USD. Owner-found: they challenged the
  amounts on a real CVC-MIP booking, and the amounts were right while the label was not.
- **v3.6.3** (2026-07-28) — **patch: two PocketSmith transfers booked as CREDITS on Fidelity Stocks.** Both were outgoing;
  both had been wrong for six years. **2020-08-26 +617,957.20** — a transfer to Fidelity Cash Mgt booked as a credit on **both**
  accounts with no opposing leg anywhere in the ledger (the counterparty shows the money *arriving*, and Quicken records it
  leaving Stocks as `XOut -617,957.20`); **2020-11-04 +100,000.00** `ELECTRONIC FUNDS TRANSFER PAID`, paired against a Chase
  Checking `+100,000.00 Fid Bkg Svc` row — both legs positive. *(Not the two Feb-2020 `Fid Bkg Svc` rows
  [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) identified as genuine deposits; those are untouched.)*
  - *Why they hid for six years:* `opening_balance` is a **plug**, so it silently absorbed them — today's balance stayed right
