    while **every date before each row was understated, by 1,435,914.40 in total**. Any check that reconciles only the current
    balance was structurally incapable of finding this. New `fix-ps-transfer-signs.js` re-plugs `opening_balance` by the same
    amount and **aborts unless today is unchanged**; dry-run by default, idempotent (a row is flipped only while it still
    carries the wrong sign).
  - *Corroboration, and it is the strong part:* correcting them collapses [CR058](../cr/cr-058-quicken-valuation-anchors.md)'s
    2020 valuation anchor from **−1,445,246.13 to −9,331.73** — Quicken and PocketSmith, two independent reconstructions, come
    to agree on 2020 to within **2.4%** of a 383,389.13 account. Much stronger than either row read alone.
  - *How they were found:* a net-to-zero sweep of every `is_transfer` row ≥ 1,000 — 15 same-signed multi-account clusters out of
    903. Only these two touch Fidelity Stocks and are confirmed; **the other 13 are left for separate triage** (some are
    legitimate, e.g. a EUR→USD conversion plus a deposit). Applied to dev and prod 2026-07-28, prod backup at
    `Backups/pre-ps-sign-fix/`; prod balance verified unchanged at **1,157,037.74** (re-verified independently at release).
  - Script only — no schema, no API, no UI. 496 backend / 211 frontend / build ✓ / six guards ✓.
- **v3.6.2** (2026-07-28) — **patch: cross-currency Book at Source, plus two bugs the owner hit using it.**
  - *Cross-currency is now **converted**, not refused* ([CR057](../cr/cr-057-book-income-at-source.md) invariant 4). The original
    refusal — "a cross-currency leg needs a rate policy this action does not take" — was too cautious. The two legs are
    equal-and-opposite on the **same** account, so they cancel in the holding's currency **and** in USD: the book value is
    unchanged whatever rate is used, and no `Unrealized G/L` mark is disturbed. `base_amount` stays an exact copy/negation, so
    invariant 1 is untouched; the rate only decides what the holding-currency figure reads in CR056's LC mode. Derived from the
    USD base at the transaction date via `fx.rateAsOf`, rounded **once** and negated verbatim so rounding cannot leave the pair
    failing to cancel, and **failing loud** on a missing/zero rate (CR051 F1 precedent). Verified on dev against the real CVC
    row: **USD 34,942.07 → EUR 30,471.93** at 1.146697 on 2026-06-22, CVC Fund VIII book unchanged at 566,258.71 EUR /
    605,722.90 USD, LC-mode `FX effect` **0.00**, and Transfer Analysis **auto-matched** the EUR leg to the USD cash row — it
    keys on `base_amount`, not `amount`. Unblocks the 6 CVC rows; *which* fund each belongs to remains the owner's call.
  - *Fix — stale selections survived a data reload.* `selectedRows` was a Map that was never pruned when the transaction list
    changed, so a row selected before a filter/period/account change stayed **counted** while rendering nowhere: the bar read
    "2 selected" with one row ticked, `isAllSelected` flipped true (`size === rows.length`) so the header ticked itself, and
    **CR057's single-row "Book at source" action silently vanished**. Edit/Delete could also have acted on a row the user could
    no longer see. Latent since the hook was written, but **v3.6.0 made it routine** by turning the Ledger category filter into a
    server-side refetch. Selections are now derived against the visible rows — a memo, not an effect, so no extra render and no
    `set-state-in-effect` debt. 5 tests; reverting turns 3 red.
  - *Fix — the Book-at-source modal read the wrong field names.* The transaction pages rename display fields to PascalCase
    (`Date`/`Amount`/`Currency`/`Account`/`Category`) while keeping the ids snake_case; the modal was written against the raw API
    shape, so on the Ledger **every display field rendered blank** and the picker's currency filter short-circuited on
    `!transaction.currency`, offering accounts in every currency (the server refused them, so no bad data — but the UI should not
    have offered them). Normalized once at the modal boundary. **Found by the owner, not by the tests** — the backend was verified
    hard and the rendered modal never was.
  - **496 backend / 211 frontend / build ✓ / six guards ✓.** No migration.
- **v3.6.1** (2026-07-28) — **patch: two date bugs in the transaction tables, both owner-found, both silent.**
  - *Single-row Edit was rejected outright.* `parseEditFormValue(v, "date")` returned a **Date object** where every other
    field type returns a primitive. It survived as far as `JSON.stringify`, which serializes to a full ISO instant
    (`"2021-08-19T00:00:00.000Z"`), and the API's `assertDateString` requires `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/` — so the PATCH 400'd with
    *"transaction_date must be a YYYY-MM-DD date string"*. Because a single-row edit always includes `Date` via **consensus**,
    this rejected **every** single-row edit on the Ledger and Actuals, whatever field was actually being changed — the owner hit
    it re-categorizing a `Transfer - Historical` row. Broken since the CR037 field validation (v3.0.54). Now returns the
    `YYYY-MM-DD` string.
  - *The tables rendered a day early west of UTC.* `new Date("2021-08-19")` parses as **UTC midnight**, so
    `toLocaleDateString()` shows **18 August** for a US reader. Every `transaction_date` is a date-only string, so the row for
    2021-08-19 read "Aug 18, 2021" while the edit picker (reading UTC parts) correctly said 19 — the two disagreed on screen.
    New shared `parseDisplayDate()` pins date-only strings to **local** midnight (the idiom the mobile pages already used);
    Ledger / Actuals / Budget now share it instead of three copies of the same broken `formatDate`. Known Issue #3 on the
    *parse* side — the `toISOString` lint guard only covers the format side.
  - *The tests had to be made able to fail.* The CI runner is **UTC**, where the buggy parse also yields the 19th, so a
    TZ-neutral display test passes against the bug. The spec pins `TZ=America/New_York` and asserts the pin took effect;
    reverting both fixes turns **6 of 11** red. 11 new frontend tests (206 total). No migration, backend untouched.
- **v3.6.0** (2026-07-28) — **minor: [CR057](../cr/cr-057-book-income-at-source.md) Book Income at Source** (migration **041**), plus two fixes found by using it. Investment income was recorded on the account where the **cash landed**, not on the holding that **earned** it, so [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) reported United Beverages at a **confident 0.00%** against ~25M PLN of average capital — worse than a blank. A per-row **Book at source** action in the Ledger writes a **three-leg booking** — income at the holding, transfer out of the holding, and the original row re-categorized as the transfer in — leaving the holding's book value, the cash account's balance, net worth and every P&L category total unchanged while moving the *attribution*. **It overturns CR056's own P2 design.** That CR rejected moving the income as identity-breaking; the objection only ever covered a **one-legged** change. With the transfer leg, `Δ totalReturn = Δ(EMV−BMV) − Δ netFlows = 0 − (−X) = +X = Δ income`, and `bucketOf` has no fall-through, so both legs land in the intended bucket by construction — so the "distributions received elsewhere" *mechanism* is deleted rather than built. **Pass 1 returned `revise` with 5 blocking findings, and its headline outlives this CR:** the draft's central test — *"the reconciliation identity must stay 0.0000"* — **could not fail**, because `fxEffect` is computed as a plug (`totalReturn − (income + price + unattributed)`) and the code comment says so. It was replaced with falsifiable before/after assertions, the load-bearing one being **`FX effect` unchanged to the cent** — the one number that moves if a `base_amount` copy is wrong. The other four were the same shape: the category guard, the provenance store, the undo guard and the scope were each *asserted* where they needed to be *constructed*. Pass 2 returned **GO**. **Then running it found two more things neither review caught:** Cash Flow **excludes** transfer categories by default (`transfers = 'exclude'`), so the restated income leaves the cash account's P&L **entirely** rather than being relabelled — rev 2 and pass 1 both had this backwards — and the double-book guard returned *"only income can be booked at source"* instead of a 409, because booking rewrites the row's own category. **Verified on a prod copy:** flows and income moved by exactly ∓5,172,998.27 PLN / ∓1,340,251.54 USD, MVs and `FX effect` byte-identical, holding book values unchanged in both currencies, the balance-sheet and cash-flow reports byte-identical to prod, all 8 UB marks untouched, forecast re-base identical, round-trip clean, undo **refuses** an edited leg, and Transfer Analysis auto-matched 2/2. Owner walked the flow on dev and booked 2 of the 7 rows. **494 backend** (22 new) **/ 195 frontend / build ✓ / six guards ✓.** Deferred with reasons: CVC (cross-currency — USD rows, EUR funds), `Other Investments` (mis-signed, not mis-placed), and `Transfer - Historical` (2,741 rows — a different shape entirely: **not** balance-neutral, and because unrecorded distributions were absorbed by later marks, each added leg needs a paired mark adjustment).
  - *Fix — the Ledger category filter was blind past its first page.* Its options were derived from the **loaded** rows (`TRANSACTION_BATCH_SIZE = 500`), and the filter itself ran client-side over that same page. On PKO (4,572 rows) it could only ever offer categories from the most recent ~11%: `Financial Income - UB Dividend` sits at position **532**, so four of the five United Beverages dividends were **unreachable through the UI** — found while walking CR057. Both halves now go through the server: new `GET /api/v2/transactions/categories` (distinct categories on the account for the selected period, no limit) and the category choice is passed to the API. Pre-existing — the old `<select>` had the identical limitation; the new type-to-filter combobox (`SearchableSelect`, tokens-only, so both themes come free where a native `<datalist>` popup would have rendered light in dark mode) merely made it visible. 5 new repo tests.
  - *Fix — the blocking lint gate had been red on `main` since v3.4.8*, and not for a real defect. `eslint-plugin-react` is deliberately not installed, so `react/jsx-uses-vars` never runs and an identifier used only inside JSX looks unused; capitalized **vars** were exempted for exactly that reason, but capitalized **args** were not — so `({ icon: Icon }) => <Icon />` in `MobileTabBar.jsx` was a false-positive **error**. It also made `check-lint-debt.sh` abort with no output and exit 1 (`set -e` + `pipefail` on a non-zero eslint), which read as a debt-ratchet failure. `argsIgnorePattern` now follows the same convention as `varsIgnorePattern`; debt re-baselined 35 → 34.
- **v3.5.0** (2026-07-27) — **minor: [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md) Investment Returns report.** New `/investment-returns` page (Reports & Graphs → Reports) + `GET /api/v2/reports/investment-returns`, additive — no existing endpoint's output changes. Pick an **account** (a parent rolls up its descendants), a **period**, and one of four **intervals** (month / quarter / year / **between valuations**) → realized income and unrealized G/L per period, absolute in **USD ⇄ original currency**, and as **realized / unrealized / total %** on average capital `(open+close)/2`. **Ledger-derived by necessity:** the CR019/CR022 securities tables are **0 rows** in prod, so the numerator is the ledger's own `Unrealized G/L` postings plus auto-derived income categories, with market value on the Balance Sheet's additive basis. **The design was wrong more often than the code was.** A pass-1 technical review returned *revise* with 6 blocking findings — every one reproduced against prod before acceptance — the worst being that the reconciliation identity was **asserted rather than constructed**: category 206 and NULL-category rows fell into no bucket, so Fidelity IRA 2025 was silently off by **$8,781.87** (19.5% of the reported gain). Reading the underlying rows then reversed a decision I had recommended myself: `Transfer - Securities Trades` legs pair exactly, so the *unpaired remainder* is real money — Fidelity Stocks 2020 carries **+$200,460.86** of `Fid Bkg Svc` ACH deposits that the original design would have reported as investment gain. A pass-2 PM sign-off returned *revise on scope* (P1/P2 split) and caught two wrong assertions in the CR's own test plan. Then the owner opened the page and four more rules fell: it **rendered in two columns** because `.page-main` is a hard-coded 2-column grid and the page never opted out (five pages now carry a class whose only job is to undo that — logged at [§4.1 item 7](#41-dry-violations-remaining)); Modified Dietz gave way to **average capital** with **three** return rows; the coverage rule was **incoherent** — the table printed United Beverages' calendar-2020 `Unrealized G/L` of +1,151,997 while suppressing the percentage that same posting implies, so a period is now covered when a valuation falls *inside* it, with misdating disclosed via **†** instead of hidden; and a fourth interval, **`marks`**, lays columns out between valuations, which is correct by construction and turned UB's entirely blank calendar grid into a real **+138.70%** series (13.74% annualized). Buckets are now **exhaustive**, and the identity was verified on prod data to close to **0.0000 in every interval** for single accounts, roll-ups and LC mode. **It also works as a data-quality instrument** — the `Unattributed` row and the income breakdown surfaced the $200K of misfiled deposits plus `Car Purchase/Sale −$29,954` sitting on a brokerage account. **470 backend** (28 new pure-arithmetic + 10 new route-contract) **/ 195 frontend / build ✓ / five content guards ✓.** No migration, no new secret. *Known limitation at ship, since resolved:* United Beverages' realized % shipped as 0.00% because its dividends posted to **PKO** rather than the holding — fixed at the source by [CR057](../cr/cr-057-book-income-at-source.md), with no change needed here. *Not clicked live in dark mode.*
- **v3.4.7** (2026-07-23) — **patch: COA add-modal hint spans use `--ink-secondary` instead of literal `#4A5568` (greens CI, fixes dark-mode).** The v3.4.6 add-account modal added three hint spans ("Inherited from the parent category" ×2, "(optional)") with a naked `#4A5568`, which tripped the `check-inline-hex` CI guard (COAEditModal 14→17) and — being a frozen light-palette literal — was the exact CR042 T6 dark-mode defect. `--ink-secondary` **is** `#4A5568` in light mode (pixel-identical) and themes to `#AEB4BB` in dark. No other change. Lesson logged: run the full `frontend-build` guard suite (`lint:*` + `npm test`), not just `eslint`/`build`, before deploying. No migration.
- **v3.4.6** (2026-07-23) — **patch: Chart of Accounts Type filter uses real account types, not "Category"; + migration 040 data fix (owner-found).** The COA Type filter listed **"Category"** as a selectable type and, when you picked a real type (e.g. *Expense*), matched on the tree's `type` — so category **containers** (which display `Category`) were filtered out, leaving expense leaves indented under nothing. Now `typeOptions` is built from the real `account_type` (**Asset / Liability / Income / Expense**, no "Category") and `filteredRows` matches on `accountType`, so a type filter also surfaces that type's containers — the hierarchy comes back. Leans on v3.4.5's `accountType`/`accountCurrency` row fields (`getTraitsMap` carries the true type for containers too). The edit modal keeps a fallback `<option>` so editing a container still shows "Category" and a legacy `Unspecified` leaf still displays. **The filter exposed a pre-existing data bug**, fixed durably in **[migration 040](../current/migrations.md)** (a one-time data correction — the current seed already types these right, so it's legacy data, not a seed bug): five accounts were on the wrong side of their (correct) `section` — `Income` container `expense→income`; `Liabilities`/`Tax Liabilities`/`Tax Reserve - PL`/`Tax Reserve - US` `asset→liability`. **Verified report-neutral before applying:** Balance Sheet sides come from `getNestedTree({section})` not `account_type`; the only forecast `account_type` branch (`isLiability` → sign of `ExpensePct` in `fcbuilder-module.js`) operates on a value `index.js` hardcodes to `0`, so the four forecast modules on `Tax Liabilities` rebuild byte-identical; Budget/Transaction reports use `account_type` only in `GROUP BY`/`ORDER BY`. Applied + ledgered to **dev** and **click-verified** on the dev HMR server (filter shows Asset/Liability/Income/Expense, Expense surfaces its containers, Income/Liability now sorted correctly); prod applied via the deploy runner (Step 2b). Frontend-only code (`COAManagement.jsx`, `COAEditModal.jsx`) + one data migration; lint 0 errors, `vite build` passes.
- **v3.4.5** (2026-07-22) — **patch: Chart of Accounts add-child dialog inherits the parent's type/currency; Account # marked optional (owner-found).** Clicking **+** on a category to add a child opened a dialog whose **Type** dropdown defaulted to the first type alphabetically and let you pick any of Asset/Liability/Income/Expense — but the backend (`server/src/v2/routes/util/coa.js`) **ignores the submitted type entirely** and always inherits `account_type`/`section` from the parent, so the choice was silently discarded (you could pick *Asset* under an *Expense* category and get an Expense). The dialog now **locks Type to the parent's real `account_type`**, rendered read-only ("Inherited from the parent category"). The wrinkle: a container displays `Category`/`—` in the tree, but its true `account_type`/`currency` live in the traits map (`getTraitsMap` returns them for **every** account, containers included — confirmed `Purchases` = `expense`/`USD` in prod), so `buildCoaRows` now carries `accountType`/`accountCurrency` and both the **+** path (`openAddModal`) and the toolbar picker (`handleQuickAddParentChange`, matched by full path) seed them. For **P&L (Income/Expense)** children the account-level **Currency** is display-only — the Cash Flow/P&L report sums the **transaction's** currency (`fetchCategoryTotals` → `t.amount`/`t.base_amount`), never the account's — so it is shown **read-only** too (owner-chosen over hiding it); **Asset/Liability** children keep an **editable** currency (a container legitimately holds mixed currencies), now defaulted to the parent's. **Account #** relabelled "*(optional)*" (server-side it was always `|| null`). **Frontend-only** (`COAManagement.jsx`, `COAEditModal.jsx`); no backend change, no migration. The **edit** path — and its `write-paths.spec.js` Type-change e2e — is untouched. Lint 0 errors, `vite build` passes. **Not** clicked live before deploy — exercise the **+** button on `/coa-management`.
- **v3.4.4** (2026-07-22) — **patch: `PeriodSelector` gains an "All" (full-history) preset.** The shared period picker offered no way to select "all time" — the widest preset was This/Last Year, and reaching the full ledger meant dropping into Custom and hand-setting from-year → to-year. Added an **"All"** preset (between *Last Year* and *Custom*) that computes the full-history span **Jan `EARLIEST_ACTUAL_YEAR` (2000) → Dec current year**. Because "all time" is only representable where a **multi-year range** exists, it is flagged `yearRangeOnly` and rendered **only where `enableYearRange` is on** — the 4 range-capable consumers (Actuals, Ledger, Budget transactions, Balance Trends); the other 9 single-year consumers are untouched, so no report that can't express a span shows a misleading button. Preset dispatch now honours a preset-supplied `toYear` (only "All" uses it — every other preset stays single-year, `toYear === actualYear`, byte-identical behaviour); the summary line renders both endpoints for a multi-year span (`January 2000–December 2026`) and `buildSummaryText` now respects `hideBudgetYear` so the wide span doesn't spuriously append a `(budget: …)` note on Actuals/Ledger. Downstream unchanged — it feeds the existing `year`/`toYear` filter, so each report already turns it into a `2000-01-01 → 2026-12-31` date range. Lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing react-refresh warnings only). No backend change, no migration. Frontend-only; **not** clicked live before deploy — exercise the "All" chip on `/transactions/actuals`.
- **v3.4.3** (2026-07-21) — **patch: [CR055](../cr/cr-055-category-suggest-backoff.md) — category-suggest progressive backoff.** The "Suggest categories" merchant key was a fixed leading **3 tokens**; any merchant whose 3rd token is a per-transaction id fragmented into a distinct key per transaction. US card feeds are the failure case: `AMAZON MKTPL*1Q8RX4GZ3`, `*F40KK8XY3 [SALE]` … each order id survives digit-stripping as leftover letters (`amazon mktpl rx` / `kk` / …), so **177 categorized Amazon rows shattered into ~177 keys and none reached the 2-example floor** — Amazon was never suggested despite ample history. Fix: key at **3→2→1 leading tokens** (`merchantKey` → `merchantTokens` + `keyCandidates`), index history at all three levels, and per target take the category from the **most-specific level that clears the unchanged ≥2-sample / >50%-majority bar** — so the narrow key wins where its 3rd token means something and we widen only on a miss (Amazon → `amazon mktpl`, 162 samples, 92%). Added `sale`/`return`/`refund` to the stop list (US `[…]` disposition tags). Guards untouched: coverage widened, precision not loosened. Verified read-only against the **37,124-row prod corpus** (Marshalls/Publix/HL Supermarket now resolve; Target/McDonald's/7-Eleven correctly abstain — genuinely split history); 7 new pure unit tests green. No backend contract change, no migration. **Not** clicked live end-to-end — algorithm verified offline + by tests; exercise "Suggest categories" on the next real feed batch.
- **v3.4.2** (2026-07-21) — **patch: [CR054](../cr/cr-054-cash-flow-by-account.md) drill-down category-filter fix (owner-found).** After v3.4.1 fixed the account filter, the drill-down still ignored the **category** filter: with a category subset selected, double-clicking a row listed categories outside the selection. Cause — the filtered report keeps the full P&L tree (unselected categories total 0 and are hidden client-side), so `collectLeafCategories(node)` returned every leaf under the node. Fix (frontend-only): the report snapshots the category filter at Generate and the drill-down intersects the node's leaves with it before querying; empty filter ⇒ no restriction, so Summary/By-Period are unchanged. Lint + 195 frontend tests + build green. No backend change, no migration.
- **v3.4.1** (2026-07-21) — **patch: [CR054](../cr/cr-054-cash-flow-by-account.md) drill-down fix (owner-found on first click).** Double-clicking a cell in the By-Account report opened a transaction modal that (1) ignored the account filter — `fetchCashFlowTransactions` passed only the category, so the modal listed that category's transactions across **every** account (Fidelity rows under a PKO-only report) — and (2) preferred `BaseAmount` (USD) formatted with the report's symbol, so USD figures read "PLN …". Fix: `GET /cash-flow/transactions` + `getCashFlowTransactions` accept a repeatable `accounts` filter (`AND a.name = ANY(...)`); the report snapshots the accounts + currency mode used at Generate and threads them to the drill-down; in Original mode the transaction list formats each row in its **own** `Currency` (native `Amount`) and the Summarize panel totals native amounts with the report's currency-aware formatter. USD mode byte-identical. New route-contract test asserts every drill-down row is on the filtered account; 13 reports-route + 195 frontend tests + all CI guards green. No migration.
- **v3.4.0** (2026-07-21) — **minor: Cash Flow "By Account" report ([CR054](../cr/cr-054-cash-flow-by-account.md)).** A third Cash Flow tab (`/cash-flow/by-account`) answering "what did *this* account (or set of accounts) spend by category over these periods, and in whose currency?" — previously the report summed **every** account in **USD** only, so a PLN/EUR account's flows were never visible in their own currency. Same By-Period period-column layout (category tree as rows) plus the Budget-Worksheet **category + account filter chips** (`HierarchyFilter`; the Income/Expense/Transfers + per-account-type group derivation was **extracted from `BudgetWorksheetV2` into a shared `hierarchyFilterGroups.js`** so both screens build the chips identically) and a **USD ⇄ original-currency** toggle. Backend is **additive and backward-compatible**: `buildCashFlowReport`/`fetchCategoryTotals` and `GET /cash-flow` gained optional `category`/`accounts` name filters (`LEFT JOIN accounts a … a.name IN (…)`, mirroring `budget.getSummary`) and `currency=usd|original` (sums `t.amount` vs `t.base_amount`), returning `meta:{currency,currencies[]}`; **absent params ⇒ byte-identical output**, so Summary/By-Period are untouched. Because rows are categories, an *original* total spanning accounts of different currencies is not a real number — the table formats with the native symbol only when the fetched transactions are single-currency, otherwise a plain decimal, and shows a **warning banner** on a mixed-currency Original total ("filter Accounts to a single currency to total meaningfully"); `CashFlowReport` took an optional `currencyCode` prop threaded through the recursive renderer (default `"USD"` ⇒ existing tabs unchanged). **12 reports-route tests** (2 new: filter+currency⇒`meta.currency='original'`; default⇒`'usd'`) green against the dev DB, **195 frontend tests**, **4 CI guards** (dead-tokens/inline-hex/button-css/modal-adoption), lint 0 errors, build passes; **USD-vs-original parity checked on real dev-DB data** (*Kasia Spending* $41,686 USD vs zł151,777 PLN). **No migration.** *Caveat:* compiles/lints/tests clean but **not clicked in a live browser** before deploy — exercise `/cash-flow/by-account` in prod. *Later polish (optional):* per-currency subtotal split in Original mode instead of a warning.
- **v3.3.0** (2026-07-16) — **minor: Forecast Auto-Adjust — solve spend-down to fund the plan ([CR053](../cr/cr-053-forecast-auto-adjust-spend-to-fund.md)).** When the cash sweep runs out of assets to sell, the owner can now pick a **set** of expense lines and have the system solve the **least uniform % cut** that keeps every forecast year at/above the low cash band — the answer to "how much must I cut spending to make this plan fundable?", previously only reachable by manual edit-regenerate-read-repeat. It is a **numerical solve, not a formula**: cutting spend feeds back through the sweep (fewer forced sales ⇒ less capital-gains tax ⇒ more cash, CR049), so a closed form is impossible. The server deep-copies the target into a **standalone scratch** scenario (a throwaway *variant* is impossible — a variant-of-a-variant is trigger-rejected, and a variant is force-synced at `generateForecast` Step 0, clobbering any factor written), **threshold-searches** the retained fraction (`expense = base × retain`, so one factor scales every year uniformly) over ~10 audit-suppressed builds until the engine's own persisted `Cash Shortfall` clears — the objective is read server-side from the engine's output, **never the client `fcWarnings` util** (that would re-arm the CR045/CR049 hand-copied-logic drift). The solve runs as an in-memory async job (poll), and the scratch is torn down (DB rows cascade; assumptions-doc rows pruned). **Apply** persists the cut as a **CR050 override** (`expense_amount = effective × retain`) on a variant — a *base* target gets a new "*… — reduced spend*" variant so the base is never mutated — then a **verification rebuild** re-reads the shortfall to prove the real scenario is funded (the scratch number is not trusted). New service `forecastAutoAdjust.js` + 4 routes (`/auto-adjust/lines|solve|solve/:jobId|apply`), `generateForecast(name,{writeAudit})`, and `FCAutoAdjustModal` launched from a button in the Cash Health panel. **Senior-review-corrected** before build (the throwaway-variant scratch and the client-util objective were both caught and rejected on paper). **9 new backend tests** (pure logic + validation + job registry), 117 forecast/variant suites green, frontend lint 0 errors / 0 new warnings, build passes. **Verified end-to-end on dev** (both solve/apply paths over real HTTP, clean scratch teardown): "2026 Downside (variant)" ($451K single-year final-year shortfall) solves to a **~2% uniform cut** (monotone curve $451K→$182K→$47K→$0 over 0→2%), applied as a variant leaving the base untouched and verified funded. **No migration.** *Open Phase-0 refinement (owner):* whether the line picker excludes expense items carrying dated `Changes` (a single `base_value` factor won't scale those steps) or scales them too.
- **v3.2.0** (2026-07-15) — **minor: foreign-currency expense lines ([CR051](../cr/cr-051-forecast-expense-currency.md)).** A forecast expense line can now be **USD/EUR/PLN** instead of always USD — so spend that is really in złoty/euro has its USD cost float with the FX assumptions, the largest FX lever on the plan and previously invisible. The rails already existed (the `currency` column, the engine's inflate-in-native-then-convert-per-year path, Modules' picker); the Expenditures modal never exposed them and the CRUD hook **hard-pinned `Currency:"USD"` in three places** (the real reason every line read USD). Now: a USD/EUR/PLN selector (**expense-only** — foreign income can't express CR047's income-tax override; **disabled while Matched** — matched lines are USD-anchored); the native amount is entered and **`base_value_usd` is derived server-side** at the scenario's base-year FX (client value ignored so it can't rot; **400** if the scenario has no FX rate for that currency); the table shows the native amount under the USD. Plus the **F1 engine fix** — the income/expense builder now **fails loud** on a missing/zero FX rate for a currency in use (it would otherwise divide by 1.0 → silent ~4× overstatement, or by 0 → Infinity; latent until a non-USD line could exist, which this CR enables). Owner-ratified design: **split-into-lines** for partial exposure (not a per-line mix), **single scenario inflation** applied in native then converted, **native is source-of-truth**. **407 backend** (13 new: engine conversion + guard, `baseYearFxRate` helper, DB route derivation, a CR050 currency-override), **195 frontend**, **7/7 Playwright** incl. a new browser round-trip (add PLN → USD derives → survives save+reopen); lint 0 errors. **No migration; no engine behavior change for USD scenarios.** Known modelling caveat (documented, not a bug): holding FX flat while inflating a PLN line at the US rate implicitly assumes real złoty appreciation — trend the FX-PLN assumption weaker to model higher PL inflation.
- **v3.1.0** (2026-07-14) — **minor milestone: forecast scenario VARIANTS ([CR050](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md)) are in production, plus the Expenses draft-add.** Rolls up the CR050 line (v3.0.108 → this) into a minor: a scenario can be a **variant** that inherits every item from its base unless overridden — JSONB patch keyed to the base row's id, materialized into real rows so the engine/Review/Compare are unchanged; overrides captured at write time across modules, income/expense, schedules, **and** assumptions (FX/inflation/tax/sweep band); the Scenarios-page panel *is* the variant's definition, revertible per field. The five post-ship fixes (v3.0.110–112) were all in how edits are **captured and reported** as overrides — a DATE compared as an instant, float noise, missing schedules, and the assumption-editing UI bypassing the override system (a silent-data-loss bug) — never in the inheritance machinery. **This release also** makes **"+ Add" on Forecast Expenditures open a draft in the edit modal** (titled "New Forecast Entry"), matching Modules: nothing is written until Save, so a mis-click no longer leaves a blank "All" row — the old immediate-POST handler is deleted. 394/394 backend, 195/195 frontend. **Still open (owner):** the `adopt-variant` decision on "2026 Downside" (22 diffs, 8 of them rotted `base_value_usd` values rather than assumptions).
- **v3.0.112** (2026-07-14) — **[CR050](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md): FX / inflation / tax edits on a variant were invisible AND erased by sync (data loss).** The inflation/FX/tax tables on the Scenarios page write the whole assumptions **document** directly, and the sweep band writes the scenario **row** directly — both predate variants and bypass the override system. On a variant that was broken two ways: the edit never showed in the panel, and the next `syncAssumptions` rewrote the variant's assumptions **from its base, silently erasing it**. *Confirmed on prod: "2026 Downside" carried a 2027 FX row (PLN 4.5 / EUR 0.9) that the next page view would have wiped.* Fix: capture the edit as an **assumption override at write time**, where intent is unambiguous (the user saved) — `PUT /assumptions` and the sweep-band `PUT /scenarios/:id` now reconcile each touched variant against its base (diff → set/clear overrides → sync). Inferring intent at *sync* time is impossible: a variant's inherited-but-stale value is indistinguishable from a deliberate one. `diffAssumptions` also no longer reports a phantom Tax Rate diff when the base has no tax entry. Display: the overrides endpoint now carries the base's assumption value and the panel renders it readably — **"FX rates: 2026: EUR 0.86 / PLN 3.9 → 2026 … · 2027: EUR 0.9 / PLN 4.5"** — instead of "value: 2 entries". +1 test: an FX edit on a variant survives a subsequent sync. 394/394 backend, 195/195 frontend. *This is the fourth defect in the same family — all in how edits are **captured and reported** as overrides, never in the inheritance machinery, which has been correct since the first build.*
- **v3.0.111** (2026-07-14) — **[CR050](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md): float noise counted as a change, and the panel hid the number that changed.** Two defects the owner found on one override. **(1)** *"Market Value (USD): 4175595 → 4175595".* The edit form derives `market_value_usd` by dividing a local-currency amount by an FX rate, so it arrives as **4175594.9999999995**. The column is `numeric(15,2)` — **once stored it IS 4175595.00**, identical to the base. The comparison was against the raw float rather than the value *as the column would hold it*, so an unchanged field became an override that said 4175595 → 4175595. Values are now compared **and stored** at the column's own scale, read from `information_schema` (never hand-listed, so a migration that changes a scale is followed automatically); the prune heals the noisy patches already written. **(2)** *"Yield Spread schedule: — → 1 entry"*, when the truth was **-0.5% → 1%**. The panel read the base's values off the modules **LIST** payload, which carries no schedules — so for the one kind of override where the number *is* the change, it showed a dash and an entry count. *"1 entry → 1 entry" is not a report, it is a shrug.* `GET /scenarios/:id/overrides` now returns each base row **with its schedules**, and the panel renders the values: **"2027: -0.5% → 2027: 1%"**. +1 test: float noise is not a change, but a change at the column's scale still is. 393/393 backend, 195/195 frontend.
- **v3.0.110** (2026-07-14) — **[CR050](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md): one change showed as three — a DATE compared as an instant.** The owner changed **one** field on a variant (Growth 1.0 → 2.0) and the overrides panel reported **three**: `base_date` and `income_pct` as well, both **byte-identical to the base**. The module edit form sends `BaseDate` as `new Date(x).toISOString()` — **UTC** midnight — while node-postgres parses the `DATE` column into a JS `Date` at **LOCAL** midnight: same calendar day, different epoch, so the equality check called it a change and every save on a variant wrote a phantom `base_date` override (and, through its `effective_date`, a phantom `income_pct` one). *An override that means nothing is worse than no override — it claims the owner pinned a value they never touched, and the panel is meant to BE the variant's definition.* These columns are calendar days with no time and no zone, so they are now compared as **days** (reading a `Date`'s LOCAL components, the zone node-postgres built it in). Sync additionally **PRUNES** patch keys that no longer differ from the base — which self-heals the phantoms already written to prod's "2026 Upside", and also catches the case where the *base* later changes to match an override, which no write path would notice. Panel, per owner: the revert control is an **X** (the circular arrow read as "refresh"), and each override renders as a table — **Field | \<base name\> | This variant** — labelled with the name from the edit form ("Growth (x Inflation)") rather than the column (`growth_rate`). +2 DB-backed tests: a **no-op save writes NO override**; sync prunes a key equal to base. 392/392 backend, 195/195 frontend.
- **v3.0.109** (2026-07-14) — **"Generate both" on `/forecast-compare`.** Compare diffs two `/entries` payloads, so a scenario that was never generated — or was edited since it last was — reads as *"only in the other one"*. That looks like a finding and isn't: with "2026 Upside" ungenerated, Compare listed **every account in the model** as "Only in 2026 Base". Review has a Generate for one scenario; Compare needs both, or you are comparing a fresh forecast against a stale one. Sequential, not parallel (the engine takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(scenario_id)` and rebuilds in one transaction, so firing both together buys nothing and makes a failure harder to attribute); the result line names each scenario and its entry count. Verified through the UI: emptied "2026 Downside" on dev to reproduce the reported state, clicked the button, and the view refreshed to a real diff.
- **v3.0.108** (2026-07-14) — **[CR050](../cr/cr-050-forecast-scenario-variants.md): forecast scenario VARIANTS — a variant inherits everything from its base unless overridden** (migration 039). A scenario copy was a severed island: 30 entities duplicated to change one field, nothing recording *which* field made it a downside, and the copies then rotted (fix a cost basis in Base and "2026 Upside" kept the wrong one). Worse, the hand-enumerated copy had silently dropped a column **twice** — `cash_sweep_priority` ([CR045](../cr/cr-045-forecast-cash-warnings-liquidation.md) §1, −$3.35M of shortfall left unfunded) and the entire assumptions slice ([CR048](../cr/cr-048-model-review-fixes.md), copies ran at 0% inflation). Overrides are now a **JSONB patch keyed to the base row's id** — *field-level*, so pinning Fidelity's growth still lets a later fix to its income yield flow through, and a patch rather than a nullable mirror column because **`NULL` is already load-bearing** in these columns (NULL `tax_rate_override` = fall back; NULL `cash_sweep_priority` = never liquidate), so "NULL = inherit" was never available. A **sync step materializes base ⊕ overrides into real rows**, so the engine, Review, Compare, AI review and the audit CSVs read an ordinary scenario and **do not change**; a read-time overlay was rejected because reads are *not* funneled (engine loaders, repo finders and `crud.getBaseYearValues` are three separate paths — the CR049 drift, re-armed). Sync is **lazy** (variant reads, override writes, and unconditionally at build), never fanned out from a base write: a variant must not be able to fail an edit to its base. Its column list comes from **`information_schema`**, never a hand-written list — the direct fix for the drop-a-column bug class. Writes on a variant's inherited rows are intercepted into overrides (at the *repository*, so `PATCH /modules/bulk-update` is covered too); a delete becomes a **tombstone**; `crud.refreshModulesFromActuals` — a set-based UPDATE the next sync would erase — is **refused** on a variant. Also lands the forecast API's **first real scenario-create route** (creation was a side-effect of `PUT /assumptions`). UI: Inherited · Overridden · Local badges, and a panel on `/forecast-scenarios` that *is* the variant's definition, with a revert per field. **Verified:** a zero-override variant of "2026 Base" builds **1,426 entries byte-identical to its base** (zero rows differing, totals to the cent); override Fidelity's growth to 0.5% and it pins while the base's *other* changes still carry (Fidelity Stock 2055: $3.98M base vs $1.96M variant). 16 new DB-backed tests, 390/390 backend, 195/195 frontend, all CI guards green. *The tests earned their keep:* the sweep-priority partial unique index tripped **transiently** mid-upsert — a valid final state reached through an invalid intermediate one — so sync now parks the ranked flags before rewriting. Dormant for existing scenarios: **no parent ⇒ byte-for-byte today's behavior**, no backfill.
- **v3.0.102 → v3.0.104** (2026-07-13) — **[CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md) CLOSES** (Phases 0–3; only long-horizon Phase 4 remains). Three items, and two of them found bugs.

  **The lint gate is BLOCKING** (advisory since 2026-06-12, when it stood at 160 errors). Errors are now **zero** — and the 7 that were left were all real: a `useMemo` below an early `return <Navigate/>` in CategoryLandingPage; **FCExpModal**'s `if (!isOpen) return null` sitting *above* its only hook while the parent mounts it unconditionally (0 hooks closed, 1 open, same fiber — it did not happen to crash, but "does not crash today" is not a contract); **FCReviewTable** reading `tableRef.current.scrollWidth` **during render** (null on first render, unsafe under concurrent rendering — and the `useLayoutEffect` below already measured it before paint, so the fallback could only ever supply a stale value); an accumulator mutated inside FCCompareTable's render loop. The gate blocks on everything that catches a **bug** — `no-undef`, `no-unused-vars`, rules-of-hooks, refs-during-render, render-mutation, the `toISOString` guard. The 56 remaining warnings (`set-state-in-effect` 35, `react-refresh` 21) are **debt, not breakage**, and are **ratcheted** by a new `check-lint-debt.sh` that fails CI if either count grows — *"it's only a warning" is how a count grows back*. Down payment: **BudgetRealization was re-seeding its collapsed rows from an effect** whenever `collapsiblePaths` changed — and that recomputes when the **filters** change, not just the data, so toggling "include transfers" silently slammed shut every row you had opened. State inverted: it now tracks what you **expanded**, and "all collapsed" is derived rather than re-imposed.

  **N8 — one response envelope, migrated with no flag day.** The v2 API had grown **two** success conventions (63 handlers `{data}`, 27 bare). A caller had to *know* which, and guessing wrong fails **silently** — `undefined.map` never runs, the page just renders empty. That is exactly how Modify Transfer broke (`GET /forecast/modules` bare, `GET /forecast/modules/:id` enveloped). Fixed in the only safe order: `Rest.unwrap()` accepts **both** shapes (4 tests pin the edges, incl. `{data: null}` staying `null`), **every consumer was routed through it first**, and only then were the endpoints enveloped. That order mattered — `FCReview` did `Array.isArray(res) ? res : []`, so enveloping without touching that line would have rendered **zero forecast modules**. The route-contract test that *pinned* the bare shape caught a consumer I had missed. The remaining 27 bare endpoints are **not** converted (a flag day across 11 more files for no gain today) — they are ratcheted by `check-api-envelope.sh`.

  **util.js — and the deferral's reasoning was half right.** CR043 §2.2 proposed extracting a COA *service*. That was **moot**: the COA handlers contain **no SQL** — they already go through the accounts repository. The missing thing was **tests, not layers**: these 13 endpoints had **none**, including the COA add/update/delete writes the Chart of Accounts page depends on. So tests came first — **and immediately found a real bug**: `POST /coa/update` destructured `type` and **never used it**, so changing an account's type returned **200**, echoed the *old* type back, and changed nothing. The same silent-drop class as CR046's window dates and CR047's tax override, and it had been live for months. Proven on dev before fixing: in one request `currency` applied and `account_type` did not — the request *half*-worked, which is why nobody noticed. Then the split: **651 → 32 lines**, four cohesive routers, every URL unchanged, five in-handler `require()`s hoisted (N13).

  Tests **374 backend** (+13) / **195 frontend** (+4). **Six** blocking CI guards now (buttons, modals, inline-hex, dead-tokens, lint-debt, api-envelope).

- **v3.0.101** (2026-07-13) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) CLOSES.** The last three IA items, settled with the owner — and two of the CR's *own* proposals were rejected on inspection, which is the interesting part.

  **The calibration pages stay under Transactions.** CR042 said "move calibration under Settings". That assumed they were configuration. They are not: *"calibrate non-fed account balances by entering the current balance by hand"* is **recurring work**, and Settings is where things go to be configured once and forgotten. Moving it there would have satisfied the ≤8 nav target and made the app worse — the target exists to reduce **noise**, not to hide **work**. What *was* misfiled: **Bank Feed Setup** (a page literally named Setup, sitting in the daily Transactions list) and **Forecast Settings**. Both moved.

  **The Forecast sidebar mirrors the stepper; it is not collapsed.** The proposal was to collapse the six Forecast steps into a single sidebar entry, since `FCStepNav` already lists them. Rejected: Forecast is the busiest area of the app, and collapsing turns one click into two on every visit. The redundancy was never the defect — the **inconsistency** was: two hand-kept lists of the same six pages, in different orders, under different names (`FC Mapping` in the stepper vs `Income & Expense Mapping` in the sidebar — exactly the developer vocabulary T2 opened by objecting to). `FCStepNav` now **derives** its steps from `routes.jsx` (new `step` / `stepLabel` fields), so the two cannot diverge again, and the sidebar renders the same numbers. The number lives in a dedicated `step` field rather than in `label`, because `label` also feeds breadcrumbs and ⌘K, where a step number is noise. **Route paths unchanged — bookmarks survive.**

  **Creating a module no longer writes before you say so.** v3.0.97's auto-open-the-editor change POSTed first and opened second, so **Cancel left a blank, nameless module behind** — a real row the engine iterates over. "New" now opens the editor on an unsaved **draft** (no `id`); `saveModule()` POSTs when there is no id and PUTs otherwise. The alternative — *delete it again if it still looks untouched* — was rejected: it gates a **destructive** action on a pristine-check that rots the first time a field is added to the editor and left out of the check. Verified end-to-end against dev before shipping (the editor's exact payload + `Scenario` POSTs **201**, so it passes the N10 write validation rather than 400ing on Create) — that check is the lesson from v3.0.98, where a fix went out on a code-read and a green unit test on an unreachable path.

  Also settled: **"Upload PS" — keep, do not delete** (already under Data Sources; retained as a PocketSmith-CSV escape hatch).

- **v3.0.98 → v3.0.100** (2026-07-13) — **The owner re-tested v3.0.97's five fixes one at a time. The first was still broken — and that is how we found that Modify Transfer had never worked at all.**

  **The lesson is worth more than the fix.** v3.0.97 changed how the modal *matches* a transfer to a year (Periodic rows span `Date`→`DateEnd`; it was matching only the year stored on the row). That was a genuine latent bug and the change is correct. But the modal fetched `GET /forecast/modules?scenario=…` — the **list** endpoint — and **the list does not return `Invest`/`Dispose`/`IncomePct` at all**; only `GET /modules/:id` joins the three child tables. So `moduleData.Invest` was **always `undefined`**, `pick(undefined)` returned `[]`, and every year of every module reported *"no transfers for this year"*. The modal had **never once displayed a transfer**, for any module, in its entire existence — while the Review sat directly behind it rendering the transfer it was denying.

  The year-matching fix sat **downstream** of a bug that meant no data ever arrived. It was diagnosed by reading code and shipped without an end-to-end check, and **its unit test passed the whole time** — because it tested a predicate that was never reached with real data. A green unit test on an unreached code path is worse than no test: it buys false confidence. The replacement is a component test that goes through the actual fetch (asserts `Rest.get` is called for the full module, and that a periodic row renders in a **middle** year of its range) and **fails on the pre-change code**. Fixed by resolving Name → id from the list, then fetching the full module — the same call the module editor already made.

  **Two more, both surfaced by the same re-test loop:**

  **`_cash_sweep`'s amount still opened a transfer editor.** The breakdown row has two click targets: the module **name** (→ audit trail, fixed in v3.0.97) and the **amount** (→ Modify Transfer). `_cash_sweep` is *synthetic* — the engine invents it to attribute swept cash, it has no `forecast_modules` row — so the editor looked it up by name, found nothing, and rendered "Module not found": an editor offered on something uneditable. Its amount now carries no edit affordance and its tooltip points at the audit trail. Guarded by the `_` prefix, so any future synthetic module inherits the behaviour.

  **Closing any modal scrolled the page away from where you were** — after Save Changes, the Review jumped to the bottom, losing the cell just edited. Radix restores focus on close to whatever was focused before the dialog opened; these dialogs open on a **double-click**, which focuses nothing, so the restore target is arbitrary and the browser scrolls it into view (twice here — breakdown + adjust transfer stack, `data-scroll-locked="2"`). `onCloseAutoFocus` is now prevented in the `<Modal>` primitive, so **every dialog in the app** is fixed at once. The focus trap on *open*, which is where the a11y value is, is untouched.

  All five of v3.0.97's fixes re-tested and passed. Frontend tests 188 → 191.

- **v3.0.97** (2026-07-13) — **Owner acceptance pass on v3.0.96: all six test steps PASS, and walking the UI found five bugs that were already there.**

  Worth recording *because* none of the five was a regression from the release under test — the migration to `<Modal>`, the report consolidation, the 63 token remaps and the self-hosted font all came through clean. What a human clicking through found were older, quieter defects that no test was ever going to catch.

  **Periodic transfers were invisible in Modify Transfer.** Clicking a Transfer amount in the Review breakdown opened a modal that said *"no invest/dispose transfers for this year"* — for a transfer the Review was, at that moment, displaying. The engine (`fcbuilder-module.js`) expands a **Periodic** row across every year from `Date` to `DateEnd`; the modal matched only on the year **stored on the row**. So a periodic transfer was editable in its first year and invisible in every year after (OCME Sp. z o.o., 100,000 PLN/yr 2026→2030: entries in five years, editable in one). The rule now lives in `features/Forecast/utils/transferYear.js` **beside its tests**, precisely because it has to mirror the engine and it silently didn't. Periodic rows display their span (`2026–2030`) rather than posing as a single year, and stay read-only with an explanation — one row drives five years, so "edit the 2028 amount" would rewrite 2026–2030 without saying so.

  **The same modal could edit the wrong row.** It re-found a transfer in the module's array by `Date + Flag`, which is not a unique key; two OneTime rows sharing both would collide on `findIndex` and the edit would land on the first. Now carries the real array index.

  **The synthetic `_cash_sweep` module 404'd.** The engine attributes swept cash to a pseudo-module that the breakdown renders as clickable, but the sweep writes its trail to `<scenario>_cash_sweep.csv`, not `<scenario>_<module>_entries.csv` — so it was the one module in the breakdown that could not be opened. The per-module route now recognises it.

  **The SWEEP badge hid the backups.** It keyed off `cash_sweep_target`, which is true **only for the primary**. A module ranked `cash_sweep_priority = 2` never receives deposits but **is drained when the primary runs dry** — so an asset that was liquidation-eligible displayed as if it were outside the sweep. The badge now shows the rank (`SWEEP 1` filled, `SWEEP 2` outlined).

  **The Review toolbar** had five equally-loud buttons (two greens, a purple gradient, two outlines), each pinned to `min-width: 150px`, wrapping into a ragged second row. Only **Generate** changes anything. It is now the one filled primary, on the shared `.btn` system, with the view/export actions quiet behind a divider — which **removed** bespoke button classes (baseline 123 → 122) and two more naked hexes (170 → 167) rather than adding any. Creating a module now opens its editor, since a new module is created blank.

- **v3.0.95** (2026-07-13) — **Overnight hardening run: the forecast write API stops swallowing fields, CR042 U4 completes, and 63 design tokens turn out to have been silently doing nothing.**

  **[CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md) N10 — the forecast write API now rejects unknown fields.** `POST/PUT /forecast/modules` and `/incomeexpense` build their update object from an explicit PascalCase whitelist, so any key a caller sent that the route did not read was **silently dropped**: 200 OK, value gone. That is precisely how CR046's window dates and CR047's tax override were lost (v3.0.86) — wired through the editor, the API, the engine and the copy path, thrown away at this one layer, and "verified" with curl, which bypasses it. The finding warned that blind strict-whitelisting would 400 real UI payloads, so both contracts were enumerated as a **superset of live traffic** (every key every caller actually sends, checked against the frontend): nothing that worked can start failing, and the next typo'd or unwired field fails loud. Enumerating them surfaced **four dead keys** — `AccountNumber`, `Expense`, `Income`, `BaseYear` — posted for months to columns that **do not exist**. Guarded in *both* directions: a frontend test reads `MODULE_WRITE_FIELDS` out of the route source and asserts the payload sends nothing outside it (the two sides can no longer drift, which is the entire failure mode), and 8 DB-backed route tests pin accept/reject. Live-verified: real create → 201, `{BaseYear}` → 400.

  **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U4 complete.** FCModulesEdit (~1.5k lines) and FCExpModal (~860) — the two heavyweights deliberately held back from v3.0.74 — are on `<Modal bare>`. **No bespoke `role="dialog"` overlay remains under `features/Forecast`.**

  **T1 was closed too early.** The dead-token finding was fixed in KpiCards and marked done. It was never just KpiCards: **63 references across 30 files** pointed at tokens that were never defined (`--text-secondary`, `--ink-muted`, `--surface-hover`, `--warn`, `--ok`, `--font-heading`…). `color: var(--text-secondary)` lints clean, looks deliberate, and silently does **nothing** — it falls back to the inherited value, so the styling quietly ignores the theme, dark mode included. Several carried a hardcoded fallback (`var(--warn, #f59e0b)`), which is worse: correct in light, frozen forever. All 63 remapped; `--font-heading` is now actually defined. New **blocking** guard `check-dead-tokens.sh` — **no baseline, the bar is zero**.

  **Outfit self-hosted.** It came from the Google CDN on every page load: a third-party request, no font at all when the PWA ran offline, and a FOUT. Outfit v15 is a **variable** font — all five weights are two files (~47KB), now preloaded and service-worker precached.

  **The hex guard was itself the bug.** `check-inline-hex.sh` only ever matched the pure `prop: "#hex"` form, so every composite (`"1px solid #hex"`, any hex inside a style string) sailed past it. Widened to count every literal hex that is not a `var(--token, #hex)` *fallback* — revealing the real number: **170 across 22 files, not 66**. Re-baselined at 170 (frozen, may only shrink). The bulk is FCReview's print exporter (69, documented light-mode-only) and the chart palettes (already theme correctly) — baselined rather than blindly rewritten, because picking a token for each is a visual judgment, not a mechanical swap.

  **Lint 124 → 108**, and the gate **stays advisory on purpose**: 43 of the remainder are `set-state-in-effect`/hooks-rules restructures that change *when* a component re-renders. Doing those unattended overnight, with no browser, is how you ship a subtle render regression into a finance app.

  360 backend / 179 frontend tests green; four blocking CI guards green. Frontend + one backend route file; no migration.

- **v3.0.94** (2026-07-13) — **[CR049](../cr/cr-049-forecast-base-year-seed-and-final-year-tax.md): the final-year sale funds its own tax; the engine stops keeping a second copy of the base-year query.** Two silent-wrong-number bugs from one owner question ("why isn't the sweep taking from Fidelity Stock?" — it does, in 2061/62; the premise was false, the numbers around it were not). **(1)** A forced liquidation's capital-gains tax is deferred +1y, which is what keeps the sweep a single forward pass — but the **last** year has no next year, so the tax landed *after* the band check: 2062 pulled $852K from Fidelity Stocks, restored cash to the $200K band, then took a **$205K tax bill on that very sale with nothing sold to cover it**, ending at **−$60,521 cash beside $4.3M of sellable stock** — and no `Cash Shortfall` entry, because the band check had already passed. The drain cascade is now **re-entrant**: sell → pay the tax → re-check the band → sell again, converging in a few passes (each pass only funds the tax *on the previous pass's tax*); the passes fold into one entry per account. If the ranked modules genuinely can't fund it, the residue is now a reported shortfall, not silent negative cash. **(2)** The sweep's opening cash is the BaseYear NCF, and the engine computed it with **hand-copied SQL** under a comment promising it mirrored `crud.getBaseYearValues` exactly. It didn't — an `account_type = 'liability' … ELSE 0` gate zeroed every non-liability module expense, so 2026's **$64,717 of Property Costs never left the bank** while the engine paid those same costs out of `Bank Accounts` in all 36 forecast years. Because the sweep pins cash to the band, the error rode the whole horizon instead of washing out. The duplicate query is **deleted**; `index.js` calls `getBaseYearValues` (now accepting the engine's transaction client). Result: "2026 Base" closes 2062 **exactly on the $200K band**, no shortfall in any year, selling ~$295K more stock and paying ~$86K more tax than before. Engine byte-parity deliberately broken; prod regenerated. 352 backend tests pass; verified against a prod restore, not only unit tests.
- **v3.0.93** (2026-07-13) — **[CR048](../cr/cr-048-model-review-fixes.md): scenario copy carries the per-scenario assumptions.** The API copy endpoint copied the scenario row, its modules and its inc/exp items but **not the assumptions** (period, inflation, FX, tax rate — keyed by scenario *name* in the `forecast_assumptions` document). The UI did that half **client-side**, so UI copies worked and an **API copy silently produced a scenario with 0% inflation and no period**, which the engine would build anyway. The same split-brain shape as the CR045 §1 copy bug that opened this run. The assumptions copy moves **into `copyScenario`** (same transaction, idempotent on re-copy) and the client-side half is deleted — one copy path. Verified: an API-only copy now reproduces its source **exactly** (1,445 entries, $780,835,162). +1 route test, fails pre-fix.
- **v3.0.92** (2026-07-13) — **Forecast Modules + Income/Expense: full-width tables, details in a modal.** Both pages put a permanent details column beside the table (`1.2fr / 0.8fr`), squeezing it to ~60% of the page: Name/Account wrapped a word per line and Base (USD) was clipped. The details panel is now a **modal on double-click** (shared `<Modal>`, Edit in its footer) and the tables get the full width with real column widths (`nowrap`, per-column %). **Behavior change:** double-click used to open the *edit form* on both pages — it now opens *details*; edit stays on the toolbar and is one click from the modal. Frontend-only, no engine/schema change.
- **v3.0.90–91** (2026-07-12) — **[CR048](../cr/cr-048-model-review-fixes.md) forecast model review.** Three engine fixes (drained sweep backups stopped paying phantom dividends; cost basis can no longer offset two sales; the convergence loop honors CR047's income rate, CR046's window and non-USD modules) + Tax/Taxes merged onto one Review row. Base/Downside byte-identical; House Purchase 3 yrs/−$732K → −$1.05M. Prod regenerated.
- **v3.0.84–89** (2026-07-12) — **[CR047](../cr/cr-047-module-income-tax-override.md) income-only tax override (migration 038)** — `tax_rate_override` moved gains *and* income together, so "the UB dividend is already taxed in Poland (3% incremental) but a sale is still an ordinary gain" was inexpressible. Plus fixes: the module **save dropped** the new CR046/CR047 fields (the PUT body is a whitelist — payload builder extracted and tested against `FIELD_SECTIONS`); module **Type** couldn't be set on an *unmatched* module (it was offered COA *account* types); the CR046 window was **ignored in the base year** (phantom rent in the BUDGET column *and* in the sweep's opening cash); the base-year edge (a window opening in the base year booked half, taxed whole, displayed whole → now halved everywhere).
- **v3.0.81–83** (2026-07-12) — **[CR046](../cr/cr-046-module-income-window-and-hierarchy-graph.md) income/expense window (migration 037) + hierarchy breakdown graph.** "Start charging rent in 2030" was inexpressible; the window bounds *when* a stream runs, never how much (year-only picker, stored as July 1, first/last year book 50%). Double-clicking any Review row now stacks the accounts beneath it (BS and P&L); the Expense stack excludes Transfers so it reconciles with the row.
- **v3.0.77–80** (2026-07-12) — **[CR045](../cr/cr-045-forecast-cash-warnings-liquidation.md) forecast cash warnings + forced liquidation.** Started from a bank line diving to −$3.7M; found **four** silent-wrong-number engine bugs: `copyScenario` never copied `cash_sweep_priority` (copied scenarios ran with **no sweep module** — 14 unfunded shortfall years / −$20.1M); the sweep never applied the **BaseYear cash flow** to its opening balance; forced liquidations were **tax-free** while scheduled disposals of the same shares were taxed, and sold funds kept compounding; a module that was both a sweep source *and* had scheduled disposals was **sold twice** (~$950K over-committed). New cash-health **warnings pane** on `/forecast-review` (W1–W6) — it found bug #2 on its first healthy-scenario run. v3.0.78 was reverted the same hour (v3.0.79) and re-shipped after a restore-and-diff against a copy of prod.
- **v3.0.76** (2026-07-12) — **fix: Forecast module Audit Trail was permanently broken.** Clicking a module name in the Review → Entry Breakdown modal always returned `500 The "path" argument must be of type string. Received undefined`. Two independent bugs in `GET /api/v2/forecast/audittrail/:scenario/:module` (and the `DELETE /audittrail/:scenario` twin): (1) the audit dir came from `dataPaths.fcAuditTrail || path.join(dataPaths.baseDir, …)`, but **neither key exists** on `dataPaths` → `path.join(undefined)` threw; (2) its filename sanitizer lowercased and collapsed `_+`, while the writers (`fcbuilder-module` / `fcbuilder-incexp`) preserve case and repeats — so on a case-sensitive FS it could never have matched a real file even with a valid dir. Both routes now use `PATHS.AUDIT_TRAIL_DIR` and the writers' exact `replace(/[^a-z0-9]/gi,'_')` convention (the two working sibling routes already did); dropped the now-unused `dataPaths` import. Verified live on dev (module trail 200 + 23 rows, inc/exp account 200, missing module → clean 404, cash-sweep untouched). +2 fs-backed regression tests (313 backend green). Backend-only, no migration.
- **v3.0.75** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U1 — Forecast inline-style migration.** `FCStepNav.jsx` → CSS classes (`FCStepNav.css`), removing the fully-inline styling and the JS `onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave` hover handlers no CSS migration can otherwise reach. Migrated 62 of 64 naked-hex Forecast inline-style values (`color: "#808E9B"` → `var(--muted)`, etc.) to theme tokens so they flip in dark mode — only exact-match or semantically-safe tokens used (two off-palette blues left for an owner-reviewed pass; `FCCompareCharts` left untouched — its palette already themes). New blocking guard `Scripts/check-inline-hex.sh` (per-file naked-hex baseline, count may only shrink; `npm run lint:hex`) wired into `ci.yml` as the third design-adoption guard. +0 tests (145 hold); frontend-only.
- **v3.0.74** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U4 — Forecast modals → `<Modal>`.** Added a `bare` mode to the `<Modal>` primitive: Radix's overlay + focus-trap + ESC + portal + correct ARIA, but the caller's own card supplies the background/border/padding, so the bespoke `fc-*-modal` overlays migrate **visually 1:1** while gaining accessibility they never had. Migrated 8 of the 10 Forecast overlays — FCExpConfirmDeleteModal, FCModulesDeleteModal, FCReviewAuditTrailModal, FCReviewBreakdownModal, FCCashTransferModal, FCReviewAdjustTransferModal, FCModulesUnmatchedModal, FCScenariosModal — matching each one's original outside-click behavior (`closeOnOutside`), and fixed two latent hooks-after-early-return violations (AuditTrail, Breakdown) by gating on `open` instead of an early `return null`. The two heavyweight forms (FCModulesEdit ~1.5k lines, FCExpModal ~860) are deferred: real regression risk without live click-testing. Modal-adoption baseline 19→15; +1 vitest (145). Frontend-only.
- **v3.0.73** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U5 — Cash Flow 2→1 + Budget-vs-Actual 3→1.** Extracted the Balances tab shell into a shared `components/ReportTabs/ReportTabs.jsx` (`.report-tabs`) and refactored Balances onto it. `pages/CashFlowTabs.jsx` merges `/cash-flow` + `/cash-flow-periods` → `/cash-flow/:view` (Summary / By Period). `pages/BudgetVsActual.jsx` merges `/budget-realization` + `/budget-graph` + `/budget-variances` → `/budget-vs-actual/:view` (Realization / Chart / Variances); Budget Worksheet + Budget FX stay separate. Old URLs redirect (App.jsx). Two nav renames ("FC Inc/Exp Mapping" → "Income & Expense Mapping", "FC Settings" → "Forecast Settings"). +3 vitest (144). Frontend-only. Deferred to owner input: calibration→Settings move, "Upload PS" fate, Forecast-step sidebar collapse.
- **v3.0.72** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U5 — Balances 4→1 consolidation.** The four sibling balance pages (`/balance`, `/balance-trends`, `/balance-sheet-periods`, `/balance-chart`) become one destination `/balances` with a deep-linkable tab switcher `/balances/:view` (Summary / Periods / Trends / Net-Worth) in new `pages/Balances.jsx` — each tab renders the existing page component unchanged (pure IA/routing, no report logic moved). Old URLs 301-redirect (App.jsx `<Navigate>`); the four route entries are removed from config, so the sidebar Reports group drops 4→1 and Command Palette/Breadcrumbs auto-update. Breadcrumbs + Sidebar active-state made param/prefix-aware so tab views resolve cleanly. +3 vitest (141). Frontend-only, no migration. **Owner-checkpointed:** paused here for owner review before Cash Flow 2→1 / Budget-vs-Actual 3→1 / sidebar re-cut ≤8 + renames.
- **v3.0.71** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U4 — shared Modal/DataTable primitives + CI adoption guards.** New `components/Modal/Modal.jsx` (Radix `@radix-ui/react-dialog` under the app tokens: focus trap, ESC, scroll-lock, ARIA the ~20 hand-rolled `role="dialog"` overlays never had) and `components/DataTable/DataTable.jsx` (codifies the BudgetWorksheetV2 table: sticky header, uppercase micro-labels, right-aligned tabular-nums, built-in column sort). Two adoption guards now **blocking in CI** (`ci.yml`): `check-button-css.sh` (baseline refreshed to 123) + new `check-modal-adoption.sh` (baseline 19, RefreshFeeds off the list). **RefreshFeeds migrated first** (highest daily exposure): 5 bespoke modals → `<Modal>`, `generate-report-button` → `.btn`, both read-only tables → `<DataTable>`. `vitest.config.js` gains automatic JSX runtime for component render tests. +9 vitest (138). Frontend-only; Forecast's 9 modals deferred to a U4 follow-up.
- **v3.0.70** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U2 + U3.** **U2:** `utils/chartTheme.jsx` (`useChartTheme` resolves live `--chart-*` tokens per theme + shared `<ChartTooltip>`); fixed the frozen light-mode gradient hex in `BalanceChartPanel` + `CategoryTrend` (now flip in dark). **U3:** Home rebuilt as a net-worth-hero dashboard — `NetWorthHero` (Recharts area chart of 12 month-end net-worth points via new `useNetWorthSeries`, reusing the Phase-3 `balanceReport` cache) + a `KpiCard` flow row + AttentionStrip + Quick Actions; demoted the All-Features grid. Full Recharts convergence of the balance/budget charts deferred (consolidated in U5). Frontend-only; 129 vitest green.
- **v3.0.69** (2026-07-11) — **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) U1 core — design-token foundation.** Owner Option A (refine Mindful Minimalist). Green split: "money up" (`--success*`/`--growth-positive`) → saturated emerald (`#1A9E74` light / `#43C9A0` dark), distinct from the sage brand. Elevation flatten: `--radius-lg` 24→16px + `.panel` hairline border + soft shadow. Added type scale (`--text-*`) + 4px spacing scale (`--space-1..16`), both additive. First deployable direction check before U2–U5 build on the palette. Frontend-only.
- **v3.0.68** (2026-07-11) — **Clean redeploy of Phase 3 for verification.** No code changes since v3.0.67; a fresh version bump + `--no-cache` rebuild so the version chip unambiguously reflects the new build and the self-updating service worker cycles once more. (Owner-requested test build.)
- **v3.0.67** (2026-07-11) — **[CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md) Phase 3 — frontend consolidation.** Adopt **TanStack Query** v5 (shared `QueryClient` at the root). **3.1:** `useCoa` → one `useQuery(['coa'])` shared by its 15 consumers (was a per-consumer 3-endpoint `Promise.all` on every mount). **3.2:** new shared cached report hooks (`useBalanceReport`/`useCashFlowReport`); `useOverview` (Home + mobile KPIs) rebuilt on them and `MobileBalance`/`MobileCashFlow` deduped onto them — same date/range fetched once across views. **3.3:** deleted five byte-identical/dead V1 `Rest` aliases, 15 call sites → V2. **3.4:** eslint config grants Node globals to `*.config.js` (−20 false `no-undef`); Phase-3 files lint-clean (158→137 errors). +6 frontend tests (`useCoa`, `useOverview`) via new `@testing-library/react`; 129 vitest green; build green; no backend/API change. Deferred (tracked in CR): 3.3 raw-`fetch` routing + envelope unification, 3.4 full lint burn-down (CI lint gate stays advisory), desktop multi-period report pages (want `useQueries`).
- **v3.0.66** (2026-07-11) — **Fetch timeout (fail-safe on hung requests).** `js/rest.js` gains `fetchWithTimeout` (AbortController, 30s default, `timeoutMs` override / `0` disables) and routes all six fetch call sites (`fetchJson` + the transactions/budget-entry writes) through it. Defense-in-depth on top of v3.0.65's SW fix: even if a request ever hangs again (stale SW, dead proxy, network stall), it now rejects with a readable "timed out" error the UI surfaces instead of spinning forever. Honors a caller-supplied `AbortSignal` and rethrows caller-initiated aborts untouched. +4 vitest (125 total).
- **v3.0.65** (2026-07-11) — **PWA stale-service-worker fix + CI green.** Two fixes: (1) **PWA** — `vite.config.js` switched to `registerType: 'autoUpdate'` + `skipWaiting: true` (was `'prompt'` + `skipWaiting:false` with **no update-prompt UI wired up**, so the first-installed SW controlled indefinitely; a stale worker could intercept `/api` and hang the report fetches — Home KPIs stuck on "…" with the backend fully healthy). Now a deployed SW self-activates + claims clients on the next visit, so users never get stuck on a stale worker. (2) **CI** — `generate-transaction.test.js` now seeds the `Bank Accounts` engine anchor (Phase 2.3's N9 guard throws without it; CI's `ci-seed.sql` lacks it, so the 3 cases had been red on CI since v3.0.63 while passing locally). Verified full suite **311/311 against a fresh migrations+ci-seed DB** replicating CI exactly.
- **v3.0.64** (2026-07-11) — **[CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md) Phase 2.1 + 2.2 backend route→service extraction.** Pure structural refactor, **no behavior change**, no migration, no flags. **Phase 2.1** — `routes/budget.js` (950→~430) and `routes/forecast.js` (1419→~1080) split into thin HTTP glue + new service modules (`services/budget.js`, `services/forecast/crud.js`); all raw `db.query`/inline `require('../db')` gone from both route files (N7/N13 closed for them), requires hoisted, one dead require removed. **Phase 2.2** — `routes/reports.js` (629→~180) over new `services/reports.js` (balance-sheet + cash-flow builders incl. the CR024 feed-balance read-override; restores the collapsed fetcher-service split, N7). Verified: forecast engine **byte-identical** on dev "2026 Base" (1442 entries) after a full regenerate, and golden before/after JSON diff on `/balance` + `/cash-flow` + `/category-trend` **byte-identical**. +10 reports route-contract tests (net written first, run green pre-change). **Backend 311 green.** Deferred (tracked in CR): util.js hygiene, 2.1's N10 write-validation.
- **v3.0.63** (2026-07-11) — **Three-lens review + CR043 code-structure hardening (Phases 0, 1, 2.3, 2.4) + first CR042 UI fix.** A UI/architecture/marketability review ([CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md)/[CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md)/[CR044](../cr/cr-044-productization-marketability.md)) then executed the safe backend program. **No user-facing behavior change** beyond one UI bug fix; forecast engine output is **byte-identical** (md5 `d3d2a746`, 1442 entries on dev "2026 Base") verified at every engine-touching step. **Backend 301 / frontend 121 green.**
  - **[CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md) Phase 0** — vitest now runs (blocking) in CI (was hand-run only); dropped 0-import deps `@tensorflow/tfjs-node`+`arquero`+root `axios`; `nodemon`→dev; removed the `/var/run/docker.sock` + Mongo-era `mongo_backups` mounts from prod+dev compose; deleted dead code/debris (`useAPI`/`useModal`/`useFormState`, `pages/Balance.jsx`+`BudgetInput.jsx`, `old/`/`psAPI/`/`mongo/`, dead `ASSUMP_FILE`). *(Reclassified after verification: `DataTable.css` kept — live global stylesheet; `ForecastContext` deferred — still a mounted provider.)*
  - **Phase 1.3 (finding N2)** — `generateForecast` now runs entirely inside one `db.transaction()` holding a per-scenario `pg_advisory_xact_lock`: a mid-build failure rolls back to the previous entries instead of emptying the scenario, and concurrent builds serialize. +3 DB-backed tests; engine ~4.5× faster as a side effect (no per-statement autocommit in the convergence loop).
  - **Phase 1.1 (N11)** — real migration runner `server/db/migrate.js` (`npm run migrate`) + `schema_migrations` ledger (checksum + baselined flag), applies-the-gap in per-file transactions, **auto-baselines** an already-populated DB, drift-warns; wired into `deploy-to-production.sh` Step 2b. Dev adopted (36 baselined). Pulled forward from CR027A. +11 tests.
  - **Phase 1.2** — route-contract characterization tests for `forecast.js` + `budget.js` (shared `_httpApp` harness, no supertest dep) pinning envelopes/validation/CRUD before the future route→service split. +25 tests.
  - **Phase 1.4 (N10 partial)** — `AppError` class; `validate.js` throws it; `app.js` error middleware now logs unexpected 5xx server-side (response shape unchanged); strict field-whitelist/type validation on `PUT /scenarios/:id`.
  - **Phase 2.3** — forecast engine restructured into **load → compute (pure) → persist**; builders split into pure `computeModule` + compat `processModule`; **danfojs-node removed — and with it the entire TensorFlow native chain** (replaced by `services/forecast/frame.js` LabelFrame; `node_modules` now 61 MB). N9 minimum: sweep fails loud if the `Bank Accounts` COA anchor is missing, and the item-mapped-to-`Bank Accounts` crash (found in 1.3) is fixed. Audit-trail CSVs byte-stable.
  - **Phase 2.4** — dropped the never-imported `pino`/`pino-pretty` deps (adoption would be a 388-site `console.*` sweep with no driver); `console.*`→stdout stays the pattern.
  - **[CR042](../cr/cr-042-ui-look-and-feel.md) T1 quick win** — `KpiCards.css` referenced undefined tokens (`--text-muted`/`--text`/`--font-heading`); KPI titles/values were rendering with fallback styling on Home/Review/Compare/Budget. Repointed to `--ink-tertiary`/`--ink`; dropped the nonexistent heading font. (Larger CR042 UI phases U1–U5 not yet started.)
  - **[CR044](../cr/cr-044-productization-marketability.md)** — marketability memo; owner decided **stay personal**; no-regret moves (pluggable LLM, SimpleFIN, demo seed) captured in §2 backlog.
  - Docs: `CR_INDEX` rebuilt (44 CRs), FC_PROJECT_STRUCTURE tech-stack/tests/engine/migrations sections updated, `MIGRATIONS.md` runner section. No new schema migration (runner is infra; prod schema unchanged).
- **v3.0.62** (2026-07-11) — **CR041 Module Ownership Gating + Edit-Form Regrouping ([CR041](../cr/cr-041-module-ownership-gating.md) — complete, designed and shipped same day).** Answers the owner's question "how do I keep a future-purchased asset's running costs from starting in the base year?" — previously a module with Market Value 0 and a future Invest transfer (e.g. a 2027 house) accrued its `expense_amount`/`income_amount` from base year. Engine (`fcbuilder-module.js`): amount-based expense/income streams now start at first ownership (`acquisitionIdx` from the first non-zero market value, computed before Full disposals), **zero before acquisition and 50% in the acquisition year** — the exact mirror of the Full-disposal halving; the Period-1 tax on base-year income is skipped when not owned at base. Yield-spread income and legacy `expense_pct` deliberately ungated (avg-MV-driven — already zero pre-purchase and naturally half in the purchase year).
