- [ ] **Bank-feed mapping: no cutoff is asked for when a mapping is created** (2026-07-14, from the Black Card incident). A new `account_source_mappings` row defaults `promote_from_date = NULL`, and the promote gate reads NULL as *"promote everything"* — so mapping a feed account whose period is already covered (by another feed account, a manual statement upload, or PS) silently back-fills the whole staged history as duplicates. Bank Feed Setup should require (or at least prompt for) a cutoff when a mapping is first pointed at a fin account that already has transactions — defaulting it to `max(transaction_date) + 1` for that account. **Nothing guards this today**; the incident was caught by a row-level match, not by any check.
- [ ] **bank-feed `upsertAccounts` never prunes** (2026-07-14). An account that disappears from the Sheet's Accounts tab stays in `feed_accounts` forever and keeps appearing on Bank Feed Setup, because fin enumerates from bank-feed's live `/v1/accounts`. Deleting the sheet row is therefore *not* sufficient — the DB row must be purged by hand (as it was for `161xwx…`). Consider marking vanished accounts `archived` on sync and filtering them out of `/v1/accounts`. Related: the same sync's `feed_transactions` conflict key is `(account_id, external_id)`, so a transaction re-attributed to a different account in the Sheet is **copied, not moved** — the old copy lingers under the dead account.
- [x] **Revolut EUR transactions were filed under the USD wallet — *fixed both sides* 2026-07-31.** Two defects, and the interesting part is that fixing either one alone makes things worse. **(1) Upstream:** bank-feed's tx→account id guard read `raw.accountId` (MX/Chase) and `raw.account_id` (Plaid) but not `raw.ext_nordigen_acc_id` (GoCardless), on a comment asserting GoCardless's id *"does not surface here"* and that its names *"don't collide"*. Both false — it is in every GoCardless raw, and **the names need not collide for the name join to be wrong**: fintable simply *mislabelled* the `⚡ Account` column on 4 rows, and the join believed it. Measured over all 1,834 stored rows: 687 carry the id, **all 687 resolve**, 683 were already right and **exactly 4 moved**. (bank-feed `6d108ec`.) **(2) fin:** a fintable id is `{provider_account_id}--{event_hash}`; the prefix changes on re-consent and on re-attribution, the suffix is the event — one Revolut top-up is on file under **three** prefixes and one suffix. `promote()` did not know that, so shipping (1) alone would have handed fin the same events under new ids and **traded a misattribution bug for a duplication bug**. It now skips a staged row whose event hash is already on a bank-feed transaction for that account, ahead of and independent of `BANK_FEED_DEDUP_ENABLED`. Verified end-to-end: bank-feed re-filed the 4 rows, prod re-ingested, **zero new transactions**, Revolut-EUR 193/98.13 and Revolut-USD 4/1.46 unchanged, and the USD mapping is live again (`ignored = FALSE`). *Confirmed live in passing:* the `(account_id, external_id)` conflict key **copies rather than moves** a re-attributed transaction — the 4 stale rows had to be deleted by hand, exactly as the item below predicts. The CR059 API converter joins on `tx.account_id` directly and never had defect (1), so the cutover retires that class rather than inheriting it.
- [x] **Forecast "View Output" opened a panel that took no clicks at all — fixed 2026-07-31.** The module editor is a Radix dialog ([`Modal`](../../frontend/src/components/Modal/Modal.jsx), CR042 U4) and stays open behind it; a modal Radix layer sets `pointer-events: none` on `<body>` and re-enables it **only on the layers it owns**, so [`FCModuleAuditModal`](../../frontend/src/features/Forecast/FCModuleAuditModal.jsx)'s hand-rolled overlay — rendered as a sibling at `zIndex: 1100` — drew on top with correct data and swallowed every click including its own ✕. It reads as a frozen page: no error, right DOM, right state, handlers attached, and the browser simply never delivers the event. Radix also `aria-hidden`s everything outside its dialog, so the panel was **absent from the accessibility tree** as well. Fixed by routing it through the shared primitive in `bare` mode (a real nested layer, plus ESC and a focus trap it never had). Regression test [`e2e/nested-modal.spec.js`](../../frontend/e2e/nested-modal.spec.js) — Playwright's actionability check *is* the assertion, and against the old component it fails with `<div class="modal__overlay"> intercepts pointer events`; it was falsified that way before the fix was kept. **The general rule: any bespoke overlay opened from inside a `<Modal>` is dead on arrival** — the other three (`FCCashSweepModal`, `FCAddFromLinesModal`, `FCAddFromActualsModal`) are safe today only because nothing opens them from within an open dialog.
- [x] **fin's bank-feed ingest asked for 500 rows, never paginated, and was silent at the cap** — *found 2026-07-31 while verifying the Revolut fix, **fixed the same day in v3.7.4**.* `ingest()` called `bankFeedClient.transactions({ since, limit: 500 })`, never used the `offset` the client already accepted, and nothing warned when the result came back *equal to* the limit — the one signal rows had been dropped. On `since=2026-06-07` the feed held **1,430** rows for fin and it took 500, reporting a contented `fetched: 500`. **This is precisely why the 2026-06-08 Revolut duplicate never reached fin.** Exactly the class [`data-import.md`](../../.claude/rules/data-import.md) exists to prevent. Now pages to the end, reports `pages`, and throws past 40 full pages.
- [ ] **The backend test suite promotes REAL staged bank-feed rows into the dev ledger** (found 2026-07-31; **pre-existing, not new**). [`bankFeedImport.test.js`](../../server/src/v2/services/__tests__/bankFeedImport.test.js) calls `orchestrator.promote()` **10 times**, and [`promote()`](../../server/src/v2/services/refreshBankFeedV2.js) selects `WHERE s.promoted_transaction_id IS NULL …` across the **entire** `bankfeed_staging` table — it has no test scoping, unlike the suite's own `TAG`-scoped cleanup. So a plain `npx jest` against dev promotes whatever is genuinely promotable at that moment into dev's ledger: **89 real rows** (Fidelity Options 19, Amazon Visa 15, PKO VISA Infinity CB 14, …, zero test rows) landed that way during one run. Harmless on dev — it is a scratch copy and the review queue is the intended destination — but it means **dev's ledger cannot be cleaned while the suite is run**, and the tests are one `DATABASE_URL` away from doing it somewhere that matters. The suite is gated only by `SKIP_DB_TESTS` / whatever `DATABASE_URL` points at; nothing asserts it is not prod. Fix: scope the test's promote (e.g. an `onlyStagingIds` option, the shape `ingestPs`'s `onlyPsIds` already uses for exactly this reason), or have the suite refuse to run against a DB it did not seed.
- [ ] **TypeScript migration** (per §4.4) — gradual: utilities → hooks/components → pages.
- [ ] **API design pass**: consistent `{ success, data, meta }` envelope, pagination, structured logging via Pino.

---

## 3. Known Issues

- [ ] **The e2e Generate specs race their own scenario load, and it reds `main`** (found 2026-08-17
  on CR083 P0a's push — three specs failed, the whole job passed on a re-run of **identical code**).
  `compare-real-terms.spec.js:23` and `money-paths.spec.js:65` do
  `selectOption({label: "E2E Scenario"})` and then immediately
  `getByRole("button", {name: /^generate/i}).click()`. But
  `FCReviewSelector.jsx:25` computes `disableGenerate = generateDisabled || !selectedScenario ||
  isLoading || !!loadError` — so while the newly selected scenario's entries load, the button is
  **disabled**, and Playwright's actionability check burns the full 30s waiting for it. The failure
  log shows exactly that: `locator resolved to <button disabled …>` retried 61 times.
  `money-paths.spec.js:41` (balance sheet) fails in the same runs on a 10s timeout, so the trigger is
  probably one slow API window rather than three independent bugs. **This is a test-side race, not a
  product defect** — but it costs a red `main`, which blocks prod deploys at Step 0b and is exactly
  the "red main nobody announced" tax (#12). Fix: await the scenario load before clicking (the
  helpers added in `2301cf5` are the pattern), or assert the button is enabled first.

- [ ] **Two P&L categories post transactions to a NON-LEAF and the cash-flow report cannot see them**
  (found 2026-08-16, [CR083](../cr/cr-083-budget-latest-estimate.md) §2.1a). `Car Expense` (181,
  −50.00, 5 rows) and `Children - Anna` (175, −10.36, 1 row) have children **and** carry transactions
  directly. `buildCashFlowNode` (`server/src/services/reports.js:397`) reads `categoryTotals[name]`
  **only on leaves**, so **−60.36 of 2026 spend is invisible** on `/budget-vs-actual` and nothing
  announces it. Small today; the shape is what matters — a category that grows children later
  silently drops its own direct postings out of every cash-flow view. Fix: sum the node's own total
  as well as its children's, or refuse to let a parent carry transactions.

- [ ] **`extractTransferCategories` is duplicated verbatim in two services** (same source, CR083
  §11.2) — `server/src/services/budget.js:72` and `server/src/services/reports.js:362`.
  Failure-pattern #4, live. It is also a **name walk**: it matches the parent node on
  `.includes('transfer')` and collects its leaves, so renaming `Transfers` silently disables the
  exclusion, and a `profit_loss` leaf named `Transfer - x` **outside** the subtree is silently
  excluded. `accounts.is_transfer` is already TRUE on exactly the right 13 accounts. Unify onto the
  predicate, one copy.

- [ ] **`/budget-vs-actual` and `/cash-flow-periods` carry CR082's print-clipping defect TODAY**
  (found 2026-08-16 drafting [CR083](../cr/cr-083-budget-latest-estimate.md) §10.6). `DataTable.css:119-155`
  fixed `.data-table-scroll`, but `frontend/src/pages/PageLayout.css` contains **zero `@media print`
  blocks** while `.budget-realization-scroll` (`:2425`) is `overflow:auto` + `max-height` with a
  sticky `thead` (`:2540-2585`). Printing those pages clips the right-hand columns off the paper and
  the result looks like a *narrower table*, not like missing data — which is exactly how the FBAR
  working papers printed without Maximum (USD). CR083 no longer inherits it (its grid does not scroll
  sideways at all) and does not fix it. Same treatment as `DataTable.css`: release the overflow,
  un-stick the header, land it in `PageLayout.css`.

- [ ] **`/budget-vs-actual` excludes transfers by NAME STRING, and never excludes `Unrealized G/L`**
  (same source, CR083 §11.2). `extractTransferCategories` (`server/src/services/budget.js:72-92`)
  walks the COA matching `node.name.toLowerCase().includes('transfer')`, which **misses
  `Return of Capital` (217) and `Valuation - Historical` (229)** — both inside the `Transfers`
  subtree — while `Unrealized G/L` (88, **+213,595 YTD 2026**) is not excluded under any of its
  `exclude|only|include` options. `accounts.is_transfer` is already TRUE on exactly the right 13
  accounts, so `NOT is_transfer AND id <> 88` is the correct predicate. **Deliberately NOT done in
  CR083** — re-basing moves numbers on a page the owner reads weekly and needs a before/after check
  on `/budget-vs-actual` (3 tabs), `/m/budget` and `/category-trend`. Worth doing on its own.

- [ ] **Two math evaluators, and the budget worksheet uses the weaker one** (same source, CR083 §10.5).
  `frontend/src/utils/amountFormula.js` is a recursive-descent parser with the load-bearing
  thousands-comma rule; `evaluateMathInput`
  (`frontend/src/features/BudgetEntry/utils/budgetInputUtils.js:440`) builds a `Function(...)` —
  charset-allowlisted, so not arbitrary execution, but it has **no thousands-comma rule**. CR083's
  grid uses `amountFormula`; migrate `BudgetWorksheetV2` to it and delete the duplicate.

- [ ] **`POST /budget/versions/:id/copy` will silently double the forecast base year, and one
  mis-click is all it takes** (found 2026-08-16 drafting [CR083](../cr/cr-083-budget-latest-estimate.md) §7).
  The route (`server/src/v2/routes/budget.js:57-69`) takes `budget_year` straight from the request
  body with **no guard against the source version's own year**, so copying 2026 "Original" to a 2026
  "Revision 2" is accepted — while **six aggregate readers of `budget_entries` filter on
  `budget_year` and ignore `version_id`**: `forecast/crud.js:494` (the CR075 base year),
  `fcLines.js:228` and `:379`, `reports.js:522-533`, `services/budget.js:320` and `:634`, plus the
  `v_budget_vs_actual` view. Only `budget.js:327 compareToActual` filters by version. The result is
  every budget figure in the app at exactly **2×**, which reads as a plausible plan rather than a
  defect, and a base-year error **does not wash out** — CR075 §2 records it riding all 36 years.
  Cheap fix: refuse a same-year copy, or make the eleven readers version-aware. Related: the two
  dimensions of `budget_entries` are already badly incomplete — **72 rows / −86,796 have no
  category**, **32 rows / −99,191 have no account**, only −38,364 has both — which distorts
  `/budget-vs-actual` variance today and there is **no unique constraint** on
  `(version_id, category_id, entry_date)` to have prevented the drift.

- [x] ~~**CR082 P0a — `/util/coa-traits` serves an `AccountNumber` field for all 230 accounts,
  unauthenticated**~~ — **DONE 2026-08-16.** The payload now carries `AccountNumberMasked` +
  `HasAccountNumber`; the full value comes from `GET /util/coa/:id/account-number`, one account at a
  time, which the COA edit form calls when it opens (the shape `routes/tax.js` already used for
  `own_account_number`). **The frontend half was the risky half, and this item's own one-line
  prescription — "drop the field and the column" — would have destroyed data**: COA rows carried the
  stored number and posted it back on EVERY save (`resolveField('accountNumber')`), so removing it
  from the payload makes that round-trip write an empty string over a real number on any unrelated
  rename, and over every selected account at once in a multi-edit. It is now sent only when that edit
  typed one; `/coa/update` already treats an absent key as "leave it alone". The gate asserts on the
  WHOLE payload rather than on its own row, because the defect was never about one account.
  [CR082 §11c](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md#11c-the-remaining-items-closed-2026-08-16).

- [x] ~~**The FBAR engine cannot distinguish "held X on 1 January" from "the account did not exist
  yet"**~~ — **DONE 2026-08-16.** Nothing can tell those apart: the ledger does not record when an
  account was opened, and inferring it from the first transaction would be a guess the form would
  then carry. So the engine reports the CONDITION — `carry_in_only`, true when the maximum came
  entirely from before the year AND the year holds no rows — and the report raises
  `warning: unverified_carry_in`. **Amber, not red:** the line has a figure, stays in the aggregate
  and does not block the verdict; red would put it beside the missing-figure rows and train the eye
  to read both as the same problem. It travels on the CSV and prints black on paper. Gated three
  ways: fires on the `PKO TFI` shape, silent for a carry-in maximum WITH in-year activity (the
  `Wise - USD` shape — the transaction count is what separates them), silent for an in-year peak.
  *It fired on both accounts again the moment TY2024 was seeded.*
  [CR082 §12b.14](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md).

- [x] ~~**`tax_foreign_accounts.review_state` is global, not per-year**~~ — **DONE 2026-08-16,
  migration 071** (dev **and prod** 2026-08-16). A **sparse per-year override**
  (`tax_foreign_account_year_states`) resolved `COALESCE(override, standing)`. Not per-year rows for
  every designation — that needs a copy-forward rule and opens every year `unreviewed` across all 36
  rows, turning the review back into a re-read, which is what §7's tri-state exists to prevent; and
  not from/to bounds, which cannot express "excluded this year, no position on next" and would make a
  year outside the range excluded **by arithmetic rather than by a recorded decision**. The list
  dropdown still writes the STANDING answer; the year exception lives in the editor with a reason
  attached to the year it describes, because one control that sometimes means "this year" and
  sometimes "every year" is the ambiguity being removed.
  [CR082 §7](../cr/cr-082-tax-section-fbar-114.md).

- [ ] **`PKO TFI` shows 6,000 PLN on the balance sheet continuously since 1990** (found 2026-08-16,
  outside CR082). The account was created in fin on 2026-03-01 with `opening_balance` 6,000 dated
  `1990-01-01`; its first transaction is 2026-02-09. Small (~$1,671) but wrong for every historical
  date, and it is a **ledger** defect rather than a tax one — the same shape will recur for any
  account added after the fact with a calibration plug and no real pre-history.

- [x] ~~**The e2e suite was testing a three-week-old server**~~ — **FIXED in v3.14.1 (CR069 P3), root cause and all.** `Scripts/e2e.sh` started its server in a `( … ) &` **subshell**, so `$!` held the SUBSHELL's pid: `cleanup` killed the wrapper and node was reparented to init, still holding `:3998`. `npx vite preview` has the same shape one level down, which is how `:4173` collected its own orphan. Found because a July-14 server rejected exactly the fields CR062 (2026-07-31) and CR064 P6 (2026-08-02) added after it, which read as a CR069 regression until the process list was checked — every run in between had reported on stale code while claiming to test the working tree. Three fixes, because the guard alone would only have made it loud: the script now **refuses to start** if either port is already bound (naming the process and the remedy), **`exec`s** so the tracked pid is the real one, and **sweeps both ports on exit** — safe precisely because the guard proved they were free at the start, so anything listening is ours. Verified: it caught two live orphans during the P3 work, and the ports release cleanly every run.

- [ ] **A forecast module can charge cash to a P&L line that does not exist — live on `2026 SRQ House Purchase` (found 2026-08-04, CR069 P1).** `Sarasota House` carries `expense_amount = 45,000` with `expense_fc_line_id` **NULL**, so `skipExpense` is false (it keys on the amount being 0 *and* the line being unset), the cost is computed, it reaches `cashChange` and therefore **Bank Accounts**, and then `df_categories.index.indexOf('')` returns −1 so it lands on **no expense row at all**. Measured on prod: **−1,203,432 across 21 years** on Bank Accounts with nothing on any P&L line. Net Cash Flow sees it; the Expenses metric does not; the two disagree by that amount and no screen says why. This is precisely the hazard [CR062](../cr/cr-062-forecast-loan-module.md) identified for loans and closed with `assertLoanHasInterestLine` — *"a blank or unknown expense category gives Bank Accounts −25,625 a year with the expense row all zeros"* — and nothing guards the same shape on an **asset**. Five modules are in this state (all named `Sarasota House`, one per scenario); four are `setup_status='exclude'` and inert, the `2026 SRQ House Purchase` one is `in_progress` and **builds**. It is also why [CR069](../cr/cr-069-forecast-streams.md)'s `forecast_streams.fc_line_id` had to stay nullable — `NOT NULL` would refuse a backfill of live data — so **CR069 P2 must decide**: either these rows get a line, or the amount is refused. Until then the fix is one field on one module. ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-06 — it materialised in the v3.17.0 regenerate, at exactly the predicted figure: `Property Costs −1,203,432.12` in `2026 SRQ House Purchase`.** The cost is on the P&L, and **the guard now exists too — [CR073](../cr/cr-073-two-recurrence-guards.md), v3.17.1**: `assertStreamsHaveLines` refuses an amount with no line on POST and PUT, keyed on the amount being non-zero (0-amount streams post nothing in any mode and stay legal — 15 such rows exist on prod), with the form checking first so the reachable path is a sentence rather than a 400. **0 prod rows were in the refused state**, so it closed the hazard without blocking an edit. ✅ **CLOSED.** ⓘ *The prediction, kept because it was made before the fact:* The variant sync at 2026-08-05 02:01 gave `2026 SRQ House Purchase` a copy of Base's stream, which carries **`fc_line_id = 18` (Property Costs)**; prod's stored entries were generated 2026-08-04 23:09, three hours EARLIER, so prod is holding an un-materialised change. `syncIfStale` runs at the top of every variant build, so the next `POST /generate/2026 SRQ House Purchase` — for any reason — moves **−1,203,432 across 2028–2048** from nowhere onto **Property Costs**. Measured on a prod copy 2026-08-05 ([CR071 §6](../cr/cr-071-forecast-numbers-vs-intent.md)). The cost stops being invisible, which is the right outcome; the hazard is that it lands silently alongside whatever else prompted the regenerate. **The guard is still missing** — nothing refuses an amount with no line.

- [ ] **A variant sync's parked `__sync_<id>` names are visible to other connections (2026-08-03).** `syncEntity` step 3 renames each row it is about to rewrite to `__sync_<variantRowId>` so a partial unique index cannot be violated transiently, and the comment states this is *"invisible outside the transaction"*. It is not: a `SELECT` from a separate psql session during a lazy sync of `2026 Buy Business` returned six modules named `__sync_439`, `__sync_440`, … , and a later query returned none — so the parked names were **committed and readable**, not merely uncommitted. Nothing persisted (0 rows now) and no data was lost, but a user on the Modules page while a variant syncs can see `__sync_446` where a property name should be. Sync is lazy and fires on the variant's own reads, so the window is reachable from ordinary browsing. Pre-existing — not introduced by CR064.

- [ ] **A feed duplicate can reach the ledger when the upstream changes its id scheme — it did, 2026-08-11 ([CR059 §22](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)).** **FIXED at source** (bank-feed `4840679`): the boundary carry-over's floor was a rolling `syncDate − 5 days` while the *fetch* floor is a fixed `FINTABLE_API_MIN_DATE`, so the two agreed on exactly one day — the cutover — and every day after, rows at the fetch floor were fetched but not protected. **28 duplicate rows and +2,888.80 of phantom income** (+2,432.38 Interest Income) reached prod; cleaned up, and a forced full sweep proved the fix by reclaiming all **108** exposed rows with **0 inserted**. **What is still open is the class, not the defect** — five dedup layers existed and none could see it:
  - [x] ~~**P0 — promote-time duplicate guard**~~ **DONE v3.28.1 ([§22.7](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)).** `promote()` now matches a staged row on **content** — same account, date, amount and description as a row the feed already owns — because no id-keyed guard can recognise a row whose id it has never seen. It **claims** candidates rather than testing existence (2 held + 3 incoming → skips 2, **inserts the third**), matches on **exact date** so a false match cannot silently drop a real transaction, and deliberately does **not** inherit the `transfer-mirror` exclusion — 8 of the 20 duplicates were core sweeps, each of which also minted a second mirror. 8 tests, **4 falsified against the unfixed code**; 908 green.
  - [x] ~~**P0 — v3.7.2's event-hash guard is silently dormant**~~ **DONE v3.28.1.** Kept rather than made to throw — the API path is legitimate and ~776 Sheet-era ids remain in the ledger where the guard is exact. What was wrong was the comment (*"no `--` … a non-fintable source"*), false from the cutover on; it now names which id shapes each guard covers, and `eventHashApplicable`/`eventHashSkips` are reported on the promote summary so dormancy is a number rather than a rediscovery. `skippedDupContent` is reported for the opposite reason: non-zero means the feed is re-delivering rows the ledger already holds — the earliest fin-side signal that an upstream id scheme has moved.
  - [x] ~~**P1 — the fetch floor**~~ **DONE 2026-08-12 ([§22.10](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)), bank-feed `955be06`.** Not by raising it — by making it **roll**, and by discovering there were **three** floors, not two. `fetchFloor()` is now the only place a floor is computed and `runSync` hands the SAME value to both the fetch and the carry-over. It rolls at `today − 30d` (sized on the real p99 of 17, not §19's "1–2 days"), with `FINTABLE_API_MIN_DATE` demoted to an absolute backstop (**08-05 → 06-01**). **The code half alone was not the fix** — minDate still clamps upward, and the first regression test passed against the unfixed code because of it; `backstopPinsFloor()` now warns and reports `floor_pinned_by_backstop` when a backstop sits inside the look-back. **The third floor:** fin's own cron asked for **14 days** while bank-feed recovered 30, so a row arriving 20 days late would sit in the feed store and never be staged — same silent loss, one layer down; `refresh-bank-feed.sh` now defaults to 30 and the crontab override is removed. Verified on a live forced sweep: `window_from` 08-05 → **07-13**, 905 considered / 841 carried over / **5 inserted**, and the content guard then marked exactly the 2 Revolut §18 double-serves `SKIP-dup` while letting 31 genuinely-new rows through. 192 tests, both regressions falsified first.
  - [x] ~~**P2 — the insert guard cannot fire on this class**~~ **DONE ([§22.11](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)), bank-feed `02104da`.** A flat 300 is meaningless across the runs this service makes — 750–10,000% of a 3–40 row tick (so it could never fire) and a third of a 905-row sweep — and the failure it guards is a **fraction** of the batch, not a count. It now trips on the lower of the flat count and **25% of the batch**, floor 25 so a percentage cannot fire on a tiny all-new batch. Live steady state 5/905 = 0.6%; total failure 100%.
  - [x] ~~**P2 — nothing detects two generations of one transaction**~~ **DONE ([§22.11](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)), bank-feed `02104da`+`9d92a53`.** The carry-over structurally cannot see it: it reports the incoming id as `already_known`, which is what a healthy row reports. `generationDrift.js` keys on **id SHAPE, not content** — content over-reports (§22.8: 35 of 42 groups were PS rows, 5 genuine option trades), but two id *generations* in one group only happens after a scheme change. **It found 2 groups on run one** — §18+§22 compounding, each Revolut row held under 3 ids; **fin correct throughout** (one ledger row each, all generations pointed at it). Since fintable keeps serving all three, it gets a **reasoned exception list** on `compare-exceptions.json`'s terms (reason + date + expiry, and stale/expired entries are reported) — without which it would warn hourly forever and train everyone to ignore it. **The stale check caught its own author within one run** (a description typed from memory). Final: `groups 0, suppressed 2, stale 0, expired 0`. 207 tests, 4 falsified first.
  - [x] ~~**P2 — the 26 pre-existing duplicate groups**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-12 ([§22.8](../cr/cr-059-fintable-api-ingestion.md)) — ALL 42 GROUPS GENUINE, zero fin defects, zero wrong money.** The real count was 42 groups / 44 rows / **16,058 gross**, which read as 5× the §22 incident. Triage showed 35 were PocketSmith-era rows with distinct `ps_id`s (fin imported faithfully) and 7 were bank-feed rows with distinct upstream ids in one batch — so no fin dedup failure anywhere. The owner then checked **every** candidate against the statements and **not one was a duplicate**: Delta WARSAW 4,459.76×2 and TRAVELOCITY 3,793.34×2 first, then the remaining 40. **The whole 16,058 was never at risk.** A duplicate-group count measures *identical money*, and identical money is overwhelmingly real — two tickets at one fare, two option contracts at one strike, two community fees on two units. **It is also the strongest evidence for §22.7's design bias:** all 42 are genuine content-identical same-day pairs on live accounts, exactly what a looser guard would have eaten — a plain `EXISTS`, or a date tolerance instead of exact-date, would have been silently deleting real money on every feed run.

1. **Test coverage:** backend Jest tests — **count in [test-overview.md](test-overview.md)**, never restated here (run on every CI push against a fresh migrations+seed DB — and they must **seed their own fixtures**, per #12) + 389 frontend Vitest tests + 8 Playwright e2e ([CR016](#cr016)); broad component coverage still deferred. Route-level coverage is thin (8 of 17 route files). Live counts: [test-overview.md](test-overview.md). Layout: [test-overview.md](test-overview.md).
2. **Cloud-init ISO** still attached to the VM as a CD-ROM. Harmless; eject with `virsh --connect qemu:///system change-media fin sda --eject`.
3. **Timezone-sensitive date handling:** DATE columns return plain `YYYY-MM-DD` strings (`types.setTypeParser(1082)` in `postgres.js`). Frontend must use local-time `getFullYear()`/`getMonth()`/`getDate()` — never `.toISOString().split("T")[0]`. *2026-07-03: swept (8 sites fixed, `formatDateOnly` helper added) and an eslint `no-restricted-syntax` ban now enforces this — [CR037](#cr037) P1.* **The ban covers the FORMAT side only, and the parse side has now bitten twice.** `new Date("2025-12-01")` is UTC midnight per spec, so *reading* a DATE column into a `Date` shifts it back a day west of UTC — no `toISOString` involved, nothing for the rule to match. v3.6.1 fixed the **render** path (`parseDisplayDate`); **v3.11.1** fixed the **filter** path, where `parseEntryDate` + local-part bucketing dropped rows dated the 1st of the from-month while admitting the 1st of the month after it. Rule of thumb: for a calendar day, compare **date-only strings** (they order lexicographically) or parse via `parseDisplayDate`/`formatDateOnly` — a bare `new Date(dateOnlyString)` is the defect. *Remaining unaudited consumers of `parseEntryDate`: `getSortValue` and `getComparableFieldValue` (both benign today — sorting is consistent across rows, and consensus round-trips through `formatDateOnly`) and `Ledger.jsx`'s sort comparator.*
4. ~~Fidelity brokerage balances wrong~~ — *Resolved by CR024* (feed_balances read-override).
5. **Mobile ledger running balance still seeds at 0.** v3.0.28 fixed desktop; `MobileLedger.jsx` still sums client-side from 0 and fetches by `accountId`, bypassing the server `running_balance` path. Fix: extend the route gate to a lone `accountId`, or consume `running_balance` when present.
6. **Secrets remediation tail (CR034):** `BANK_FEED_API_KEY` is still the pre-2026-06-12 value in git history — rotate it (requires updating the bank-feed service + the OCME consumer); revoke the leaked PocketSmith API key at pocketsmith.com (the integration is retired, the key may still be live upstream); optional `git filter-repo` history scrub once rotated.
7. **dockerd reboot race can detach containers from their networks.** Seen 2026-07-04: after a host reboot, `fin-postgres` + `fin-postgres-dev` came back running (healthcheck is in-container) but with **no network endpoint** — every DB-backed endpoint 500'd with `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND fin-postgres`. Immediate fix: `docker network connect --alias fin-postgres psproject_fin-network fin-postgres` (and the `-dev` twin), or `docker compose up -d`. Prevention: `Scripts/boot-reconcile-docker.sh` + `Scripts/fin-docker-reconcile.service` run an idempotent `compose up -d` on all three stacks after boot — **installed, enabled, and test-run verified 2026-07-05** (`systemctl status fin-docker-reconcile.service`). *Considered mitigated; reopen if a reboot still detaches a container.*
8. **13 same-signed transfer clusters await triage (v3.6.3).** The net-to-zero sweep that found the two Fidelity Stocks sign errors flagged **15** same-signed multi-account clusters among 903 `is_transfer` rows ≥ 1,000. Two were confirmed and fixed; the remaining **13 are heterogeneous and unexamined** — some are certainly legitimate (a EUR→USD conversion plus a deposit will look same-signed), some may be more sign errors. Worth a pass, because this class is **invisible to balance checks**: `opening_balance` is a plug, so a wrong sign leaves today's balance correct while understating every earlier date (these two hid for six years and 1,435,914.40). Re-run the sweep in `fix-ps-transfer-signs.js` to regenerate the list.
9. **Foreign-currency dividend conversions arrive mislabeled from the feed.** When Fidelity auto-converts a foreign dividend to USD (e.g. EUR div → "ADJUST WIRE TRANSFER (Cash)" + "YOU EXCHANGED (Cash)"), the feed delivers a *single* row carrying the **foreign** numeric amount tagged `USD`, with the wrong sign and magnitude — the real post-conversion USD value (Fidelity's fill rate, not mid-market) exists only on the statement and never arrives structured. Auto-FX via `exchange_rates` would inject permanent recon drift on a USD cash account, so do **not** reinterpret as the foreign currency. Handling: accept the row, then on the **Ledger** (or Actuals) open the edit modal and set the correct USD `Amount` from the statement + recategorize (amount editing shipped v3.0.42 — the review-queue grid is still read-only on amount). For a USD row the read-only `USD Amount` mirrors the amount automatically. The row stays feed-linked via `bank_feed_external_id` and balance recon ties out. Corrected occurrences: 2026-06-19 (tx 2700481, EUR 1,891.09 → USD 2,146.29) and 2026-06-22 (tx 2700544, −30,887.00 → statement USD value), both Fidelity Bond. **Optional, not built:** flag `ADJUST WIRE TRANSFER (Cash)` rows in USD investment accounts as "verify USD value" so one can't be accepted on autopilot — *scoped into [CR038](#cr038) P2 (attention strip), 2026-07-03.*
10. **ESLint has a JSX blind spot that can delete-then-hide a live binding** *(found & fixed 2026-07-26, v3.4.8).* The flat config lacks `eslint-plugin-react`, so ESLint's `no-unused-vars` does **not** count `<Icon />` JSX usage as a use, and `no-undef` does **not** flag an undefined JSX component. This bit `MobileTabBar.jsx`: the v3.4.3 "delete 44 dead variables" sweep (`ba4ef7f`) removed `icon: Icon` from the tab-map destructure as "unused", turning `<Icon />` into an undefined reference that threw on render. Because `MobileTabBar` mounts via `MobileLayout` **outside** the route `ErrorBoundary` ([App.jsx](../../frontend/src/App.jsx)), the throw blanked the **entire mobile shell** (desktop unaffected — it never mounts that path). Build + all tests were green (jsdom runs desktop-width). Fixed by restoring the binder. **Update (v3.6.0):** the *misfiring* half is fixed — `argsIgnorePattern` now exempts capitalized args the same way `varsIgnorePattern` already exempted capitalized vars, so `({ icon: Icon }) => <Icon />` no longer reports a false `no-unused-vars` **error**. That false positive had left the blocking `npm run lint` gate **red on `main` from v3.4.8 to v3.6.0**, and made `check-lint-debt.sh` abort with no output (`set -e` + `pipefail`), reading as a debt failure. **The *missing* half remains:** without `eslint-plugin-react` there is still no `react/jsx-no-undef`, so a deleted binder that turns `<Icon />` into an undefined reference is still invisible to the gate — which is the failure that blanked the mobile shell. **Follow-ups (not yet done):** (a) add `eslint-plugin-react`/`react/jsx-no-undef` + JSX-aware `no-unused-vars` (which would also let the capitalized-arg exemption be narrowed again); (b) hoist `<ErrorBoundary>` above `MobileLayout`/`Layout` so a layout-level throw shows a fallback, not a blank screen.
11. **"2026 Downside" has no sweep backup ranked** *(found 2026-07-13, [CR049](../cr/cr-049-forecast-base-year-seed-and-final-year-tax.md) §5).* `Fidelity Stocks` carries no `cash_sweep_priority` in that scenario — it does in "2026 Base" and "2026 Base - Market Returns" — so the engine reports **−$229,616 (2061) and −$1,018,589 (2062) of shortfall while $1,199,353 of stock sits untouched** (pre-CR049 this read as −$766,103 in 2062 alone; that release made it both larger and honest — the model had been $65K/yr too rich and never funded its final-year tax). This is [CR045](#cr045) §5 working as designed (an unranked module is the owner saying "I cannot sell this", so the engine reports the shortfall rather than silently liquidating it), but for a liquid brokerage account it is almost certainly a data slip from when the scenario was created. Fix is one row (`cash_sweep_priority = 2`) + regenerate; **left to the owner** because it changes Downside's conclusions.
12. **CI was red for 30 consecutive pushes and nothing said so** *(found & fixed 2026-08-01).* `main` went red on 2026-07-30 and stayed red through v3.10.0, v3.11.0 and v3.11.1 — releases and a prod deploy shipped over a failing gate, because a red run is only visible to whoever opens the Actions tab. **Two independent causes, the second hiding the first.** (a) 2026-07-30: four DB-backed suites borrowed a row the *dev* database happens to hold — `SELECT id FROM accounts WHERE account_type IN ('asset','liability') … LIMIT 1`, `SELECT id FROM fc_lines … LIMIT 1`, `WHERE section = 'balance_sheet' LIMIT 1` — and a fresh CI database has none of them, so 29 tests died in `beforeAll` with `Cannot read properties of undefined`; the promote suite additionally wanted an `Interest Income` leaf the CI seed never had. (b) 2026-07-31: migration **046** asserted a production data fact unconditionally and aborted the whole chain on an empty DB, which took `backend-tests` and `e2e` down *before* the tests ran and buried (a). Fixed by making each suite seed and clean up its own fixtures, adding `Interest Income` to `ci-seed.sql`, and giving 046 a zero-mapping skip branch (see [migrations.md](migrations.md) 046). Now 714 backend / 8 e2e green on a from-scratch database. **Open, and the reason this could run for two days:** nothing notifies on a red `main` — no branch protection, no failure notification, and `deploy-to-production.sh` does not consult CI. The self-inflicted half is the pattern: a DB-backed test may not read ambient data, and the gap is worth an explicit check rather than a convention.

    **PARTLY CLOSED 2026-08-12, after the fifth instance ([#21](#3-known-issues)) ran seven pushes.** Two of the three gaps named above are now shut. **(1) `deploy-to-production.sh` consults CI** — new **Step 0b** refuses a commit that is red, unfinished, or has no run at all, `--allow-red-ci` to override, mirroring Step 0's dirty-tree shape. The three refusals are one idea: *nothing has verified this code*. **(2) `Scripts/check-ci.sh` makes the verdict askable** in the terminal the work already happens in, instead of a browser tab nobody opens. **Two deliberate non-refusals, both to protect the gate from being bypassed by habit:** it *waits* rather than refuses while CI is mid-flight, because `/close` pushes and deploys back-to-back and a gate that fired on every legitimate release would be reflexively overridden within a week; and "could not ask GitHub" (exit 4) warns and proceeds, because blocking a prod deploy during a GitHub outage is how a guard gets deleted. **(3) The news now arrives without being asked for** — a `SessionStart` hook (`.claude/hooks/session-start-ci-status.sh`) runs `check-ci.sh --latest` and puts the trunk's verdict at the top of every agent session, which is where this repo is actually worked. It asks about **the newest run on `main`**, not this checkout's HEAD: the tree is shared by several threads and is routinely mid-edit or behind origin, so HEAD's verdict is not the question. Red prints in full and says deploys are blocked; "no run" and "could not ask" stay silent, because a hook that cries wolf while offline teaches you to ignore the line that matters. It cannot wedge a session — every path exits 0, under a 10s ceiling. **(4) `Scripts/test-fresh-db.sh`** makes the ambient-data class catchable *before* the push rather than 90 seconds after: its own throwaway Postgres, the migration chain, `ci-seed.sql`, the backend suite, torn down after — never dev or prod.

    **The away-from-terminal half: owner decision 2026-08-12 — GitHub's built-in Actions failure email, NO webhook.** A `ntfy`/Slack job in `ci.yml` was offered and **declined**: it buys a channel the owner does not already watch, at the cost of a repo secret, a `secrets-inventory.md` row and another moving part in the release path, when GitHub already sends exactly this email for free. **The one owner action left is a settings toggle** — [github.com/settings/notifications](https://github.com/settings/notifications) → *Actions* → email on failed workflows — which is plausibly why five red runs never reached anyone. Not something an agent can set or verify: there is no API for notification preferences, so this line is the only record that it is pending. **Revisit only if a red `main` survives a day with the hook and the email both live.**

    **The audit that would have justified a new lint gate came back empty, and deliberately so:** all 24 `LIMIT 1` reads in the suite are either by name (rows `ci-seed.sql` guarantees) or run after the suite seeds its own fixture, and the suite is green from scratch. **The detector was never the gap** — CI caught all five instances within 90 seconds. Noticing was, which is what (2)–(4) address.
13. **Legacy unlinked transfer legs — characterised, and deliberately left** *(2026-08-02, [CR065 §13.3](../cr/cr-065-neutralize-pair-identity.md)).* 1,859 pre-CR065 legs in `Transfer - Securities Trades` carry no `paired_with_id`, because the column did not exist when they were made. **1,756 of them (94%) have an opposite-amount partner in the same account within 3 days** — genuine pairs, merely unrecorded — and every affected account now ties to the custodian at 0.00, so linking them buys tidiness and no correctness. The forward check is watermark-bounded for exactly this reason. Of the 86 with no partner anywhere, two were found to be misfiled IRS payments and corrected (§13.3); the rest are pre-CR032 sweeps, reinvestments and sales whose mirrors were never created, plus one near-pair the exact-amount matcher misses by $11.61. **Revisit only if** the historical shape is ever needed for period reporting.
14. **The feed's lag is real, and how long it is remains unproven** *(guard shipped v3.11.6, [CR065 §11](../cr/cr-065-neutralize-pair-identity.md)).* The feed labels a balance with the date it **synced**, and it syncs in the small hours — so the row dated D was taken before D traded. Proven by a calendar: 2026-07-31 was a Friday, so with markets shut all weekend Friday's close must equal Sunday's, and it did not (07-31 row 1,141,170.68 vs 08-02 row 1,165,523.25 on Fidelity Stocks). Two wrong marks came of it before it was caught — Stocks booked −44,600.45 (24,352.57 below the custodian) and Cash Mgt was proposed +40,150.79, a 3.6% one-month unrealized gain on a **CD ladder held at par**. **Fixed:** guard (c) in `reconcileToFeed` refuses any observation synced before the end of the day it would mark, and `balanceDate` lets the caller name the observation that does contain it. **Still open — deliberately:** whether the settle lag is 2 calendar days or 2 business days. One weekend sample cannot tell them apart, and encoding the wrong rule would silently mis-mark every future month-end, so the code refuses rather than guesses. Resolving it needs a mid-week month-end observed across several days.
15. **Migrations reach PROD first and dev silently falls behind** *(found 2026-08-02, second occurrence of the class).* `.claude/rules/migrations.md` says dev first, then prod. Reality is the reverse: nobody applies migrations to dev by hand, while `deploy-to-production.sh` **Step 2b applies every pending file to prod automatically**. So a migration lands on prod at deploy time and dev keeps running without it. On 2026-08-02 migration **055** (CR064 P6) went to prod and not dev, and dev's test gate went red — **50 failures across 6 suites** on `column "income_growth_rate" of relation "forecast_modules" does not exist` — which is indistinguishable from "someone broke the tests" until you check `schema_migrations` on both. Caught only because an unrelated session ran the full suite. This is the same runner behaviour behind the migration-044 incident (an unfinished file parked in the directory rode a deploy), seen from the other side. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-05 (`11e9e59`) — the second fix candidate, chosen because the mechanism is structural rather than behavioural.** `deploy-to-production.sh` **Step 2b(i)** now computes pending-on-prod from the two ledgers and **refuses** to deploy when a file is absent from both — prod would otherwise be the first database it ever ran against. A stopped dev stack **warns and continues** rather than failing: a guard that fires for an unrelated reason is one people learn to bypass, the same reasoning that scoped `--allow-dirty` to build-affecting paths. `--allow-unverified-migrations` is the deliberate override, and the registry row is expected to say why. All four branches were exercised against the real databases (pass; refuse with 060 removed from both ledgers, exit 1; override warns and continues; dev stopped warns, exit 0), both ledgers restored and verified afterwards. **Three more instances had accumulated between the fix candidates being written and the fix landing, all inside CR069:** 057 applied with `psql -f`, which does **not** write the ledger; 058/059 applied to dev by hand, leaving dev's ledger stale so the runner later re-ran 058 and was refused by `idx_fc_stream_changes_unique` (rolled back clean, entries verified byte-identical); and 060 reaching prod first at the v3.14.1 deploy. *Note what the guard does NOT cover:* a file applied to dev by hand with `psql -f` is invisible to it for the same reason it was invisible to the runner — **apply through `migrate.js`, or the ledger does not know**. The other two candidates stay open as complements: the test bootstrap asserting `schema_migrations` is current would have caught the 055 red-suite case *from the dev side*, which this guard does not.

16. **Twelve COA categories with real activity map to no FC line, and nothing reports it** *(found 2026-08-03, now [CR066](../cr/cr-066-fc-line-mapping-completeness.md)).* **−78,689 of 2025 expense and +31,474 of income** sit outside the forecast entirely — no base-year P&L, no FC-line row, no modelled years. Found by reconciling v3.11.14's new actuals column: the 2025 `Expense` stack totals **487,897** while the header row above it reads **566,586**, because the header takes the ledger's COA level-1 total and every child is an FC line resolved through `categoryToLineMap` — a leaf with no line is counted by one and not the other. **Neither number is wrong and this is not a regression**; the two had always disagreed in that column and nothing had ever drawn them together. Largest: `Property One-Off` −47,187, a five-row `Patrick - *` cluster −36,500 (+`Travel - Patrick` −2,502) that looks deliberately held apart, and `Tax Adjustment` **+8,078** — a credit inside Expense. On the income side `Rental - Spain` +31,306 is probably *already* modelled through the SP modules' `bs_module_income` path, so mapping it would **double-count**. That ambiguity is the real defect: "unmapped" means both *modelled elsewhere, correctly* and *not modelled at all*, and no screen distinguishes them. Recurs by construction — a new COA category is unmapped by default. Identical on dev and prod.
17. **`deploy-to-production.sh` builds from the shared WORKING TREE, so uncommitted work ships** *(fourth occurrence 2026-08-03).* The deploy script builds the frontend image and applies pending migrations from whatever is on disk — not from a tag, a commit, or even the index. With more than one agent thread active on one tree, that means **another thread's unfinished work rides your deploy**, and the resulting prod build corresponds to no commit. Four instances now: migration **044** untracked and incomplete (2026-08-01); migration **055** + the CR064 P6 engine (2026-08-02); and **CR067 P1+P2** inside the v3.11.16 deploy (2026-08-03 02:11) — the frontend bundle carried `/forecast-multi-compare` while the tag contained only half of it. Harm has been nil every time *because it was checked each time*, which is not a control. Issue #15 is the same runner seen from the migrations side. **GUARDED 2026-08-03 (v3.12.0 follow-up).** `deploy-to-production.sh` now refuses to deploy when `git status --porcelain` reports anything under **`server/`, `frontend/` or `docker-compose.yml`** — the paths that actually reach the image or the database — and prints the offending list plus the short SHA it *would* deploy. `--allow-dirty` still ships an uncommitted tree, loudly, for the deliberate case. **Scoped on purpose:** docs churn constantly across threads, and a guard that fires on every doc edit is one people learn to bypass reflexively, which is worse than no guard. Untracked files in those paths count, which is the migration-044 case. Verified both ways: the current tree (docs modified by another thread) passes, while a planted untracked migration and an uncommitted page edit are both blocked.

18. **A fresh DB enforces a constraint dev and prod do not** *(found 2026-08-06, [CR073](../cr/cr-073-two-recurrence-guards.md) §3).* Migration 007 creates `fc_lines.line_type` with an inline `CHECK (line_type IN ('bs_module_expense','bs_module_income','forecast_expense','forecast_income','unassigned'))`. **Neither dev nor prod has any check constraint on that column** — 007 was auto-baselined on both when the migration ledger landed (CR043 Phase 1.1), so it never actually ran there and the pre-migration table survived without it. CI and `Scripts/e2e.sh` build a fresh DB from the whole chain and DO enforce it. Found the way it will always be found: new test fixtures inserted `line_type = 'expense'`, passed on dev, and failed only in the e2e run. **Live data is clean — 0 violating rows on dev and on prod** — so a forward `ALTER TABLE … ADD CONSTRAINT … IF NOT EXISTS` migration would close it safely. Not done here (outside the instructed scope); the risk until then is that any code path writing a bad `line_type` is caught in CI and never on the machine it runs on. **Worth assuming this is not the only baselined table with the same gap** — the audit is `pg_constraint` on a fresh DB vs prod.

  **STAMPED 2026-08-03 (second half).** Both images now carry the commit they were built from —
  `org.opencontainers.image.revision` plus `com.fin.build.dirty` — exported by the deploy script and
  **read back out of the running containers** at the end of the deploy, so the script proves what is
  running rather than trusting what it just exported. `docker inspect fin-frontend --format '{{index
  .Config.Labels "org.opencontainers.image.revision"}}'` now answers "what commit is prod?" directly.
  That is the question the 2026-08-03 incident could not answer without archaeology (grepping the
  bundle for a route name, comparing container timestamps against `git log`). The ARG/LABEL pair sits
  in the **last layers** of each Dockerfile on purpose: the value changes every commit, and any earlier
  it would invalidate the `npm ci` cache on every local build. Verified — a cached rebuild stamps the
  right SHA, and containers predating this read back empty and are reported as such rather than
  silently passing.

  *Considered and REJECTED — building from `git archive HEAD`.* It was the original proposal here, and
  reading the compose file killed it: prod mounts **five host paths** (`./server/db/migrations`,
  `./components/data`, `./components/reports`, `./certs`, `./frontend/nginx.conf`), so running compose
  from an exported tree would point the bind mounts at an empty temp directory — `./certs` is not even
  tracked. Building in the archive while *running* from the real directory is possible, but it needs
  `.env` (with the DB password) copied into a temp dir, couples the two invocations through the image
  tag, and silently diverges the moment the compose file changes. **The guard already delivers the same
  property**: with build paths clean, the working tree *is* HEAD for everything that reaches an image —
  and the stamp now makes that checkable after the fact instead of assumed. The after-the-fact remedy
  stays what v3.11.8 and v3.12.0 did: cut a release so the tag catches up with the running build.

19. **Nine parent rollup accounts are labelled `USD` over non-USD children, and a forecast module
    inherits that label** *(measured 2026-08-10; the live instance was fixed by
    [CR064 P13](../cr/cr-064-forecast-annual-close-and-assumptions.md) + migration 056 on
    2026-08-03).* Every one of these holds **no transactions of its own** — they are pure rollups —
    so the balance sheet is unaffected: `reports.js` converts each child by its own
    `account_currency`. What is affected is `forecast_modules.currency`, which is seeded from the
    linked account.

    | account | children | child ccy | fc modules |
    |---|---:|---|---:|
    | 11 `EUR Bank Accounts` | 5 | EUR | 0 |
    | 17 `PLN Bank Accounts` | 7 | PLN | 0 |
    | 23 `Other Bank Accounts` | 1 | **GBP** | 0 |
    | 32 `CVC Investments` | 2 | EUR | 0 |
    | 38 `SP - Properties` | 3 | EUR | 0 |
    | 42 `PL Investments` | 4 | PLN | 0 |
    | 46 `PL - Properties` | 2 | PLN | 0 |
    | 65 `PLN Credit Cards` | 4 | PLN | 5 ✅ now `PLN` |
    | 215 `Tax Liabilities` | 1 | PLN | 5 ✅ correctly `USD` |

    **Why it matters:** `fcbuilder-module.js` reads the FX assumptions only when
    `Currency !== 'USD'`, so a module wrongly labelled USD keeps `fxrates`' `fill(1)` and posts its
    LOCAL amount onto a USD balance sheet, for every year. That is exactly what account 65 produced
    — **18,250 USD of liability that does not exist** in `2026 Base` and `2026 Downside` until
    migration 056. **The wrong direction is the silent one:** a wrong *non-USD* label announces
    itself (the value is divided by ~3.9 and is visibly off by a factor); a wrong *USD* label is
    invisible, because the `MarketValueUSD` override repairs index 0 only — the `base_date` year,
    which is not even an output column when `PeriodStart` is 2027.

    **Partly guarded, and the gap is specific.** CR064 P13's engine guard throws when a module
    claims USD while its local and USD value columns disagree by more than a cent — verified live
    (0 such modules on prod). It does **not** cover two adjacent shapes:
    - a module labelled USD whose two columns happen to *agree* because both were entered in local
      currency;
    - a currency the engine cannot map at all. `fxColumn` handles **only PLN and EUR**; the
      valuation branch is an `else if` with **no else**, so a **GBP** valuation module would
      silently keep `fxrates = 1`. The *flow* branch does throw (CR069/CR076 hardened it). Latent,
      not live: prod's modules are EUR 25 / PLN 30 / USD 115 and no GBP module exists — but
      `WISE - GBP` (account 24) is a real account under rollup 23.

    ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-11 (v3.27.0) — all three shapes, and the audit was run first.**

    - **The labels (migration 064).** Eight parents relabelled, not seven: 056 fixed the five
      MODULES on account 65 and left the ACCOUNT saying USD, so the source was still in place.
      `Tax Liabilities` is deliberately untouched — genuinely mixed, no single correct label.
      Derived from a predicate rather than a hardcoded list, which is safe here (unlike 056)
      because the children's currency is a stored fact, not an inference from a ratio.
    - **Shape 3, the unmappable currency (engine).** The valuation branch was an `else if` with
      no `else`, so a currency outside PLN/EUR silently kept rate 1. Falsified before fixing:
      a £10,000 module posted **$10,000** instead of $12,700, a 21% understatement with nothing
      to say so. Now throws. Scoped to the unmappable case only — a *mappable* currency whose
      assumption column is missing keeps its rate-of-1 behaviour, because tightening that WOULD
      move numbers, and this change is held to "no number moves".
    - **Shape 2, the self-consistent lie (`fcWarnings` R11).** Values that AGREE because both
      were typed in local currency under a USD label are invisible to both engine guards. The
      linked account is the only independent witness, so the module payload now carries
      `AccountCurrency` and R11 reports a disagreement. **A warning, not a throw** — `Tax
      Liabilities` is a legitimate disagreement, and refusing it in the engine would block a
      generate over a judgement call. Two id prefixes so the classifier splits them correctly:
      USD-over-local is *integrity*, one non-USD over another is *advisory*.

    **The audit that gated it**, every reader of `accounts.currency`: `reports.js` and
    `getOpeningBankCash` convert each account's OWN balance (zero on a rollup, so zero either
    way); `refreshModulesFromActuals` keys on `t.account_id` and never matches a rollup;
    `manualReconciliation.js` excludes any account with children *by design*; `restate-mtm.js`
    is invoked per account and never on a parent. All nine `account_source_mappings` rows are
    `source='pocketsmith'` — retired, name-keyed, does not read currency.

20. **A DB-backed test suite borrowed a row the CI seed does not create, and `main` went red
    unannounced** *(found 2026-08-11 — the fourth instance of Known Issue #12's shape).*
    `crud.openingBankCash.test.js` self-seeds every fixture it needs except one: it looked up the
    `Bank Accounts` root and **threw** when absent. Dev and prod have that root; `ci-seed.sql` does
    **not** create it, so all six tests in the suite failed on every CI run while passing locally.
    Confirmed with `gh run list`: **five consecutive red runs on `main`**, back to 2026-08-09,
    including a docs-only commit — and nothing announced it, which is the recurring half of this
    class rather than the test itself. **✅ FIXED (v3.27.0):** the suite now seeds the root when it
    is missing and deletes only what it created, so dev and prod stay byte-identical (their real
    root survives — verified) while CI stands its own up. That is the documented pattern for a
    DB-backed suite here; the root was simply the one borrowed row. **What this does NOT fix:**
    nothing still watches CI, so the next red `main` is found the same way — by someone happening
    to look. Known Issue #12 stays open on exactly that point.

21. **A hardcoded category id turned CR080's new suite red on the day it shipped** *(found
    2026-08-11 — the fifth instance of Known Issue #12's shape, and the first where the borrowed
    row was an **id** rather than a lookup).* `reconcileAccrue.test.js` declared
    `const INTEREST_INCOME = 74`. 74 is `Interest Income` **on dev only**: a database built the way
    CI builds one (the migration chain + `ci-seed.sql`, verified by replicating it) holds 12
    accounts, gives `Interest Income` **id 11**, and has no id 74 at all — so every one of the
    suite's mapping inserts was rejected by the FK to `accounts(id)` and **all 12 DB-backed tests
    failed in the helper**, on the same push that introduced them. The suite passed locally and
    could never have passed in CI. **Note what made this one harder to see than #20:** the seed
    already carried the row the test wanted. Adding it to `ci-seed.sql` — the fix that closed the
    previous instance — would have changed nothing, because the id, not the row, was what dev
    happened to hold. **✅ FIXED:** the id is resolved by name in `beforeAll`, which throws naming
    the missing row rather than leaving the next occurrence to surface as an opaque constraint
    violation from the helper. Verified 14/14 on dev and **66 suites / 908 tests green on a
    CI-replica database**. **The generalisation this class has now earned:** a DB-backed test may
    not read ambient data *and may not name a surrogate key* — ids are per-database facts, so
    "seed it in `ci-seed.sql`" only closes the half of the class that matches by name.

    **SIXTH INSTANCE 2026-08-16 — and it landed FOUR DAYS AFTER the audit that found none.**
    [CR084](../cr/cr-084-save-time-consequence-preview.md)'s `forecastPreview.test.js` shipped in
    v3.29.0 borrowing `SELECT id FROM fc_lines ORDER BY id LIMIT 1`, and CI went red on the release
    commit — 4 backend failures. The audit recorded under **#12** ("all 24 `LIMIT 1` reads are
    either by name or run after the fixture") was true when it was written on 2026-08-12; a new one
    arrived on the 16th. **A point-in-time audit of a pattern is not a control over it** — which is
    the argument for the lint gate that audit concluded was unnecessary, and it is now the second
    time this class has been declared closed and reopened. Fixed in `2301cf5`, and the same suite
    turned out to borrow **two more** ambient facts behind the first: the install-wide
    `FCAssump.category` document and the `Bank Accounts` cash-sweep anchor, each hidden by the one
    before it. Worth noting for the next debugger: the missing `FCAssump.FX` document surfaced as
    `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')` because `fcbuilder-setup.js:139`
    iterates it unguarded, while the inflation path two functions up fails loud and names itself —
    the loud one cost seconds, the silent one cost a bisect. **`Scripts/test-fresh-db.sh` reproduces
    all of it in ~25s and was not run before that release.**

22. **FIXED v3.28.3 (2026-08-14) — AI Review's one-click Apply wrote wherever the model pointed, and showed the model's own `current_value`** *(found 2026-08-11 while scoping [CR081](../cr/cr-081-ai-line-assistant.md); live
    since CR006/CR040).* The drawer's confirm modal renders `field`, `current_value`,
    `proposed_value`, `reason` — but `aiReview.applyAction` destructures only
    `{ type, module_id, field, proposed_value }`, so **`current_value` is never read server-side.**
    It is whatever the LLM asserted, displayed to the owner as the current state of their plan. If
    the model states it wrongly, the owner approves a change against a false premise and one click
    writes it. This is [CR077 §4](../cr/cr-077-assumption-advisor-tab.md)'s own rule — *nothing
    generated may state a number* — already violated on a shipped path, on the one surface where a
    wrong number is acted on rather than merely read. Second half: the modal is a CONFIRMATION, not
    a preview — it shows the INPUT (`growth_rate 0.5 → 1.0`) and never what the change does to the
    plan. **Blast radius is also unstated:** the allowlisted fields sit on modules, so applying to a
    base scenario moves its four variants with no mention of it. Bounded today by a deliberately
    narrow allowlist (`growth_rate`, `tax_rate_override` on valuation modules only, plus the sweep
    bands) and by `applyAction` refusing retired tables — so the damage a wrong apply can do is real
    but small. CR081 P0 closes both halves; the `current_value` read is a few lines and worth doing
    even if the rest is never built.

23. **Concurrent agent threads on one working tree commit over each other** *(observed twice in
    two hours, 2026-08-12; the hazard itself is documented in
    [.claude/rules/git-concurrency.md](../../.claude/rules/git-concurrency.md), which is what
    failed).* A roadmap entry written by one thread was swept into another thread's
    `fix(cr059):` commit, and a `test(cr080):` commit was pushed by an unrelated
    `docs(status):` push. **Nothing was lost either time, and that is the trap** — the rule's
    own advice (explicit pathspecs, verify what landed) was followed by the thread that lost
    its work, because a pathspec commit takes the *worktree* state of the paths it names, and
    another thread's edit to a file you also touched rides along. The cost so far is only an
    audit trail that attributes two changes to the wrong commits; the cost available is a
    half-finished edit committed and pushed by someone else's release. **Options, needing an
    owner decision:** (a) a git worktree per agent thread — real isolation, some setup, and
    `EnterWorktree` already exists; (b) accept it and keep the rule as the mitigation, on the
    grounds that the observed damage is cosmetic. **Documentation is not a third option** —
    it is what was in place when both incidents happened.

## 4. Frontend Improvement Themes (ongoing)

### 4.1 DRY Violations (remaining)

1. ~~Transaction filter logic duplicated~~ — *Resolved* (CR002).
2. `collectCollapsiblePaths()` duplicated in `Balance.jsx` and `BalanceChart.jsx` — move to shared `treeHelpers.js`.
3. ~~Date initialization logic in BudgetInput~~ — *Resolved* via `PeriodSelector`.
4. Month options array recreated in multiple components — move to shared constants.
5. FX rate lookup duplicated in BudgetInput and transaction modals — move to shared `currency.js`.
6. `formatCurrency` redefined locally in 9 files (FCModulesTable, BudgetGraphModal, BalanceReport, CashFlowReport, budgetInputUtils, BudgetRealizationGraph, BalanceChart, CategoryTrend, BudgetVariances/BudgetRealization) despite the canonical export in `utils/formatters.js:30` — consolidate + lint-guard (2026-07-03).

7. **`.page-main`'s default layout is backwards** (found building [CR056](../cr/cr-056-investment-returns.md), 2026-07-26). `PageLayout.css:95-105` makes `.page-main` a hard-coded **two-column grid** (`minmax(0,1.65fr) minmax(320px,0.95fr)`), so every new page silently splits its top-level sections across two tracks unless it opts out. **Five** pages already carry a class whose only job is to undo it (`trans-budget-main`, `refresh-ps-layout`, `balance-grid--single`, `budget-realization-main--single`), and CR056's page shipped visibly broken to dev for exactly this reason — the toolbar and a 12-column table were crushed into the 0.95fr track with half the viewport left empty. Invert it: single-column default, `.page-main--split` opts in. Mechanical, but touches every page's root class, so do it with the app in front of someone.

### 4.2 Missing Shared Components

| Component | Used in | Current state |
|-----------|---------|---------------|
| `<Modal>` | 5+ places | Each modal custom-built |
| `<DataTable>` | 6+ places | Tables custom each time |
| ~~`<FilterPanel>`~~ | 4+ places | Partially via `HierarchyFilter` (CR008) and `PeriodSelector` |
| `<FormField>` | 10+ places | Inputs custom per form |
| `<LoadingSpinner>` | All pages | "Loading..." text varies |
| `<ErrorMessage>` | All pages | Error display inconsistent |
| `<ConfirmDialog>` | 5+ places | Partially via `components/ConfirmModal/` (CR028) |
| ~~`<DateRangePicker>`~~ | 4+ places | Partially via `PeriodSelector` |
| `<CurrencyInput>` | 3+ places | Amount inputs inconsistent |

### 4.3 UI/UX Improvements

| Issue | State | Proposed |
|-------|-------|----------|
| Loading states | Mix of "Loading...", spinners | Unified `<LoadingSkeleton>` |
| Error display | Different per page | Unified `<ErrorBanner>` with retry |
| ~~Empty states~~ | — | *Resolved:* `EmptyState` with 8 unDraw variants, 14 pages |
| Button styles | `.btn` family canonical; legacy `*-btn` families migrating | `Scripts/check-button-css.sh` guardrail |
| Form validation | Scattered | Centralized validation with error messages |
| Date selection | Different controls per page | Partially via `PeriodSelector`; other pages pending |

### 4.4 Performance & TypeScript

- Component memoization for expensive components; virtual scrolling for 1000+ row tables.
- Debounced filters; SWR-style caching for API reads (start with `useCoa`).
- TypeScript migration: utilities → hooks/components → pages.

### 4.5 Decisions Made

| Decision | Choice |
|----------|--------|
| Component library | ~~Radix UI + custom styling~~ → **Selective Radix only** (decided 2026-07-11, CR042/CR043): Dialog/DropdownMenu/Select under existing tokens for the CR042 U4 `<Modal>`; `<DataTable>` and everything else stays hand-rolled |
| Charting | Recharts |
| Mobile support | Responsive breakpoints + dedicated `/m/*` shell |
| State | Enhanced React Context (upgrade to Zustand if complexity grows) |

## 5. Backend Improvement Themes (ongoing)

### 5.1 Service Layer Complexity

> **⚠ Stale as of 2026-07-11 — see [CR043](../cr/cr-043-code-structure-program.md) §Verification:** `cashFlowFetcher.js`/`balanceSheetFetcher.js` no longer exist (their SQL moved *into* `routes/reports.js`, 629 lines); `fcbuilder-module.js` is now 578 lines, `fcbuilder-incexp.js` 271; `services/forecast/index.js` (895) is the biggest service file and missing below. Refreshing this table is CR043 Phase 0.5.

| Service | Lines | Notes |
|---------|-------|-------|
| `fcbuilder-module.js` | 835 | Monolithic; mixes data access with business logic |
| `fcbuilder-incexp.js` | 436 | Duplicated patterns from module builder |
| `cashFlowFetcher.js` | 619 | Complex aggregation, hard to maintain |
| `balanceSheetFetcher.js` | 324 | Could be simplified with SQL views |

Also oversized: `routes/forecast.js` (1,441), `routes/budget.js` (912), `repositories/transactions.js` (826) — split opportunistically when touched.

### 5.2 Missing Abstractions

- **Repository pattern** — partially adopted; continue extracting where services still embed SQL.
- **Input validation** — no schema layer (zod/joi); routes trust `req.body`/`req.query` (e.g. `POST /transactions` passes `req.body` wholesale to `repo.create`). The money-writing 80/20 slice (hand-rolled field whitelists, no new dep) is scoped as [CR037](#cr037) P6; full schema-library adoption stays here.
- **Error handling** — ~~centralize with an `AppError` class~~ *(done CR043 Phase 1.4, 2026-07-11: `server/src/v2/utils/AppError.js`; `validate.js` throws it; `app.js` middleware logs unexpected 5xx)*.

### 5.3 API Design

- Consistent response envelope `{ success, data, meta }` or `{ success, error }`.
- Pagination on list endpoints; standardize `sortBy`/`sortOrder`/`fromDate`/`toDate`.
- Structured logging: ~~via Pino~~ *(pino dropped as unused, CR043 Phase 2.4 — revisit with a lightweight logger over the `console.*` sites if/when observability is prioritized).*

## 6. Testing Strategy

See [test-overview.md](test-overview.md) for the inventory and run commands.

- **Phase 1 — Backend unit/DB tests (Jest, in place):** 226 tests; CI runs them on a fresh migrations+`ci-seed.sql` database (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`).
- **Phase 2 — HTTP smoke tests (in place):** `smoke-after-021.js`; add new smoke scripts for major schema changes.
- **Phase 3 — Frontend unit tests (Vitest, [CR016](#cr016)):** 96 helper tests; hook/component tests deferred.
- **Phase 4 — E2E (Playwright, future):** feed refresh + accept, budget entry, forecast module creation.
